# EXECUTION PLAN — reordered for launch (2026-07-17)

Groups are ordered most-important-first for the alpha release. Completed groups
(43.5, 62-65, 67, 68, 71, 75, 79 and the done phases of 72/76/83) were pruned
on 2026-07-17 after a coverage audit; their doc debt is carried by Group 84
P338. The `.protocol/agent-traits` spec was pulled from the repo pre-launch
(see .protocol/README.md for the standardization intent) — it returns when the
surface stabilizes, tracked as part of the docs-site work in Group 57.

## Status convention (settled 2026-07-27, P486)

**The board (`RUN_PROBLEMS_LOG.md`) is authoritative for phase status. This file is the contract.** A phase's `- [x]` here is a derived convenience for scanning, owner-maintained, and is never written by a run.

That is not a preference, it is a constraint. `/.plans/` is gitignored with no exceptions, so a `--worktree` run physically cannot deliver an edit to this file: the worker writes it inside the worktree, `git add -A` skips it as ignored, the commit carries nothing, and the worktree is pruned with the only copy. This is why every phase read `- [ ]` on 2026-07-27 — including P493, P505, P510, P543, P544, P547 and P548, all of which had landed with real payloads. The mark was never unmaintained; it was **unlandable**. Generating it from the tree would not have helped either, because the generated file cannot land by the same mechanism.

Consequences, and they are binding:
- A run NEVER records phase status. Any trait instructing a scribe to mark this file is instructing a no-op on the worktree path; that instruction is being removed from the implement family's shared scribe rather than left to mislead a reviewer into thinking status was recorded.
- A phase whose DELIVERABLE lands under `/.plans/` or `/.docs/*` cannot be completed by a worktree run at all. Today that is P486, P487, P350's doc half and P533's doctrine half; those are owner- or host-run. The cheap general fix is a `!`-negation per doc deliverable in `.gitignore`, exactly as already exists for `DEEP_RESEARCH_P395_VALIDATION.md`, `CONDITIONAL_SYSTEM_V2.md` and the P372 fixture.
- Checkboxes were reconciled against the tree on 2026-07-27 (210 ticked, 63 open) and are accurate as of that date. They drift only by owner neglect now, not by run behavior.

## Group 84: Built-in trait packages — ship the meta-traits inside the binary (proposed 2026-07-16)

Terminology guard: built-in **vocabulary** (Group 76 — the `modules/core/builtins.toml` slug catalog rendered by `compile.rs`) and built-in **trait packages** (whole executable first-party meta-trait packages) are different things; this group makes the second real and gives both one home under `modules/core/builtins/`. Today `generate`/`refine`/`critique`/`generate-evals` run their meta-traits through `run_builtin_trait` (`modules/cli/src/app/generate.rs:461-560`, hardcoded id→role map `:468-478`), which resolves `layout::trait_manifest_path(".", id)` → `./.ctx/traits/<id>/generated/trait.toml` — **cwd-relative, so the four LLM commands work only inside this repo**; anywhere else the missing package surfaces as a generic IO error dressed as "candidate blocked" (`generate.rs:616-625`). The six first-party meta-traits (`generate-trait`, `refine-trait`, `critique-trait`, `generate-evals`, `explain-trait` (contract-only, never executed), `trait-spec` (the shared dependency — every other declares `source = { path = "../trait-spec" }`; its 57KB schema JSON + rubric are the group's real payload, read from the live sibling package at `run.rs:1195-1301`)) move into the product: authored + compiled under `modules/core/builtins/traits/`, inlined into the binary at build time, materialized to a real per-version store dir at runtime (the resource layer requires real files — canonicalize + no-follow walk, `modules/io/src/resource.rs:704-847`), and resolved everywhere by id with a repo-local `.ctx/traits/<id>` always winning. All other traits (implement-phase, guarded-change, deep-refactor, …) stay in `.ctx/traits/`. *(Count note 2026-07-19: this preamble and the landed P336–P338 text below say "six" — accurate for their moment. The inventory is SEVEN since P315 landed `import-trait` (82c4f68); PRODUCT.md line ~136 is the live authority.)*

- [x] **P335** — Builtins home: split `modules/core/builtins.toml` into per-category files under `modules/core/builtins/vocabulary/`.
      **Why:** one `builtins/` home for everything the binary ships; the flat 15KB `builtins.toml` already spans 16 sections and is about to gain sibling trait packages — splitting per category makes the vocabulary reviewable and the folder self-describing before the packages land next to it.
      **Where:** `modules/core/build.rs` (`SECTIONS` table `:11-76`; reads `builtins.toml` at `:80-86`, emits `$OUT_DIR/builtins.rs`); `modules/core/src/builtins.rs:13` (`include!`); consumer `model_view/compile.rs:453-486`; the current single `modules/core/builtins.toml` (`[intent]`/`[tone]`/`[method]`/`[verbosity]`/`[behavior_*]`/`[intent_*]` sections).
      **Approach:** create `modules/core/builtins/vocabulary/<family>.toml` — one file per section family (intent + intent_require/focus/avoid/block together; the behavior_* axes together; tone/method/verbosity) — moving entries verbatim; build.rs walks the directory (per-file `rerun-if-changed`), concatenates sections in the fixed `SECTIONS` order, and emits `builtins.rs` unchanged.
      **Watch:** `$OUT_DIR/builtins.rs` and the `sdk-generate` mirror (`packages/ts/src/generated.ts`, Group 49 single source) must stay **byte-identical** — this is a pure file reorganization, not a vocabulary change; coordinate with Group 82 (P331–P332 CDK vocabulary casing) which touches the same catalog; build-time only, `modules/core` runtime purity untouched.
      **Done when:** `modules/core/builtins.toml` is gone; the split files under `modules/core/builtins/vocabulary/` produce a byte-identical generated table; `just sdk-check` green; `cargo build` clean.
- [x] **P336** — Move the six meta-trait packages to `modules/core/builtins/traits/<id>/` and inline them into the binary.
      *(Amended 2026-07-17 after run-d73c4380 parked on `unresolved-p336-contract`: the original text predated the v2 package layout and left the P336/P337 resolution boundary and the wasm gate undefined. All three disputed points are now fixed below — owner disposition.)*
      **Why:** the six are product features, not dogfood content — the binary must carry them for the LLM commands to run anywhere, and a crates.io `cargo install` build can only embed files that live inside the publishing crate, so the packages must leave `.ctx/traits/`.
      **Where:** move `.ctx/traits/{generate-trait,refine-trait,critique-trait,generate-evals,explain-trait,trait-spec}/` — each in the CURRENT v2 package shape: `trait.toml` (package manifest) + `source/index.ts` + `generated/{index.toml,index.map}` + `trait.lock` (+ declared `resources/` where present, e.g. trait-spec's `agent-traits.schema.json` + `design-rubric.md`) → `modules/core/builtins/traits/<id>/`; extend `modules/core/build.rs` with a static package table (id → [(rel-path, bytes)] + digests) behind a cargo feature (e.g. `builtin-trait-packages`) enabled by `ctx-traits-io`/CLI, NOT by `modules/wasm-*`; remove the stale `.ctx/traits/vendor/trait-spec/` copy (verification-only, missing its resources anyway — `dependency.rs:483-492`).
      **Approach:** relocate the packages verbatim — dependents keep `source = { path = "../trait-spec" }`, still siblings in the new tree; embed each package's `generated/index.toml` + `trait.toml` + **declared** resources only (`source/index.ts` and `generated/index.map` stay repo-only authoring artifacts, never embedded); add a Justfile drift recipe: per id, `ctx traits build` the package at `modules/core/builtins/traits/<id>` and fail-on-diff against its committed `generated/index.toml`, wired into `just test`. **Runtime-resolution boundary:** P336 changes NO general resolution; the ONLY runtime touch is `run_builtin_trait`'s repo-local candidate list (`modules/cli/src/app/generate.rs:480-482`) gaining `modules/core/builtins/traits/<id>` so the LLM commands keep working in THIS repo after the move — fresh-repo/store resolution stays entirely P337.
      **Watch:** core gains static byte tables + a typed accessor ONLY — no IO, no runtime filesystem access; the embedded set is exactly manifests + declared resources (an undeclared file riding along would violate the trait's own audit posture); dogfood discovery (`discovery.rs:163-216`) will no longer enumerate the six — the P337 built-in surface covers them; editing a builtin then requires a binary rebuild to test through the store — the fast dev loop is a repo-local `.ctx/traits/<id>` override copy (repo-local wins, P337).
      **Done when:** the six packages live under `modules/core/builtins/traits/` in v2 shape; `.ctx/traits/` no longer contains them (nor `vendor/trait-spec`); the binary embeds manifests + declared resources feature-gated; the drift recipe fails on a stale generated `index.toml`; `ctx traits generate --help`-class builtin commands still resolve their driving traits in this repo; **wasm gate (defined):** `builtin-trait-packages` does not appear in `modules/wasm-core`'s or `modules/wasm-plugin`'s resolved feature set (`cargo tree -p ctx-traits-wasm-core -e features | grep -c builtin-trait-packages` = 0) — no bundle-size artifact exists, so feature absence IS the gate.
- [x] **P337** — Materialized builtin store + universal id resolution (repo-local wins).
      *(Amended 2026-07-17 after run parked with six blockers — owner dispositions inline: store root decided, four reproduced defects promoted to hard requirements, fresh-repo LLM gate made environment-independent.)*
      **Why:** embedded bytes are unreadable by the runtime as-is — every resource and dependency read is a real filesystem operation (canonicalize + no-follow component walk `resource.rs:704-847`; dependency hard-join `trait_root.join("../trait-spec")` at `run.rs:1207` with no override) — and id resolution is cwd-only (`resolve_trait_path`, `run.rs:632-659`), so a fresh repo can neither run nor even see a built-in.
      **Where:** new `modules/io/src/builtin_store.rs` (materializer; safe-path conventions from `cache.rs`); **store root DISPOSITION:** a dedicated `layout::BUILTIN_STORE_ROOT = ".ctx/cache/builtin-traits"` — a sibling of `CACHE_ROOT`, NOT beneath `.ctx/cache/traits` (the two caches have different lifecycles; the earlier "under CACHE_ROOT" wording is void); resolution fallback in `resolve_trait_path` (`modules/io/src/run.rs:639-648`) and `run_builtin_trait` (`modules/cli/src/app/generate.rs`); list surface `modules/cli/src/app/lifecycle_reporting.rs`.
      **Approach:** materialize ALL embedded packages as siblings under `.ctx/cache/builtin-traits/<cli-version>/<id>/…` on first use (per-file digest verify; idempotent; refresh on version/digest mismatch) so `../trait-spec` resolves naturally in-store; resolution order everywhere: `.ctx/traits/<id>` if present, else the store; `ctx traits list` gains a `built-in` section (id, version, source=binary) distinct from repo packages; `run-info`/`session start`/`preview`/`check` need no changes beyond resolution — they receive a real file path.
      **Hard requirements (reproduced defects from the first run — each must be demonstrably closed):**
      (1) ONE shared no-follow component-walk guard applied to EVERY store path before it is read or accepted — a store whose `<version>/<id>` directories are symlinks must be rejected, and staging roots are atomically claimed before writing;
      (2) repo-local shadow decided by `symlink_metadata`: ONLY `NotFound` falls through to the store; any existing node at `.ctx/traits/<id>` (including a plain file or unreadable entry) shadows the built-in and must resolve as a valid package or produce the local-package error — never a silent fall-through;
      (3) publication is race-safe under a cross-process lock (reuse/extract the P339 merge-lock primitive): exactly one publisher may verify-and-replace a destination, every waiter re-verifies under the lock — no stale-verification rotation of a freshly published store;
      (4) the store is a READ-ONLY input for every mutation surface: lockfile writes and vendor scaffolding into store roots are centrally rejected (`ctx traits sync <builtin-id>` must refuse to write `trait.lock` into the store or scaffold `.ctx/traits/vendor/` from a store package).
      **Watch:** the repo-local override wins unconditionally (dogfood + user-customization path); the store is generated cache, never canonical source (gitignored under `.ctx/cache`, never reported as a repo package, no lock entries); IO stays in `modules/io` (core exposes bytes only); built-ins pass the same lifecycle/trust gates as any loaded trait (`status = active`/`trust = local`) — shipping in the binary is not a gate bypass. Also remove the orphaned `.ctx/traits/vendor/trait-spec` left behind by P336 (nothing in `.ctx/traits` depends on trait-spec anymore).
      **Done when (LLM gate DISPOSITION — environment-independent):** in a fresh repo with no `.ctx/traits` and no agent config, each of `generate`/`refine`/`critique`/`generate-evals` resolves its driving trait from the store and reaches session dispatch (i.e. fails no earlier than `blocked-agent-unassigned`); provider-backed completion is explicitly NOT this phase's gate (owner-run when credentials matter). Plus: `ctx traits list` shows the six as built-in; a repo-local copy shadows; deleting `.ctx/cache/builtin-traits` self-heals; the four hard requirements each have a demonstrated negative test (symlinked store rejected, non-directory shadow errors, publisher race excluded under lock, sync refuses store writes).
- [x] **P338** — Document + gate the built-in trait family. *(Landed 2026-07-18, HAND-FINISHED: run-35e12ca0 parked needs-owner on six documentation-accuracy blockers — claim-gate discipline working as designed. Doc work harvested from the worktree, all six fixed by hand (five-schema rule, `.agents/traits` sweep completed, guard `empty`/`count` documented per actual validation (local `slot:*` list refs only), union list-wrapped members documented as accepted, package-family definition no longer overstates `[procedure]`, critique target-vs-driver corrected), `claim-gate` green on doc rows; the 3 blocked rows are the standing runtime-family policy gates, owner's call. Docs are gitignored — no merge involved.)*
      **Why:** PRODUCT.md documents `generate`/`refine` as LLM-by-default but never mentions `critique`/`generate-evals` at all, nor where any of these commands' driving traits come from — the code and plan are ahead of the docs, and claim-gate discipline (P249) forbids shipping an undocumented mechanism as a launch surface.
      **Where:** `.docs/PRODUCT.md` (LLM Assistance Policy table ~`:2544`, Authoring/Composition/Refinement table ~`:2518`, and the stale storage-layout section `:85-130` still showing the pre-Group-75 `.agents/traits` layout); `.docs/QUICKSTART.md`; claim-gate wording (`ctx traits claim-gate`).
      **Approach:** add a "Built-in trait packages" PRODUCT.md section: what ships in the binary (the six ids + versions), the embed → materialize → repo-local-override mechanism, and the vocabulary-vs-package terminology guard; document `critique`/`generate-evals` in the command tables; sweep the remaining pre-v2 `.agents/traits` path references across PRODUCT.md to the shipped `.ctx/traits/<id>/{trait.toml, source/, generated/}` layout; add Procedure-section paragraphs for the shipped branch/when step + guard predicates (not/empty/count/scalar comparators) and the schema forms integer/union + write-ops merge/set-field/increment (Groups 63/64 doc debt, carried from the 2026-07-17 prune); update QUICKSTART's first-run flow (the LLM commands now work before any scaffolding).
      **Watch:** claims must match shipped behavior exactly (claim-gate, P249); leave Group 76 "built-in vocabulary" wording intact and cross-link the distinction rather than renaming either concept.

## Group 86: NPM distribution — publishable CDK + shareable trait packages (proposed 2026-07-17)

The launch story's dependency half: `@ctx-traits/cdk` must be installable by strangers, and a trait package must be shareable as an ordinary npm package — "here is an npm package with our agents/resources defined; reuse them anywhere" is the demo. The mechanics half-exist: sync already resolves `[dependencies] npm = "..."` from node_modules (installing via pnpm/npm when missing) and vendors + locks; what's missing is the published CDK and a real end-to-end shared package.

- [ ] **P345** — Publish `@ctx-traits/cdk` to npm (alpha).
      **Why:** every external consumer's first step is `pnpm add -D @ctx-traits/cdk`; today the specifier resolves only through the repo's own pnpm workspace/node_modules link to `packages/cdk` (`modules/io/src/cdk_build.rs` has no repo-local resolver preload — it resolves `@ctx-traits/cdk` the same way any Node import resolves, by walking `node_modules`) — outside this repo, nothing publishes `@ctx-traits/cdk` to a registry, so a fresh checkout with no workspace link has no `node_modules` entry to find and `ctx traits build` dies at module resolution.
      **Where:** packages/cdk (package.json name/version/exports/files, dist build via the existing `just ts-build`); confirm a fresh checkout with no workspace link resolves the published registry copy via node_modules only.
      **Approach:** ship `0.1.0-alpha.x` with dist/ + types; **access DISPOSITION (owner, 2026-07-18): publish RESTRICTED (private) for now** — omit `--access public`; the "installable by strangers" story activates later by flipping access, not by republishing. Verify in a scratch repo **authenticated as the scope owner**: `pnpm add -D @ctx-traits/cdk` → author `source/index.ts` → `ctx traits build` green with NO repo-local preload; npm README points at github.com/ctx-company/ctx-traits (owner-confirmed URL).
      **Watch:** the emitted draft-JSON contract is the compatibility surface — the published version must match the binary's synth expectations (version-pair note in both READMEs); no new deps; no unrelated lockfile churn (first run flagged platform-metadata rewrites — keep package-lock.json deltas to version/metadata only); NOTE: restricted scoped publishes require a paid npm plan — a 402 here means pay-or-public, park with the error, do not flip access unilaterally; feeds P346 and the blog quickstart.
      **Done when:** the restricted package is published and an authenticated clean checkout can author and build a trait from scratch through the registry copy.
- [ ] **P346** — Shareable trait package on npm, end to end (the reuse demo).
      **Why:** the blog promises `import { resources, agents } from "@ctx-company/useful-traits"` and `[dependencies] useful-traits = { npm = ... }` — make both literally true with one published example package, or the composition story is vapor.
      **Where (AMENDED 2026-07-18 — a loop run cannot create a new repo):** the package lives IN THIS REPO at `packages/useful-traits/` (published independently to npm as `@ctx-company/useful-traits`): TS entry exporting reusable `agent(...)`/`resource(...)`/schema handles for authoring-time reuse, plus a complete trait package (root trait.toml manifest + source/ + generated/ + resources/) inside the npm tarball for `[dependencies]` consumption; consumer side already shipped (`dependency.rs` ReadNpm + vendoring + lock).
      **Approach:** publish RESTRICTED (same access disposition as P345; flip later with the CDK); if the `@ctx-company` npm scope is not owned by the logged-in account (403/404), fall back to `@ctx-traits/useful-traits`, log the rename for the blog sweep, and park nothing; in a fresh repo demo BOTH reuse modes **authenticated as the scope owner**: (a) TS import of shared agents/resources into a local trait's source; (b) `[dependencies] useful-traits = { version, npm = "<published scope>/useful-traits" }` → `ctx traits sync` installs via pnpm behind the scenes, vendors, locks, `check` green with dependency digests; capture as the launch demo + blog snippet.
      **Watch:** the vendored subset (canonical doc + resources/) must be what ships in the tarball — files field includes generated/ + resources/; the demo must not depend on this repo's checkout at all.
      **Done when:** a stranger can `pnpm add @ctx-company/useful-traits` and both reuse modes work from the public artifacts alone; blog composition section verified against it (claim-gate).
- [x] **P347** — Git dependency source (post-launch rider).
      **Why:** `[dependencies] x = { git = ... }` currently returns the typed "deferred" error; teams without a registry will ask for it within a week.
      **Where:** `dependency.rs` `SourceAction::CloneGit` arm; literal-argv git clone into a cache under `.ctx/cache/`, ref pinning per the manifest `ref`, then the existing vendor/lock path.
      **Done when:** a git-sourced dependency vendors + locks with a pinned rev recorded; floating branches warn.

## Group 87: Agent-facing trait use over the CLI (proposed 2026-07-17)

Any agent (Claude Code, OpenCode, whatever) should be able to CONSUME a trait's behavior/knowledge with one command — no MCP, no session runtime (deferred deliberately; sessions stay owner tooling for now). The primitive exists (model-view compilation); it needs one ergonomic verb and an instruction pattern.

- [x] **P348** — `ctx traits use <name>`: print a trait's compiled model-visible text for direct agent ingestion.
      **Why:** the lightest possible integration — an AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md line like "before styling terminal output, run `ctx traits use terminal-style` and follow it" gives every CLI-capable agent typed, digest-locked behavior with zero host integration; today the nearest verbs are hidden (`render`) or file-bound.
      **Where:** new visible `Use` variant beside the 12 (surface/cli.rs); resolves by name (`resolve_trait_target` helper); body = the existing model-view compilation (`plan_render_with_resource_body_evidence` → model_view.full_text) — the same bytes check digests.
      **Approach:** plain markdown to stdout: the compiled guidance with on-activation resources inlined and on-demand resources as real path references (the agent opens them itself); `--json` variant carries text + digests for tool use; a short "for agents" README/PRODUCT section documenting the pattern; sessions/frames explicitly NOT part of this surface.
      **Watch:** output is exactly the model-visible artifact (digest-consistent with check — that IS the pitch: what you reviewed is what the agent ingests); no interactive elements; guidance-only traits are first-class here.
      **Done when:** `ctx traits use <any-guidance-trait>` prints clean ingestible markdown; an agent following the documented pattern demonstrably picks up the behavior; 13th visible command documented.

## Group 56: CLI/UX first-run polish (proposed 2026-07-09)
- [x] **P241a** — Shared styled-line helper + `list` on the check grammar. *(Landed 2026-07-18, d44e4b6 — salvaged from run-1555bee9, which parked because the full sweep exceeds one run; tone divergence (list Bold→Default) fixed in the salvage.)*

Purely mechanical; grammar, helper, and four ported precedents are on main. Slice to what one run delivers — if the sweep is still too wide, land per-surface. Done when: listed surfaces two-tone on TTY, `--json`/non-TTY byte-stable, no bare `println!` on ported paths, gates green.
- [x] **P241 (original — COMPLETE via the a/b/c split; all surfaces styled)** — Extend the settled `check` two-tone grammar to ALL commands via a shared styled-line helper — ends "beautiful `check`, plain everywhere."
      **Why:** `check` (and `import`) render a settled two-tone styled report while every other command dumps raw prose — the product looks polished on one screen and unfinished on the ~40 others HN will actually type first.
      **Where (NOTE 2026-07-18: file refs below predate a handler-module split — e.g. check styling now lives in `report_check.rs`, list in `lifecycle_reporting.rs`; treat every path/line here as a HINT and locate the current grammar by grepping `Tone::`/`emit_lines` — the settled check grammar itself is the authority):** the grammar lives in `report_handlers.rs` — `emit_check_report` (`:1807`) branches styled-vs-plain on `stdout_supports_color`, `emit_styled_check_report` (`:1909`) → `styled_check_lines` (`:1921`) → `tui::emit_lines` (`tui.rs:265`), built from `Line`/`Tone` (`tui.rs:42`/`:26`). `Tone::` appears today only in `report_handlers.rs`, `tui.rs`, `run_view.rs`. Extend to the ~385 raw `println!` in `entry.rs` (e.g. `handle_list:85`, plus inspect/cost/hygiene/diff/explain/policy/render/evidence/…) + the ~98 in `report_handlers.rs`.
      **Approach:** lift the styled/plain split into a shared styled-line helper (build `Vec<Line>` → `emit_lines`, fall back to plain when color is unsupported); port each command surface onto it, reusing the check report's two-tone grammar (Muted labels, Default values, Pass/Fail/Warn status); leave `--json` untouched.
      **Watch:** named-ANSI only (green/red/yellow/faint, `tui.rs:14-18`) — no truecolor, no background, so it reads on light + dark alike; `--json` stays byte-stable (styling is TTY-only, gated on `stdout_supports_color`, `tui.rs:162`); this is the scrollback command surface (line/tone renderer), distinct from the Group 57 runner TUI.
      **Done when:** list/inspect/cost/hygiene/diff/explain/policy/render/evidence render in the same two-tone grammar as `check`; non-TTY / `--json` output is unchanged; no bare `println!` remains on a user-facing report path.
- [x] **P242** — DX pre-launch gates: D1 (remove/implement placeholder commands — `init`/`list`), D4 (group the ~45-command flat help into sections), D6 (install: prebuilt binaries + `cargo binstall`/brew), plus a first-run "start here" quickstart.
      **Why:** `init`/`list` are the first commands a new user types, `--help` is a flat wall of ~45 subcommands, and there is no non-`cargo` install path — three first-run cliffs that cost the HN trial in the first two minutes.
      **Where:** D1 — there is **no `Init` variant** in the `Command` enum (`surface/cli.rs:20`; the enum has `List` at `:36` + `TraitsCommand` at `:34`), and `handle_list` (`entry.rs:85`) emits raw prose — decide remove-or-implement `init` and finish `list`. D4 — the flat help across `Command` (`cli.rs:20`), `TraitsCommand` (`:34`), `SessionCommand` (`:1001`) (~45 leaves) needs clap `help_heading` grouping. D6 — a release workflow producing prebuilt binaries + `[package.metadata.binstall]` + a brew formula. Quickstart — a "start here" path (init → check → session).
      **Approach (AMENDED 2026-07-18 — pre-flight caught stale layout + owner-gated done-when):** D1 implement a real `init` scaffolding the CURRENT v2 layout — `.ctx/traits/` (+ optional starter package `trait.toml` + `source/index.ts`), NOT the retired `.agents/traits/` — or cut it and make `list` first-class + styled (P241); D4 group subcommands into author / inspect / run / deps sections via `help_heading` (12 visible commands today; hidden ones stay hidden); D6 author the release workflow + `[package.metadata.binstall]` + a formula FILE; write the first-run quickstart into `--help`/README around the shipped flow (`init → check → run`, not the hidden `session start`).
      **Watch:** D1/D6 are user-facing surface, not core — keep `modules/core` untouched; a `list` restyle must go through the P241 helper (named-ANSI, `--json` byte-stable); Group 60 P249's cold-machine test validates D6 on fresh macOS+Linux.
      **Done when (run-scope vs owner-scope split):** RUN SCOPE — `init` either works (v2 layout) or is gone; `--help` reads as grouped sections; the release workflow + binstall metadata + formula file exist and CI-validate (dry); the quickstart exists. OWNER SCOPE (explicitly NOT this run's gate) — pushing the tag, cutting the first GitHub release, creating the brew tap repo, and the live `cargo binstall`/brew install proof.

## Group 54: Benchmark & evidence (proposed 2026-07-09)
- [ ] **P235** — Corpus + run: fetch a dated, digest-pinned ≥1,000-skill marketplace corpus (owner network access), run the audit harness, and commit the digest manifest + run output — the Show HN number and flag-plant post.
      **Why:** the headline "% hidden-content / drift / near-dup on real marketplace data" claim is the launch hook, and today it is unbacked — only one fixture is committed (`.fixtures/mattpocock/grilling/SKILL.md`); the harness is built + self-test-green but has never seen a real corpus. The owner-network fetch is the single live dependency between here and a real number.
      **Where:** driver = `scripts/audit_marketplace.py` (or the Rust port from Group 51 P219) — `discover_skills` (`scripts/audit_marketplace.py:56`), `audit_one` (`:63`) runs `ctx traits import --source <path> --json` as a dry run and reads `import-report.hidden-content-findings[]` (`:87`) + `synth-provenance.canonical-digest` (`:91`); `aggregate` (`:100`) rolls %-flagged / by-code / by-severity / duplicate-by-digest; `run_audit` (`:160`) writes `audit-report.json`; `just audit-marketplace <corpus>` (`Justfile:25`). Detectors under audit live in `modules/core/src/audit.rs` (`scan_hidden_content:135`) + `model_view/sanitize.rs:66`.
      **Approach:** owner fetches ≥1,000 `SKILL.md` from the public marketplaces (Skills.sh / ClawHub / SkillsMP, per `LAUNCH_STRATEGY.md` §Corpus), snapshots with pull-date + per-source digests; point `--corpus` at the local files (import refuses remote by design); run; capture the JSON report + human summary (`print_summary:138`); commit the corpus **digest manifest** (digests, not payloads) + the run output under evidence.
      **Watch:** import runs dry (no repo writes) and stdout stays valid JSON even when the audit fails the exit code — read stdout only, never merge `2>&1`, never gate on exit code (the `LAUNCH_STRATEGY.md` harness-capture rule); `modules/core` detectors stay pure; byte-stable `--json` is what makes the aggregate reproducible from the committed manifest; this is an owner-run job (network + marketplace access), not a headless CI step.
      **Done when:** a dated, digest-pinned ≥1,000-skill corpus is audited end-to-end; the digest manifest + report are committed; the %-hidden-content / drift / near-dup headline numbers are real and re-derivable from the committed manifest.
      **Watch (cont.):** harness ships today (self-test green); the Rust port (Group 51 P219/P229) is an optional future replacement, not a prerequisite; feeds Group 60 P249 (flag-plant + Show HN) and Group 59 P247 (matrix).
- [ ] **P236** — Detector precision/recall: a labeled known-good/known-bad fixture set to characterize false-negative/false-positive rates; fix the documented base64 false-negative; report honest precision/recall.
      **Why:** "what's your false-negative rate?" is the first adversarial HN question, and a short base64 blob is already documented as slipping past `base64-blob` — shipping a catch-rate claim without a labeled set is over-claiming, and the honest P/R number is itself a small publishable "how good is the audit" result.
      **Where:** detectors in `modules/core/src/audit.rs` — `scan_hidden_content` (`:135`) dispatches `scan_bidi_controls` (`:425`), `scan_homoglyphs` (`:458`, range `0x0370..=0x052F` at `:471`), `scan_base64_blobs` (`:487`); the documented false-negative is the >80-char length gate in `scan_base64_blobs` (`:495`) — a short blob never fires. Also `model_view/sanitize.rs:66`. The caveat is recorded in `LAUNCH_STRATEGY.md` ("Detector-sensitivity caveat", ~`:91`).
      **Approach:** build a labeled fixture set (known-clean + known-bad, one file per injection class: zero-width / bidi / homoglyph / base64 / data-url / html-comment / shell-substitution), each tagged with its expected finding `code`; run the detectors; compute per-code precision + recall; tune the base64 length/entropy threshold (and homoglyph coverage) so the short-blob case fires without wrecking precision.
      **Watch:** `modules/core` stays pure — fixtures are read via IO, detectors consume text only; move recall without tanking precision and report **both** sides (no cherry-picking); the labeled set doubles as the P238 fuzz-seed corpus and a regression guard.
      **Done when:** a committed labeled set yields per-code precision/recall; the documented base64 false-negative now fires as `base64-blob`; the audit's catch rate is publishable with honest two-sided numbers.
      **Watch (cont.):** the detectors themselves are already shipped (`modules/core/src/audit.rs`); feeds P238 (fuzz seeds) and the P237 comparison arm.
- [ ] **P237** — Comparison arm + secondary/perf benchmarks: a markdown-lint / native-Claude-Code-review baseline (catches ≈0 → "the gap is the result"), plus activation precision/recall, drift %, and cheap perf/parity numbers.
      **Why:** the audit number only lands next to a baseline that catches ≈0 of the hidden content — the gap *is* the result; the secondary throughput / bundle-size / native==wasm numbers pre-empt the "is it fast / is the wasm real" follow-ups.
      **Where:** comparison arm = run a markdown linter + a native Claude-Code review over the same P235 corpus (expected ≈0 hidden-content catches, per `LAUNCH_STRATEGY.md` "Comparison arm"); drift % from the `check` drift sections (`report_handlers.rs` `drift_summary:1049`) and `eval_run.rs` GoldenRender (`modules/core/src/eval_run.rs:120-122`); perf = parse/check throughput via `encoding::decode_trait` (`encoding.rs:162`) + `scan_hidden_content` (`audit.rs:135`) over the corpus; WASM bundle size + native-vs-wasm digest parity via `modules/wasm-core/src/abi.rs` (`audit_json:311`, `normalize_json:290`).
      **Approach:** per corpus file, record what markdown-lint / native review flag vs what `ctx` flags → the gap table; compute activation precision/recall on a labeled activation set; aggregate drift %; time parse/check throughput (wall-clock, no criterion rig); measure the built wasm artifact size and byte-compare `audit_json`/`normalize_json` against native for the pure paths.
      **Watch:** keep perf "cheap" (throughput, not a micro-benchmark harness); byte-stable `--json` so the gap table is diffable; native==wasm parity is a **correctness** claim — any mismatch is a bug, not a footnote — and is the seed of Group 55 P240.
      **Done when:** the gap table shows baseline≈0 vs ctx's catch; activation P/R + drift % + throughput + wasm size + native==wasm parity are all reported and reproducible from the committed corpus.
      **Watch (cont.):** the parity check hands off to Group 55 P240.

## Group 91: Trait variants — families, colon resolution, `plan` + `refactor` lineups (proposed 2026-07-17) — open remainder rehomed to Group 103 (2026-07-21)

First-class variants: a family is a set of packages named `<family>-<variant>` with an optional bare `<family>` package as the default variant. Resolution is naming-convention based (`ctx traits run plan:quick` desugars to package id `plan-quick`; `plan:default` → bare `plan`); `[metadata] family/variant` is display-only (list grouping + consistency advisories, never activation). Decisions locked with owner: one package per variant on disk (no single-source/multi-emit CDK change), colon syntax only, mechanism + renames now with refactor-quick/style deferred. Design-verified facts: ONE desugar seam (`resolve_trait_path`, `modules/io/src/run.rs:649`) covers run/check/preview/export/explain/critique/refine/compose/MCP/session-start because the CLI seam (`command_handlers.rs:1089`) delegates extension-less names to it; one bypass exists (`resolve_trust_subject`, `lifecycle_reporting.rs:214-220`, resolves `trait:<id>` via `layout::trait_manifest_path` directly); zero goldens regenerate (no golden fixture declares `[metadata]`; absent `Option` fields serialize to nothing); CDK metadata is an opaque draft-JSON passthrough (typing change only, Rust synth/encode picks up new fields automatically).

- [x] **P355** — `[metadata] family/variant` fields + consistency advisories.
      **Why:** families need a declared identity for grouping/display; convention-vs-metadata mismatch should be visible without ever breaking loads.
      **Where:** `modules/core/src/trait/metadata.rs` — add `family: Option<Slug>` + `variant: Option<Slug>` as the first two `Metadata` fields (`#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]`); extend `validate_taxonomy()` with `check("metadata.family")`/`check("metadata.variant")` (slug validation = decode-time error, same as other taxonomy slugs). `modules/core/src/check.rs` — new `family_variant_advisories(trait_id, metadata) -> Vec<CheckWarning>` next to `resource_root_advisories` (advisory only, never pass/fail): section `"metadata"`, codes `metadata-variant-without-family` (variant without family) and `metadata-family-id-mismatch` (with variant V: expected id is `F` when `V=="default"` else `F-V`; family alone: id must be `F` or start with `F-`). `modules/cli/src/app/report_check.rs` `build_check_report` — extend `check_warnings` right after the `resource_root_advisories` call (~:124); renderers print warnings generically, no renderer change.
      **Watch:** byte-stability — absent options must emit nothing in canonical TOML/JSON (existing traits' digests unchanged); `Metadata` has no `deny_unknown_fields` so old binaries ignore the new keys (no schema-version bump).
      **Done when:** inline `mod tests` cover decode, bad-slug rejection with field path, absent-field byte-stability (metadata.rs) and advisory rules incl. `default` special case + hyphenated families (check.rs); goldens pass unchanged.

Lesson: done-whens must be satisfiable at the phase's queue position.)*
      **Why:** `plan:quick` everywhere for free; a bare family name that isn't a package should teach, not 404.
      **Where:** `modules/core/src/shared.rs` — pure helper `desugar_variant_ref(value, field_path) -> Result<Option<String>>` next to `validate_slug_shape` (:377): no colon → `Ok(None)`; one colon → validate both halves as slugs (`{field_path}.family`/`.variant`), `family:default` → bare family, else `family-variant`; multiple colons → shape-mismatch error. `modules/io/src/run.rs` `resolve_trait_path` (:649) `(None, Some(id))` arm — desugar BEFORE `validate_slug_shape` (:657); on not-found, derive the family (left colon half, else raw id), scan `crate::discovery::trait_inventory_ids(".")` (discovery.rs:145 — sorted/deduped, includes source-only) for `id == family` (report `default`) or prefix `family-`; any hits → error listing `available variants in the "<family>" family: … — run as <family>:<variant>`, else current not-found message. `modules/cli/src/app/lifecycle_reporting.rs` `resolve_trust_subject` (:214-220) — apply the desugar after `strip_prefix("trait:")` so `trust trait:plan:quick` resolves.

- [x] **P377** — The variant axis: `quick` / `default` / `smart` / `strict` — shared doctrine (owner direction 2026-07-19, first draft). *(Landed 2026-07-21, ae54d6b.)*
      **Why:** every executing family should offer the same four-position dial, so users learn it once: the axis is a REVIEWER-AUTHORITY LADDER over the plan (owner refinement 2026-07-19): **quick** — one pass, one reviewer, cheap models; the reviewer may FORGIVE problems, even ones declared in the plan, when it judges them unreachable or immaterial in practice (forgiveness is recorded with a one-line reason, never silent); **default** — dual review; the reviewer strictly enforces the plan's declared MUST/MUST-NOT lists — the explicit lists are the whole bar: no forgiveness on them, no inventions beyond them; **smart** — frontier models + research-informed drafting; the reviewer may AMEND THE PLAN itself when it finds contradictions, using best judgment — every amendment is a typed record (what changed, the contradiction it resolves, rationale) carried in the verdict so the landed contract is the amended one, with provenance; **strict** — ZERO drift: the plan must be FULLY satisfied verbatim; the worker may not deviate, reviewers block ANY departure (even improvements), each would-be deviation becomes a typed deviation report, and an unsatisfiable plan means ABORT (or raise the HITL need once Group 124 lands) — never silent adaptation. When Group 124 (HITL) lands, strict-mode deviation reports become live intercept points: the human approves or rejects each departure mid-run.
      **Where:** ONE shared doctrine fragment per level in `@ctx-traits/agents` (`packages/agents`) — `VARIANT_DOCTRINE = { quick, default, smart, strict }` prompt fragments + the `deviation-report` schema (id, planned, actual, rationale, disposition) — no per-family duplication; families import and compose.
      **Watch:** the axis dials PLAN-FIDELITY and EFFORT, never review integrity — reviewers block genuine defects at every level (quick's single reviewer still blocks; strict's reviewers additionally block departures). `smart` ≠ more rounds; it's better models + a deeper draft. Cross-ref Group 124 (HITL consumes the deviation report).
      **Done when:** the four fragments + the deviation-report AND plan-amendment schemas export from `@ctx-traits/agents`; each level's fragment states its authority rule in one paragraph (forgive-with-reason / enforce-lists / amend-with-record / verbatim-or-abort); doctrine text is generic pseudocode-flavored per house rules; consumed by P362/P363 without edits.
- [x] **P362** — `refactor` family on the axis (first draft): `refactor:{quick,default,smart,strict}` + `direct` kept as the annotation-driven special. Reviewer authority per level follows P377's ladder (forgive / enforce-lists / amend / verbatim-or-abort). *(Landed 2026-07-21, 9a5da90.)*
      **Approach:** `refactor-deep` is RENAMED `refactor-default` (the axis replaces the old deep/style naming; `deep` was always this family's default — one `git mv` + metadata/id sweep, list keeps grouping); **quick** = survey-free checklist from the task → implement → ONE review round → commit (cheap models via P378 profile); **default** = current deep shape (survey → plan → implement → dual-review loop → commit); **smart** = default with frontier models + a research-informed survey step; **strict** = default consuming the P377 strict doctrine + deviation-report slot. `refactor-direct` (ctx-annotate-driven) stays as-is — it is an INPUT-MODE variant, orthogonal to the effort axis. The old `refactor-style` idea is DROPPED from the lineup (owner 2026-07-19); a behavior-preserving style pass becomes a candidate `@ctx-traits/rust`-style pack concern, not a family variant.
      **Done when:** four axis packages + direct build/check green from shared doctrine; `ctx traits list` groups all five under `refactor`; `refactor:strict` given a draft with a deliberate suboptimality executes it verbatim and files the deviation report; `refactor:quick` lands a trivial fix in a single review round.
- [ ] **P363** — `implement` family on the axis (first draft): `implement:{quick,default,smart,strict}`. Reviewer authority per level follows P377's ladder (forgive / enforce-lists / amend / verbatim-or-abort).
      **Approach:** the existing lean `implement` trait BECOMES `implement-quick` (it already is the single-reviewer fast shape); **default** = the implement-phase procedure published as `implement-default` (clerk extraction → draft → implement → dual-review loop → commit; implement-phase itself stays the in-repo dogfood driver and becomes a thin consumer or alias of the family default — decide at implementation, bias to alias to avoid drift); **smart** = default + frontier models + a pre-draft research step reading the repo like the deep-research probe pattern (Group 97); **strict** = default + P377 strict doctrine (drafts execute verbatim; deviations become typed reports — the mode for regulated/high-stakes changes and for consuming machine-generated plans that were separately ratified). Family metadata + colon resolution throughout; Justfile stays on the dogfood driver until the alias question settles.
      **Done when:** four packages build/check green from shared doctrine + `@ctx-traits/agents` roles; `implement:quick "Pxxx"` completes with one review round on a trivial phase; `implement:strict` files a deviation report on a deliberately-imperfect draft; `list` groups the family.
      **Watch (cont.):** per-variant routing/budgets ride the landed P312 budget sidecars + config role assignment (P378 superseded).
- [x] **P378** — Variant-aware profiles + recipes. *(SUPERSEDED 2026-07-22, run branch NOT landed — owner-ordered hand-merge of the parked wave stopped here.)*
      **Disposition:** the goal (per-variant routing + budgets) was reached by other landed work while run-cf560c8e sat parked: per-variant models/budgets live in trait-package `config.toml` budget sidecars (P312, 7d862a7) + `.ctx/config.toml` role assignment, and P314 (28f48b1) retired the run-profile file mechanism this phase's `.ctx/runs/profiles/*.toml` implementation is built on (P426 then moved `.ctx/runs` out of the checkout entirely). Landing the branch would have deleted the three live budget sidecars, re-added a profiles dir in a retired location, and replaced `OutputTokenCounter` with a rewrite that predates the b94aeb4 0-token delegation fix — i.e. reverted three landed decisions. Branch `ctx/run/wt-4808bfacbff8` (2c4093c) left parked for reference; prune once P445 lifts the salvage.
      **Salvage → P445:** the branch's two still-novel strands (ledger budget/token evidence, narrator usage accounting) are re-scoped there against current substrate.
- [x] **P396** — Single-package variant families: `variants` map on `trait()` + `variant.import()` — design SETTLED 2026-07-21 (owner), build to spec.
      **Why:** the landed mechanism is one package per variant — correct for artifacts, repetitive for authors and heavy in the store (the implement axis alone is four near-identical packages plus a shared.ts reached via cross-package relative imports, the exact class P436 errors on). Owner vision, settled in the 2026-07-21 brainstorm: ONE package whose trait declares its variants directly.
      **Where:** CDK: `trait(id, { ..., variants: { default: variant.import("./variant/default.ts").default(), quick: variant.import("./variant/quick.ts"), ... } })`. `variant.import(path)` is a thin convenience wrapper over dynamic `import()` returning a chainable handle; `.default()` marks the family default ON THE FAMILY MAP — never inside the variant file (a variant does not know it is the default). Map KEYS name the variants (`implement:quick` from key "quick"); variant files export a COMPLETE definition (name/summary/intent/behavior/procedure — everything but id and version, which the family owns) via a `variant({...})` constructor; sharing stays in userland TS (package-internal `./shared.ts`), never overlay/merge semantics in canonical. Maps are NESTABLE: a map value may be another map, keys join with `:` (`implement:smart:x`), and exactly ONE `.default()` is required per level so every bare prefix resolves. Emitter: one entry module fans out one canonical PER VARIANT to `generated/<variant-path>/index.{toml,map}`. Canonical identity is FIRST-CLASS: `id = "implement"` plus a new `variant = "quick"` field (canonical schema-version bump) — never a composite id string. Package manifest gains the variant table (paths + default); ONE variant-keyed trait.lock; ONE family version. Resolution: bare family → the `.default()` variant (this also fixes the current family resolver refusing bare names), `family:variant[:sub]` selects; the landed name-convention desugar stays as the fallback for existing multi-package families; a family package AND name-convention sibling packages for the same family name is a `check` ERROR, never a silent preference.
      **Watch:** per-variant canonical digest and per-machine trust are UNCHANGED — the trust model does not bend for families (five variants = five approvals). Byte-stability for every existing variantless package (no `variants` field ⇒ output byte-identical). `check` reports per-variant; `list` groups the family. Migration stays OPT-IN per family (P450 does implement).
      **Done when:** a demo family authored as a variants map builds N canonicals from one entry; bare name resolves to the `.default()` variant and `family:variant` selects, including one nested level; each variant canonical carries first-class `variant` identity; every pre-existing package's canonical bytes and digests are untouched; the family/sibling coexistence conflict is a check error; `list` and `check` present the family coherently.
      **Watch (cont.):** P450 migrates the implement family on top of this once it lands.

Verification for the group: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` (no new `#[allow]`); `just test` with goldens passing UNCHANGED (byte-stability proof); `just sdk-check` (expected no-op) + `just ts-build`/`ts-typecheck`/`ts-lint`/`ts-test`; live smoke: `check refactor:deep`, `check refactor` (variant listing), `check plan:quick`, `list` grouping, `check --file … --json` byte-identical pre/post for traits without family metadata.

## Group 92: Run reliability — harness/dir/config failure modes (proposed 2026-07-17)

Root-caused from the 13:22 parallel batch (4 runs) + ctx-gate: workers (claude-code, per-frame) wrote to run worktrees correctly, but every opencode frame operated on the MAIN checkout — `opencode run` anchors to its server's project and ignores the spawn cwd (ctx's `apply_exec_dir` sets child cwd correctly; opencode disregards it). Reviewers judged a clean main tree, escalated `needs-owner`, stop-if parked (mislabeled as `signal:refinement-exhausted`). Separately, scribe frames 404'd: ctx.toml routes scribe to opencode, but the per-trait profiles overrode scribe to claude-code while its model is an openrouter id the claude CLI cannot serve (4 retries → `harness-failed` at summarization → approved work never committed).

- [x] **P364** — scribe→opencode profile routing: `[assign.scribe] harness = "opencode"` in `.ctx/runs/profiles/{implement-phase,implement,capture-task,refactor-complex}.toml` (models unchanged; clerk already proves the openrouter id works on opencode). Shipped 2026-07-17.
- [x] **P365** — opencode execution-dir pinning: `HarnessCliConvention.dir_flag` (modules/io/src/harness_config.rs), `harness_argv` appends `[dir-flag, exec-dir]` on every cold/persistent frame argv (modules/cli/src/app/drive.rs, all five build sites), `dir-flag = "--dir"` in ctx.toml `[harness.opencode.cli]`. No-op when unset or for non-worktree runs; claude-code keeps cwd-only (proven correct). Shipped 2026-07-17 (landed a3cea07). Companion fix same day: `--auto` added to `[harness.opencode.cli]` argv in ctx.toml — headless parity with claude's `--dangerously-skip-permissions`; without it, `--dir`-pinned frames auto-REJECT the external_directory prompt when reading the main repo's untracked `.plans`/`.docs` from a worktree (run-dc67960c died `harness-output-invalid` on exactly this; live-verified fixed). Explicit denies in opencode config still win over `--auto`.
- [x] **P366** — Fail fast on invalid model/harness combos. *(Landed 2026-07-18, d54edb5 — dual-approved via session RESUME after the classifier content-sniff deadlock (b903c9e, entry 33); NOTE the resume ran on main, not the worktree — see P376.)*
      **Why:** run-3a48a246 burned 4 retries on a deterministic 404 `model_not_found` and parked AFTER dual approval — a config error should fail at frame 1 (or before) naming role/harness/model, not exhaust retries mid-run.
      **Where:** drive preflight next to the existing probe/model-flag guards (modules/cli/src/app/drive.rs ~:569-609): on the first frame for each (harness, model) pair, treat a definitive model-resolution error from the harness (claude `api_error_status: 404` / `error: model_not_found` in the result event) as non-retryable — fail the run immediately with `blocked-harness-model-invalid` and the offending `role@harness model=<id>`. Optionally a static allowlist check (claude-code accepts only anthropic-style ids) at profile resolution for zero-spawn detection.
      **Done when:** a role routed to claude-code with an openrouter model id blocks at frame 1 with the named combo and zero retries; valid combos unaffected.
- [x] **P367** — Distinct park signal for stop-if matches.
      **Why:** a stop-if match at iteration 0 currently emits the loop's `signal:refinement-exhausted` — "exhausted" is false (nothing was exhausted) and hides escalation parks from tooling that reads emitted signals.
      **Where:** runtime loop control (core procedure session loop handling): emit a dedicated `signal:stop-if-matched` (or reuse the stop reason in the emitted signal's ref) distinct from the exhausted signal; keep `stop-reason: stop-if-matched` as-is. CLI status output prints the escalation blocker ids when parked by stop-if. Cross-ref P361 (implement-phase v0.11 flat tail — the stop-if IS the park).
      **Done when:** a stop-if park's `emitted-signals` no longer claims refinement-exhausted; attempts-exhausted still does.
- [x] **P373** — Worktree setup commands: `[worktree] setup = ["pnpm install"]`.
      **Why:** post-P369, fresh run worktrees lack node_modules — `just test`'s builtin drift gate fails until someone runs pnpm install (P356's salvage hit this; the P369-added error message pointed straight at it). Seeding can't help (node_modules is huge and machine-specific); the worktree needs a setup hook.
      **Where:** `WorktreeConfig` gains `setup: Vec<Vec<String>>` (literal argv lists, same anti-shell posture as command steps); `prepare_worktree` runs them after seeding, failures surface as worktree-preparation errors; implement profiles declare `setup = [["pnpm", "install", "--prefer-offline"]]`.
      **Done when:** a fresh `just implement` worktree passes `just test` with no manual install; setup failures block the run with a typed error naming the command.
- [x] **P376** — Resume restores the worktree execution dir. *(Landed 2026-07-18, 194e93e — clean loop landing; the run missed its own checkbox tick, corrected by hand 2026-07-19.)*
      **Why:** `ctx traits drive --session <ledger>` on a worktree run ignores the ledger's worktree provenance — the resumed frames (incl. git-stage/git-commit) executed in the MAIN checkout (P366's landing, entry 34). It worked out because main was clean and the commit was dual-approved, but a dirty main would have absorbed unrelated state into a phase commit, and the original worktree becomes an orphan.
      **Where:** the standalone drive entry (drive.rs `prepare_standalone_worktree` / DriveInputs.execution_dir): when the ledger records worktree provenance and no explicit override is given, resolve and re-enter that worktree as the execution dir (verify registration like merge preflight does); refuse with a typed error if the worktree is gone.
      **Done when:** resuming a worktree run's session executes frames in that worktree (command evidence paths prove it); resuming with the worktree deleted fails typed instead of silently running in main.
- [x] **P375** — Dependency pre-flight at dispatch: refuse to start what cannot finish.
      **Why:** ctx-gate run-125ca22e burned a full 10-round budget re-verifying a byte-identical tree because the dispatched phase's declared prerequisites (4a-flow, 4a-gov) had not landed — knowable from the phase text BEFORE frame one. The owner's no-early-exit rule stands: loops never self-terminate; instead the RUN never starts. Refusal at dispatch spends zero and prints the same sentence the reviewers spent ten rounds writing.
      **Where:** the clerk/phase-extraction boundary or a pre-session static check in the implement flow: parse the extracted phase section's `Deps:`/prerequisite lines for phase ids; verify each is checked `[x]` in the plan (and, where a commit is named, present in history); on failure exit before session start with "phase <X> declares unlanded prerequisites: <list> — dispatch those first".
      **Watch:** advisory-strict balance — only refuse on EXPLICIT phase-id prerequisites (`Deps: P…`/named sub-phases), never on prose inference (no phantom-rule reintroduction); an owner override flag (`--force-dispatch`) exists and is recorded in the ledger.
      **Done when:** dispatching a phase whose named Deps are unchecked refuses before any frame with the listed ids; `--force-dispatch` proceeds and records the override; a phase with landed deps dispatches normally.
- [x] **P374** — Credit-exhaustion pause: runs survive an empty wallet.
      **Why:** run jWCIRFsk died `harness-failed` when OpenRouter hit 402 mid-frame — four retries burned the dying balance, the run became terminal, and the operator had to diagnose a transcript to learn the cause was money. A person running out of credits/subscription mid-run should get a PAUSE and a plain sentence, not a corpse: inform → pause → top up → resume where it stopped.
      **Where:** harness output classification (modules/io/harness.rs / harness_stream parsing); drive loop retry policy + a new resumable outcome; CLI messaging; Justfile batch exit handling.
      **Approach:** (a) classify deterministic payment/quota errors per provider (OpenRouter 402 "requires more credits", provider quota/subscription-exhausted shapes) as a typed `ProviderCreditsExhausted` — NEVER retried (each retry spends the dying balance; P366 sibling); (b) the run transitions to a RESUMABLE paused state (session non-terminal, frame position persisted — the BlockedCommandPermissionRequired precedent), drive outcome `paused-provider-credits`; (c) CLI prints exactly what a person needs: which provider, which role/frame, the top-up URL, and the one resume command (`ctx traits drive <session>`); (d) resume re-enters at the same frame with a fresh attempt budget; (e) the batch recipe treats this exit distinctly (pause ≠ failure — print the resume line, stop cleanly).
      **Done when:** a stub harness returning the 402 shape pauses on the FIRST occurrence with zero retries; the printed message names provider/frame/resume-command; `ctx traits drive` on the paused session resumes at the same frame and completes once the stub stops 402ing; the batch script surfaces "paused (credits)" distinctly from failure.
- [x] **P397** — Command output routing, understandable at first glance: `schema:text` receives the command's raw stdout; `schema:any`/absent keep the whole envelope; typed schemas keep parsed JSON (P351). Then family tails skip the commit on clean trees with a plain `equals ""` guard.
      **Why (owner decision 2026-07-19):** a dual-approved run whose work leaves the tracked tree untouched (docs-only phases, already-present work) currently marches into scribe → stage → `git commit`, which fails on the clean tree and kills the run AFTER approval. The clean-tree check is expressible today only by byte-matching the report envelope (`format_command_report`, io run.rs:1400) — a load-bearing magic constant coupled to the envelope's Debug formatting (`Some(0)`). Root cause: `schema:text` slots receive the ENVELOPE, not the text the command printed — surprising at first glance. Owner's rule replaces it: **text means what the command printed; any/absent means the full report envelope; a typed schema means parsed JSON** — one line, no special cases.
      **Where:** modules/io/src/run.rs `advance_command_frames` (~1215): route `Builtin::Text` output slots like the typed branch but submit `Value::String(outcome.stdout)` VERBATIM — no JSON parse, no trim (leading porcelain spaces are significant; commands that end in a newline keep it, and guard literals must match — document this); stderr + truncation facts go to submission warnings exactly as the typed path already does (run.rs:1309-1319). `schema:any` and absent-schema slots keep `format_command_report` byte-for-byte. No evidence loss: exit-code/timed-out/argv already persist per call in `CommandExecutionEvidence` (session.rs:483) independent of the slot.
      **Migration (breaking for text slots):** every existing text command slot (stage-output, commit-output across implement/refactor families here + the ctx-gate copy) now holds raw stdout instead of the envelope — audit each consumer (today they are terminal evidence sinks; no guard or prompt reads envelope text); regenerate goldens; canonical bytes/digests unchanged (runtime-behavior change under a new binary — changelog note).
      **Then (same phase):** implement/refactor family tails gain a leading `git status --porcelain` command step into a `slot.text` + a branch item `if: not(equals(git-status, ""))` whose then-arm holds the existing summarize/stage/commit trio, no otherwise-arm — a clean tree falls through and the run completes without invoking the scribe.
      **Done when:** a run over an untouched tree completes with the scribe never invoked and no commit attempted; a dirty tree commits exactly as today; `schema:any`/absent-schema command-slot goldens byte-identical; text-slot goldens show raw stdout; the family guard literal is `""` (no envelope constants anywhere in family sources).
      **Watch (cont.):** deferred follow-up, only if P385/P391 need exit-code guards beyond success-exit-code + on-failure: a typed envelope object schema (`schema.commandEnvelope(...)`) populating {exit-code, stdout, stderr, timed-out, truncated} as real fields.
- [x] **P401** — `ctx traits merge` skips the LLM merger on a clean fast-forward; the seed harvest merges three-way instead of parking on any difference; stale-base overlap lands by default. (owner-ordered 2026-07-20; overlap-default clause added 2026-07-21, owner-ordered)
      **Why:** two costs surfaced the same evening (RUN_PROBLEMS_LOG entry 71). (a) Every clean landing still pays merger latency + tokens: `[agent.merger]` (a resolved harness/model/reasoning-effort in `merge.rs`) is dispatched even when git reports a pure fast-forward with zero conflicts — there is nothing for a model to decide, yet the run waits on and bills a full LLM turn. (b) The post-merge SEED HARVEST (`harvest_seeds`, `modules/io/src/worktree.rs:689`) parks the whole merge as `post-merge-cleanup-failure` the instant a gitignored seed file (`.docs`, `.plans`) differs on both sides — it compares base/main/worktree digests for EQUALITY ONLY, with no content merge — even when the two edits are DISJOINT hunks a three-way merge resolves cleanly. Live case: `run-5bf27fc3…` landed the fast-forward, then parked because `.docs/PRODUCT.md` had P344's paragraph on the worktree side and P342's new section on main — non-overlapping, hand-reconciled in minutes. For a file nearly every phase edits, park-on-any-difference recurs daily.
      **Where:** (a) `modules/cli/src/app/merge.rs` — before resolving/dispatching `[agent.merger]`, detect the fast-forward-with-no-conflicts case (git already knows: the merge base is an ancestor of the incoming ref and the target has not diverged) and take a deterministic fast path that fast-forwards without a model, recording in the ledger that the merger was skipped as clean (not that it ran). The LLM merger stays the path for genuine divergence. (b) `modules/io/src/worktree.rs` `harvest_seeds` — when base ≠ main AND base ≠ worktree, attempt a real three-way merge (`git merge-file -p` / libgit2 merge with the `SeedSnapshot` as the ancestor it already captured at prepare time) and write the merged result; park ONLY on a genuine overlapping (conflict-marker) hunk, reporting which file and which region. (c) `merge.rs` stale-base overlap check (P343 lineage) — flip the default: a detected overlap (branch and main both changed the same paths since the branch's base) no longer parks; the merge proceeds and records the SAME overlap evidence in the ledger that the park used to carry, so the audit trail is undiminished. Parking on overlap becomes opt-in via a `--park-on-overlap` (name at implementer's discretion) strictness flag; `--allow-stale-overlap` remains accepted as a deprecated no-op alias for one release. Genuine git conflicts still route to the merger/park exactly as in (a)/(b) — this clause changes only the policy precaution, never conflict handling.
      **Watch:** the fast path must be provably identical in OUTCOME to the current merger on the clean case — same landed rev, same ledger effect minus the model turn — verified by a test that lands a clean FF both ways and diffs the ledger. The three-way harvest must never silently drop a side: an unmergeable hunk still parks with the same `changed independently on both sides` code + the conflicting region named. `--strict`/an owner flag to force the LLM merger even on a clean tree is acceptable if a caller ever wants the model's eyes; default is skip. No canonical/digest/trait change — this is runtime + merge tooling only.
      **Done when:** a clean fast-forward merge lands with NO `[agent.merger]` dispatch (ledger records the skip) and byte-identical result to the LLM path on the same input; a merge whose only seed divergence is disjoint hunks in `.docs`/`.plans` HARVESTS the three-way result and completes instead of parking; a merge with a genuinely conflicting seed hunk still parks with the file + region named; a truly divergent (non-FF) git merge still routes to the LLM merger unchanged; a merge with a detected stale-base overlap but no git conflict LANDS by default with the overlap evidence recorded in the ledger, and the strictness flag restores the old park.
- [x] **P372** — Rule provenance: the product-brief may not mint law.
      **Why:** run-5740e135 burned a full 10-round budget because the clerk distilled a stale July-5 snapshot doc into a standing "no cargo test" rule and both reviewers enforced the phantom over the phase's own DoD. The clerk can currently elevate ANY .docs content — stale, point-in-time, or simply unrequested — into rules; the user should never have to untangle contradictions the pipeline invented.
      **Where:** implement-phase (and siblings) product-extraction prompt + resource declarations; reviewer instructions; optionally a typed `rule` schema for the product-brief.
      **Approach:** (a) the distillation step draws standing rules ONLY from resources the trait declares rule-authoritative (PRODUCT.md; explicitly NOT the rest of .docs — reference material may inform, never bind); (b) every distilled rule carries its source path + section inline; (c) reviewer instructions: before using any house rule in a blocker, verify its citation against the authoritative source — a rule you cannot source is not a rule, drop it; (d) blockers that cite a rule quote the source line. Consider a typed product-brief (list of {rule, source} objects) so (b)-(d) are structural rather than prose discipline.
      **Done when:** a fixture doc under .docs containing a plausible-but-false "rule" can no longer surface in any verdict blocker; every rule in a live run's product-brief carries a source; blockers citing rules quote them.
- [x] **P368** — Harvest gitignored context back from worktrees at merge. *(Landed 2026-07-18.)*
      **Why:** `[worktree] seed` (now declared in the implement profiles: `seed = [".plans", ".docs"]` — the mechanism existed since P267-era but no profile ever configured it) copies untracked context INTO the worktree, but writes to those copies are destroyed with the worktree at merge cleanup — gitignored files never merge. Any phase that authors/updates docs inside a worktree run silently loses that work.
      **Where:** `ctx traits merge` cleanup stage (modules/cli/src/app/merge.rs, before `remove_worktree`): for each declared seed root, copy changed/new files back to the invocation repo (newest-mtime-wins is not acceptable — detect conflicts: if the main copy also changed since seeding, park the harvest with both paths listed instead of overwriting). Seed-time snapshot digests (record per-file digests when seeding, worktree.rs `copy_seed`) make changed-vs-unchanged and both-sides-changed detection deterministic.
      **Done when:** a worktree run that edits `.docs/X.md` lands with the edit present in the main repo's `.docs/X.md`; a both-sides-changed file parks the harvest naming the file; unchanged seeds copy nothing.
- [x] **P464** — Rejection retries always submit against a fresh frame: no valid correction ever bounces off a stale template (owner-ordered 2026-07-24).
      **Why:** run-3a1ddae3 (P427) exhausted its full retry budget — four ~25-minute apply-fixes attempts, ~100 minutes of paid work — and recorded drive outcome `rejected` even though attempt 4 returned a schema-conformant `{"work-summary": …}` (re-validated by hand against the step's output contract). The signature: NONE of the four rejections persisted a slot revision to the ledger, which is the identity/stale-state class (state-digest preflight), not a content rejection. The run-3a97a64c fix (drive.rs ~:2051-2060) adopts a refreshed call template only from `response.session.next_frame` — which non-persisting identity rejections may not carry — so a streak of them leaves every retry, including a perfect one, resubmitting against the same stale template until the budget burns out. Attempts 1-3 were genuine content failures (prose status endings — the one-turn-discipline prompt fix rides P450), which is exactly the interleaving that strands the template.
      **Where:** drive.rs rejection branch (~:2027-2095): on EVERY rejection — persisting or not — re-resolve the current frame/call template by re-reading the session state before the next attempt, instead of only adopting a `next_frame` the response happened to carry; classify the rejection on the drive report and progress line (content-rejection vs stale-identity — today both print the same "submission rejected — correction retry N/M", hiding the class that matters); when a rejection carries no correction text, synthesize one naming the class so the retry prompt always says why the previous answer bounced.
      **Where — retries resume, never silently cold-start (added 2026-07-24, verified on the same run):** all four apply-fixes attempts launched as fresh harness sessions with the byte-identical 35k full frame prompt — no `--session`, no correction note — so each "reformat your answer" retry cost ~25 minutes of full re-reasoning instead of seconds. The resume/correction machinery exists (missing-slot path ~:1775-1826, rejected path ~:2059-2089) but every leg is conditional with SILENT fallback: no extracted session id ⇒ argv untouched, no correction text from core ⇒ prompt untouched, and no evidence of which variant ran. Rework: a correction retry MUST resume the previous conversation whenever the harness declares a session/resume flag (find out why extraction yielded nothing here despite `sessionID` present in every event — fix that root cause, don't just add another fallback); on a resumed conversation send the correction alone, not the full frame prompt again; when resume is impossible, say so in the retry evidence. Every retry records `resumed=true/false` + which correction text was sent, so a degraded retry is visible instead of silent.
      **Watch:** the fix is refreshing the TEMPLATE, never loosening validation — a submission that is genuinely stale against the refreshed state must still reject; identity rejections stay non-persisting (that semantic is correct — they carry no content worth a revision).
      **Done when:** a cargo test under `modules/cli/tests/` (stop-the-bleeding rule — no new byte_compare mode) reproduces the stranded streak — rejections whose responses carry no refreshed frame, followed by a schema-valid submission — and proves the valid submission is accepted after the in-loop re-resolve; a second test proves a correction retry against a session-capable harness fixture launches with the resume flag + correction-only prompt, and a resume-incapable one records the cold-start in evidence; rejection progress/evidence lines name the class and the resumed/cold distinction; existing rejection-path proofs stay green.

## Group 93: pnpm workspace + real package imports — kill the hardcoded resolver (proposed 2026-07-18)

Owner decisions (2026-07-18): Option A local resolution (CDK `exports` → `./src/index.ts` in the workspace, `publishConfig.exports` → `dist/` at publish); `packages/ts` renamed `packages/cdk`; package lineup `@ctx-traits/{cdk,agents,rust}`; domain packs are DUAL-USE (npm tarball carries a full trait package for `[dependencies]` consumption); pnpm everywhere. "How do trait dependencies install?" has two layers, both settled: authoring-time TS imports resolve via ordinary package management (`pnpm install` links the workspace in-repo; external consumers `pnpm add -D @ctx-traits/<pkg>`), and trait-level `[dependencies]` installs via `ctx traits sync` (npm/path/git all shipped — sync installs pnpm-first, vendors, locks). Determinism untouched: authoring imports are build-time only; canonical `index.toml` stays self-contained and editing a shared package surfaces as cdk-drift on consumers (rebuild refreshes with a reviewable diff). SUPERSEDES P346 (the `useful-traits` placeholder — P370's `@ctx-traits/agents` is the real publishable example); P345's npm publish stays owner-gated (account 2FA) and deliberately skipped for now. ORDERING: P369 runs BEFORE Group 91 so the plan family (P359) is born on real workspace imports instead of fragile relative paths.

The builtins drift recipe (modules/core/builtins/traits) builds through the same resolution — must stay green.
