## Group 95: Field ownership — status to the package manifest, trust to the machine (owner design decision 2026-07-19)

Owner doctrine, recorded verbatim in intent: neither `source/index.ts` nor `generated/index.toml` carries `status` or `trust`. **Status** is the team's description of readiness — it lives in `.ctx/traits/<id>/trait.toml` ([package]), edited when the team puts the trait in the repo. **Trust** is always machine-local: anyone who uses a trait — teammate's from the team repo, a stranger's, an imported SKILL.md — reviews and approves it on THEIR machine before it runs. We are explicitly against yolo-import engineering; a half-assed review is the reviewer's fault, but the tools make a real review cheap and safe. Consequence discovered by GP-2 (the trigger): lifecycle transitions currently edit generated/index.toml, so every activated CDK-built trait is permanently cdk-drift-blocked — field ownership was wrong, not the drift check. PRIORITY: before launch/publish — this changes every canonical digest, so it lands before digests are public.

- [x] **P385** — Move the fields: canonical document loses status/trust; manifest gains status; machine store owns trust.
      **Design (settled):** canonical Trait model drops `status` and `trust` (decode tolerates-and-ignores them with a deprecation warning for one transition period); `[package]` in trait.toml gains `status` (draft | ready; team-edited, human-meaningful); the existing machine trust store (`~/.config/ctx/trust.toml`, per-digest — the P371 dependency-trust mechanism) becomes the ONLY trust surface for every trait, local or imported. `ctx traits review --approve/--deny` records the MACHINE trust decision keyed by canonical digest; `activate`/`deactivate` become manifest status edits (or fold into an `edit`-style verb — implementer's call, keep one obvious verb); check re-sources its sections (lifecycle-status from the manifest, declared-trust → machine-trust with the digest it was approved at); a trait whose digest changed since approval is back to unreviewed — approval is OF a digest, never of a name.
      **Migration (in this phase, deliberate):** strip status/trust from every in-repo trait source + generated doc (dogfood packages, builtins under modules/core/builtins/traits, packages/rust nested packages, test fixtures); set [package] status; regenerate goldens knowingly; builtins re-embed; all digests/locks/projections re-baseline. The workspace CDK (trait()/TraitFields) drops the fields with a helpful type error pointing at trait.toml.
      **Done when:** no canonical document in the repo contains status or trust; `check` on an unreviewed trait says exactly how to review+approve it; approving then editing the trait re-blocks (digest-keyed); the full suite + goldens green; `list` shows status (manifest) and this machine's trust verdict side by side.
- [x] **P386** — Gate semantics + first-run flow on the new model.
      **Design (settled):** `run`/resolver eligibility requires BOTH manifest status ready AND machine-trust approved-at-current-digest — for every trait including the team's own; the refusal message names the one command to fix it. First-run flow becomes: init (status=draft in trait.toml) → build → check (says: draft + unreviewed, with next actions) → team edits status to ready when appropriate → `review --approve` (machine trust) → run. Import flow: import persists the candidate package (verify/fix the --out/default-persistence semantics GP-1 exposed), check says unreviewed, review --approve gates it exactly like local traits — one mental model everywhere.
      **Done when:** GP-1/GP-2 (redefined in P239 on this flow) execute the documented sequences literally in a fresh project; an unapproved trait cannot run anywhere; approving is one command; README/PRODUCT.md/QUICKSTART document the model in the owner's framing (team status, machine trust, no yolo imports).

## Group 88: OpenCode plugin — automatic trait activation (proposed 2026-07-17)

DX answer to "is it automatically adding based on trigger?": yes — the designed flow (PRODUCT's OpenCode section) is deterministic pre-prompt activation: the plugin collects host facts (task text, files, language, signals), calls the resolver, and injects the SELECTED traits' model-view text before the model sees anything; the LLM never decides activation. Manual override + explain keep it legible.

- [x] **P349** — Minimal OpenCode plugin: resolver-gated auto-injection + manual command.
      **Why:** the first host where traits activate themselves; turns `[activation]` rules from documentation into felt behavior, and is the template for every other host plugin (thin adapter, no resolver logic duplicated).
      **Where:** new plugin package (OpenCode plugin API): pre-prompt hook → shell out to `ctx traits resolve --json` (hidden verb stays the machine surface) with collected facts → append each selected trait's `use`-output; a `/trait <name>` manual command → same injection path; config for repo opt-in.
      **Approach:** thin-adapter rule from PRODUCT holds: the plugin does IO + host glue only; resolver scoring, gates, budgets all stay in the binary; injected content is wrapped with a one-line provenance header (trait id + digest) so transcripts show what activated and why (`ctx traits explain` link).
      **Watch:** no silent activation claims — if the host hook can't inject, report unsupported (never pretend); restricted/untrusted traits never auto-activate (existing gates); token budget respected via the existing resolve/pack budgeting.
      **Done when:** in an OpenCode repo with an activation-ruled trait, a matching prompt gets the trait injected automatically with visible provenance; `/trait <name>` works; a non-matching prompt injects nothing.

## Group 55: Testing & CI beyond the base story (proposed 2026-07-09)
Extends Group 51 P227 (committed byte-compare runner + core tests) and P230 (CI) with the credibility-critical net-new coverage.
- [ ] **P238** — Fuzz the audit detectors + the parser (`audit.rs`, `model_view/sanitize.rs`, canonical decode) — the audit is the launch hook, so a fuzzer over the detectors is the obvious credibility move and pre-empts false-negative attacks.
      **Why:** the hidden-content audit is what the launch rides on; a byte-level fuzzer over the detectors + parser is the highest-credibility test we can ship — it pre-empts the "here's an injection your scanner misses" reply and any panic-on-malformed-input embarrassment.
      **Where:** all targets are pure-core — `scan_hidden_content` (`modules/core/src/audit.rs:135`) + its sub-scanners (`scan_bidi_controls:425`, `scan_homoglyphs:458`, `scan_base64_blobs:487`); `sanitize_model_text` (`model_view/sanitize.rs:66`); canonical decode `decode_trait` (`encoding.rs:162`), `Reference::parse` (`reference.rs:264`), `Digest::parse` (`digest.rs:141`). Land a `fuzz/` dir (`cargo-fuzz`) or a proptest harness under `#[cfg(test)]`.
      **Approach:** property targets — detectors never panic and are total over arbitrary UTF-8/bytes; `decode_trait` never panics on arbitrary TOML; `decode → canonical_json → decode` round-trips stably; seed the corpus from the P236 labeled set + the P235 real corpus; assert every seeded injection class is still caught.
      **Watch:** this group **deliberately ADDS tests** — it reverses the standing no-`cargo test` rule for exactly this credibility-critical surface (core has ~1 `#[test]` today, in `digest.rs`); detectors/decode are pure so fuzzing needs no IO; keep targets deterministic and CI-fast.
      **Done when:** fuzz targets for the detectors, sanitizer, and canonical decode build + run; a seed corpus is committed; a crash/panic or a missed seeded-injection fails the run.
      **Watch (cont.):** runs under CI + the P239 matrix.
- [x] **P239** — CLI golden-path integration tests (GP-1/GP-2 automated, not just manual) + a macOS+Linux matrix in CI.
      **Why:** GP-1/GP-2 are the demo the launch rides on but are only ever run by hand; automating them across both OSes is what lets us claim "fresh-clone, <5 min" without a live surprise on either platform.
      **Where:** drive the built `ctx` binary end-to-end, asserting on byte-stable `--json` rather than the styled TTY surface; extends the Group 51 P227 committed byte-compare runner (`just test` + Rust `scripts/`) and the P230 `.github/workflows` CI; add an `os: [macos-latest, ubuntu-latest]` matrix.
      **GP-1/GP-2 DEFINED (owner, 2026-07-19 — run-RZWAnzJI correctly refused to invent these; both paths are PROVIDER-FREE, no LLM/network calls anywhere):**
      *GP-1, the consume path:* in a fresh temp project — `ctx traits init` → copy the committed fixture `scripts/goldens/gp1-skill/SKILL.md` in → `ctx traits import <path> --json` (arrives as a restricted review candidate) → `ctx traits check <imported-id> --json`. Byte-compare both JSON outputs to committed goldens (temp paths normalized).
      *GP-2, the author path (FINAL 2026-07-19, on the landed Group 95 two-gate model — this is the README Start-here sequence, byte-asserted):* `ctx traits init gp2-demo` → `ctx traits build .ctx/traits/gp2-demo/source/index.ts` → `ctx traits check gp2-demo --json` (asserts status=draft + trust=unreviewed reported with remedies) → `ctx traits activate gp2-demo` ([package].status → ready) → `ctx traits review gp2-demo --approve` (machine trust at current digest) → `ctx traits use gp2-demo` (stdout byte-golden) → `ctx traits run gp2-demo --no-drive --json` asserting the park is `blocked-agent-unassigned` — both gates cleared, provider-free boundary proven. Untouched starter; trust-store writes isolated via a test-scoped trust store (HOME or config override) so goldens never touch the real ~/.config/ctx/trust.toml.
      **Approach:** script the golden paths against a temp project, capture `--json`, byte-compare to committed goldens, wire into `just test`; add the CI matrix over macOS+Linux running fmt / clippy / `cargo test` / the golden-path runner; Group 60 P249's cold-machine install test reuses this.
      **Watch:** assert on `--json` (byte-stable, path-normalized), never the ANSI/styled surface; keep goldens deterministic (pin versions/digests); this is net-new coverage beyond P227's byte-compare of render output. (AMENDED 2026-07-18: the "no cargo test rule" this sentence referenced never survived — cargo test has been a standing gate all week; the phrase is struck as brief-poison, see log entries 24/33.)
      **Done when:** GP-1/GP-2 run green in CI on both macOS and Linux from a clean checkout; a regression in either golden path red-builds.
- [x] **P240** — Native==WASM parity check: assert the `modules/wasm-core` ABI output byte-matches native for the pure-core paths (deferred today).
      **Why:** "the wasm is the same engine" is a portability claim in the pitch; without a parity gate a native/wasm divergence ships silently and falsifies it.
      **Where:** the wasm ABI wrappers in `modules/wasm-core/src/abi.rs` — `validate_json` (`:276`), `normalize_json` (`:290`), `audit_json` (`:311`), `render_json` (`:339`) — each wraps the same pure-core fn native uses (`canonical_digest`/`canonical_json` at `:14`, `scan_hidden_content` at `:313`, `compile_model_view_with_evidence` at `:20`). Byte-compare wasm output to the native call across a fixed input set.
      **Approach:** for the P235 corpus + labeled fixtures, run each pure path natively and through the wasm-core ABI, byte-compare the JSON/digests, fold into `just test` + the P239 CI matrix, gate on any mismatch.
      **Watch:** only the pure-core paths are in scope — `modules/core` is pure (no IO, no host time/env) so parity is well-defined; `--json` byte-stability is the whole point; a mismatch is a correctness bug, not a warning.
      **Done when:** a committed check asserts native==wasm byte-for-byte on validate/normalize/audit/render across the fixture set; a deliberate divergence red-builds.
      **Watch (cont.):** runs in the P239 matrix.

## Group 69: Adoption surface — the techniques→types funnel (proposed 2026-07-10)

The product's biggest adoption risk is the vocabulary count (~15 concepts), not any technical gap — while the SKILL ecosystem thrives on folklore "techniques" that brute-force on hope what ctx.traits enforces structurally. Strategy: those techniques are the funnel, not the competition. Every circulating trick — "have a second model review it", "keep a checklist file", "re-read before editing", "restate constraints at the end" — is an unnamed requirement people already believe in, implemented on hope; we show the enforced version of each. This is the TypeScript playbook: don't teach type theory, name the bugs people already have, and offer gradual adoption from their existing files. The complexity is amortized (progressive disclosure, templates, types-as-teacher), never removed — watering down the typed spine converges back to skills. Priority pair inside the group: P268 + P269 (the content wedge and the free front door); the rest ride the groups that own their surfaces.

- [x] **P268** — Rosetta essay + interactive: "Every SKILL technique is a missing type."
      **Why:** it converts the ecosystem's existing energy into our narrative — it validates readers' techniques instead of lecturing them, meets them exactly where they are, and is the natural follow-up to the `#00` launch post; the strongest single piece of adoption content we can ship.
      **Where:** net-new `.docs/content/31-every-skill-technique-is-a-missing-type.md` + `.docs/content/interactive/31-….html`, following the locked grammar in `.docs/content/INTERACTIVE_WALKTHROUGHS.md` (assertion → visualization → reasoning; one concept per widget; required "How ctx.traits ends this story" resolve block with real command output); fixture traits under a content-fixtures dir so every shown encoding is real.
      **Approach:** catalog 8–12 circulating techniques (two-model review, checklist files, re-read-before-edit, restate-constraints, plan files, verify-before-done, "don't let it see X", …); render each as hope-version → its silent failure mode ("works until the model ignores it — and nothing tells you it did") → the trait encoding (CDK snippet with the authored/stored toggle per the house widget pattern). Hero widget: a technique picker showing hope-vs-enforced side by side with a failure toggle. Every encoding is backed by a committed, compiling fixture trait — the shown TOML is generated output (drift-checked), never hand-typed.
      **Watch:** claim-gate wording on runtime-leaning encodings (loops, multi-agent) — marked gated/planned per the P247 matrix discipline, never asserted as shipped; owner-voice rule + DRAFT ribbon until re-voiced (P248); build against the canonical token outcome of P245 (gold chrome), not the coral leftovers.
      **Done when:** essay + interactive exist with N≥8 techniques, each backed by a check-green fixture trait whose rendered TOML matches the built output; every claim passes claim-gate wording; the piece is listed in the gallery index.
- [x] **P269** — Import as front door: `ctx traits doctor` (skills-folder x-ray).
      **Why:** the only onboarding that starts from THEIR artifact, and it is free — zero config, zero keys, zero model calls; the import scaffold's warnings are a personalized first lesson in the vocabulary, and "run it on your skills folder, see what it finds" is the eslint-moment utility that spreads on its own.
      **Where:** new read-only CLI verb `doctor` — `Command`/`TraitsCommand` enum (`modules/cli/src/app/surface/cli.rs`), handler in `entry.rs`, styled report through the P241 shared line/tone helper; machinery = the P195 import analyzer (scaffold with confidence, source anchors, review warnings, injection/homoglyph audit) run in dry-run mode, plus the hygiene-report aggregation patterns.
      **Approach:** `ctx traits doctor [path]` walks known skill layouts (`.claude/skills`, `.opencode/skills`, plain dirs of `SKILL.md`/`AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`), runs the import analyzer per file WITHOUT writing packages, and aggregates: security audit findings (the P196-fix-hardened hidden-content checks), missing verification steps, untyped outputs, cross-skill conflicts/overlaps, staleness; ends with a "what `import` would give you" summary + the exact next command. Two-tone styled for TTY, `--json` for CI.
      **Watch:** v1 reuses import's detection only — no new analysis engines; read-only by default (no package writes, no network); honest severity vocabulary (Advisory, not verdicts) — `doctor` suggests, `check` verifies; core stays pure (directory walking is io).
      **Done when:** `doctor` over a real skills folder emits a styled aggregate report + stable `--json`, runs with zero config/keys, and the P242 quickstart names it as step one.
- [x] **P270** — Vocabulary ladder + three-rings glossary (docs-site information architecture).
      **Why:** fifteen flat concepts are a wall; a ladder where each rung solves ONE named pain makes the vocabulary pay-as-you-go, and three rings of five are graspable where a flat glossary is not. Nobody should meet `condition.all` before they have felt the pain it solves.
      **Where:** content architecture for the P243 docs site: a "Start here" ladder + a glossary page with one three-rings diagram (authoring: trait/intent/prompt/resource/metadata · contract: port/slot/schema/verdict/signal · runtime: sequence/agent/harness/profile/ledger); rung fixture traits committed beside the P268 fixtures.
      **Approach:** five rungs, each ending in a runnable artifact: (1) package a behavior — a ~10-line trait (intent + prompt), already better than a loose SKILL.md via digest + check; (2) make it verifiable — digests, lockfile, drift; (3) typed I/O — ports/slots/schemas; (4) multi-step — sequence + conditions; (5) multi-agent — roles, profiles, drive. Plain-English pairing on every first use of a canonical term ("inputs and outputs (ports)", "working values (slots)"); the diagram as inline SVG in the canonical tokens.
      **Watch:** every rung example compiles and passes `check` (fixtures, drift-checked); rungs 4–5 wording respects the runtime claim gate; the ladder teaches the CDK view first — TOML appears as "what is stored", consistent with the `#00` explorer framing.
      **Done when:** the docs site carries the ladder + glossary; each rung's trait exists as a check-green fixture; the first-use pairing rule is applied across concept pages.
- [x] **P271** — First-party template pack + `ctx traits new`.
      **Why:** people adopt working artifacts, not vocabularies — learning by dissection beats learning by reference, and the blank page is the biggest authoring cliff. Our own dogfood trait is the crown jewel teaching artifact: "this exact trait produced this exact commit, here is its ledger."
      **Where:** five teaching templates as real first-party packages under the Group 47 layout decision (code-review; implement-phase — the live dogfood trait annotated with its P197 evidence; research-summarize; test-writer; pr-description); a `new` verb beside the existing authoring surface (`surface/cli.rs`, `entry.rs`), scaffolding via the existing `build` path.
      **Approach:** `ctx traits new <id> --from <template>` = deterministic copy + rewrite (id/name substitution in `trait.ts`, then rebuild `trait.toml` through the normal CDK build so digests are correct by construction); bare `new` lists templates. Teaching comments live in `trait.ts` (comments do not survive TOML synthesis — that is the point: the authored file teaches, the stored file ships). Division of labor stated in help text: `init` scaffolds the workspace (P242 D1), `new` scaffolds a trait, `generate` remains the LLM path.
      **Watch:** templates are real packages — check green, cdk-drift green, canonical digests committed; `new` is fully deterministic (no model calls); byte-stability discipline applies to the template packages like any committed trait.
      **Done when:** five templates committed and check-green; `new --from <template>` yields a building, checking package with correct digests; the implement-phase template cites its real P197 run evidence.
- [x] **P272** — CDK as the teacher: JSDoc coverage + first-hour error audit.
      **Why:** for this audience, hover docs on `sequence.loop` explaining WHY `until` exists are worth more than a docs site — the `.d.ts` is the tutorial; and error messages are onboarding: the planner's unbounded-loop refusal ("loop step 'repeat' is unbounded and will not run") is the standard every newcomer-reachable error should meet.
      **Where:** doc comments across the exported CDK builders in `packages/ts/src` (rides or follows the Group 48 modular restructure); the error audit spans the newcomer-reachable diagnostics in `modules/cli` (loader shape mismatches, missing trait file, schema mismatch, missing input, unassigned role, unbound loop).
      **Approach:** every exported builder gets: one-line concept, the pain it solves, a 3–5-line example, and cross-disambiguation where confusion is predictable (port vs slot on both). The audit enumerates every error a newcomer can hit in the first hour and holds each to "names the thing + says what to do next"; fixes follow the existing structured-diagnostic shapes.
      **Watch:** doc comments must not change emitted draft JSON (byte-stability of built output; cdk-drift green on committed traits); `tsc` + `just sdk-check` stay green; error-message changes update the golden CLI captures where output is captured.
      **Done when:** every exported CDK builder carries concept+why+example docs; the first-hour error list is enumerated in evidence with each message meeting the standard; goldens updated; drift green.
- [x] **P273** — Consumption before authoring: the managed-skill distribution path.
      **Why:** adoption friction is lowest inside the harness people already use — let them CONSUME verified traits as plain skills first, with the provenance and upgrade path visible; consumption-before-authoring inverts the learning curve, and the export machinery already exists.
      **Where:** the `export --format skill` render (P194, managed `Render profile: skill` marker) gains a provenance/upgrade block; the P271 templates publish in both forms; a docs page for the consume → verify → author ladder.
      **Approach:** extend the skill export with a compact provenance section — source trait ref, version, canonical digest, and the exact verify command (`ctx traits check …`) — so a consumed skill carries its own upgrade path; document the ladder; wire the landing/docs pages to it.
      **Watch:** additive only on the byte-locked agent-skills compat path (existing captures stay byte-identical where they are locked; the new block lands behind the managed marker discipline); registry/distribution-index wording stays "planned" per the P247 honesty rule — this phase ships the artifact shape, not a registry.
      **Done when:** exported skills carry the provenance/upgrade block; the templates exist in both forms; the consume-first docs page is live; the P247 matrix row reflects exactly what shipped.

## Group 77: Config consolidation — `ctx.toml`→`.ctx/config.toml` + retire `runs/profiles` (proposed 2026-07-14) — open remainder rehomed to Group 103 (2026-07-21)

Continues the `.ctx` consolidation (Group 75) and **supersedes Group 71**. Two moves: (1) pull the repo-local runtime config into the branded tree; (2) delete the `runs/profiles/<trait>.toml` mechanism, whose routing is redundant. The profile self-admits *"this file only routes"* and its fields split cleanly: `harness` duplicates `ctx.toml [agent.role.*]` byte-for-byte, `transport` is always `"cli"` (the default) — both dead weight; only `session-mode` (persistent/per-frame) and `[budget]` carry behavior. session-mode is a per-role property → moves to `ctx.toml [agent.role.*]` (already valid there — `[agent.narrator]` uses it; `[agent.role.*]` and profile `[assign.*]` share the `ProfileAssignment` struct, `harness_config.rs:188-219`). Budget → per-trait sidecar (Group 71's design). **Grounding:** config discovery is `harness_config.rs` — consts `:14-15`, walk `runtime_config_paths():515-528`, global `:530-543`, merge `:492-502,565-571`; `layout/mod.rs` doesn't own the path today. Profiles: `RunProfile`/`RunProfileBudget` (`:137-148,232-247`), `resolve_run_profile:336-374`, `resolve_profile_path:616` (**already broken** — joins the non-existent `.agents/runs/profiles` post-migration), budget in `budget_from` (`drive.rs:991-1019`), session-mode at `drive.rs:597,870,3159` via `plan_from_assignment:1253-1279`. Run `--profile`/`--assign`: `cli.rs:603-612,361-370,409-418,455-464,484-493` — **distinct from** render/export `--profile` (`cli.rs:121,137,157,229,250,509`, untouched). No tests/goldens guard any of this — verify by driving. **Decisions:** session-mode→ctx.toml (sidecar budget-only); keep `--assign`+attach, drop profile files + run `--profile`; dual-path (legacy+new, new wins) so gitignored dev-local configs survive; global path keeps its own `~/.config/ctx/config.toml` const (not nested `.ctx`); `ctx-harness.toml`→`.ctx/harness.toml` too; sidecar is outside canonical digest bytes.

- [x] **P311** — `ctx.toml` → `.ctx/config.toml` (+ registry), dual-path, layout-owned. *(Landed 2026-07-21, bee3aca.)*
      **Where:** add `PROJECT_CONFIG=".ctx/config.toml"` + `LEGACY_PROJECT_CONFIG="ctx.toml"` (and `.ctx/harness.toml`/legacy `ctx-harness.toml`) to `layout/mod.rs:37-53`; point `harness_config.rs:14-15` at them; `runtime_config_paths()` (`:515-528`) push **both** legacy + new per ancestor (legacy first → new wins); give `global_runtime_config_path()` (`:539`) its **own** `~/.config/ctx/config.toml` const (+ legacy fallback); `.gitignore:9-11` add `/.ctx/config.toml` + `/.ctx/harness.toml` (keep legacy lines).
      **Approach:** dual-read migration; move the on-disk dev-local `ctx.toml` → `.ctx/config.toml` (gitignored, no commit).

SEQUENCING: the batch pipeline itself runs on profiles — land P314 LAST in Group 77 and outside the release window.
## Group 78: `import` + `explain` (+ optional `compose`) become LLM-assisted like refine — self-produce candidates (proposed 2026-07-14)

`ctx traits import --llm-assisted` errors *"model provider adapter is unavailable; no candidate was produced or written"* whenever no `--candidate` file is supplied. `import` (and `compose`) were left as stubs — they hardcode `provider_available: false` and have **no model fallback**, so `--llm-assisted` can only *evaluate* a candidate produced elsewhere. The rest of the family already self-produces: `generate`/`refine`/`critique`/`generate-evals` set `provider_available: candidate_path.is_none()` and, with no `--candidate`, run a **built-in meta-trait through the harness loop** — `run_builtin_trait("generate-trait", …)` (`generate.rs:461`) → role map (`:468-472`) → `layout::trait_manifest_path(".", id)` → `.ctx/traits/<id>/generated/trait.toml` (`:481`) → `io::run::start` → `drive::drive` → `HarnessSession::spawn` — and feed the model output to `evaluate_supplied_candidate` (`generate.rs:119`). `import` lacks all three hooks (no meta-trait, no role-map entry, only evaluates when `candidate_path` is `Some`, `import_handlers.rs:769`; errors `:936-940`). **Chosen: dedicated meta-trait + drive** (mirror the family; not the one-shot `harness::run` shortcut, not reusing `refine-trait`). **Invariants:** `modules/core` stays pure (all spawn in CLI/io, `assist.rs:3-10`); model output is untrusted through the existing gate (`assist.rs:446`) → forced restricted/untrusted + audit; `--candidate` remains the offline/reproducible override; import still refuses remote sources (only the enrichment step calls a model).

**`explain` gets the same treatment (now in scope).** Same stub class: the command is deterministic (`explain_inspect.rs` `handle_explain_scaffold` → `plan_deterministic_boundary`, `provider_available:false`, `:106-115`), and `explain-trait` already exists but is an **agent-less** package — a receipt-grounded scaffold *contract* (ports source-trait-id/check-receipt/source-map → explanation), never executed as a model step. Migration: add an LLM narration step to `explain-trait` **grounded in the deterministic scaffold**, register it in the runner, and give `explain` the refine-style `--llm-assisted`/`--candidate` path. Deterministic explain stays the default; the narration is grounded in the receipt, never invented. `compose` (`:184`) is the same stub too — the optional trailing phase.

- [x] **P315** — Author the `import-trait` built-in meta-trait. *(Landed 82c4f68, 2026-07-19 — salvaged from run-e654c81a: 10/10 rounds dual-verified with zero code defects; the sole stable blocker was PRODUCT.md's stale "Six first-party packages" count contradicting this owner-ordered seventh — law updated to seven (line 136 + table row + materialization sentence), work cherry-picked from the run's worktree branch, standalone-drive evidence harvested to .ctx/runs/evidence/p315-*, builtin tests green. See RUN_PROBLEMS_LOG entry 57.)*
      **Where:** new `.ctx/traits/import-trait/trait.ts` (+ `generated/trait.toml`), modeled on `.ctx/traits/refine-trait/trait.ts`.
      **Approach:** inputs = the deterministic import scaffold (`trait.toml` text) + raw source (SKILL.md) + source-profile + trait-id + target-schema `agent-traits/canonical-trait`; single authoring step enriching intent/behavior/metadata/descriptions **grounded in the source**; output = the **same typed draft wrapper** `generate-trait`/`refine-trait` emit so the existing decode+evaluate path applies unchanged; declares one agent bound to role `generator`.
      **Watch:** output contract must satisfy `decode_generate_candidate` (`generate.rs:110`) or add a sibling `decode_import_candidate`; build byte-stable + `check` green; commit the package.
      **Done when:** `ctx traits check --file .ctx/traits/import-trait/generated/trait.toml` green; the trait drives standalone.

Rebuild + `check` green.
      **Watch:** preserve receipt-grounding — the model narrates the deterministic evidence, never invents; do not disturb the deterministic scaffold path (still the default).

Today's deterministic scaffold becomes the grounding input to the narration.

# POST-LAUNCH — runtime depth, polish, partial remainders

## Group 94: Dynamic trait synthesis — improvise with provenance (proposed 2026-07-18)

Competitive context (AMENDED 2026-07-18 after the Onyx article, randomlabs.ai/blog/onyx, 2026-07-02 — supersedes the March-era Slate picture): Onyx is Random Labs' VM for `*.program.ts` — programs persist, compose with typed I/O, and run against a runtime with declared persistent typed state, a schema-gated agent finish gate (convergent with our typed slot acceptance — the market agreeing with our design), typed orchestration errors, budgets, and model routing. What Onyx still cannot offer, by construction: the program body is arbitrary Turing-complete TS (no canonical form, no static validation of the orchestration graph, no digests/locks/diffable behavior artifact); prompts remain opaque strings (no model-view, no audit, no injection scanning — the finish gate types what agents RETURN, not what they SEE); there is NO trust layer (models write programs you execute and import — the SKILL.md supply-chain problem rebuilt in TS with more privileges; their ten VM requirements never mention trust, audit, provenance, or locks); durability/resume is admitted unsolved in their own future-work section. Frame: **Onyx makes orchestration programmable; ctx.traits makes agent behavior provable** — their guarantees live at runtime (throws, gates, budgets), ours live before and after the run (validation, audit, digests, locks, re-verifiable ledgers). Our answer is not a competing runtime; it is making the typed path feel dynamic: ask → synthesize → validate → run → keep the artifact.
WATCH: Onyx's procedural-vs-declarative argument (models natively understand TS) is their strongest point — P381's skeletons are our counter (declared shapes, authored ergonomically); monitor whether we eventually want a procedural-authoring veneer that COMPILES to declared procedure (never a runtime).

- [ ] **P380** — `ctx traits do "<task>"`: ephemeral trait, full provenance.
      **Why:** the dynamic path today is generate → inspect → build → run, four manual steps; Slate makes improvisation one utterance. Ours must be one command WITHOUT skipping a single gate.
      **Where:** new visible CLI verb `do` (surface/cli.rs + a handler beside the generate machinery in modules/cli/src/app/generate.rs); ephemeral package home `.ctx/cache/ephemeral-traits/<canonical-digest>/` (cache-class, gitignored, outside `.ctx/traits` discovery); the existing run pipeline unchanged.
      **Approach:** task text → the generate builtin drafts a minimal trait → build → validate → canonicalize → digest → `check` (+ audit) MUST pass → session starts immediately against the ephemeral package; the session ledger records synthesis provenance (generating model, prompt digest, generated-source digest) alongside the normal run record. `--keep <id>` promotes the package into `.ctx/traits/<id>` (ordinary package from then on); un-kept ephemerals age out with the cache but their ledgers persist. Trust stays `local`; ephemeral packages never auto-activate and never shadow named packages.
      **Watch:** no gate skipping — an ephemeral trait that fails check/audit does not run, period (that IS the product); the ephemeral home must be invisible to `list`/discovery (a `--ephemerals` flag can reveal them); provenance fields go in the ledger, not the canonical bytes (canonical stays model-only).
      **Done when:** `ctx traits do "summarize the diff and write a changelog entry"` synthesizes, validates, digests, runs, and leaves both a ledger and a re-runnable artifact; a deliberately injection-poisoned task string produces a trait that FAILS audit and never runs; `--keep` yields a package indistinguishable from a hand-authored one (check green, buildable, lockable).
      **Watch (cont.):** P381 upgrades quality but is not a prerequisite of this phase.
- [ ] **P381** — Skeleton-constrained synthesis: families as typed templates.
      **Why:** freeform generation produces plausible-but-novel shapes; the failure economics of this week (contract bugs burning full budgets) say synthesized traits should START inside proven shapes.
      **Where:** the generate builtin's instructions + a skeleton catalog derived from the family lineup (Group 91: plan:{direct,quick,default,complex}, refactor family, implement, guarded-change) and the `@ctx-traits/agents` doctrine package (P370).
      **Approach:** synthesis selects a skeleton by task shape (one-shot task → plan:direct-like single step; iterate-until-good → implement-like capped loop with typed verdict; risky change → guarded-change) and parameterizes it — prompts filled, slots renamed, gates chosen — rather than inventing structure; the chosen skeleton id + parameters land in synthesis provenance. Freeform remains a `--freeform` escape hatch.
      **Done when:** the same `do` request produces a trait whose procedure graph byte-matches its skeleton modulo declared parameters; provenance names the skeleton; a task that fits no skeleton falls back to freeform with that fact recorded.
- [ ] **P382** — `ctx traits distill <run-id>`: session ledger → trait package draft.
      **Why:** Onyx programs are authored by a model and reused as code — file-grade, unversioned, unlocked, unaudited. Distillation is reuse FROM EVIDENCE: our ledgers are TYPED — steps, roles, slot schemas, verdicts, command evidence — so distillation can emit a BUILDABLE package draft, not prose.
      **Where:** new hidden-then-visible verb `distill` beside `do`; input = one or more session ledgers (`.ctx/runs/session-*.json`); output = a draft package under `.ctx/traits/<proposed-id>/` (source/index.ts via the CDK emit path + generated candidate), NOT auto-trusted, marked `status = draft` until the owner reviews/refines/keeps.
      **Approach:** reconstruct the effective procedure from the ledger (frames → steps, roles → agents, accepted-slot schemas → slots, loop iterations → loop with observed cap, command frames → command steps with their argv), synthesize prompts from the resolved prompt texts (digests already recorded), and cite source run ids + digests in a provenance header comment. Multi-ledger input generalizes (shared shape across runs wins, divergences become parameters).
      **Watch:** distillation must never fabricate what the ledger doesn't evidence — steps that appeared only in one of N runs become optional/flagged, not silently included; secrets/paths hygiene per audit rules (their PRIVACY note is prose; ours is the audit gate).
      **Done when:** distilling an implement-phase run reproduces a recognizably implement-shaped draft that builds and checks green; distilling a `do`-created ephemeral run round-trips to an equivalent trait; the draft's provenance names every source ledger.
- [x] **P383** — `ctx traits story <run-id>`: the run, told straight. *(cheap; launch-demo candidate — may be pulled forward)*
      **Why:** Slate ships a consumer-grade iteration history; our ledgers contain strictly more (typed verdicts, blockers with done-whens, escalation reasons, command evidence, costs) but present as JSON. The story view is the demo artifact that makes "the loop you can prove" legible in ten seconds.
      **Where:** new verb `story` in the report family (two-tone styled-line grammar from P241, plain + `--json` passthrough).
      **Approach:** render per-iteration: who did what, verdicts (approved/revise + blocker ids), what got fixed, escalations with reasons, commands run with exit evidence, ending in the terminal state and landing commit; `--markdown` emits the same as a PR-comment-ready block.
      **Done when:** `ctx traits story <any session>` renders the full arc; the P369 phantom-rule run's story reads as the self-explanatory case study it is; `--json` byte-stable.

Q&A bullets (claim-gated): (a) you can't diff, digest, or statically check a running program — you can a trait; (b) who audits the program the model wrote? their stack has no answer, ours is `check` + audit + trust + locks; (c) their own future-work admits durability/resume is unsolved — our runs park, persist typed ledgers, and resume. Be generous about the convergence: schema-gated completion arriving in Onyx validates typed acceptance. Cite the `do`/`distill` pair once they exist; until then the Q&A rests on the shipped check/lock/ledger stack.

## Group 98: Auto-research — experiment loops as provable artifacts (proposed 2026-07-19) — open remainder rehomed to Group 103 (2026-07-21)

Random Labs' flagship Onyx demo is Karpathy's autoresearch as a program: agents do side effects, "code owns the keep/discard decision" (`kept = status ok && val_bpb < best`). Our answer is stronger by one full level: **the decision is DECLARED, not coded** — a guard condition in a validated, digested artifact, evaluated deterministically by the runtime over a TYPED command output. Everything needed has already landed: typed command-step outputs (P351), guard comparators (`less-than`/`at-least`), branch/when, per-frame fresh agents (session-mode), write-ops for loop-carried state, and the ledger as a byte-stable experiment record. Their demo trusts the program author; ours produces an artifact where the experiment protocol itself is reviewable before a single GPU-hour is spent — pre-registration for agent experiments.

- [x] **P385** — `auto-research` trait: the generic experiment loop (first draft).
      **Approach:** ports: `objective`, `experiment-command` (argv), `metric-field`, `max-experiments`. Sequence: setup (worker prepares the workbench + seeds baseline; typed readiness output gates continuation via branch — their ABORT arm, declared) → loop over experiments: propose (FRESH per-frame agent given best + typed history slot — no context rot by construction) → apply (worker edits per proposal) → train/evaluate (COMMAND step, typed JSON output: `{status, metric, artifacts}` — P351) → decide (branch/when guard: `status == ok` AND `metric less-than best` — THE DECLARED DECISION) → keep-arm: write-ops update `best`/`best-ref` slots + record row; discard-arm: command step `git reset --hard <best-ref>` + record row. History threads through a pre-loop-seeded list slot (the loop-carried-state pattern from the P349-era lesson). Terminal: typed summary (experiments, kept, best, per-experiment table — the ledger already holds it; P383 `story` renders it).
      **Watch:** the guard fields must reference the typed COMMAND output (adversarial: an agent cannot spoof the metric — command evidence anti-spoof already enforces this, which is a guarantee Onyx does not have: their agent writes state the program branches on; our metric comes from the command's own captured stdout); fresh-agent-per-experiment via per-frame session mode in the profile.
- [x] **P386** — Showcase: self-improving traits — auto-research over our own eval harness.
      **Why:** we don't have an A100; we have something better for the demo — OURSELVES. The experiment loop's `experiment-command` is `ctx traits eval <trait>`: propose a prompt/doctrine mutation to a target trait, rebuild, run its evals, keep iff the eval score improves. Auto-research where the artifact under optimization is ITSELF a validated artifact, every kept mutation is a digested diff, and the whole optimization run is a byte-stable ledger. "Self-improving agents with provenance" — the blog post Onyx's GPU demo cannot write.
      **Watch:** the mutated trait rebuilds through the full gate stack each iteration (check+audit gate the mutation, not just the score); the kept-chain is a sequence of canonical digests — the experiment tree IS the git history, same as their demo, but each node is a checkable artifact. VALIDATION DIMENSIONS (owner 2026-07-19): improvement is judged multi-dimensionally, not on eval score alone — per-iteration wall-time, token/cost spend, iteration count, and code/prompt DELTA SIZE are all recorded as typed metrics, each guardable (e.g. keep only if score improves AND delta stays small AND cost per iteration under cap) — controlled self-improvement means the controls are declared.
- [ ] **P387** — Auto-research writeup + `story` integration (launch-content rider).
      **Approach:** run P386 on one real trait (e.g. optimize plan:quick's distill prompt), publish the run as the case study: protocol artifact (pre-registered), per-experiment story view, kept-chain digests. Pairs with P383. Claim-gate everything.
      **Watch (cont.):** P383 helps presentation but plain ledger dumps suffice for v1.
- [x] **P391** — Benchmark-driven refactor loop (owner research #2, 2026-07-19 — first draft).
      **Why:** auto-research where the metric is a real benchmark over a real codebase area: performance refactoring that provably paid for itself, every round.
      **Approach:** ports: `target` (codebase area), `benchmark-command` (argv, typed output: `{status, metric}`), `improvement-target` (metric goal), `time-limit` (SOFT). Loop: plan (smart role scopes this round's attempt against target + history) → draft (for the worker) → implement (worker) → LIGHT review (single reviewer, quick-level authority per P377 — this loop's assurance comes from the benchmark, not deep review) → benchmark (command step, typed output) → decide (declared guard: metric improved vs best?) → improved: keep (write-ops update best, commit round) + CONTINUE (re-plan next round); worse: revert (command step back to best ref) + CONTINUE with the failure recorded in history. STOP conditions: improvement-target reached (until-condition → completed) OR soft time-limit reached (completed-with-partial, NOT a failure).
      **Watch:** RUNTIME RIDER (pre-flight this before dispatch): loops today terminate exhaustion as `blocked` — this trait needs a SOFT-STOP semantics where hitting the iteration/time budget with recorded progress is a COMPLETED outcome (typed as `completed/budget-reached`), and elapsed-time is not currently a guard input; both are small runtime gaps this phase owns (they also serve P386). Benchmark noise: require the guard margin to exceed a declared noise threshold (port), or the loop chases jitter.
      **Done when:** on a fixture repo with a deliberately slow function and a benchmark script, the loop lands ≥1 kept improvement, reverts ≥1 regression, and stops cleanly at the soft limit with a typed partial-success summary; ledger + story show the round history.
      *(GPU note: owner can rent A100s via Lambda/Modal LATER — the Karpathy-style training variant of P385 unblocks then; nothing in this group waits on it.)*

## Group 96: Orchestration parity — gaps surfaced by the Onyx analysis (proposed 2026-07-19)

From the 2026-07-18/19 read of both Random Labs articles: items where their runtime has genuine capabilities we lack, filed deliberately (adopt the capability, keep the artifact discipline). Also noted as COVERED (no action): model-per-step routing (profiles), state-driven branching (guards), durability/park-resume (ahead of them — their future-work admits it's unsolved), episode-style compressed handoffs (clerk briefs/typed slots), typed failure taxonomy (stop reasons). Group 54 rider (recorded here, executed there): a public benchmark presence — run the implement family against Terminal Bench 2.0-class tasks and publish ledgers, since Slate's credibility rests on that bench.

- [ ] **P388** — Trait-invokes-trait: typed sub-procedure composition (design-first). *(Owner 2026-07-19: affirmed as the longer-term vision — priority anchor for the post-launch arc.)*
      **Why:** Onyx composes programs by import/spawn with typed I/O — our biggest structural gap. Their composition is code calling code; ours should be an ARTIFACT declaring a dependency on another artifact: a `sequence.call` step naming a trait ref + typed port mapping (child input ports ← parent slots; child outputs → parent slots), version-pinnable via `[dependencies]`, locked, drift-checked.
      **Where:** design doc first (core model: call step kind; session semantics: nested run vs inlined frames; ledger: child run-id reference + digest; CDK: `sequence.call(traitRef, {inputs, outputs})`); implementation phases follow the design review.
      **Watch:** composition must not launder trust — a local trait calling a restricted trait inherits the gate, not the bypass; recursion/depth bounds declared (their RLM overdecomposition lesson, typed).
- [ ] **P389** — Run handles: background sessions, steering, cancellation (design-first, runtime-family gated).
      **Why:** Onyx has spawn/notify/cancel on live agents; we have foreground drive + park. The typed version: `ctx traits jobs` (list running sessions), `ctx traits steer <session> "<note>"` (append a typed steering record the next frame's prompt carries — auditable, unlike an opaque mid-stream message), `ctx traits cancel <session>` (typed terminal state, ledger records who/why).
      **Watch:** steering is a LEDGER EVENT, not a side channel — every steer is provenance; claim-gate keeps this out of launch copy until the runtime family unblocks.
      **SDK direction (owner 2026-07-19):** longer-term, expose a programmatic SDK for a freer Onyx-like coding experience over runs — but STARTING FROM OUR STRICT LIFECYCLE, not from free-form scripting: the SDK's verbs are exactly the lifecycle (start/drive/steer/cancel/resume over sessions with typed ledgers), so programmatic orchestration inherits provenance by construction; loosening comes later, if ever. Design lives with this phase.
- [ ] **P390** — Cost/token budgets per run (P374 sibling) — per-model split + subscription-aware estimation (owner 2026-07-19).
      **Why:** Onyx caps spend per program; our `[budget]` caps frames/seconds only. Add token/cost ceilings per run + per role-tier, enforced at frame dispatch with the typed pause (P374's `paused-provider-credits` precedent → `paused-budget-exhausted`), resumable after an owner raises the cap.
      **Cost model:** spend splits PER MODEL (ledgers already carry per-frame token counts where harnesses emit them) against a per-model pricing table — and the estimator must be SUBSCRIPTION-AWARE: models billed through a flat subscription (claude/copilot/codex seats) are marginal-cost-zero and must not be priced as API tokens; the report shows both views (tokens by model, estimated $ by billing mode), so a "cheap" run through a subscription and a cheap run through API credits are distinguishable truths.
