## Group 99: CDK ergonomics — the handle surface is its fields (owner design decision 2026-07-19)

Verdict recorded (owner question "should we just use zod, or is our own typesystem right?"): the layering STAYS — zod is an authoring frontend (`schema.zod` compiles through the JSON-Schema adapter into the house vocabulary with loud subset rejection, cdk schema.ts:257/384) and the closed vocabulary stays the canonical form, because four product properties depend on it: frame-time validation is Rust (schema_validation.rs — zod cannot run there), canonical schemas are digest-stable DATA (zod is closures + version-dependent emission), guards are statically checkable only over a closed vocabulary (`.refine()` is arbitrary JS), and model-view/audit need serializable semantics (zod-as-core would rebuild the Onyx weakness in our own core). Same pattern as protobuf/avro: IDL-as-data canonical, language bindings as frontends. What deserves investment is the authoring surface, unified by one owner decision: **handle internals go private; a handle's public surface is exactly its fields** — then field access and composition both fall out of the language itself.

- [x] **P398** — Handle surface = fields: bare typed field accessors, spread composition, typed guards, schema templates (generic spots, monomorphized). *(Landed 2026-07-21, c9e1f6f.)*
      **Why:** guards on object-slot fields are stringly today (`condition.field(slot, "foo", true)` — no editor checking, typos surface only at build) and spreading a schema HANDLE silently spreads its declaration keys (`id`/`fields`/`description`) into garbage fields instead of its field set. Both are one defect: declaration internals live on the handle's enumerable surface.
      **Where:** packages/cdk ONLY. meta.ts: declaration keys join the already-symbol-keyed non-enumerable meta (meta.ts:63). slot.ts/handles.ts: object-schema slot handles expose each declared field as a typed FieldRef — bare `slot.foo`, bracket `slot["exit-code"]` for kebab-case ids — via a Proxy validated against the carried declaration (unknown field THROWS at authoring time, named); TS `SlotHandle<Value> & { [K in keyof Value]: FieldRef<Value[K]> }` for completion + literal type-checking. schema.ts: schema handles enumerate as their normalized field record, so `schema.object("z", { ...x, ...y, baz: schema.text() })` yields foo/bar/baz verbatim (normalized `{schema, required, description}` entries are already accepted field input); duplicate ids under spread stay JS last-wins (documented) with a loud-on-collision `schema.extend(x, y, {...})` helper beside it; add `schema.optional(x)` shorthand for `{ schema: x, required: false }`. condition.ts: accept FieldRefs in guard positions compiling to the SAME canonical `{slot, field, equals}` predicate, and parameterize the currently-untyped `equals(slot, value: JsonValue)` signatures via the slot generics. NEW `schema.template(id, spots, build)` (owner-ratified 2026-07-19): generic spots as factory parameters with per-spot defaults (`schema.any()` = graceful bottom, or any declared schema); each specialization emits its OWN concrete declared schema under an explicit caller-provided id (existing duplicate-declaration checks apply; identical re-instantiation may dedupe, conflicting id reuse throws); TS generics flow spot types into handle value types so specialized slots get typed accessors; template id + params recorded in the symbol-keyed meta as provenance, NEVER in canonical bytes — the canonical layer stays monomorphic. NEW union-composition sugar: `schema.union(...)` accepts union handles as members and FLATTENS at authoring time (order-preserving, loud duplicate rejection) — canonical form stays flat `(a|b|c)`; union-of-unions stays forbidden in the vocabulary (unions are associative: nesting adds zero expressive power and nested spellings would fragment digests). Named union declarations deliberately NOT added — TS-const reuse inlines the flat form.
      **Watch:** ZERO canonical/runtime change — authoring surface only: every existing package rebuilds byte-identical (cdk-drift + unchanged digests across ALL traits, not a sample); the emit path reads via meta.declaration and must be unaffected; defaults-as-data is explicitly OUT of scope (it changes acceptance semantics — separate decision if ever); same pass documents the `schema.zod` subset contract (type/properties/required/items/enum/description; additionalProperties=false; refine/transform = build errors forever) as the first-class "bring your zod" path in the CDK README. Also pin what already works but is unexercised: nesting BY REFERENCE is production-proven (`reviewVerdictSchema.blockers = schema.list(blockerSchema)`, agents/src/index.ts:128, through the fully recursive validator schema_validation.rs:526) — but deep list nesting (`[[schema:x]]`, list-of-union) is supported by construction only; add pinning fixtures before any trait relies on it. The zod adapter's nested-object rejection is an ADAPTER limitation, not vocabulary — teaching it to lift nested objects into named declarations is future scope, not this phase.
      **Done when:** `condition.equals(slot.foo, true)` and `condition.equals(slot["exit-code"], 0)` build to canonical guards byte-identical to the string forms; unknown-field access throws at build-script time naming the field; the x/y/z spread example yields exactly foo/bar/baz and `schema.extend` errors on a planted duplicate; a wrong-typed equals literal is a TS error in the editor; a full workspace rebuild leaves every trait digest unchanged; the README documents the zod subset. Templates: a `commandOutput`-style template instantiated bare (spot defaults to `schema:any`) and specialized (spot = a declared object schema) both build, validate, and produce distinct declared schemas whose canonical bytes match hand-written equivalents; conflicting reuse of a specialization id throws naming both sites. Unions: composing two union handles via `schema.union` emits canonical bytes identical to authoring the flat union directly; a planted duplicate member throws. Nesting: `[[schema:text]]` and list-of-union pinning fixtures validate accept/reject correctly through the runtime.


## Group 100: loop exhaustion is a pause, not a failure (owner-ordered 2026-07-19)

Under v0.13 (contract binding: approve only at 100%) an honest run that cannot finish inside its iteration budget MUST end without approval — and today that ending is a dead terminal: status `blocked`, outcome `max-iterations-exhausted` (ctx-annotate G6.P13 even surfaced as `rejected` after a mere bookkeeping failure), batch recipes exit 1, `merge` refuses, and the only way forward is a fresh run that rebuilds all context from zero. Live evidence tonight: run-06aa7243 (P285) and run-e654c81a (P315) both ran 10/10 rounds with the worker's work verified and a single owner-level conflict outstanding — then died terminally with their context stranded. The P374 credit-exhaustion pause proved the right shape: exhaustion should PAUSE the run resumable, not fail it.

**STATUS 2026-07-20 — PARTLY LANDED, PARTLY CONTRADICTED BY MAIN. Do not dispatch P399 as written; it needs owner re-authoring (RUN_PROBLEMS_LOG entry 68).** Landed: `on-failure` retired from loops (1afc83c), exactly as P399's Where/Done-when specify. Contradicted: P399's Shape says a loop declaring no `onExhausted` "still stops the run blocked" — main now CONTINUES by default (ad70d2e, owner decision 2026-07-20), with `on-exhausted = "block"` as the opt-out. Unbuilt: the signal-carrying `onExhausted`; and the `refinement-exhausted` signals it would carry were deleted along with the loop `on-failure` retirement, so its example no longer resolves. OPEN QUESTION: is signal-carrying still wanted on top of a continue-by-default loop?

- [x] **P399** — `onExhausted` owns loop exhaustion end to end; retire `onFailure` from loops.
      **Why:** exhaustion is a normal outcome, not a failure (owner decision 2026-07-20, landed as `on-exhausted = "continue"` in d48d184). But the current shape is half-done: `onExhausted` is a magic string that decides *whether* to continue, while the signal it emits is still borrowed from `onFailure` — a field whose name and type (`FailureTarget`, including forward routing) now describe something the loop no longer does. One concept, two fields, and the surviving one is misnamed. Authors reading a loop cannot tell what running out of rounds actually does.
      **Where:** `modules/core/src/trait/procedure/model.rs` (`SequenceItem.on_exhausted` becomes a signal declaration — one or more `signal:<id>` refs — and loops stop accepting `on_failure`), `modules/core/src/trait/procedure/validate.rs` (loop shape message + reject `on-failure` on `kind = "loop"`; every declared signal must resolve), `modules/core/src/procedure/runtime/control_flow.rs` (the exhaustion branch emits the declared signals and completes the control item; delete the loop's `route_failure`/`route_enclosing_failure` path), `modules/core/src/procedure/runtime/state.rs` (`ControlFrame.on_exhausted` carries the signal list), `packages/cdk/src/sequence.ts` (`LoopSequenceFields.onExhausted?: SignalValue | readonly SignalValue[]`; `onFailure?: never` on loops).
      **Shape:** a loop that declares no `onExhausted` still stops the run blocked at exhaustion (`max-iterations-exhausted`) — the safe default for authors who mean "this must converge". A loop that declares `onExhausted: refinementExhausted` emits that signal and the sequence proceeds to its next step. Later steps MAY react — `sequence.branch({ when: condition.signalMatched(refinementExhausted), … })` — and equally may ignore it; the signal is recorded evidence either way. `--strict-loops` (landed) keeps overriding declared continuation back to blocking.
      **Watch:** signals are already the runtime's general "something happened, act on it if you care" channel — this makes loops speak it instead of owning a bespoke failure vocabulary. Do NOT extend this to prompt/command steps: their `onFailure` (including `FailureRoute` forward recovery) is a different concern and stays. Historical ledgers carrying loop `on-failure` frames must still deserialize. The emitted signal must remain visible to guards in the ENCLOSING sequence, not just inside the loop body.
      **Done when:** a loop declaring `onExhausted` with a signal runs its budget, emits exactly that signal, and the procedure continues to the next step; a downstream branch guarded on that signal selects its exhaustion path, and an identical trait without the branch completes anyway; a loop declaring `on-failure` fails validation with a message naming `on-exhausted`; a loop declaring neither still ends `blocked` with `max-iterations-exhausted`; `--strict-loops` still blocks a declared-continue loop; implement-phase rebuilds on the new field with unchanged behavior.

## Group 66: Deterministic parallelism (extends Group 40 + Group 63; P262–P264 + P344; detailed 2026-07-17, replacing the "approved, detail pending" placeholder)

**Forensics that forced this rewrite:** three loop runs burned against the one-line placeholder. Run 1 (P264 before P262/P263 existed) correctly parked on missing prerequisites; run 2 (P262, `wt-2d540023d79b`, WIP preserved at `d1e6997`) wrote ~2,700 lines and exhausted 10 rounds — its terminal blocker was transactional rollback (a rejected branch output must leave zero accepted effects); run 3 (P262 retry, killed at round 4) showed the blocker onion: authoring/canonical shape → independence validation → barrier runtime → gates, each a phase-sized layer. Lesson encoded here: **one dispatch = one layer**, each with its own Done-when, and semantic correctness lands before actual concurrency. The order is shape → semantics → join policies → true concurrency; every phase is independently green. The P255 `Branch` addition is the house precedent for adding a control kind (model → validate → frame_builders → control_flow → CDK) — follow its file trail exactly.

- [x] **P262** — `Parallel` canonical shape + CDK authoring + static independence validation + dry-plan (NO runtime execution).
      **Why:** the authored contract must exist and be provable before any executor work: run 3's first recurring blocker (`parallel-authoring-and-planning-absent`) was exactly this layer, and reviewers had nothing typed to review against. Landing shape-only also makes every later phase reviewable as "runtime for an already-agreed shape."
      **Where:** `SequenceKind` + `SequenceItem` in `modules/core/src/trait/procedure/model.rs` (follow the `Branch` variant, P255); per-kind validation in `trait/procedure/validate.rs`; CDK `sequence.parallel(id, [...])` and `forEach({ concurrent: true })` in `packages/ts/src/sequence.ts` (beside `when`/`loop`/`forEach`); dry-plan rendering where `branch`/`loop` control kinds already print; SDK mirror via `just sdk-generate` (Group 49 single source).
      **Approach:** add the `parallel` container kind (ordered list of branches, each a sequence item/named sequence) and the `concurrent` flag on `for-each`; **independence validation is static and total**: branches must write disjoint slots (reuse the output-collection walk from `collect_guaranteed_item_outputs`), no branch may consume another branch's outputs, `max-branches` bound required. The runtime REJECTS the new kind with a typed `unsupported: parallel execution lands in P263` error — never a silent sequential fallback that would misrepresent semantics. Token-free traits stay byte-identical; dry-plan shows the panel and its branches in authored order.
      **Watch:** core pure; canonical/`--json` byte-stable for every existing trait (additive kind only); no new Rust deps; `cdk-drift` byte-match for a hand-written equivalent; spec text goes to the `.docs/PRODUCT.md` Procedure section (AMENDED 2026-07-17: the `.protocol/agent-traits` destination was retired pre-launch — see .protocol/README.md; run-2651e446 parked on this). Budget: this is ONE run — if the draft wants executor work, that is scope creep into P263.
      **Review findings to satisfy (from run-2651e446, fold into the implementation):** (a) a `kind = "parallel"` item must REJECT a co-declared `sequence` field — exclusive shape, no silent sequential child expansion; (b) the independence/effect summary must include the implicit `for-each.item` slot write and must NOT count schema-backed ephemeral outputs as slot writes; (c) failure-route binding, recovery-read validation, and first-producer discovery must traverse parallel branches, not only `sequence`/`otherwise`; (d) projections preserve authored structure — dry-plan retains `max-branches`, `inspect --dry-plan` prints authored branch sequence refs (not positional labels), check receipts keep branch order without sort/dedup.
      **Done when:** a trait declaring `sequence.parallel` + `forEach { concurrent }` builds, validates (disjoint-writes enforced, violation = typed error), round-trips byte-stably, renders in dry-plan/preview; runtime rejects execution with the typed unsupported error; **every** existing package re-checks green with unchanged digests (all traits, not a sample) and a hand-written Parallel TOML byte-matches its CDK-authored equivalent; `sdk-check` clean; findings (a)–(d) each demonstrably closed.
- [x] **P263** — Barrier execution semantics on the single cursor: deterministic sequential branch execution, isolation, ordered merge, transactional rollback (still no concurrency).
      **Why:** "parallel" must be semantically correct before it is fast: run 2 died precisely on rollback semantics — a branch whose output fails validation must leave zero accepted effects. Executing panels deterministically (branches in authored order, one cursor) with full barrier/merge/rollback semantics gives byte-stable ledgers and makes P344's concurrency a pure scheduling change with identical observable state.
      **Where:** `enter_control_frame`/LIFO completion in `modules/core/src/procedure/runtime/control_flow.rs`; frame lowering in `procedure/runtime/frame_builders.rs`; acceptance/rollback in `core/src/procedure/session.rs` (`submit_run_call`, the accepted-outputs filtering that already rejects unproven schema claims); append-only revisions in `procedure/runtime/state.rs`.
      **Approach:** a `Parallel` control frame executes branches **sequentially in authored order** on the single cursor; branch outputs accumulate in an isolation buffer, merged into the ledger only at the barrier, in authored order (default join = collect-in-order); a branch rejection rolls back that branch's buffered effects entirely (`RejectedCorrectionRequired` with empty accepted-slot-values — run 2's exact Done-when) and routes per the panel's `on-failure`; loop/branch nesting inside branches follows existing LIFO discipline.
      **Watch:** determinism is the contract — identical inputs → byte-identical ledger; rollback leaves no revision residue (revisions stay append-only: buffer-then-commit, never write-then-delete); `modules/core` pure; the isolation buffer is runtime state, never canonical; budget: ONE run — join *policies* beyond collect-in-order belong to P264.
      **Done when:** a stub-harness fixture trait with a 3-branch panel executes end to end: outputs land at the barrier in authored order; a constructed invalid branch output rolls back with zero accepted effects and the typed rejection; re-run byte-identical; existing sequential traits byte-stable.
- [x] **P264** — Join policies + failure routing: `reduce-merge`, `quorum-verdict`, per-branch `on-failure`.
      **Why:** collect-in-order (P263's default) covers fan-out/fan-in, but the review-panel use cases (N reviewers → one verdict) need reduce/quorum joins with typed, deterministic resolution — this is the layer that makes parallel reviewers a first-class pattern instead of prompt glue.
      **Where:** join declaration on the panel in `trait/procedure/model.rs` + validation in `validate.rs`; barrier resolution in `control_flow.rs` where P263 merges; quorum predicates reuse the closed guard vocabulary (`condition.rs`, P256 count/threshold predicates); CDK join options in `packages/ts/src/sequence.ts`.
      **Approach:** `join = collect-in-order (default) | reduce-merge | quorum-verdict`; reduce-merge folds branch outputs into one object slot via the existing `Merge`/`SetField` write-ops (P258) — runtime owns the mutation; quorum-verdict evaluates a declared guard over branch verdict slots (e.g. `count(approved) >= 2`) into a typed panel outcome; per-branch `on-failure` routes a failed branch to skip/park/panel-fail deterministically, recorded in the ledger.
      **Watch:** joins are total and deterministic — no LLM involvement in resolution; quorum guards stay inside the closed condition vocabulary (no new expression language); canonical additions byte-stable for P262/P263 traits; budget: ONE run.
      **Done when:** fixture panels demonstrate all three joins deterministically (incl. a quorum that fails and routes on-failure); ledger records the join resolution as evidence; spec + sdk mirrors updated; existing packages unchanged.
- [x] **P344** — True runtime concurrency + supervision: concurrent branch dispatch, per-branch sub-ledgers, max-in-flight, `--wait`/`interrupt` (proposed 2026-07-17; the only phase that changes wall-clock, none that changes semantics).
      **Why:** with P263/P264 fixing observable semantics, concurrency becomes a scheduling optimization: dispatch independent branches' frames concurrently while producing the SAME final ledger the sequential execution would — the strongest possible review contract ("byte-identical to sequential") and the payoff the group exists for.
      **Where:** the conductor loop in `modules/cli/src/app/drive.rs` (single-run frame pump today) + `modules/io/src/run.rs` frame advancement; per-branch sub-ledger identity beside the run ledger (`.ctx/runs`, atomic-write discipline); role-queue dispatch where roles map to harness sessions (`drive.rs` assignment plumbing); CLI knobs on `run`/`session start` (`max-in-flight`, `--wait`, interrupt handling).
      **Approach:** branches whose frames are independent (P262's validation guarantees it) dispatch concurrently up to `max-in-flight`; each branch writes a sub-ledger (parent run id + branch index) merged at the barrier exactly as P263 defines; supervision: a branch harness failure retries per existing per-frame retry budget, then routes per P264 `on-failure`; interrupt drains in-flight branches to a clean parkable state. Equivalence gate: a fixture panel run concurrent and sequential must produce byte-identical final ledgers (modulo operational timestamps/ids, per the existing ledger-id discipline).
      **Watch:** concurrency lives in cli/io ONLY — core's executor semantics are P263's and do not change; sub-ledger ids are operational, never canonical; the sequential==concurrent equivalence check is the phase's soul — if it cannot hold for some construct, that construct stays sequential rather than weakening the contract; interacts with Group 85 (concurrent runs) — same repo-level locks apply; budget: ONE run, likely the hardest of the four — if the draft cannot fit supervision + equivalence in one run, split supervision out rather than thinning the equivalence gate.
      **Done when:** a 3-branch fixture panel runs with `max-in-flight=2` faster than sequential while the final ledger is equivalence-checked against the sequential execution; interrupt parks cleanly with intact sub-ledgers; Group 85's race test extended with one concurrent-panel case.

## Group 70: implement-phase upgrade — ground the verdict in machine-checked reality (extends Group 40 + Group 46; proposed 2026-07-10) — open remainder rehomed to Group 103 (2026-07-21)

From an honest review of `.ctx/traits/implement-phase/source/index.ts` (path swept 2026-07-21; was `.agents/traits/implement-phase/trait.ts`): its real edge is genuine (enforced multi-model bounded approval — `smart-1`=GPT-5.6, `smart-2`=Claude Fable are different families — with a safe halt on exhaustion). But the typed verdict attaches to `workSummary` (the worker's self-report), not the actual diff, and "approved" has no deterministic backing. This grounds the verdict in machine-checked reality (real diff in, real gates gating the loop-exit), converting the weakest joint into the strongest demo of the runtime vs a skill. (Numbered Group 70 — a concurrent session took Group 69 / P268–P273 for the adoption funnel.)

- [x] **P274** — `check` step primitive: a command run for its typed pass/fail verdict, condition-readable + re-evaluated per loop iteration. *(Landed 2026-07-21, 4b9fc9f — landed without its declared P397 dep; verify command-output routing gaps against P397's contract when that phase runs.)*
      **Why:** gating a loop on "the build is actually green" is only ergonomic if a step's exit-code result is a first-class, condition-readable value; today a loop `until` can't read command success, forcing a wrapper-script→enum→condition hack. A `check` step (run-for-verdict, distinct from `command`'s run-for-output) makes it one line and is reusable for any test/lint/typecheck/health gate. This is the owner's "slot but dynamic — an emitter of value."
      **Where:** new `Check` variant on `SequenceKind` (`modules/core/src/trait/procedure/model.rs`, alongside Prompt/Command/Sequence/Loop/ForEach [+ Branch P255]) + validation (`validate.rs`); the io runner already computes `success` from the exit code (`modules/io/src/command.rs:103-117`, `success:bool` at `:109`) — surface it as a typed pass/fail output; frame build in `frame_builders.rs`/`frames.rs`; CDK `sequence.check(id, {argv, success_exit_code?})` (`packages/cdk/src/sequence.ts`; was `packages/ts` pre-P369).
      **Approach:** `check` runs argv like a command but its output is a **typed pass/fail** (Boolean or `pass`/`fail` enum) derived from `success` (exit ∈ `success_exit_code`, default `[0]`), NOT stdout; stdout/stderr captured as evidence and available as input to downstream steps. Inside a loop it re-runs each iteration and overwrites its output slot → latest-value semantics the `until` reads.
      **Watch:** `modules/core` stays pure (exit→pass/fail is a pure mapping; io runs the process); deterministic pass/fail → byte-stable evidence/ledger; keep the no-shell argv spawn; a `check` is a new audited canonical kind — spec text → Group 52 P231 `50-procedure`; `sequence.check` is a CDK method (no collision with the `ctx traits check` CLI verb); no new Rust deps.
      **Done when:** `sequence.check({argv:["cargo","check"]})` yields a typed pass/fail a `condition`/loop-`until` reads; a failing check leaves `fail` (loop continues), passing leaves `pass`; stderr captured as evidence; canonical/`--json` byte-stable.
      **AMENDED 2026-07-21 (release-eve pre-flight):** land AFTER P397 (command-output routing) — both touch command output surfaces; P397's text-slot rule first so the check kind's stdout/evidence story aligns with it.
- [x] **P275** — Ground the review: feed the real diff to reviewers + gate the loop-exit on the `check`. *(Landed 2026-07-21, 6b6915d.)*
      **Why:** reviewers currently judge `workSummary` (the worker's self-report) and "approved" has no deterministic backing — the typed verdict decorates the *chatter*, not the *work*. Feeding the real diff + requiring a green build to exit makes "approved" mean *two independent models signed off AND the build/lint/typecheck provably passed* — the un-skill-able property.
      **Where:** `.ctx/traits/implement-phase/source/index.ts` (+ `generated/index.toml`; line refs below are 2026-07-10 vintage — re-derive): a `git diff` command step → slot → `input` to `review-1`/`review-2` (`:174,:185`); a `check` (P274) running the repo gates inside the `refinement-loop` (`:171`); loop `until` (`:206-209`) → `all([check==pass, verdict1 approved, verdict2 approved])`.
      **Approach:** `git diff` (`--stat`+scoped for large diffs) produces the diff slot reviewers read; the gates `check` runs `cargo build && clippy -D warnings && fmt --check && tsc --noEmit` each iteration → pass/fail; a failing check keeps the bounded loop going (`apply-fixes` re-runs with the check's captured output as input), exiting only on green + double-approval.
      **Watch:** cap/scope the diff (evidence, not the whole tree); the gates `check` runs after `apply-fixes` (or at loop head) so its verdict reflects current work; still bounded by `iterations:4` → escalation (P277); byte-stable canonical; the diff is *added* evidence — reviewers still inspect the tree.
      **Done when:** a phase whose gates fail keeps looping and exits only when the `check` is green AND both models approve; the receipt shows the check result + both typed verdicts + the diff each reviewer saw.
- [x] **P276** — Richer verdict schema + shared review-rubric knowledge resource.
      **Why:** `{status, feedback}` lets a reviewer rubber-stamp with one word, and `review-1`/`review-2` duplicate near-identical prompt prose (DRY violation + drift risk). A per-dimension verdict citing `file:line`, plus one shared rubric, makes the review accountable and single-sourced.
      **Where:** `.ctx/traits/implement-phase/source/index.ts` — `reviewVerdict` schema (`:85-97`) → per-dimension; a new `review-rubric` resource under `resources/` both reviewers `use`; the `review-1`/`review-2` prompts (`:174-195`) reference the rubric instead of inlining it.
      **Approach:** verdict `{scope, correctness:[{finding,file,line}], house_rules, gates_ran, verdict: approved|revise}` (`schema.object` + findings list); factor "what a good review checks" into the shared rubric resource (single source of truth); reviewers cite concrete `file:line` findings.
      **Watch:** pure `schema.object` (today); byte-stable canonical; cleaner with inline `content` (P233) + the Group 46 knowledge-trait pattern once they land; keep a scalar `verdict` field the loop `until` can read.
      **Done when:** a reviewer must emit per-dimension findings with `file:line`; both reviewers share one rubric resource; the trait checks green + synth byte-stable.
      **AMENDED 2026-07-21 (release-eve pre-flight):** sequence AFTER Group 102 P408 — the leftovers field lands in the shared reviewer-verdict surface first; build the richer schema ON it rather than forking the verdict shape in two queued phases.
- [ ] **P277** — Escalation branch on exhaustion + drop the commit file-hop.
      **Why:** the `refinement-exhausted` signal is decorative today (the loop halts silently as `Blocked`), and the commit message travels through a `.git/CTX_COMMITMSG` file because argv couldn't take a slot value. A branch on exhaustion makes the failure loud/actionable; P265 removes the file-hop.
      **Where:** `.ctx/traits/implement-phase/source/index.ts` — the loop `onFailure`/`refinementExhausted` (`:116-119,:211`) → a `branch` (P255) to an escalation/report step; the `git-commit` step (`:229-235`) `argv=["git","commit","-F",".git/CTX_COMMITMSG"]` → `["git","commit","-m","{slot:commit-message}"]`, and the scribe step (`:213-222`) drops the file-write.
      **Approach:** on loop exhaustion `branch` (P255) into a step that writes a report / halts visibly (not a silent Blocked); rewrite `git-commit` to interpolate `{slot:commit-message}` (P265) and remove the scribe's `.git/CTX_COMMITMSG` write.
      **Watch:** needs the `branch` primitive (P255, Group 63) — sequence after it; keep the halt safe (the branch escalates, it does NOT commit unapproved work); byte-stable canonical; the commit-message interpolation is scalar (P265 rules).
      **Done when:** a phase exhausting 4 rounds fires the escalation step (not a silent halt); the commit uses `-m {slot:commit-message}` with no file-hop; the scribe no longer writes `.git/CTX_COMMITMSG`.
      **AMENDED 2026-07-21 (release-eve pre-flight):** DO NOT DISPATCH AS WRITTEN. The commit file-hop clause is already satisfied (3df765f: argv injection in `implement`; `implement-phase` always injected — verified 2026-07-21, no `CTX_COMMITMSG` anywhere). The exhaustion-escalation premise predates v0.15 (exhaustion continues with verdicts attached) and Group 102 (leftovers own the unresolved-at-exhaustion story). Re-author against those two before queueing.
## Group 74: Resource model — three axes (whether/when/how), drop `required` (proposed 2026-07-13)

Writing the launch blog exposed four tightenings to the resource model. Reframe resource inclusion as **three orthogonal axes** — *whether* it is included (a step-input guard), *when* it loads (`trigger`), *how* it renders (`render`) — and remove the redundant `required` flag. The spec text is **already updated this session** (`.protocol/agent-traits/40-prompts.md` owns the resource fields + trigger/render semantics; `50-procedure.md` owns the guarded resource input; `90-rendering.md` owns render-mode honoring); these phases bring the struct, schema, CDK, and traits into line (definition-first). Two standing rules: `schema_for!(Trait)` regenerates the JSON-schema field lists (P231 Watch — never hand-mirror), and the **installed `ctx` binary predates inline `content`** (added Group 53) so a `cargo build --release` + reinstall is mandatory before render/guards can be exercised. Grounding: `modules/core/src/trait/resource.rs` (struct :45-80, `ResourceTrigger` :22-34, `content` :58, `required` :75), `resource_plan.rs` (inclusion reasons/warnings :104-127), `modules/core/src/schema.rs:82-83` (`schema_for!(Trait)`), `validate.rs:509,:539` (step input-ref validation), `packages/cdk/src/{procedure,sequence,condition}.ts`. Blast radius mapped: **10 resource declarations across 5 traits** (`engineering-standards`, `deep-refactor`, `guarded-change`, `trait-spec`, `implement-phase`) each set `required: true` + `trigger: "on-activation"`; none use `variant`/`input`/`content`.

- [x] **P287** — Narrow `ResourceTrigger` to `{on-activation, on-demand}`; default `on-demand`.
      **Why:** the enum carries four values (`discovery`/`on-activation`/`on-demand`/`manual`) but only two are wanted, and the vocabulary was undocumented until this session's `40-prompts.md` edit. `discovery`/`manual` drag planner machinery no trait uses.
      **Where:** `resource.rs:22-34` (drop `Discovery`/`Manual` variants); `resource_plan.rs` inclusion reasons `DiscoveryTriggered`/`DeferredTrigger` (:104-127) + omitted-trigger default; regenerate `agent-traits.schema.json` `ResourceTrigger` oneOf (`schema.rs:82`).
      **Approach:** two-variant enum; omitted `trigger` resolves to `on-demand`; remove the discovery/manual inclusion-planner reasons and any wiring; regenerate schema.
      **Watch:** `60-activation.md`'s `load-level` (`discovery`/`summary`/`full`) and `[activation] manual` are SEPARATE concepts — do not touch them; existing traits all set `on-activation` explicitly, so timing is unchanged for them; keep `modules/core` pure; regenerate schema (the vendored trait-spec copy lags — re-sync).
      **Done when:** the enum has exactly two variants; the schema oneOf lists two; an omitted trigger resolves to `on-demand`; every trait checks green.
- [x] **P288** — Remove `required` from the resource model.
      **Why:** `required` already defaults `false` and is advisory-only (it only feeds `RequiredMissing*` inclusion warnings, never a hard gate — core never reads files); "whether to include" is now the guard + trigger's job, so the flag is redundant.
      **Where:** `resource.rs:75` (struct field + serde); schema resource object; `resource_plan.rs` warnings `RequiredMissingDigest`/`RequiredFileMissing`; CDK ResourceFields (pass-through — stop emitting); strip `required: true` from the 10 declarations across the 5 traits; `40-prompts.md` field list (done).
      **Approach:** delete the field; drop the two inclusion warnings; strip the flag from the 10 decls; regenerate schema; rebuild every trait TOML.
      **Watch:** do NOT touch `schema.field({ required })` options (a different concept — `deep-refactor:110,115`, `guarded-change:121,126`, `implement-phase:110,115`); every built TOML's canonical digest changes → rebuild + re-check + re-sync all.
      **Done when:** `required` is absent from struct/schema/traits; no `RequiredMissing*` warnings remain; checks green.
- [x] **P289** — Add explicit `render` mode `{reference, inline}`, decoupled from source.
      **Why:** make inclusion FORM an explicit choice so a `path` resource can be embedded inline (context-hygiene / prompt-caching control) instead of the form being implied by path-vs-content — the blog's realization.
      **Where:** `resource.rs` (new `render` field + `ResourceRender` enum + validation); the renderer/inclusion path (`resource_plan.rs` + render surface) to embed `inline` bodies vs emit `reference` pointers; schema regen; CDK ResourceFields (`render?`); `40-prompts.md` + `90-rendering.md` (done).
      **Approach:** enum {Reference, Inline}; source-aware default (`path`→reference, `content`→inline); allow explicit `render = "inline"` on a `path` resource (host-IO read at render, embed); reject `render = "reference"` on a `content` resource; wire the renderer to honor it.
      **Watch:** NAMING — a `render` reference KIND already exists (`render:<id>`, `20-references.md:13`); confirm the field name `render` vs e.g. `render-mode` to avoid reader confusion (owner call). Inline-embedding a path resource is host I/O at the edge — keep `modules/core` pure. Byte ripple on every trait that sets it.
      **Done when:** `render` validates per the rules; a `path` + `render="inline"` resource embeds its body; a `content` + `render="reference"` resource is rejected; schema lists the enum; checks green.
- [x] **P290** — Conditional resource inclusion — a `when`-guarded resource input on a step.
      **Why:** include a resource in a step's context only when a condition holds — the fourth tightening — reusing the guard system that already gates steps (`branch`/`when`, fully implemented in Group 63).
      **Where:** the step-input model (`modules/core/src/trait/procedure/model.rs` + `validate.rs:509,:539`) to allow a guarded resource entry `{ ref, when }`; `resource_plan.rs` new `ResourceInclusionReason::ConditionalInput { sequence_index, guard }` evaluated at step readiness; CDK `sequence.ts` input to accept `{ resource, when }`; guard grammar reused from `condition.ts`; schema regen; `50-procedure.md` (done).
      **Approach:** a step's resource input MAY carry an optional `when` guard (standard depth-limited guard model); the planner includes that resource only when the guard holds at readiness; port/slot inputs stay unconditional; static analysis validates guard + ref; keep decision-record discipline consistent with `branch`.
      **Watch:** reuse Group 63 guards — do not reinvent; guard evaluation is deterministic (request facts only); this gates a RESOURCE (not a step), distinct from `branch`; byte ripple.
      **Done when:** a step declares a `when`-guarded resource input; the planner includes it only when the guard holds; a fixture drive proves both branches; the schema documents the shape; checks green.
- [x] **P291** — Regenerate, rebuild, reinstall; land the model coherently.
      **Why:** the spec (this session) leads the struct; close the loop so spec/struct/schema/traits/binary are consistent.
      **Where:** `schema_for!(Trait)` → `agent-traits.schema.json` (+ the stale `.ctx/traits/vendor/trait-spec/resources/` copy); rebuild all `.ctx/traits/*/generated/index.toml`; `ctx traits sync` for dependents; `cargo build --release` + reinstall `ctx`.
      **Approach:** regenerate schema (never hand-edit); rebuild + re-check every trait (canonical digests change — expected); re-sync vendored deps; reinstall the binary; confirm `check` green cross-tree and a `guarded-change`/`implement-phase` fixture still drives.
      **Watch:** until P287-P290 land, a spec↔struct mismatch is expected (definition-first); the installed binary predates inline `content` so the rebuild is mandatory; `.plans`/`.docs` gitignored (commit trait/schema/spec changes with `git add -f` where needed).
      **Done when:** schema regenerated; all traits rebuilt + check green; binary reinstalled; spec, struct, schema, and traits consistent.

## Group 80: Session abstraction in the CDK — declare sessions, share context between agents (proposed 2026-07-15) — ROUGH / design-first, not settled

Today an agent's session lifecycle (`per-frame` vs `persistent`) lives in the **profile** (`.ctx/runs/profiles/*.toml` `[assign.<role>].session-mode`) — decoupled from the trait, per-role only, and with **no way for two agents to share one session**. Make the session a first-class, declarative CDK construct expressed **on the agent in code**, and add the new capability it unlocks: two agents bound to the **same session share context** (one underlying harness session / transcript). Motivation is expressiveness (session topology is a trait-*design* concern, not routing) plus the speculative payoff of a **smart planner + a fast worker sharing context** (the fast one inherits the smart one's understanding) — conditional smart/fast splits later. Also folds the session-lifecycle half out of profiles (aligns with Group 77's retire-profiles direction; here the *trait* declares the session graph, `ctx.toml`/profile still binds harnesses/models).

**Rough shape (nothing detailed yet):**
- **API:** `const s1 = session("session-1", { … })` → a session handle; built-in `session.PerFrame` / `session.Persistent` constants for the no-sharing case. Role constructors (`worker()`, `reviewer()`, `planner()`, …) gain a `session` field accepting either a named handle (shared) or a mode constant. So an agent reads `worker("worker", { session: s1 })` or `worker("worker", { session: session.PerFrame })`.
- **Sharing semantics:** two agents with the *same* handle drive one underlying harness session — agent B sees agent A's transcript. A bare mode constant = today's per-agent lifecycle, no sharing.

**Rough phases (loose; design before code):**
- [x] **P326** — CDK surface + shape. `session(id, opts)` constructor + `session.PerFrame`/`session.Persistent` constants; add the `session` field to the role constructors (`packages/cdk/src`). Draft-only: define the authored surface and how it normalizes into draft JSON; no runtime behavior yet.
- [x] **P327** — Core model + validation. Represent session declarations + agent→session binding in the canonical trait; constraints: agents sharing a session must resolve to the **same harness** (you can't share a claude-code session with an opencode agent) and compatible transport; a session graph that's coherent under the single-cursor runtime. Decide the relationship to profile `[assign].session-mode` (supersede / redirect / dual-read during migration).
- [x] **P328** — Runtime wiring. `drive` keys harness sessions off the declared **session graph** (shared id → one warm/resumed session with an interleaved transcript) instead of per-role `session-mode`; warm/resume semantics move from role to session.

**Open questions to settle first (why it's design-first):**
- **Cross-harness sharing** is impossible — a shared session pins its agents to one harness. Does the model reject a mismatched binding, or does declaring a shared session *imply* the harness for its members?
- **Interleaving coherence:** if two shared agents run in different sequence steps, their transcript interleaves — is that always sensible, or only for adjacent steps? Single-cursor determinism must hold.
- **Home of session topology:** trait (digest-locked, portable — "who shares with whom" is design) vs profile/ctx.toml (runtime binding — harness/model). Likely: **trait declares the graph; ctx.toml binds harnesses.** Confirm this split before building.
- **Migration** from profile `session-mode` (Group 77 was moving it to ctx.toml; this proposes the CDK instead — pick one home).

## Group 81: Runner output fixes — loop iteration render + thinking-only narration (proposed 2026-07-15)

Three owner asks on the `--progress tui` live panel, all presentational polish on the same surface as Group 43.5 (`run_view.rs`/`tui.rs`/`harness_stream.rs`). **Load-bearing constraint (same as Group 43.5): the loop token/time counters and the narration are wall-clock / stream-derived (non-deterministic) — they stay strictly presentational, out of every byte-stable surface, no clock or counter in `modules/core`.** Owner's verbatim asks preserved in the phases below.

- [x] **P329** — Loop iteration render semantics on the live panel.  *(not implemented — slot:phase-brief arrived empty, no contract to execute; owner must repopulate brief and re-run)* *Owner ask, verbatim:* "I want to fix looping, if it starts new iterations, sub-steps should be reset… loop should itself hold total token & time counter on itself too, this one is not reset throughout all iterations, but sub-steps are reset back to pending fully. Additionally when loop is done ALL of it's sub-steps should be set to 'done' not only ones that ran." Three behaviors: **(a)** on a new iteration (iteration index increments) the loop's sub-steps reset to `Pending` fully — no stale prior-iteration `Done`/status carried over; **(b)** the **loop container** holds its own cumulative **output-token + wall-clock** counters that accumulate across ALL iterations and are **not** reset on iteration rollover (only the sub-steps reset); **(c)** on loop completion ALL of the loop's sub-steps render `Done`, not only the ones that ran in the final iteration.
      **Why:** today the loop repaint re-derives sub-step state from the active runtime path, so a new iteration shows leftover state instead of a clean reset, the loop line has no aggregate token/time (the P198.5-era per-step counters reset with the sub-steps), and a completed loop leaves un-run sub-steps looking unfinished — all three misread the actual loop progress.
      **Where:** `modules/cli/src/app/run_view.rs` — `StepState` (`Done` `:84`, `Pending` `:86`, the mid-iteration container variant `:98`), `step_state`/`step_status_text` (`:1125`,`:1161`), the loop container line + `iteration` field (`frame.iteration_index? + 1` `:1235`; `iteration: None` sites `:920`,`:992`,`:999`), progress counting (`counts_progress && state == StepState::Done` `:420`), status cell render (`:830`,`:814`); token/elapsed aggregation rides the same non-deterministic presentational path Group 43.5 added — accumulate per-frame output-token + elapsed onto the loop container keyed by structural path, do **not** reset on iteration change.
      **Approach:** key sub-step display state to the *current* iteration so rollover blanks them to `Pending`; hold a per-loop accumulator (tokens + elapsed) on the container that ignores iteration boundaries; when the loop's runtime status is terminal/completed, force all its sub-steps to `Done` for the final paint.
      **Done when:** starting iteration N+1 shows the loop's sub-steps freshly `Pending`; the loop line shows a running token + time total that never drops on rollover; a finished loop paints every sub-step `Done`. No `modules/core` change; no byte-stable surface touched.

- [x] **P330** — Narrate thinking blocks only, self-replacing, no slow-path fallback + trim the window. *Owner ask, verbatim:* "Let's narrate only thinking blocks, and don't show timeout or show direct text when narration is slow, just update whenever new narration comes, then it gets replaced by next one etc etc." and "Let's trim thinking blocks to 100-150 characters for narration, this way we will speed it up." Three behaviors: **(a)** feed the narrator **only** thinking/reasoning deltas — drop plain assistant text from the narration window; **(b)** when narration is slow, show **no** timeout warning and **no** direct-text passthrough floor — hold the last narration line and simply replace it when the next narration arrives; **(c)** **trim the thinking window to 100–150 chars** before narrating so the narrator's input (and thus latency) stays small.
      **Why:** the narrator currently ingests all text deltas and, on a slow/failed cycle, surfaces a `⚠ narrator: disabled… passthrough` warning + raw agent text — noisy and off-topic; thinking blocks are the signal worth narrating, and a short window is what keeps the narrator fast enough to keep up with the stream.
      **Where:** `modules/cli/src/app/harness_stream.rs` — `ingest_chunk`/`drain_text_deltas` (`:239`,`:519`) to keep thinking/reasoning deltas only; `append_window` + a window-length cap (~150 chars, tail-biased) in `ingest_chunk`; the slow/empty + failure branches that emit the passthrough floor / disable-warning (`:202`,`:219`,`:227`) to drop the direct-text + timeout display and just leave the prior line until the next `narrate_once` result lands; `narrate_once`/`narrator_prompt` (`:250`,`:251`). Live-line paint in `run_view.rs` `render_live_line_locked` (`:223`).
      **Approach:** filter the delta stream to thinking-only at ingest; cap the accumulated window to the last 100–150 chars; on a slow or failed narration cycle, no-op the panel (keep the current line) instead of writing a warning or raw text; the next successful narration replaces the line.
      **Done when:** the live panel narrates only from thinking blocks, the narrator input is capped at ~150 chars, and a slow/failed cycle changes nothing on screen (no timeout text, no raw agent text) until the next narration replaces the line.

## Group 82: CDK vocabulary casing — `intent.avoid.ScopeCreep` (lowercase namespace, PascalCase leaf) (proposed 2026-07-15)

Fixes the three-case mix in the typed vocabulary — `Intent.avoid.scopeCreep` = PascalCase root + lowercase axis + camelCase leaf — and folds away the `Intent` (value-object) vs `intent` (bare custom-slug function, `packages/cdk/src/trait.ts:316`) dual. Target author-facing convention, applied **uniformly across every facet**: a **lowercase, callable namespace + a PascalCase leaf constant**, with the axis sub-namespaces kept (`require`/`focus`/`avoid`/`block` mirror the `[intent_*]` sections in `modules/core/builtins.toml` — they carry real meaning, so `intent.avoid.ScopeCreep`, **not** flat `intent.ScopeCreep`). Reuses the existing `caseAliases` mechanism (`trait.ts:322-335`, which already makes `tone.FindingsFirst` resolve today), so camelCase + SCREAMING_SNAKE spellings stay as working aliases — **non-breaking**, no consumer migration forced. **Load-bearing:** the slug *values* stay kebab (`"scope-creep"`) and the canonical TOML is untouched — this is author-surface ergonomics only, outside the byte-stable digest. **Two decisions were settled on the recommended defaults (all facets; PascalCase-canonical-plus-aliases); revisit before build if the owner wants Intent-only or a one-true-form.**

- [x] **P331** — Recased callable vocabulary namespaces in the TS CDK. Build `intent = Object.assign(customSlugFn, { require, focus, avoid, block })` with each axis wrapped in a nested `caseAliases`, so `intent.avoid.ScopeCreep` (canonical), `intent.avoid.scopeCreep` (alias), and `intent.avoid.SCOPE_CREEP` all resolve to `"scope-creep"`; add the lowercase builders that don't exist yet (`directness`/`scopeControl`/`initiative`/`uncertainty`/`format`) mirroring the shipped `tone`/`method`/`verbosity` pattern (`trait.ts:311-313`); extend `caseAliases`/`CaseAliases` (`trait.ts:322-335`) with a one-level **nested** variant for Intent; demote the PascalCase raw objects (`Intent`, `Tone`, …) to internal source maps (keep-but-deprecate or drop their `index.ts` exports), removing the `Intent`-vs-`intent` dual. **Where:** `packages/ts/src/trait.ts`, `packages/ts/src/index.ts`; `packages/ts/src/generated.ts` stays camelCase (machine-generated, read-only — no codegen change on this path). **Done when:** `intent.avoid.ScopeCreep` type-checks and `=== "scope-creep"`; every facet reads `x.PascalLeaf`; old camelCase spellings still compile; `packages/ts` build + typecheck green.
- [x] **P332** — Canonical form in docs + a stated convention note. Update the `.docs/PRODUCT.md` vocabulary tables + examples to show the `intent.avoid.ScopeCreep` / `tone.FindingsFirst` form, and write the naming rule that is currently unwritten (lowercase callable namespaces; PascalCase leaf constants; kebab slug values; camelCase/SCREAMING as aliases). *(One-true-form variant, only if that policy is chosen instead: flip codegen `camel_property`→`pascal_property` at `modules/cli/src/app/sdk_generate.rs:472`, drop `caseAliases`, regenerate `generated.ts`, and migrate consumers — larger, breaking.)* **Done when:** docs show the canonical form and state the rule; `ctx traits sdk-generate` diff is clean (non-breaking path) or regenerated (one-true-form path).

**Decisions to confirm before build (defaulted, owner was away):**
- **Facet scope:** all facets uniform *(default/recommended — else Intent reads one way and Tone/Method/… another)* vs Intent-only now.
- **Spelling policy:** PascalCase canonical + keep camel/SCREAMING aliases *(default/recommended; non-breaking; reuses `caseAliases`)* vs PascalCase-only one-true-form *(breaking, but cheap today — `.ctx/traits/*/source/index.ts` use string literals, so typed-vocabulary consumers are near-zero)*.
- [x] **P412** — Bare-leaf vocabulary aliases: `intent.ScopeCreep` works without naming the facet. (owner-ordered 2026-07-21; **ABSORBED into P458 2026-07-23** — lands with the grammar migration's vocab finish; this contract text stays authoritative for the collision rule + synthetic-collision test)
      **Why:** P331/P332 landed lowercase callable namespaces with a full alias surface and documented the convention — but every leaf still requires its facet: `intent.avoid.ScopeCreep`, `intent.require.Leanness`. The facet is load-bearing for the CANONICAL slug, not for authoring ergonomics: the leaf name is almost always unambiguous, and typing the facet is friction the author pays on every line. Owner call: the leaf alone should resolve.
      **Where:** packages/cdk vocabulary namespace generation (P331's file trail, commit f6cc983); the P332 convention note in PRODUCT.md gains one paragraph.
      **Approach:** every facetted vocabulary namespace (`intent`, `behavior`, and siblings) additionally exposes each leaf at the namespace ROOT, in the same casing set the facet level exposes (`intent.ScopeCreep` + `intent.scopeCreep` if both casings exist below). The alias is pure sugar: it resolves to the identical declaration and identical canonical slug — zero canonical/digest change, pinned by fixture. **Collision rule:** a leaf slug that exists under more than one facet (e.g. hypothetically both `require.X` and `avoid.X`) is NOT flattened — bare access is absent from the types and throws at authoring time naming both qualified forms; the qualified spellings remain the only way to disambiguate. No collision exists in today's vocabulary — add a synthetic-collision unit test so the rule is exercised before it is ever needed.
      **Watch:** authoring sugar only — canonical bytes byte-identical either spelling (drift-checked fixture builds one trait each way and asserts equal digests); keep the namespace surface enumerable-clean (aliases non-enumerable or filtered wherever declarations are collected, so a bare leaf never double-declares); do not fold this into P398 (separate concern, separate review).
      **Done when:** `intent.ScopeCreep` compiles with editor completion and builds to the same canonical bytes as `intent.avoid.ScopeCreep`; the synthetic-collision test proves ambiguous leaves stay qualified-only with a named error; CDK tests + typecheck green; the PRODUCT.md convention note documents bare-leaf resolution and the collision rule.

## Group 85: Parallel batch safety — N concurrent worktree runs land without racing (proposed 2026-07-16)

What is already safe: P267 gives every run its own worktree + `ctx/run/<id>` branch off HEAD (trees never collide, the trait's own git steps land on the run branch), ledgers are collision-safe (unique `session-<hash>.json`, atomic writes) and both `implement` recipes write mktemp-derived unique ledger paths, and P287's merge parks on anything non-mechanical with the branch + worktree intact. What is NOT safe when several `just implement`/`just implement-lean` batches run at once: **merges race** — `ctx traits merge` takes no cross-process lock (`merge.rs` preflights at `:180-195` but two merges landing together mean the second sees main move mid-flight → park, or trips a raw git `index.lock` → error), a park exits the batch script non-zero and kills its remaining phases; git-level lock contention can also hit concurrent `git worktree add`/`remove`; a run branched from an older HEAD can merge cleanly while being semantically stale against what a sibling batch just landed; and every fresh worktree cold-builds `target/` so N parallel batches do N simultaneous full cargo builds. Goal: several batches over independent phases run unattended — mechanical races queue instead of parking, stale-base overlap is detected and typed, and only genuine judgment conflicts park. Distinct layer from Group 66 (parallelism *inside* one run); this group is about independent runs/batches racing at the repo boundary. Evidence 2026-07-17: the 13:04 4-run batch abort left a **0-byte** `session-implement-kvNRwA7r.json` — the "atomic writes" claim above does not hold under concurrent batch startup; session-store writes need write-then-rename verified under contention (fold into this group's scope).

- [x] **P339** — Cross-process merge lock: `ctx traits merge` serializes via an atomic lockfile, queueing instead of parking on mechanical races.
      **Why:** the merge step is the only main-mutating operation in the whole flow and currently has no mutual exclusion — two batches finishing phases near-simultaneously produce a spurious park (ff check fails mid-flight) or a raw git lock error, and the recipe's stop-on-park then kills the rest of that batch; a queue turns the race into a wait.
      **Where:** `modules/cli/src/app/merge.rs` (`handle_merge` `:49`, `merge` `:81`, preflight `:180-195`); lock home under the run store (`layout::RUN_ROOT`, e.g. `.ctx/runs/merge.lock`); lock file IO in `modules/io` (atomic `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` create-with-pid, or flock — pick one, document why); both Justfile batch recipes (drop any shell-level locking so there is exactly one mechanism).
      **Approach:** acquire the lock before preflight; default behavior waits with a bounded timeout + poll interval (flag `--no-wait` to fail fast instead); **re-run preflight and re-resolve `main` after acquiring** (main may have moved while queued — the rebase-onto-current-main path already handles the moved base); record lock evidence (wait duration, holder pid/run-id) in the merge outcome so queueing is visible in the ledger, not silent; release on every exit path including park (RAII guard); stale-lock recovery: a lock whose holder pid is dead is reclaimable with a warning.
      **Watch:** core stays pure (locking is io); no new deps (std fs atomics or rustix already in tree — do NOT add a lock crate); the lock serializes *merges only*, never runs; park semantics unchanged — the lock removes mechanical races, judgment conflicts still park; timeout must not default to infinite (a wedged merge would deadlock every batch); lock evidence is operational (ledger/report), never canonical/digested.
      **Done when:** two `ctx traits merge` invocations racing on completed runs both land, serialized, with the second's wait recorded; `--no-wait` fails fast with a typed reason; a dead-holder lock is reclaimed with a warning; recipes contain no shell locking.
- [x] **P340** — Git-lock resilience: bounded retry-with-backoff on `index.lock`-class contention in worktree/merge git operations.
      **Why:** even with merges serialized, `git worktree add`/`remove` from concurrent run *starts* and teardowns share the repo's `.git` and can trip transient lock files; today any such failure is a hard error on an operation that would succeed 200ms later.
      **Where:** `modules/io/src/worktree.rs` (the literal-argv git executors: worktree add/remove, `rev_parse`, `rebase`, ff-merge — the same no-shell `command.rs` mechanism); classification of retryable stderr (`index.lock`, `shallow.lock`, "Another git process seems to be running").
      **Approach:** wrap the git executors in a bounded retry (e.g. 5 attempts, exponential backoff, jitter-free for determinism of logs) that triggers ONLY on the classified transient-lock signatures; anything else fails immediately as today; retries are recorded as warnings in the operation's evidence.
      **Watch:** retry ONLY on transient-lock signatures — retrying a real failure (conflict, missing ref) would mask bugs; bounded and short (worst case ~seconds); io only; no new deps; do not retry the trait's own in-run `git add`/`git commit` command steps (those live in isolated worktrees where contention means something is actually wrong).
      **Done when:** a synthetic `index.lock` held briefly during `worktree add` and during merge's ff no longer fails the operation; a held-forever lock still fails with the typed error after the bounded retries; retries appear as warnings in evidence.
- [x] **P341** — Stale-base overlap detection at merge: typed `stale-base-overlap` park when a sibling batch landed overlapping files since this run branched.
      **Why:** under parallelism a run can rebase cleanly onto a main that changed *the same files* its review never saw — mechanically clean, semantically stale; today that merges silently. Disjoint parallel work (the intended use) should land untouched; overlapping work should surface as a typed decision, not a silent absorb.
      **Where:** `merge.rs` between rebase and gates (`:203` onward): compute the run branch's changed paths (branch base..HEAD) and main's changed paths (branch base..main) via the existing worktree git executors; intersection test; new typed park reason alongside the existing park outcomes.
      **Approach:** empty intersection → proceed exactly as today; non-empty → park with `stale-base-overlap` naming the overlapping paths and the main commits that touched them (the ledger's agreed-design/work-summary context makes the human triage fast); an explicit `--allow-stale-overlap` override lands it anyway (recorded in the merge outcome) for the cases where overlap is known-benign (e.g. lockfiles, goldens).
      **Watch:** path intersection is a cheap conservative proxy, not a semantic diff — false positives on hot shared files (Justfile, goldens, EXECUTION_PLAN ticks) are expected; the override must be recorded evidence, never silent; park-don't-guess discipline unchanged; consider a small default allowlist for known-churn paths only if real batches prove too noisy (measure first, don't pre-tune).
      **Done when:** two parallel runs over disjoint subsystems both land unattended; a constructed overlap parks with the typed reason + named paths; `--allow-stale-overlap` lands it with the override recorded.
- [x] **P342** — Worktree build-cache strategy: opt-in shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (worktree env overlay) so N parallel gate runs are not N cold builds.
      **Why:** a fresh worktree has no `target/`, so every parallel batch's first gate run is a full cold cargo build — the dominant wall-clock and disk cost of running three batches at once; cargo's own file locking already makes a shared target dir safe across concurrent invocations.
      **Where:** worktree setup in `modules/io/src/worktree.rs` + the env applied to harness spawns and command steps executing in the worktree (harness spawn env in `drive.rs`, command steps inherit env via `command.rs`); configuration home: a `[worktree]` table in the run profile (`.ctx/runs/profiles/*.toml`) or `ctx.toml` — e.g. `env = { CARGO_TARGET_DIR = ".ctx/cache/build-target" }` resolved against the invocation repo.
      **Approach:** a declared worktree env overlay applied to every process the run spawns inside the worktree; document the trade (shared cache = warm builds + cargo-serialized compile sections vs per-worktree = fully parallel but cold; fingerprint churn between branches is cargo's problem and it handles it); default stays per-worktree (current behavior) — this is opt-in configuration, not a behavior change.
      **Watch:** env overlay is operational config — never canonical, never digested; generic mechanism (env map), not a cargo-special-case, so other ecosystems get it free; absolute-path resolution against the invocation repo (a relative CARGO_TARGET_DIR inside the worktree would recreate the cold-build problem); the shared dir lives under `.ctx/cache/` (gitignored, prunable via the cache verb family).
      **Done when:** a profile-declared env overlay reaches harness and command processes in the worktree (verified via a stub run's evidence); with the shared target configured, the second parallel batch's first gate run is measurably warm; default behavior without the config is byte-identical to today.
- [x] **P343** — Concurrency proof: deterministic two-run race test — parallel worktree runs, racing merges, assert serialized landing / typed parks / zero corruption.
      **Why:** "safe to run parallel batches" is a claim about races, and races only stay fixed under a regression test; the stub-harness fixture drive (P281 evidence) already proves single runs end-to-end — this extends it to the racing pair, the exact scenario the group exists for.
      **Where:** extends the `just test` runner (`scripts/`, Group 51 P227 pattern) with a concurrency case: an isolated temp git repo, a stub-harness trait, two `--worktree` runs launched concurrently, merges raced both with and without P339's lock-wait; assertions over ledgers, branches, and main's final state.
      **Approach:** assertions: both runs complete with distinct worktrees/branches/ledgers; racing merges serialize (second records a wait) and main contains both commits in some order; a constructed P341 overlap parks with the typed reason; after everything, `git fsck` clean, no stray lock files, worktrees pruned for landed runs and retained for parked ones. Deliberately ADDS a test under the Group 55 reversed no-test rule — concurrency is a credibility-critical surface.
      **Watch:** determinism: stub harness only (no models, no network), fixed inputs; the race itself is inherently timing-dependent — assert on *invariants* (serialization evidence, final state), never on ordering; keep it CI-fast (<~30s) or mark it a `just` target outside the hot path; macOS+Linux both (P239 matrix) since file-lock semantics differ.
      **Done when:** the race test runs green locally and in the P239 matrix; deliberately breaking the P339 lock or P341 detection red-builds it; the group's claim ("N parallel batches land safely") is backed by a committed, repeatable proof.

## Group 72: Deep-refactor traits — the codebase to house standards before release (proposed 2026-07-12)

**Revision (2026-07-12, owner):** collapsed to ONE trait — `deep-refactor` carries the two standards resources directly (no dependency package). Hand-authored canonical TOML (no CDK source) so it shipped while Group 48's CDK split was mid-flight; a CDK source can be added later once the split lands. Originally: two first-party packages that turn the owner's architecture standards into a runnable refactoring procedure, adapted from the deep-module-refactor skill (Ousterhout deep modules) fused with the owner's 12-point dialect. Invocation: `ctx traits session start deep-refactor --profile deep-refactor --set target="<module|file|entity>" [--set focus="…"] --progress tui`. Pre-release goal: sweep the codebase module by module until the survey step reports no material candidates — this is the release gate for code quality. Sources authored (2026-07-12) and parked in scratchpad during the live Group 48 run; restore + build once it lands. **Ledger incident (2026-07-12):** the P282 block silently vanished from this file between its midday write and the pilot review — unattributable (gitignored file, no version history; most plausible culprit: a concurrent whole-file rewrite by a run scribe on a small model or an overlapping session); restored from session context the same day. Guard proposal for the owner: cheap local versioning for `.plans/` (timestamped rotation before scribe-owning runs, or a private `git add -f` shadow branch) — fourth incident of the unversioned-operational-context class, first to destroy ledger content; relates to the P283 typed-context direction.

**Owner-point traceability (all twelve, checked into the knowledge resources + enforced by prompts):**
(1) hexagonal/onion/DDD without jargon → `architecture-dialect.md` §1-§3, plain language, Rust-shaped; (2) hexagonal = testability (Interface/Service + mock injection), reuse (`store::Interface` ← blob/dynamodb Services), layer abstraction (`entity::api::Interface` + Request/Response over axum/lambda), small responsibility packages (control/view/trace) → dialect §1, enumerated verbatim in the design-dialect step prompt; (3) onion = Event-as-Effect handled in `flow::Service`, Command normalized once from CLI (`entity::Command::InitSession`) → dialect §2; (4) DDD = entity containment (events, mutations, commands, queries under the entity module) → dialect §3; (5) hardcoded/hacked (string-matched commands vs `Command` enum, string-line TUI assembly vs builder) → smell S1; (6) over-specific helpers (`infer_repo_root_from_trait_file` vs `find_repo_root`) → S2; (7) stringly fields (`entity_refs: Vec<String>` vs `entities: Vec<entity::Reference>`) → S3; (8) parameter bloat vs `some_fn::Request`/`Options` → S4; (9) value re-threading vs Go-style `Context` carriage → S5; (10) text errors (`crate::Error::Command{message}`) vs typed module-owned errors with Display → S6; (11) `Kind`-suffix indirection vs `matches!`/`is_not_found()` → S7; (12) surface-blind `String` returns vs `Response` enums → S8. The survey step's sweep is MANDATORY and complete: all eight smells in order (findings or explicit "none found") plus the three dialect pillars — no run can skip a point. Reviews must cite smell ids / dialect violations and verify the refactor introduced no NEW instances.

- [ ] **P282** — Pilot + pre-release standards sweep. **(RESTORED 2026-07-12: this block silently vanished from this file between midday and the pilot review — unattributable, the gitignored ledger has no version history; reconstructed from session context. See the incident note in the preamble.)**
      **Why:** the owner's release gate: the codebase refactored to the standards before ctx.traits ships.
      **Approach:** pilot one target and audit the run end to end; then sweep module by module via `just refactor`-batched sequential runs (comma-split, ledger-outcome gated), one commit per target, auditing each; targets whose commits list deferred candidates re-queue, as do targets whose commits show unjustified accretion (S9); a module is done when a fresh survey reports no material candidates; the sweep is done when every module is.
      **Done when:** pilot commit source-approved by the owner-side audit; sweep coverage recorded per module; final surveys clean.
      **Watch (cont.):** batching pattern from the `just implement` recipe (ledger-outcome gate); the two owner adjudications in the amendment below still block first dispatch.
      **Pilot complete (2026-07-12):** `deep-refactor` on `io::export` — session fbb6aab1, commit `68ab58c`, graded A on the owner-side audit. 15 frames, zero retries, 3 iterations, ~62 min; full mandatory S1-S8 sweep (four honest "none found", seven candidates); genuine design synthesis ("A as the spine, with a private host seam from B" → the shipped `host::Interface`); typed `export::Error` replacing `io::ErrorKind` flattening (S6+S7), `control::Interface`/`Request`/`Response` + `fs::Service` (S4/S8 shapes), pure identity types in core (verified no fs/io/process); reviewers demanded and got byte-level render pins for the persisted marker protocol; deferred candidates #3-#6 preserved in the commit body (re-queue `io::export`). Independently re-verified: clippy `-D warnings` green, both traits check green, export path smoked end to end post-refactor (marker + inlining byte-intact). **Two open owner adjudications:** (a) the run added hermetic unit tests (`io/src/export/policy.rs`, `host.rs`) while the stage convention says no test harness — bless (add scoped `cargo test -p ctx-traits-io` to the gates; recommended) or strip; (b) deep-refactor carries no repo-specific conventions (by design it never reads PRODUCT.md) — add a conventions line to its implement/review prompts or a small conventions resource before the sweep campaign.
      **AMENDED 2026-07-21 (release-eve pre-flight):** the every-module sweep is not a pre-tag deliverable — pre-release slice = owner-named high-value modules (one run each, land-per-module), exhaustive sweep moves post-tag; the two in-contract owner adjudications (test-harness blessing, gate set) must be answered before first dispatch; do NOT run concurrently with P362's refactor-deep rename.
## Group 76: Built-ins carry real instruction text — typed, single-sourced + per-step prompt preview (proposed 2026-07-14)

Today a built-in like `rubber-stamp-review` is an **opaque token**: authors write bare strings into `intent`/`behavior`, and unless the slug is one of ~55 hardcoded in `compile.rs`, it renders to the agent as `"- rubber-stamp-review - custom guidance with no summary supplied"` **+ a warning**. The concepts our own traits lean on most (`unbounded-loop`, `weaken-tests-to-pass`, `accretion`, `duplication`, `reuse-over-reimplement`, …) all sit in that dead zone. The "unfold a slug into a sentence" machinery **already exists** but is fragmented across three drifting sources — `.docs/PRODUCT.md` §"MVP Built-In Vocabulary" (`1815-1974`, authored prose) · `modules/core/builtins.toml` (thin — 4 categories, ~6 slugs, JSDoc-only, never reaches an agent) · `modules/core/src/model_view/compile.rs` `behavior_builtin`(`451-615`)+`intent_builtin`(`621-720`) (the *only* place a slug becomes agent-facing text, hardcoded in Rust). The core already carries the plumbing (`GuidanceItem { id, summary?, description? }`, `guidance.rs:16-22`; resolution `compile.rs:401-449`: author override → built-in default → warning). **Digest-safe:** built-in default text lands in the `model_view`/`render`/`skill` digests, **never** the `canonical` TOML digest (`projection_lock.rs:67-71`). The CDK barely types any of it (`generated.ts` types only `direct|technical`/`evidence-first`/`brief`; `intent` + `metadata` are opaque `JsonValue`, `trait.ts:47-48`; no `intent.*` constants, no `block` facet, no `format`/`directness`/`scope-control`/`initiative`/`uncertainty` constants). Goal: one catalog feeds render + SDK types + docs so `Intent.avoid.unboundedLoop` is a typed object that unfolds to a real instruction sentence — plus a `ctx traits preview` that faithfully mirrors the exact per-step prompt an agent receives — a read-only reflection of the shared compose path (built-in enrichment appears there automatically once P301/P302 land, with **no preview-side logic**; until then it honestly shows bare slugs). **Decisions:** intent API = facet-scoped (`Intent.avoid.unboundedLoop`); metadata stays a classifier (typed + documented, never rendered into prompts) — *flag if you want it to render as guidance*; scope = text axes + typed structurals (structurals isolated as trimmable P310); **byte-stable refactor first** (seed the catalog with verbatim current `compile.rs` text so P301 is a pure no-op refactor; only new slugs bump `model_view` digests); AGENTS.md is a new flat render target. Right-size the phases (`run-6760` `context_length_exceeded` / `run-f452` `frame-seconds` lessons). **Ordering vs Group 75:** P300-P304, P307, P309 are independent of the `.ctx` consolidation; P305 re-authors the three dogfood traits Group 75 (P298) migrates, and P308's debug-path ground-truth check reads the runtime dir Group 75 (P296) moves — sequence those after Group 75 lands, or against whichever layout is current.
