## Group 83: implement-phase recurring-blocker mechanism — make "consolidate, don't patch" machine-enforced (proposed 2026-07-15)

The **prose** version shipped in implement-phase v0.7.0 (reviewers mark a re-raised structural defect `RECURRING` and require consolidation; the per-frame worker's apply-fixes prompt treats a `RECURRING` tag as "delete the duplicate, share one path — don't patch the cited cases"). That relies on the persistent reviewer remembering and on the model choosing the harder structural fix under review pressure — a nudge, not a guarantee. This group makes it **mechanical**: typed recurrence, then a bounded escalation. Motivation: the P308 runs where the worker patched a synthetic-frame copy case-by-case for 3+ rounds instead of sharing core's frame builder (`run-c14b77fb`, `run-6003a1d9`, `run-f2aa88af`). **Load-bearing:** recurrence/escalation are runtime/presentational signals — keep out of canonical TOML / digests; the breaker is a loop guard (protocol shape), sequence behind its own review.

- [x] **P334** — Recurrence breaker: park after N unresolved recurrences instead of looping to exhaustion. **Substantially delivered trait-side in implement-phase v0.10.0 (2026-07-17):** verdicts carry `escalation = none|needs-owner`, the loop `stop-if`s on either reviewer's escalation, and a `land-or-park` branch emits a typed PARKED report instead of committing — covering contract/deps/authority/gate/scope stalls. Still open (the HITL-gated half): a runtime waiting-on-human park state + host approve/resume routing (Group 124); the pure-recurrence auto-breaker (park after N recurrences without reviewer escalation) rides on that. When the same blocker is marked `recurring` for ≥N consecutive rounds (default N=2–3), the loop stops with a distinct terminal reason (`recurring-blocker-unresolved`) and parks for human rather than burning all 10 iterations narrowing edges. **Where:** a `stop-if` loop guard on `refinement-loop` reading the typed `recurring` evidence (`:233-275`); new terminal signal + `onFailure` distinct from `refinementExhausted`. **Watch:** this **re-introduces a `stop-if` breaker**, which we removed pending HITL (see the disagreement-breaker removal) — so it's **gated on HITL landing** (Group 124 approve/reject), where the park routes to a human decision rather than a dead stop. `iteration-at-least` predicates are valid only in loop guards; keep recurrence evidence presentational (not canonical). **Done when:** a run whose blocker recurs N× parks with `recurring-blocker-unresolved` + the offending blocker surfaced, not a silent 10-round exhaustion.

## Group 101: Landed-phase remainders — the deferred work earlier phases shipped without (owner-ordered 2026-07-20)

Collected from the git-log bodies of phases that landed (and in some cases got ticked) while explicitly recording work they did NOT do. Each item cites the commit that deferred it. This group is the honest home for "shipped, but…" — separate from the phases' own ticks so a `[x]` never silently swallows a known gap. NOT collected here (already homed): `P399`/loop-exhaustion signal shape (Group 100), the merge non-LLM fast path (P401), `P397` command-output routing (Group 92, unticked).

- [x] **P402** — Finish P344's concurrency: the pieces its own commit calls absent.
      **Why:** `1085684` (P344) landed a PARTIAL bounded-concurrency implementation — wave-based branch dispatch, per-branch session forking, cached-outcome replay into the parent ledger in authored order — and its own message lists what is missing: **per-branch sub-ledgers, concurrent `for-each`, P264 terminal-failure supervision applied to concurrent branches, `--wait`, interrupt/resume, and the P343 concurrency proof.** Group 66 reads COMPLETE because P344 is ticked, but the wall-clock concurrency the group exists to deliver is only half-built. Ledger writes are already strictly sequential/in-order (the correctness guarantee holds); this is the supervision + observability + proof layer on top.
      **Where:** `modules/cli/src/app/drive.rs` (`attempt_concurrent_wave`, `--max-in-flight` plumbing); per-branch sub-ledger persistence; concurrent `for-each` (today only `parallel` branches dispatch concurrently); wiring P264's per-branch terminal-failure policy through the concurrent path; `--wait`/interrupt/resume of in-flight concurrent branches (cross-run resume is the hard part — the ledger must record in-flight dispatch state).
      **Done when:** concurrent branches persist per-branch sub-ledgers; a concurrent `for-each` runs; a branch that hits its terminal-failure policy mid-wave is supervised per P264; `--wait` and interrupt/resume work across a run boundary; the P343 concurrency proof (N parallel branches land without racing the cursor) is a committed, repeatable test.
- [x] **P403** — Resolve the import subcommand's `--profile` naming conflict.
      **Why:** `b2938e7`/`10e4b3f` (P318/P319) shipped `--model`/`--assign` on `import` but explicitly DEFERRED the run-profile flag as an owner CLI decision: `import` already has a `--profile` that selects the SOURCE profile, so a run-profile flag collides. The recorded options: rename the source selector to `--source-profile` and let `--profile` select the run profile (with a stated compatibility policy), or add a distinct `--run-profile`. Until decided, `import` cannot take a run profile the way every other verb can.
      **Where:** `modules/cli/src/app/surface/cli.rs` (import args), `import_handlers.rs`; a deprecation/alias policy if `--profile` changes meaning.
      **Done when:** `import` accepts a run profile through a non-colliding flag; the source-vs-run distinction is documented; any renamed flag keeps a deprecation alias for one release.
- [ ] **P404** — Checklist coverage under `for-each` (or a stated refusal).
      **Why:** `6e47d70` (typed checklists) checks coverage — every declared item answered exactly once — only for a whole-list `replace` write, and REFUSES `append` for checklist verdicts (a single appended element cannot be judged against the whole list). A `for-each` driven over checklist items has no coverage story at all: it is neither the replace shape coverage validates nor the append shape that is refused. Fine while nothing uses that shape; a silent hole the moment something does.
      **Where:** `modules/core/src/procedure/runtime/schema_validation.rs` (`checklist_coverage_validation`) + the for-each control path in `control_flow.rs`; decide whether a for-each accumulating verdicts gets coverage checked at loop close, or whether that wiring is statically refused like `append`.
      **Done when:** a for-each over a checklist either has its coverage verified when the loop completes, or fails validation at authoring time with a message pointing at the replace shape — no third silent option.
- [x] **P405** — Markdown → typed-checklist reconciler (import path).
      **Why:** typed checklists (`6e47d70`) are authored as `[[resource.item]]` data. There is no path from an existing markdown checklist to that typed form — the seed-then-reconcile import flow (recognize task-list/heading items → assign stable ids → on re-derivation, match against existing items rather than re-slugifying) was designed and parked this session. Without it, adopting a checklist means hand-transcribing it, and re-derivation cannot preserve item identity across rewordings.
      **Where:** the `import` planner (`modules/core/src/import/`); reuse the quarantine-until-reviewed posture; item id stability is the crux — reword-tolerant matching against the prior typed items, never a pure re-slugify.
      **Watch:** this is IMPORT-TIME and human-reviewed — it must not become runtime markdown parsing, which `report_check.rs:1504` states as an explicit anti-goal. One-way: after derivation the typed items are canonical.
      **Done when:** `ctx traits import` turns a markdown checklist into typed items with stable ids; a re-import after a reworded bullet keeps the item's id and reports the change rather than dropping+adding.
- [x] **P406** — Make generated `.map` files repo-relative, not absolute.
      **Why:** `render`/`build` writes source-map (`.map`) files containing ABSOLUTE paths to the source tree. Anyone who builds outside the canonical checkout — a run worktree, a fresh clone, a different machine — rewrites every `.map` to their own path, producing spurious diffs and, this session, a real cost (`2efdd88` was an owner commit doing nothing but repairing poisoned `.map` paths; and the seed-harvest of `.map`s churns for the same reason). A build artifact that encodes the builder's absolute filesystem location is not reproducible.
      **Where:** the CDK build/render map emitter (`packages/cdk` build, and `modules/core/src/render.rs` / wherever `index.map` is written); store paths relative to the trait package root (or the repo root) and resolve at read time.
      **Done when:** building the same source at two different absolute locations produces byte-identical `.map` files; drift check and seed harvest stop churning on `.map` paths.
- [ ] **P407** — P329's live TUI smoke (owner-run verification, not code).
      **Why:** `a89c638` (P329, salvaged) is ticked, but its own scribe message records the one thing that was never done: the token-producing wall-clock TUI observation the phase requires — rollover reset to Pending, monotonic loop token/time totals, unchanged header totals, nested-loop isolation, terminal all-Done painting. The source-level behavior was reviewer-certified; the live render was not observed.
      **Where:** run a real `implement`/looped trait under `--progress tui` and watch the loop panel across an iteration rollover and to completion.
      **Done when:** the owner confirms the five behaviors on a live token-producing run, or files a defect if any diverges.
- [x] **P415** — Machine-readable `Deps:` + current paths on every OPEN phase — give P375's door-check eyes. (traversal catch 2026-07-21)
      **Why:** P375 refuses phases whose `Deps:` cite unlanded ids — but it cannot read prose. P344 stated its prerequisites in prose and burned a budget; tonight Group 74 did it ×5: P288–P291 each dispatched AFTER P287 hit the PRODUCT.md wall their own texts describe, because nothing machine-readable said "P287 first". P291's final blocker also names stale artifact paths inside Group 74's own contracts — the P358 lesson (a landed rename obligates a queued-contract sweep) applied at plan scale.
      **Where:** `.plans/EXECUTION_PLAN.md` only. Every OPEN phase gets (a) an explicit `Deps:` line listing phase ids (or the literal "none"), including intra-group ordering (e.g. P288 → `Deps: P287`); (b) paths current post-P369/v2 layout (`packages/cdk`, `.ctx/traits/<id>/source/index.ts`, `generated/index.toml`).

## Group 102: Leftovers — honest partial completion as a first-class outcome (owner-ordered 2026-07-20/21; deadline-relevant despite file position — owner slots queue order)

A run today knows two endings — done or blocked — while the state a real engineer ships from is the third: **done, with a short list of what was knowingly left.** P344 proved both halves (RUN_PROBLEMS entries 68/69, run-5bf27fc3…): the worker declared its partial honestly, and the system had nowhere to put the truth except commit prose that nearly died unread; the same night the full pipeline was run BY HAND (receipt → Group 101/P402 with Deps → self-waking preflight), proving every arrow works on landed machinery. The product's job (owner decision): standardize the FORMAT of honesty — declared, two-question co-signed, typed at the run boundary — and stay silent about destinations. Exit-code analogy; "the receipt is the API." Governing rule for every phase here: **honesty must never render or route as failure** — the paradigm dies the day a declared gap looks red. NON-GOALS this group (future; all consume Tier-1 data retroactively): rollup CLI (`ctx traits leftovers`), done-with-N-open display, aging + per-area ceilings, auto-routing/HITL fates (Group 124), product-side destination adapters, owner-items/`remaining` unification (P408 declares it as its own first leftover). Naming: `leftovers` in schema and UI; "punch list" is reserved as the post's metaphor. Open design questions preserved in `.docs/LEFTOVERS_DESIGN.md` (declare mid-task vs at end — lean mid-task; do accepted-forever items resurface once, months later).

- [x] **P408** — The leftover contract: schema + two-question doctrine in the shared agents package; both implement traits adopt it.
      **Why:** P344's run burned five rounds because the worker's true statement — "this cannot be finished here" — had no typed home and the reviewer had no vocabulary but revise; the honest record survived only as commit prose. Make the declaration typed and co-signed so honesty is the cheapest move and silence is invalid.
      **Where:** `packages/agents` (leftover schema + doctrine text, one edit consumed fleet-wide); `.ctx/traits/implement/source/*` and `.ctx/traits/implement-phase/source/index.ts` (worker/reviewer/scribe prompts; review step gains a second output).
      **Shape:** leftover = `{what (one sentence), reason ∈ needs-unlanded | needs-human | out-of-budget, needs (phase ids/refs, may be empty), evidence, done-when}`. The worker PROPOSES leftovers in its summary — never silently skips. The reviewer adjudicates each with the two questions: (1) could it have been done in this task? yes → move to blockers, loop continues; (2) does the shipped work stand alone without it? no → nothing ships, escalate. Survivors are emitted by the reviewer as a `slot:leftovers` list ALONGSIDE the verdict in the same frame — co-signed by construction. `status = approved` WITH leftovers is legal iff blockers is empty; that clause is the paradigm. The list is required: a blank is a schema error; an empty list is a signed claim of none. Scribe quotes a `Leftovers:` section verbatim (v0.14.1 owner-items pattern).
      **Watch:** leftovers are never treated as failure anywhere (perverse incentive). implement-phase's existing `owner-items`/`remaining` fields stay untouched this phase — their unification into leftover classes is OUT OF BUDGET and must ship as this phase's own first declared leftover (the mechanism's birth commit demonstrates it). Optional belt-and-braces: gate loop exit on `count(slot:leftovers where verdict = rejected) == 0` via the field-filtered count guard (landed 2026-07-20) — only if cheap.
      **Done when:** both traits rebuild, check green; a no-drive fixture run round-trips: worker proposes two (one legitimate, one lazy) → reviewer converts one to a blocker, co-signs one → final state approved-with-1 → receipt shows the `Leftovers:` section verbatim; owner trust re-approval recorded for both digests.
- [x] **P409** — Leftovers at the boundary: a typed output of the run, plus the adapter pattern documented.
      **Why:** genericity (owner 2026-07-20): our workflow reads `.plans/EXECUTION_PLAN.md`; users read Jira, GitHub Issues, or nothing. The product standardizes the format of honesty, not the destination — any host must read gaps as structured data at the run boundary with zero knowledge of trait internals.
      **Where:** implement + implement-phase declare terminal `port:leftovers` valued from `slot:leftovers`; the ledger's final-output summary then carries it automatically (tonight's ledgers already print port values), so `run --json` exposes it with no engine work. Docs: a short "leftover contract" section — the schema, the two questions, and two example adapters: a 3-line `gh issue create` loop, and the owner's plan-stub writer (explicitly labeled a USER adapter, not product).
      **Watch:** rejected proposals never reach the slot — `blockers` remains the reject channel. Empty list ⇒ absent in JSON output (zero-noise). The stub-writer example carries `Deps:` lines so the existing dispatch preflight (P375) makes promoted stubs self-waking — state this as the pattern, do not build routing.
      **Done when:** `run --json` shows the structured list under final outputs on a fixture run and nothing when empty; `use`/`explain` render the port in the contract section (free — ports already render); the adapter docs section exists with both examples.
- [x] **P410** — Rendering the third state: one generic aligned-rows renderer + the three surfaces.
      **Why:** current output prints structured results as raw JSON at run end. Leftovers must be unmissable without looking like failure: never red (punishes honesty), never dim (invites skimming), zero-noise when empty (absence is a signed claim), and the green verdict stays green beside `— 2 leftovers`.
      **Where:** engine, ONE generic rule keyed on the existing format-hint mechanism: an output whose schema is a list of small objects renders as aligned rows (compact) or per-item stanzas with evidence + done-when (`--verbose`). Surfaces: (a) run-end summary — `verdict: approved — 2 leftovers` + one row per item, `what — waiting on X` normal fg with the reason dim-parenthesized as the only faint element; (b) stream/status line at the review step — `review: approved — 2 leftovers declared`; (c) TUI minimal only: header counter `leftovers N` (rendered only when > 0) + review-row suffix `(N open)` + completion banner `completed — approved, N leftovers`; deeper TUI work stays behind the P329 live smoke (P407).
      **Watch:** two-tone discipline holds — no new colors (yellow considered and rejected: light-theme unreadable); no leftover-special-case in engine code — the renderer must improve ANY list-of-objects output (verify with a non-leftover list through the same rule).
      **Done when:** a fixture run reproduces the mocks in `.docs/LEFTOVERS_DESIGN.md` for compact, `--verbose`, `--json`, and stream; zero leftovers renders nothing on every surface; a non-leftover structured list renders through the same rule unchanged.
- [x] **P411** — "The agent that tells you what it didn't do" — the truthful-agents post (owner voice; loop scaffolds).
      **Why:** truthful agents are what people are begging for, and we hold the receipt: a real commit in which the worker declared its partial plainly (P344). The thesis is ours by construction: honesty as a WORKFLOW property, not a model virtue — it survives model swaps, which is the product's core cross-model pitch.
      **Approach (beats):** (1) open with the P344 receipt verbatim — "my agent told me the truth, and it almost died in a git log"; (2) why agents bury: benchmarks and training reward looking-done; (3) the turn: make honesty structural — typed declaration, two-question co-sign, silence-is-invalid, approve-WITH-leftovers; (4) the lineage: construction's substantial completion + punch list + retainage — a century-old answer to exactly this; (5) the honest boundary in house voice: this proves gaps were declared and co-signed, not that every gap was found — hiding becomes a two-party conspiracy instead of the default. Interactive-post house pattern applies; Group 59 adjacency; claim-gate pass over every line of copy.
      **Watch:** owner voice — the loop drafts scaffold only. No capability overclaims: Tier 1+2 ship; rollup/aging/routing are future and must not be implied live.
      **Done when:** scaffold drafted from `.docs/LEFTOVERS_DESIGN.md`; owner rewrites and ratifies; claim-gate green; published with or after the mechanism.
- [ ] **P414** — Truly blocked is an ERROR: no commit, non-zero exit, halted batch, refused siblings. (owner-ordered 2026-07-21)
      **Why:** overnight, all five Group 74 phases burned full budgets against ONE known wall and each landed a "blocked" chore commit (c912f99, cd4d2b7, b7e12da, 05a8b77, 764c9c6) — implementing nothing, yet reading as SUCCESS to the batch's ledger-outcome gate, so the chain marched on ×4 after the first wall-hit. A blocked ending that produces a commit is a lie with a receipt: git history gains landings that changed nothing and the batch cannot tell them from real ones. Leftovers (P408) cover approved-WITH-gaps; this phase covers the opposite cell of the two-questions matrix — "does the work stand alone? NO" — which must never ship anything, including a chore commit.
      **Where:** implement/implement-phase tails (branch on the final verdict class — branch step landed); scribe doctrine; drive/batch exit semantics (Justfile recipes); dispatch preflight (P375 extension).
      **Shape:** classify run endings: (1) approved → land; (2) approved-with-leftovers → land + leftovers (P408); (3) **BLOCKED** — final verdict still `revise` carrying a contract-conflict/needs-owner blocker with no certified work — the tail BRANCHES AWAY from scribe→stage→commit entirely: zero commits, a typed park report in the ledger (blocker verbatim, wall source cited), a DISTINCT non-zero exit code, batch recipes halt the chain on that code, and the park records a wall id so dispatching a SIBLING phase citing the same standing wall refuses at preflight ("wall <id> standing since run <x>") — the ×5 cascade becomes ×1.
      **Watch:** never conflate with approved-with-leftovers (that ships); the clean-tree-but-APPROVED case belongs to P397, not here; historical blocked-chore commits stay untouched in history; `--strict-loops` unaffected; the branch guard reads verdict fields — coordinate the verdict surface with P408 (P408 first).
      **Done when:** a blocked run produces ZERO commits + a park report + the distinct exit code; a two-phase batch fixture halts at the first blocked ending (second never dispatches); a sibling citing the same recorded wall refuses at dispatch; an approved-with-leftovers run still lands normally.
