## Group 104: Provable-execution primitives + auto-research unification (owner-ordered 2026-07-21, from the trait source review)

Born from the 2026-07-21 review of the agent-built auto-research / self-improving-traits / deep-research sources and the design discussion after it. Owner decisions taken in that discussion: ONE auto-research trait (the self-improving variant is DELETED, not deferred to a P396 family variant); a proper presence-aware conditional system gets built (not a narrow cap hack); inline scripts leave argv only once content pinning exists. Numbering note: P416-P429 are reserved for the release-UX lift staged in `.docs/RELEASE_UX_AND_SCENARIOS.md` §10; this group starts at P430.

- [x] **P430** — Protected resources: content digests inside the trust boundary.
      **Why:** a `[[resource]]` declaration is canonical-covered but its FILE CONTENT is not — editing a resource file moves no digest and reverts no trust; the lockfile snapshot is detection, not enforcement. auto-research's evaluator works around this with a hand-rolled launcher embedding an expected-digest constant in inline argv (`protocol.ts` evaluatorLauncher) — the workaround proves the need and should be a primitive.
      **Where:** core resource model: optional `digest = "sha256:..."` on `[[resource]]` (presence = protected). Frame delivery verifies content against the pin before handing the file to an agent. Command steps: an argv entry may reference a resource; the runtime resolves it to a real path only after the pin verifies. `check`: a protected resource whose on-disk content mismatches its pin is a BLOCKER, and any resource referenced in an executable argv position without a pin is a blocker ("resources launched as code must be pinned"). CDK: `resource.file(..., { digest })` + argv resource refs.
      **Done when:** editing a pinned resource's file fails closed at use with the mismatch named; legitimizing the edit requires updating the pin, which moves the canonical digest and reverts machine trust; an argv resource ref spawns only after verification; check enforces executable-must-be-pinned; unpinned doc/template resources behave exactly as today.
- [x] **P431** — Projection literals: constants without a process spawn.
      **Why:** projections require a source slot, so auto-research runs a `node --eval` command emitting `{zero: 0, one: 1, ...}` into a constants slot purely to seed counters and decision markers (`initializeConstants`) — a spawn, an inline script, and a dead-field-prone schema for values that should be canonical bytes (its `completed`/`detail` fields are already produced-but-never-consumed).
      **Where:** core projection model — a typed literal value as a projection source, declared in canonical; CDK authoring shape (e.g. `operation.literal(...)` as a projection source); runtime applies without spawning; static validation that the literal's type matches the destination slot schema.
      **Done when:** auto-research's constants command and summary-constants schema are deletable with identical ledger semantics for every projected value; a literal/destination type mismatch is a static validation error.
- [ ] **P432** — Conditional system v2: presence-aware guards, designed properly.
      **Why:** guards are static predicates over always-present fields. They cannot express "this cap binds only when the caller supplied it", "this field may be absent", or "these two list counts must match". That wall is what forces trait forks: auto-research needs a 2-condition and a 6-condition keep-guard as two separate traits, and deep-research's cardinality guard cannot compare two counts. Owner call 2026-07-21: build the proper system, not the narrow cap hack.
      **Where:** DESIGN-FIRST — a design doc before code. Candidate scope: presence conditions over optional ports and optional fields (present/absent with defined truth semantics per operator); conditional binding with FAIL-CLOSED semantics (a cap that is supplied but unmeasurable in the result DISCARDS rather than passes); count-to-count comparison; composition with existing all/any/not; static validation that every referenced optional field/port is declared optional. Explicitly OUT: arithmetic between arbitrary fields, ordered folds/replay logic (stays in pinned scripts by design — the auto-research summarize auditor is the canonical example), and any general canonical expression language (standing house refusal).
      **RIDER (2026-07-23, from the CDK audit):** loop `until:`/`stop-if:` gain the same check-polymorphism P458 gives branch `check:` — a `sequence.check` gate step directly as the loop guard, killing today's boolean-slot + `condition.equals` two-step for the gate-then-loop pattern; same desugaring, no extra schema surface beyond what P458's branch already needs.
      **Done when:** the design doc enumerates the vocabulary with absent/present truth semantics for every operator; implementation passes a fixture matrix covering absent-port, absent-field, supplied-but-unmeasurable (fail-closed), and count-vs-count; P434's single keep-guard is expressible.
- [x] **P433** — Auto-research quick fixes (dep-free slice, dispatchable now).
      **Why:** three review defects need no new machinery. (a) The discard arm runs `git clean -fdx`; `-x` deletes IGNORED files — including the worktree's `node_modules`, which `[worktree]` setup installs exactly once — so the first discarded candidate likely destroys the toolchain the evaluator needs and every later measurement errors into discard. (b) `.ctx/traits/self-improvement-target` is test material occupying the trait store with status/trust bookkeeping. (c) `protocol.ts` is 882 lines of by-variant ternaries and inline scripts.
      **Where:** (a) `cleanCandidate`: drop `-x` (or `-e node_modules`) so ignored toolchain files survive a discard. (b) Move the target package to `.fixtures/auto-research-target/`; update the evaluator `--target` constant and eval declarations. (c) Split source by kind WITHIN the package (schemas/agents/slots/steps/index) — NO workspace-package promotion: P434 deletes the second consumer, so the single-trait-package rule applies.
      **Done when:** a discard leaves `node_modules` intact and a subsequent measurement succeeds in the same worktree; the store lists no self-improvement-target; regenerated canonicals are byte-identical to before the file split (pure reorganization).
- [x] **P434** — Auto-research unification: one trait, variant deleted.
      **Why:** owner decision 2026-07-21 — one auto-research trait, not three packages and not a deferred family variant. The self-improving variant adds no capability: it is the generic protocol plus 9 more result fields, 4 cap conditions, and a pinned evaluator. With P430-P432 those become configuration of ONE typed contract.
      **Where:** result schema: the 9 extra fields become `required: false` optionals on the one experiment-result schema; the 4 cap ports become optional inputs; ONE keep-guard via P432 (caps bind when supplied; supplied-but-unmeasurable discards). Evaluator + summarize replay validator become protected package resources (P430). Constants become projection literals (P431). DELETE the self-improving-traits package (its `../../` cross-package import dies with it). The showcase becomes a documented saved invocation (justfile recipe or doc) running generic auto-research with the pinned evaluator as experiment-command. Keep the inline git-capture wrappers until P397 lands; then a rider replaces them with direct digest-pinned git spawns capturing text slots.
      **Done when:** the store has exactly one auto-research trait; the demo invocation reproduces the self-improvement run end-to-end with keep/discard parity against a recorded pre-unification run; a plain 3-field experiment-command still validates and runs; the only surviving inline script is the git capture pending its P397 rider.
      **Watch (cont.):** P397 additionally feeds the capture rider (git-capture wrapper replacement) — not a blocker for the rest of this phase.
- [x] **P435** — deep-research: wire or delete the manifest-streams orphan.
      **Why:** 2d4a9c0 landed a slot whose description promises the cardinality guard compares two independently-authored stream lists — but nothing produces or reads it, and the emitter silently dropped it from canonical (only procedure-referenced slots are collected). The artifact does not do what its source claims.
      **Where:** deep-research plan step + plan-gate. DEFAULT: wire it — the plan step emits streams AND manifest-streams; the guard requires both counts valid (count-vs-count equality once P432 lands; both-in-3..6 as the honest weak form until then). Fallback with stated rationale: delete the const and its description.
      **Done when:** the canonical contains no promise the runtime doesn't keep — either both lists exist and are guarded, or the orphan is gone; the digest move is flagged for trust re-approval.
- [x] **P436** — Build lints from the review: escaping imports and orphan slots.
      **Why:** two silent classes let defects land. self-improving-traits/source imported `../../auto-research/source/protocol.ts` — an undeclared cross-package dependency invisible to vendoring/export and outside the consumer's drift hashing (owner: "we need a lint for that"). And an authored-but-unreferenced slot (manifest-streams) drew no warning while the emitter silently dropped it.
      **Where:** build/check advisory pipeline. (a) ERROR: a source-relative import resolving outside the package's own `source/` root that is not a bare package specifier. (b) WARN: a slot/schema authored in source but absent from the emitted canonical because nothing references it.
      **RIDER (2026-07-23, from the CDK audit — make the P458/P459 migration checkable, not vibes):** (c) WARN: an explicit `input:` entry duplicating a ref already interpolated in the step's prompt (40 live sites today); (d) WARN: deprecated PascalCase vocab namespace usage (`Tone.*`/`Intent.*` — 112 live sites) once P458 rule 8 lands; (e) WARN: `prompt.template` (17 sites) and the `prompt:` field dual (2 sites) once P458 deletes them from the surface.
      **Done when:** a fixture package with an escaping relative import fails build with the offending path named; a fixture with an orphan slot warns naming the const; both now-fixed live instances would have been caught.
      **Watch (cont.):** complements the P404/P415 validation family (restored 2026-07-23 — dropped by the P415 sweep).
**P437 (ABSORBED into P450 2026-07-23 — one family rewrite per P458's sequencing, not independently dispatchable; this contract text stays authoritative for the variants-map fold mechanics)** — Implement family migration onto P396 variants (owner-ordered 2026-07-21).
      Original why: the P363 four-package axis predates the settled variants mechanism; once P396 lands, keeping four near-identical packages (plus shared.ts reached from siblings) is pure store noise and the live offender for P436's import lint.
      Fold mechanics (executed as part of P450): fold `implement-{quick,default,smart,strict}` into ONE `.ctx/traits/implement` package: `source/variant/{quick,default,smart,strict}.ts` + `source/shared.ts` (all imports become `./shared.ts`), variants map with `default` marked via `.default()`; DELETE the four packages after byte-diffing each variant canonical against its pre-migration sibling (only identity fields may differ). `implement-phase` STAYS a separate package (owner call 2026-07-21 — repo-specific dogfood, not a built-in): its shared import re-points at the family package's source and must be reconciled with P436's error (a manifest-declared source-dependency exemption, or promoting the needed helpers to @ctx-traits/agents — implementer picks). Justfile invocations (`implement:quick`, `implement-phase`) must resolve identically post-migration.
      **Run disposition (2026-07-25, this run, re-verified against tree — no code written):** confirmed still blocked. `resolveTraitFamily` (`packages/cdk/src/variant.ts:226`) has zero runtime callers; `scripts/goldens/fixtures/variant-family/` is referenced by nothing else in the repo; `.ctx/traits/implement-{quick,default,smart,strict}` still exist as four separate packages; P449 and P458 are still unlanded (`[ ]` at lines 1323/1352). Folding now would delete the substrate `just implement`/`just implement-lean` run on before the family-aware build/resolve vertical (section 2 of the draft) exists. Re-dispatch only as part of P450. Since nothing landed this run, no `.docs/PRODUCT.md` section went stale — the Family Axis text (~3113-3124) and the family enumerations at 2766/2770/2800 remain accurate as of this run.

## Group 105: Interactive TUI on ratatui (owner-ordered 2026-07-21; lifted from .docs/RELEASE_UX_AND_SCENARIOS.md §9 — ids kept from the doc's reserved P416-P429 block)

Owner directives driving this group: ratatui is sanctioned for interactive surfaces (two-tone palette doctrine carries over unchanged — named ANSI fg + faint only, no backgrounds, no truecolor, no theme detection); the live view is wanted NOW; the endgame is one TUI replacing the terminal multiplexer for session management. P422 is dispatch-ready today; P423 follows its landing.

- [x] **P422** — ratatui foundation + live session view at parity.
      **Why:** the live run view is a hand-rolled ANSI redraw (`modules/cli/src/app/tui.rs`, ~760 lines) — fine for one pane, structurally unscalable for interactive management (keyboard input, screen switching, `$EDITOR` round-trips need alt-screen + an input/layout engine). Owner ordered the ratatui move 2026-07-21; ratatui/crossterm are functional deps, not cosmetic ones, so the no-cosmetic-deps stance is unchanged in spirit.
      **Where:** add ratatui + crossterm to `modules/cli`. Bridge the existing `Tone` enum (`Default/Muted/Pass/Fail/Warn/Bold`) 1:1 onto ratatui `Style`s — named ANSI foreground + faint ONLY; nothing here may emit truecolor, background colors, or light/dark detection. Alt-screen lifecycle with clean restore on quit AND panic (crossterm owns cursor/terminal restore — no libc). Rebuild the `--progress tui` single-run live pane at parity with today's renderer: frame title/agent, elapsed, token text, narrator status lines, verdict coloring. Non-TTY / CI / `--no-tui` / `--json` keep the current line-mode path byte-identical; `check`'s line-mode styling is untouched.
      **Done when:** a `--progress tui` run renders through ratatui with information parity on light and dark terminals; quitting the pane never kills the run; line-mode fallback output is byte-identical to today; no truecolor/background escape appears anywhere in the new path (grep-provable); the hand-rolled redraw remains in place until P423 retires it.
- [x] **P423** — session-manager dashboard: bare `ctx traits` opens the TUI.
      **Why:** the owner's workflow today is one multiplexer pane per run; the target is one TUI that lists, attaches, spawns, and kills sessions, plus entity screens so trust approval and merging stop being memorized commands (v2 design §9).
      **Where:** bare `ctx traits` on a TTY opens the dashboard (non-TTY prints help exactly as today). Screens: SESSIONS — live + recent runs read from the run store; attach opens the P422 pane, detach returns to the list without touching the run; `n` spawns a detached drive child that writes to the store — killing the TUI never kills runs, and externally-started runs appear identically (the store is the interface; NO daemon). TRAITS — name/version/status/trust verdict/drift; `t` approve/block via the trust verbs; `e` opens source in `$EDITOR`. MERGES — parked queue with park evidence; `m` merge; a deep-merge key appears only once P420 exists — do not build P420 here. TRUST — pending/stale digests, approve/block with reason via `$EDITOR`. Every textual action suspends the TUI into `$EDITOR` and reloads on exit — no in-TUI text editing. Scope filter ships THIS-REPO only; the ALL-repos toggle lights up when the global session store lands (release-UX P426) — do NOT build that store here. Retire the hand-rolled tui.rs redraw once parity holds.
      **Done when:** one dashboard lists and attaches to two concurrent live runs and survives their completion; spawn and kill from the TUI work and TUI exit orphans nothing; a trust approval and a plain merge complete from their screens end to end; the `$EDITOR` round-trip works; line-mode/CI behavior is unchanged; the old redraw path is deleted.
      **Watch (cont.):** lights-up-later (explicitly NOT deps): P420 deep-merge action, P426 ALL-repos filter.
- [x] **P454** — Narration sidebar v2: strict no-overlap layout bounds + ctrl-c-only input on the live view (owner-ordered 2026-07-22, amended same day).
      **Why:** the v1 column (f88b074 — flat 60/40 split, ≥100-col gate, bottom-anchored ring buffer) recovered the owner's live-view layout from a spec gap, but (a) a bare percentage is wrong at BOTH extremes — mid-width terminals starve the tree, and on a wide screen 40% is dumb (a 120-col sidebar for ≤150-char one-liners); (b) overlong lines clip mid-word with no ellipsis, no visual seam, resize behavior is incidental; (c) the live view's key handling is wrong: today `q`/`Esc` (and on the old binary, stray keys) detach the pane with a "detached from the live view" note — owner rule: on the live run view EVERY key is IGNORED except ctrl-c; an accidental keystroke must never detach or print anything.
      **Where:** `tui_ratatui.rs::{render_split, poll_detach}` + `run_view.rs::narration_history_lines`. Layout = min AND max bounds, not percentages: tree column keeps a floor (widest current tree line, or a measured fixed floor ~72 cols); the sidebar exists only when ≥ ~36 cols remain after that floor plus a 1-col dim separator (`│`, Muted — the one new glyph); sidebar width is CAPPED (~60 cols — the P330 window makes entries ≤~150 chars, so beyond the cap wrap depth stops improving); surplus width beyond both bounds goes to the TREE, then to margin — never to a sprawling sidebar. Ellipsize (`…`) every clipped line in both columns; sidebar entries wrap OR ellipsize per one fixed rule (try both on a real run; two-line wrap is the likely winner); bottom-anchoring counts RENDERED rows (post-wrap), not entries. Resize re-evaluates appear/disappear/cap every frame — shrink collapses cleanly, growth re-expands, no torn frame. Input: `poll_detach` matches ctrl-c ONLY (request_stop + detach + the one-line "stopping after the current frame" note); `q`/`Esc`/everything else is drained and discarded — no detach, no message. Dashboard screens keep their own richer keys via `poll_key` (untouched); a later phase may reintroduce a deliberate detach binding, but it must be a decision, not a reflex.
      **Watch:** two-tone doctrine unchanged (named-ANSI fg + DIM/BOLD only; separator is Muted, never a background); `--progress stream`/line-mode/`--json` untouched; presentation-only — no core, no byte-stable surface; the width/appear decision lives in ONE place (the pane), never duplicated in run_view.
      **Done when:** on a real `--progress tui` run: wide terminal shows tree + separator + capped sidebar with surplus going to the tree (zero overlap at every width tried); shrinking mid-run collapses to full-width tree on the next frame and re-expands on growth; every clipped line shows an ellipsis; mashing letters/Esc/q on the live view changes nothing on screen and prints nothing; ctrl-c stops-and-detaches with its note; palette grep shows no new colors.
      **Watch (cont.):** optional rider if trivial while in the file: dump the narration ring buffer into the run's debug dir at pane close so the history survives the TUI.
- [ ] **P455** — Finished-step summaries in the narration sidebar ("wrote a draft for …").
      **Why:** owner ask (2026-07-22): the sidebar should carry the run's story, not only the live thinking ticks — when a step FINISHES, one narrated summary of that whole step ("wrote a draft covering X and Y", "reviewer approved with two nits") joins the history, so scrolling the sidebar reads as a narrative of what the run did.
      **Where:** step-completion detection already exists (`run_view.rs` marks steps finished on refresh/rollover — the same transition that credits `finished_durations`); on that transition, enqueue ONE summarization request to the existing narrator machinery (`harness_stream::StreamNarrator` — reuse the pacing/timeout/disable discipline; never a new narrator kind) with a step-summary prompt fed from what the panel already holds for that step (its narration window tail + step title/role + elapsed/tokens), NEVER from slot values (presentation stays presentation; no ledger reads). The result lands in the same ring buffer as a distinct entry kind — step summaries render with the step label emphasized (Bold, still two-tone) so they read as chapter marks between thinking ticks; live thinking entries stay Muted-stamped as today. A narrator that is absent/disabled simply produces no summary entry (no fallback text, P330 discipline).
      **Watch:** COST is the design constraint — exactly one extra narrator call per finished step, riding the existing pacing floor; batch nothing, retry nothing (a missed summary is a blank, not an error); entries stay presentation-only and out of every byte-stable surface; prompt is generic (no owner-specific phrasing baked in).
      **Done when:** a real run's sidebar shows step-summary entries appearing within a few seconds of each step finishing, visually distinct from thinking ticks, correct under iteration rollover (one summary per completed iteration-step, not per repaint); disabling the narrator yields zero summary entries and zero warnings; token cost of a run grows by ≈ one narrator call per finished step and is visible in the P445 narrator-token evidence.
      **Watch (cont.):** P454 renders the layout this feeds into (soft — can land either order); narrator config present (existing `[agent.narrator]` machinery, unchanged).

## Group 106: Release UX hardening (lifted 2026-07-21 from .docs/RELEASE_UX_AND_SCENARIOS.md §§2-8/§10; owner slots dispatch order)

Reserved-id accounting for the P416-P429 block: P422/P423 went to Group 105; P424 is RETIRED (absorbed into P421's packaging scope); P425 stays PARKED (keychain-gated trust-store writer — roadmap line, do not build). Design detail lives in the doc (worktree runs receive it via [worktree] seed); each contract below binds on its own text.

- [x] **P416** — Command finalization: obvious names, owner-curated visibility.
      **Why:** shipped verbs must say what they do; `use` says nothing. Owner rule: nothing important gets hidden — visibility is owner-curated, not unilaterally pruned.
      **Where:** rename `use` → `prompt` (old name stays a hidden alias for one release); propose `sync` → `vendor` (owner call at review, alias either way); add plain `list` (traits: name/version/status/trust verdict; `--json` for scripts); regroup `TRAITS_HELP` by workflow (author → verify → run → land); deliver a visibility inventory table (visible/hidden per verb, my defaults marked) as a doc artifact for the owner to tick — apply NO hides unilaterally.
      **Done when:** every visible verb's first help line states what it does to a stranger; `prompt` and `list` work; the inventory table exists for owner curation; no verb was hidden without an owner tick.
- [ ] **P417** — Output doctrine: headline + evidence path + next action; no walls. **ABSORBED 2026-07-25 into Group 113** — the doctrine became rules 1/4/5 of the locked ctx.gate house style; P465 builds the kit that enforces it, P466/P467 apply it. Do not dispatch P417 on its own; the Why below survives as motivating evidence.
      **Why:** failure paths dump multi-line essays and raw JSON (the merge-park wall; the bare TOML "parse error at line 69, column 1" that hid WHICH key and WHY twice today). The debug store already exists — errors must point, not dump.
      **Where:** the four headline verbs (`run`, `merge`, `check`, `import`): error shape = one headline line, one `evidence:` file path, one `retry:`/next-action line; the merge park essay moves verbatim to `park.md` under the run's debug dir; harness stderr and correction transcripts go to the debug store, never stdout; hidden `logs [run-id]` viewer (list artifacts, `--step N` prints one transcript); `--json` universal on visible verbs; internal tracing stays behind `CTX_LOG`.
      **Done when:** no multi-line inline dump remains on the four verbs' failure paths; every error names one file and one next command; the TOML-decode error names the offending key and the accepted schema.
- [ ] **P418** — Config model v2: machine facts vs project facts (amended 2026-07-21 — P314 already retired profiles, so the profile-removal half of the original contract is LANDED; what remains is precedence + tables).
      **Why:** current merge order is global-then-repo — the repo silently overrides the user's `[agent.*]` assignments, i.e. the project picks which paid model runs on a person's machine. Owner doctrine: "the machine decides WHO runs; the project decides WHAT runs." Run-shape knobs are also still flag-only.
      **Where:** two-class precedence: machine facts (`[agent.*]`, `[harness.*]`) resolve flag > env > USER-GLOBAL > repo-as-suggestion (repo honored only for roles the user has not assigned) > built-in; project facts (`[run]`, `[merge]` shape) resolve flag > env > REPO > user-global > built-in. New `[run]` table (worktree policy, budgets, timeouts, max-in-flight, strict-loops) and `[merge]` table (wait, overlap policy) readable at both levels; flags become overrides. Retire model-tier resolution from the surface (bare-tier selectors + `[model-tier]` stop resolving; code stays; doctor warns on tier selectors in config). Config-provenance print: `doctor --config` shows every effective knob with the layer it came from. Doctor hint when a foreign `~/.ctx/config.toml` exists.
      **Done when:** a user-global `[agent.worker]` beats the repo's for the same role; this dogfood repo runs unchanged before the owner moves tables; a fully-flagged invocation and its config-only equivalent produce byte-identical ledgers; provenance print names the winning layer per knob.
- [x] **P419** — Trust UX unification: one verb, never paste a digest.
      **Why:** one concept splits across visible `review --approve` and hidden `trust verify <raw-digest>` with two grammars; every digest move today meant hand-carried hashes.
      **Where:** unhide `trust` as the verb: `trust <trait>` (status/digest/verdict/drift), `trust approve|block <trait> [--reason]` resolving the digest from the name (`--digest` stays for scripts), `trust list [--stale]` (stale = digest moved since approval — today's recurring chore, currently answered by nothing); `review` demoted to hidden alias for one release; doctor flags orphaned records (digest matches no local trait) and stale approvals.
      **Done when:** approving a rebuilt trait never requires pasting a digest; `trust list --stale` names today's five implement-family moves; review-as-alias still works.
- [x] **P420** — Merge deep-resolve: `--deep` + `[agent.merger-deep]` (AMENDED 2026-07-22 with the resolution doctrine distilled from the owner-ordered hand-merge week — P402/P289/P423/P438/P441 — after the owner's direct ask: the merger must be able to land these and take judgment, at least behind the flag). *(Landed 2026-07-23, ec2282f — opt-in deep rung + universal pre-landing gates; the optional harvest-adjudication clause was NOT built in this slice → P463.)*
      **Why:** the merger parks on genuine semantic divergence — correct as the default (parking is the honesty property), but every park this week was resolvable by KNOWABLE policies a capable merger could have applied. The owner needs a sanctioned "dig in anyway" that decides and documents instead of parking. P423's Merges screen exposes it once present.
      **Where:** `ctx traits merge <run-id> --deep`: amended merger charter — resolve-with-receipts ("both sides are yours to judge; record every hunk-level choice; parking is permitted only for red gates and lost fast-forward races — facts, not judgment"); per-file decision log written into merge evidence + commit trailer; `[agent.merger-deep]` assignment with fallback to `[agent.merger]`. Composes with P401's ladder (fast path → standard → deep).
      **Charter doctrine (the week's precedents, spelled out for the merger prompt — each cites a real resolved park):**
        1. *Surface-vs-payload:* when main landed a rename/regroup/refactor of a surface the branch predates, main's landed decision WINS for the surface and the branch's payload is PORTED into it — never re-litigate a landed rename (P438: five verbs joined the landed P453 six-group help; the branch's older Verify/Run/Land grouping discarded. P289: main's split-validator factoring kept, branch's payload intact).
        2. *Additive-union:* same-anchor additive interleaves (proof-suite lists, usage lines, dispatch chains, help rows, module registrations, dep lists) are UNIONED, both sides sequenced — git's interleaving of same-anchor appends is textual noise, not divergence (every merge this week had one; P402's byte_compare was 3,900 lines of it).
        3. *Duplicate-component unification:* an add/add of the same designed component is ONE component after the merge — keep the stronger implementation as the primitive, re-express the weaker side's API on top of it, port callers (P441-vs-P438 audit_journal: flock/symlink-guard/fsync primitive won; typed records reimplemented over it).
        4. *Landed-decision supremacy:* a branch side that would revert a landed fix or re-add a retired mechanism is REFUSED even if textually clean — check the colliding region's main-side history before choosing (the P378 branch would have reverted the b94aeb4 token fix and resurrected retired profiles; correct outcome was refuse-and-supersede, not merge).
        5. *Factual verification:* when two sides make contradictory FACTUAL claims (doc comments, help text, plan prose), read the code and keep the true one — never pick by recency or side (P453-harvest: the branch's "resolver preload" claim was disproven by cdk_build.rs; the checkout's wording kept).
        6. *Gates decide:* every resolution is validated by the full gate (`just test`, not implement-phase-gates — see the P290 corruption the narrow gate waved through); a red gate parks exactly as before. Rider (P290 lesson, binding here): the merge gate must at minimum compile scripts/byte_compare.rs and `just --list` before ANY landing, deep or standard.
      **Mechanics the deep merger gets that today's lacks:** per-conflicted-file three-way inputs (base/ours/theirs bytes, not just the conflicted working file); the main-side log of commits touching each colliding path since the branch base (rule 4's evidence); the run's draft/work-summary for payload intent; permission to edit ANY file needed to honor a landed constraint (e.g. proof expectations, goldens regenerated under the controlled env — P438's help goldens), each edit named in the decision log. Optional composition: under `--deep` the merger may also adjudicate SEED-HARVEST conflicts (the 2026-07-22 P453 park class) with the same receipts; without the flag harvest parks exactly as today.
      **Done when:** a P438-style park (branch predating a landed surface rework) replayed under `--deep` lands with a decision log applying rules 1-2 and green FULL gates; an audit-journal-style add/add lands applying rule 3; a would-revert-a-landed-fix branch is REFUSED with rule 4 cited in the park reason; a red gate still parks; the standard no-flag behavior is unchanged.
      **Watch (cont.):** P401 optional composition; P423 Merges screen exposes the `d` key once present.
- [ ] **P421** — Mode B host plugins: the agent operator's manual + packaging (absorbs retired P424).
      **Why:** owner call — Mode B ships as plugins whose commands teach the agent to drive the ctx CLI; MCP server code stays in-tree but is not the story.
      **Where:** one canonical model-facing operator's manual (~a page): `run <trait> --no-drive --json` → session id → loop `next`/`call` (typed outputs validated, wrong shapes refused) → receipt; `prompt <trait>` for pure ingestion; `list --json` for discovery; what a digest/trust refusal means (the human owns `trust`). Ergonomics pass on `next`/`call`: stable `--json` shapes, loose session-id acceptance (prefix), documented exit codes — they become a de-facto public API for agents. Packaging: claude-code plugin (`/ctx:run`, `/ctx:list`, `/ctx:status` + per-trait commands rendered via the existing export targets), opencode command pack, pi skill export. RIDER (2026-07-21): the claude-code pack also emits `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` so exported traits are one-click installable through Claude Code's marketplace UI.
      **Done when:** a cold agent completes a trait run driven only by the manual; all three host packs install from the repo; `next`/`call` JSON shapes are documented and stable.
      **Watch (cont.):** P397's routing improves `call` output handling if landed first (not blocking).
- [x] **P446** — Committed `.ctx/.gitignore` + doctor gitignore diagnostics (owner-accepted 2026-07-22, born from the dotfiles/global-store incident + the commit-vs-ignore doc question).
      **Why:** which `.ctx/` paths are machine state vs project content is tribal knowledge today; the failure modes are both real — run droppings committed to the repo, or a blanket `.ctx/` ignore that silently unversions the team's trait packages. ctx should own this file, not the user's root `.gitignore`.
      **Where:** mechanism = a NESTED `.ctx/.gitignore` that ctx writes and the user commits (git honors nested ignore files; never touch the root `.gitignore`): `worktrees/`, `config.toml`, `harness.toml`, `traits/vendor/`, plus transitional `runs/`, `debug/`, `cache/` (drop after the P426 legacy-read release). The file must NOT ignore itself — committed so the whole team inherits it (deliberate difference from uv's `.venv/*` pattern; `.ctx` is half state, half committable content). Surface = `ctx traits doctor` (NO new top-level verb — P416 froze the surface; housekeeping lives where `--migrate-state` lives), reporting three findings with `--apply` fixing the first: (1) missing entries → idempotent write/append, never rewriting user edits; (2) already-tracked files matching the patterns (`git ls-files` under `.ctx/worktrees`/`runs`/`debug`/`cache`, a tracked `.ctx/config.toml`) → print the `git rm --cached` remedy, never perform git surgery on the user's repo; (3) global store physically resolving inside a git repository (walk up from the canonicalized `~/.config/ctx` looking for `.git` — the 2026-07-22 dotfiles-symlink incident class) → warn with the dotfiles-side ignore recommendation (`config/ctx/{runs,debug,cache}/`). Auto-scaffold: the operations that CREATE the dirs (first `--worktree` worktree add, vendor sync, P438 `install`) ensure the file exists as a side effect — idempotent, only at explicit user-invoked setup moments, NEVER mid-run (a run dirtying the invocation checkout collides with P397's clean-tree discipline).
      **Watch:** docs get the commit-vs-ignore table (ignore: worktrees/config/harness/vendor/legacy-state; commit: `.ctx/traits/<id>/` whole packages incl. the budget sidecar `config.toml` — call out the sidecar-vs-top-level `config.toml` name collision explicitly — and, once P438 lands, `traits.toml` + `traits.lock`); trust never appears in-repo by design, so it needs no entry.
      **Done when:** fresh repo + first worktree run → `.ctx/.gitignore` exists with the entries and `git status` shows only it (committable) plus intended content; doctor on a repo with a tracked `.ctx/config.toml` and a committed run ledger names both with remedies; doctor `--apply` is byte-idempotent on a second run; a user-edited file keeps its edits; dotfiles-symlinked config home triggers the store-inside-a-repo warning.
      **Watch (cont.):** P438 adds `install` as an ensure-point when it lands (not blocking); transitional entries retire with the P426 legacy window.
- [x] **P457** — Optional TypeScript config authoring (`config.ts` → `config.toml`) with a source-drift guard; TOML stays first-class (owner-chosen 2026-07-22 over Pkl/CUE/HCL — reasons recorded in Watch).
      **Why:** flat TOML gets unwieldy once the assignment matrix is qualifier (P451) × list (P456) × variant deep — the exact "TOML gets iffy quick" the owner named. Authors want expressive config (shared pools as variables, spread/override, loops), but two hard constraints hold: the loader must NEVER need node on the hot path (config is read on every command), and config must stay a plain, diffable, provenance-stampable artifact — so an authoring layer compiles TO TOML, it never replaces it. Dogfoods the product thesis on ctx itself ("even our config compiles to canonical").
      **Where:** THREE pieces. (1) `@ctx-traits/config` npm package: `defineConfig()` + typed `assignment()` builders whose TYPES ARE GENERATED from the Rust config model through the existing `sdk-generate` machinery (packages/cdk pattern) — so the TS surface can never accept a harness/model/effort/qualifier the loader would reject; hand-maintained types would just be a second drift source. (2) `ctx config build [path]`: compiles `config.ts` → `config.toml` via the SAME CDK build runner traits use (`cdk_build.rs`) — node is author-side only, invoked here and nowhere else. (3) Loader precedence PER config layer (global `~/.config/ctx` and repo `.ctx`, independently): a `config.ts` present ⇒ the sibling `config.toml` MUST be a generated artifact (carries a `# ctx:generated` marker + an input source-manifest); a `config.toml` with NO marker and no sibling `config.ts` ⇒ hand-authored, TOML-first, ZERO drift check, ZERO node (this is the "TOML source still valid" guarantee — the default, most common case, byte-identical to today).
      **Drift guard (the crux, reusing landed P406 source-map machinery):** the generated `config.toml` header stores the marker plus an input manifest = list of `(repo-relative-path, sha256)` over the FULL resolved module graph of `config.ts` (the top file AND every local file it imports — a shared `pools.ts` edit must be caught, the exact P406 transitive-anchor lesson; a top-file-only digest is insufficient). On every command that loads config, IF a sibling `config.ts` exists: the loader re-hashes exactly the manifest's listed files (cheap — N file reads, NO node, no module resolution) and compares. Match ⇒ use the generated TOML. Mismatch ⇒ refuse with `config.ts (or one of its imports) changed since config.toml was built — run ctx config build`. A `config.ts` beside a `config.toml` that lacks the marker (hand-edited a generated file, or hand-wrote a toml while a ts exists) ⇒ refuse naming the file and both fixes (rebuild, or delete `config.ts` to go TOML-first). Same idiom as trait `cdk-drift`/`projection-lock`, applied to config.
      **Watch:** node NEVER on the load/hot path — only `ctx config build` touches it (verify: a TOML-only repo runs every command with node absent); the manifest MUST cover transitive imports (top-file-only misses shared-pool edits); TOML-only configs resolve byte-identically to today (regression-gated); the TS types are GENERATED not authored (sdk-generate owns TS-surface-vs-Rust-loader parity — a mismatch there is itself a drift bug); the generated TOML stays human-readable and diffable (this is WHY we compile-to-TOML rather than evaluate at load time). **Language decision recorded:** TS chosen over Pkl/CUE/HCL — Pkl's only real edge was load-time evaluation via the young pure-Rust `pklr` (no compile step), but direct-eval of an `amends`/import graph makes config a moving target with no inspectable artifact for provenance, and puts a young third-party evaluator permanently on a provenance-first CLI's load path; TS keeps node author-side only, reuses the trait CDK idiom + the P438 npm transport already built, and the drift guard answers the "forgot to compile" worry with a loud one-command fix. Pkl stays a DEFERRED second frontend: since the loader only ever accepts TOML, `config.pkl → config.toml` (via matured pklr, behind this same guard) is a contained future feature, not a format decision.
      **Done when:** a repo with only `config.toml` (no `config.ts`) runs every command with zero node and zero drift check, byte-identical to today; a `config.ts` compiles to a marker+manifest `config.toml`; a subsequent edit to `config.ts` OR to a local file it imports is caught as drift on the next command with the rebuild remedy; a hand-edit to a generated `config.toml` is caught and named; `ctx config build` is the only node-touching path; the generated TS types reject a model/harness/effort the loader rejects (generated-from-Rust proof); the TS surface expresses P451 qualifiers and P456 role lists.
      **Watch (cont.):** `cdk_build`/`sdk-generate` machinery this rides is landed; composes with P451/P456 (TS surface must express qualifiers + lists) once they land; non-dep: P438 npm transport already ships `@ctx-traits/*` packages, so `@ctx-traits/config` distribution is free.

- [x] **P460** — Merge-on-completion: `run --worktree --merge[=standard|deep]` — the product absorbs the Justfile's temporary orchestration (owner-approved 2026-07-23; decisions: opt-in flag + config default, valued flag with explicit-value-beats-config).
      **Why:** automatic landing exists today only as ~60 lines of bash duplicated across the Justfile batch recipes (ledger polling, jq outcome checks, run-id extraction, `ctx traits merge`, stop-on-park) — the Justfile's own comment calls it "temporary shared orchestration until successful driven runs can request merge", and that comment is the feature's entire written record. Every ingredient landed (P339 lock, P401 fast path/harvest, P420 deep rung); only the seam connecting drive-completion to merge is still shell.
      **Where — surface:** `--merge[=standard|deep]` on `ctx traits run` and `session start`; parse error without `--worktree`. Rung resolution (owner-settled): explicit flag VALUE > `[merge] deep = true` config > built-in standard — so bare `--merge` uses the config's rung (else standard), `--merge=deep` forces deep, and `--merge=standard` OVERRIDES a config-deep. Config default `[merge] auto = true` (project-fact precedence per P418's `[merge]` table: flag > env > repo > global) makes it a standing default; `--no-merge` overrides a config-on. Lock waiting via landed P339, bounded by `[merge] wait`.
      **Where — semantics (the Justfile's, made typed):** (1) merge fires ONLY on drive outcome `completed` with a run-id in the ledger — every other outcome exits as today, no merge attempt; (2) credits-pause: no merge, resume command printed; (3) merge INTENT PERSISTS in the ledger (operational provenance field, never canonical) so a paused-then-resumed run still lands itself when the resume completes — the one thing the bash could not do; `--no-merge` on the resume clears it; (4) a park stays a park: branch + worktree intact, park reason in the report, distinct exit code, never auto-retried, never auto-escalated to deep; (5) typed exit codes coordinated with P417: completed+merged = 0 · completed-but-merge-parked · run-not-completed — batch stop conditions become exit-code checks, jq deleted; (6) `--json` gains a `merge` section embedding the existing MergeReport — one invocation, one machine-readable record of run AND landing.
      **Out of scope (v1):** `--no-drive`/Mode B sessions (no process end to hook — the manual verb and P423's dashboard remain their path); auto-deep-on-park escalation; any retry policy.
      **Watch:** merge machinery itself UNCHANGED — this is plumbing from drive-completion into the existing `merge` entry point, never a second merge implementation; the intent field is operational ledger state (out of every byte-stable surface); P423's spawn (`n`) passes the flag through; both Justfile batch recipes shrink to thin loops over `run --worktree --merge` preserving stop-on-park + completed-list echo, and the "temporary shared orchestration" comment is DELETED.
      **Done when:** `run --worktree --merge` on a trivial phase lands end-to-end with merge evidence in the run's `--json`; `--merge=standard` under a `[merge] deep = true` config uses the standard rung (and bare `--merge` uses deep); a park exits with its own code leaving branch+worktree intact; a credits-paused run resumed later still auto-lands, and `--no-merge` on the resume prevents it; `--merge` without `--worktree` is a parse error; both batch recipes are reduced to the loop with identical observable behavior; the Justfile comment is gone.
      **Watch (cont.):** P418 is soft (the `[merge]` config table home — flag-only works before it lands); P417 is soft for the exit-code shape (codes can land here and P417 adopts them).

- [ ] **P462** — Merge/worktree hygiene: park prunes regenerable caches, gate disk preflight, doctor debris sweep (owner-ordered 2026-07-23 after the ENOSPC incident chain; LANGUAGE-AGNOSTIC by owner directive — the machinery consumes DECLARED cache names, never a toolchain's literal directory).
      **Why:** the 2026-07-23 incident: parked worktrees each retain a multi-GB regenerable build directory, a park-heavy stretch stacked several until the disk filled, a landing gate then died mid-build with a cryptic toolchain exit code (misdiagnosed as a code defect), and the ENOSPC also killed merge cleanups midway — leaving 47 dead branches and 87 phantom worktree registrations swept by hand. Parking has an invisible multi-GB price and nothing reclaims it, bounds it, or cleans up after an interrupted cleanup.
      **Where — (a) park prunes regenerable artifacts:** when ANY merge path parks a run, the worktree is retained for triage MINUS its regenerable artifact directories — defined as the directories named by the project's build-cache declarations (P428's named caches once landed; until then, the cache-shaped entries of the worktree env overlay). Deletion is safe BY DEFINITION — a declared cache is regenerable; sources, ledger evidence, and seeded files are untouched; a later merge retry cold-rebuilds. The merge machinery never hardcodes any language's build dir — today's Rust `target/` is merely the current instance of a declared cache (P428 doctrine: no toolchain literals in generic verbs).
      **Where — (b) gate disk preflight:** before the pre-landing gate starts its build/test chain, check free bytes on the volume holding the worktree against a configurable floor (`[merge]` or `[run]` table per P418's project-facts precedence; conservative default). Below the floor ⇒ a typed park BEFORE anything builds, whose reason names the freed-space remedy (including the prune command from (a)) — never a mid-build toolchain exit code that reads as a test failure. The check is bytes-free on a volume — toolchain-blind.
      **Where — (c) doctor debris sweep:** `doctor` learns three findings with `--apply` fixes, all idempotent: worktree registrations whose directory no longer exists (prune); run branches whose tip is already contained in the default branch (delete — the exact corpses an interrupted cleanup leaves); stale landing-gate temp binaries (delete). Doctor NEVER touches unmerged branches, present worktrees, or anything outside those three classes.
      **Watch:** park semantics otherwise unchanged (parking still preserves everything triage needs); pruning is limited to DECLARED caches — an undeclared dir is never deleted no matter how cache-shaped it looks; the free-space floor is config, not code; grep-provable absence of cargo/pnpm/npm literals in the merge/park/doctor code paths (P428 gate).
      **Done when:** parking a run leaves zero declared-cache bytes behind while sources/evidence survive; a low-disk simulation parks at preflight with the remedy named (no mid-build death); doctor on a debris-seeded fixture reports exactly the three classes and `--apply` clears exactly them, twice-idempotent; the toolchain-literal grep over the touched code paths is clean.

- [ ] **P463** — Deep-merge charter v2: harvest adjudication, merger transcripts, rebuild-don't-reconcile (successor to landed P420; owner-ordered 2026-07-23 from the ENOSPC-week postmortem — three hand-brokered harvest parks and the first live receipt park). **SECOND live receipt park 2026-07-25 (run-12b7ce77, P460 — nine overlapping files against three fresh main commits): deep merger's reply failed receipt validation twice, and AGAIN zero forensics — the run's debug dir holds only its own frames, no merger transcript. Candidate causes untestable without transcripts: receipt scale (one perfect structured reply enumerating every hunk across nine files), output truncation (an explicit park cause at merge.rs:1806), or prose-wrapping — the same output-contract failure family P464 documents for drive frames; the merger path has NO correction/reshape retry, so one non-conforming reply is a park. P463 must also give the merger dispatch P464's retry semantics (resume + correction naming what the receipt lacked), not only persist transcripts. THIRD finding same night: the merger call budget was a hardcoded 300s (raised to 900s by hand, 2026-07-25 — live deep merges were timing out mid-judgment and the kill surfaced as the invalid-receipt park, masking the real cause); P463 adds a `[merge]` timeout knob (per-call seconds, deep and standard separately if wanted) and makes a timeout park SAY timeout — never report a killed call as a malformed receipt. **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED 2026-07-25 (transcripts found in the harness's OWN store — `~/.claude/projects/<worktree>/*.jsonl`; the "zero forensics" claim was wrong for claude seats, and opencode keeps sessions too — P463's transcript work should LINK these, not rebuild capture):** fable's receipt run was read end to end — it resolved the single conflicted file correctly (three clean edits, verified `just --list`, staged, well-formed one-decision receipt) and STILL parked, because the validator additionally requires every `touched_paths_delta` path covered as a decision path or `supporting_edits` entry — and the 171KB merger prompt contains ZERO statement of that rule (no "supporting", no "covering", no "every file"). The model is graded on a contract it is never shown. FIXES: (1) the deep prompt states the full receipt contract verbatim — fields, `supporting_edits`, the hunk-and-touched-path coverage rule; (2) a coverage-failed park NAMES the uncovered paths instead of the generic sentence — SHIPPED as hotfix bcc2ed0, and its first live use SOLVED THE CASE: the "uncovered touched paths" were `scripts/golde` (a name cut mid-word) + a file nobody touched — the worktree fingerprint was built from a 65,536-byte capture of a 73,943-byte `git ls-files --stage -z` listing, pre and post truncating at slightly different offsets once the merger's `git add` shortened the index, so paths straddling the cut flickered between the two maps as phantom touches. Every receipt park on run-12b7ce77 (and plausibly run-e26dbea's at the P409 landing) was this truncation, not a model failure — fable's receipts were substantively correct each time. Fixed same day (`run_git_full_listing`: 16MiB ceiling + hard error on truncation for repository-listing plumbing); P463 inherits the doctrine: ANY internal capture that feeds state decisions must fail loudly on truncation, never parse a cut listing; (3) separately, an `exit=None` merger park (process killed by outside signal — observed same morning, most plausibly a ctrl-c during the merger's SILENT multi-minute think) should say "killed by signal", and the merger call should emit periodic progress lines so silence stops inviting the ctrl-c.**
      **Why:** P420 landed the deep rung and the universal gates, but three gaps surfaced in its first week: (1) its harvest-adjudication clause stayed optional and unbuilt — so `--deep` is inert on exactly the park class that recurred three times (the plan-file seed-harvest conflict; P415's twelve attempts were the extreme case, since that phase's entire deliverable was the harvested file); (2) the P409 receipt-validation park — the charter's fail-closed path working as designed — was UNDIAGNOSABLE because deep-merger calls leave no transcript anywhere; (3) the deep merger was handed two generated artifacts (a projection output and a lockfile) no model should ever be asked to merge, and its charter has no rule saying so.
      **Where — (a) harvest adjudication (in scope, not optional):** under `--deep`, seed-harvest conflicts route through the deep merger with the SAME typed proceed/refuse decision receipts as code conflicts — per-file three-way inputs (seed-time baseline / checkout / worktree bytes), decisions logged into merge evidence; a refusal or invalid receipt parks exactly as today. Without `--deep`, harvest parks mechanically, unchanged. This is what lets plan-writing phases land unattended.
      **Where — (b) merger transcripts:** every merger call (standard confirmation, deep reconciliation, harvest adjudication) persists its prompt and raw output to the run's debug store exactly like frame calls do — an invalid receipt becomes a readable artifact, not a mystery. Applies to the standard merger too, not just deep.
      **Where — (c) rebuild-don't-reconcile:** the charter classifies conflicted files before prompting: a file the project's own declarations mark as GENERATED (projection outputs, lock evidence — anything a deterministic command regenerates from sources) is NEVER sent to the model; the merger reconciles the sources, the machinery re-runs the generating commands, and the regenerated artifacts land — the exact playbook every hand-merge this week used. Language-agnostic: "generated" comes from the project's projection/lock declarations, never filename patterns or toolchain knowledge.
      **Watch:** fail-closed posture unchanged everywhere (invalid receipt ⇒ park; refusal ⇒ park with the partial decision log); no new judgment authority for the NO-flag path; transcripts are debug-store artifacts (operational, never canonical/digested); the classification in (c) must be declaration-driven so it generalizes past any one language's artifacts (P428 doctrine).
      **Done when:** a seeded-plan conflict lands under `--deep` with a per-file decision receipt in merge evidence (and still parks without the flag); a deliberately malformed merger reply parks AND leaves a readable transcript in the debug store naming prompt + output; a conflict touching a declared-generated file never reaches the model — the sources reconcile, the artifact regenerates, and the landing's evidence records the regeneration; the P415 shape (plan-wide sweep phase) replayed end-to-end lands unattended.

- [x] **P476** — One agent namespace: every seat is `[agent.role.<name>]`, `master` becomes `default` (owner-ordered 2026-07-25).
      **Why:** the config spells one concept three ways — bare `[agent]` scalars as the unmapped-role fallback, self-described `[agent.master]`/`[agent.merger]`/`[agent.merger-deep]`/`[agent.narrator]` tables, and `[agent.role.<name>]` tables for trait roles. Owner: inconsistent, "having both agent & agent.master seems dumb." The special tables also need special plumbing at every consumer, which taxes everything built on seats (P475 budgets, P451 qualifiers, P456 lists).
      **Where:** every seat becomes a role: `[agent.role.default]` (renamed from `master` — it is the default DRIVER and the fallback assignment for any role not explicitly mapped), `[agent.role.merger]`, `[agent.role.merger-deep]`, `[agent.role.narrator]`, alongside the existing trait roles. The bare `[agent]` scalar fields are DELETED from the schema — the fallback is `[agent.role.default]`, full stop. Resolution: exact role lookup, miss falls back to `default`; the master loop, both merger dispatches, and narrator spawns read their seats through the SAME resolver as trait roles, and the special-table structs/plumbing are deleted. P456 role-list form is valid for every seat uniformly. CLI: the `--master` override folds into the existing `--assign default=...` (one mechanism); `--master` is removed pre-launch and its parse error names the replacement. Migration is a clean break with precise errors, not a silent dual-read: `deny_unknown_fields` already rejects the legacy tables — the rejection message names the exact new key ("[agent.master] moved: declare [agent.role.default]"), and `doctor --config` flags legacy shapes with the same hint. Config comments and the PRODUCT.md config section move in the same change.
      **Watch:** the narrator/default seats must still never share a model (the 2026-07-21 latency doctrine — the narrator exists to be small and fast); P418 classified-layer provenance keeps working per role with sources intact; `--assign`/attach semantics otherwise untouched; future per-seat configurability (budgets, qualifiers, session-mode) now has exactly one place to land — that is the point.
      **Done when:** a config containing only `[agent.role.*]` tables drives a full run including a deep merge and live narration; `[agent] model=` and `[agent.master]` each fail parse with the message naming the new key; `--assign default=harness/model` overrides the driver seat; `doctor --config` renders every seat through one uniform listing; a grep finds no master/merger/narrator-specific config structs left.
- [x] **P477** — The merge gate is DECLARED, never assumed: no repo tool named in the product (owner-ordered 2026-07-24 — "users WON'T HAVE THAT JUSTFILE"; AMENDED 2026-07-24: the run never writes ANY config file — see Watch).
      **Why:** the universal pre-landing gate hardcodes `just --list` + `just test` on every landing path — our repo's workflow baked into the product. With P460 landing merge inside `run --worktree --merge`, any user repo without `just` parks EVERY merge at the gate. The product must imply nothing about a repository's tooling beyond safety and robustness; our own workflows stay in OUR config.
      **Where:** the gate's command list comes from config — `[merge] gate = [["just","--list"],["just","test"]]` (ordered literal argv lists, the house anti-shell posture, same shape as `[worktree] setup`) resolved through the P418 classified layers so `doctor --config` shows the effective gate and its source. THIS repo's declaration is an OWNER-RUN step AFTER landing + reinstall — never the run's (see Watch); the PRODUCT DEFAULT with no `[merge] gate` declared is an EMPTY gate — landing proceeds with a single advisory line naming that no gate is configured (safety still holds: reconciliation, receipts, clean-tree checks, and typed parks are all gate-independent). Timeouts: one configurable per-command ceiling (default generous), summed into the lock-wait derivation exactly as today's consts are; the per-command capture/park/evidence flow (incl. the persisted gate-output file) is unchanged. The gate doctrine comment in merge.rs is rewritten to name the invariant: the merge machinery may reference NOTHING repo-specific — it executes declared commands and judges exit codes, full stop.
      **Watch:** CONFIG FILES ARE READ-ONLY TO THE RUN — the worker must NOT create or edit `.ctx/config.toml` ANYWHERE: not the worktree's copy, not the invocation checkout's (2026-07-24 incident: three worker frames across runs wt-b97eb24f2049/wt-0bb162823239 edited the MAIN checkout's config by absolute path with the not-yet-parseable `[merge] gate` key, killing every subsequent ctx invocation at config decode — the declaration cannot even parse until the binary built from this phase is installed). All validation goes through fixture repos the run creates itself; this repo's own declaration is a by-hand OWNER step after landing (the owner knows the line: `[merge] gate = [["just","--list"],["just","test"]]`). The two 2026-07-24 run branches predate this amendment and contain exactly that forbidden edit — harvest from them with the config edits stripped. Dogfood must not weaken — our config keeps the full chain (owner-added, post-install) and the loop still lands nothing unvalidated; existing merge proofs parameterize their fixture repos' gates through the new config instead of relying on the hardcoded chain (fixture Justfiles stay only where a proof exercises just specifically); no auto-detection magic in v1 (no "found a Justfile, running it" guessing — declared or absent, generic and flexible first).
      **Done when:** a fixture repo with NO justfile and no `[merge] gate` lands a worktree run end to end with the advisory line; the same repo with `gate = [["false"]]` parks with the declared command in the park evidence; a fixture repo declaring the full just chain behaves byte-identically to today's hardcoded chain; the run's diff touches NO config file and nothing outside its worktree (explicitly checked at review); `doctor --config` renders the effective gate; a repo-wide grep of `modules/` finds no literal `just` invocation in the merge machinery.
## Group 107: State model v2 — global store, zero-config harnesses, generalization (lifted 2026-07-21 from the same doc §§2-3/§7; owner-run migration window required for P426)

- [x] **P426** — Global state store: runs/debug/cache → `~/.config/ctx/<area>/<repo-key>/`.
      **Why:** owner order — state out of `.ctx/`; also the prerequisite for P423's ALL-repos session view. `.ctx/` becomes fully committable (traits + project config only); `git clean` can never eat run history.
      **Where:** layout consts (`modules/io/src/layout/mod.rs`: RUN_ROOT/DEBUG_ROOT/CACHE_ROOT) become resolver functions rooted at `~/.config/ctx/{runs,debug,cache}/<repo-key>/` where `<repo-key>` = dirname-slug + 8-hex of the canonical (symlink-resolved) repo-root path; `repos.toml` index (repo-key → path, last-seen) maintained on every run — the TUI's machine-wide iterator; worktrees deliberately STAY repo-local (git working copies belong beside the repo); dual-read of old `.ctx` paths for one release; `doctor --migrate-state` prints the plan, `--apply` moves it — OWNER-RUN with no live runs (standing rule: the loop never migrates its own substrate); gitignore cleanup follows.
      **Done when:** a fresh run writes only under `~/.config/ctx`; old runs remain readable; `repos.toml` lists this repo; orphaned state for a moved repo surfaces in doctor.
      **Watch (cont.):** P423's ALL filter consumes this once it exists; MIGRATION IS OWNER-RUN.
- [x] **P427** — Zero-config harnesses: built-in registry + PATH detection.
      **Why:** a fresh machine currently needs harness definitions before anything runs; harness config should be invisible until the user overrides it.
      **Where:** built-in registry (claude-code, opencode, pi invocation/transport shapes) compiled into the binary; first run with no config detects binaries on PATH, picks built-in defaults for the roles the trait needs, prints one line naming the choice; `[harness.*]`/`[agent.*]` config overrides built-ins; no detected harness → the error names the exact table to write and the binaries probed; doctor shows the detection table. RIDER (2026-07-21): the built-in definitions must absorb the operationally-learned flags currently living only in this repo's gitignored config — claude-code: `--disallowedTools Agent` (driven frames must not delegate to subagents; tool is named "Agent", not "Task" — run-dd85b362), stream-json + partial messages, `--append-system-prompt` delivery; opencode: `--auto` (headless permission parity), `--dir` (worktree anchoring — run-840c9d0d), `--variant` effort flag. A fresh machine must get these defaults without ever writing config.
      **Done when:** with config removed, `ctx traits run <trait>` works on a machine with claude-code on PATH; the no-harness error is copy-paste actionable.
      **Watch (cont.):** composes with P418's precedence tables (restored 2026-07-23 — dropped by the P415 sweep).

## Group 108: Trait distribution — install / remove / publish + global tier (owner-accepted design 2026-07-21; npm as a dumb CDN, everything ctx-native)

Settled decisions from the 2026-07-21 brainstorm: pure-Rust registry client for CONSUMING traits (no node/npm binary required to install or run); committable `.ctx/traits.toml` manifest + `.ctx/traits.lock` (Cargo-shaped, never the project's package.json); vendoring is the home (node_modules-style live resolution never is); package-granular trust approval; publish shells out to real `npm publish` (auth/2FA not worth reimplementing). Layer distinction to preserve everywhere: package-level `trait.toml [dependencies]` (a trait needs resource packs) vs project-level `traits.toml` (a project uses traits) are different relations sharing the vendor/lock plumbing. `import` stays a different verb (foreign source → authored package, quarantine+review).

- [x] **P438** — `install` / `remove` / `update` / `outdated` / `info`: project-scope porcelain + pure-Rust registry client.
      **Why:** owner UX target: `ctx traits install @ctx-traits/useful-agents` and nothing else — ctx owns the manifest, the fetch, the integrity, and the trust gate; the npm registry is transport. Group 93's dual-use tarballs and the existing sync/vendor/lock machinery are the plumbing; this is the front door.
      **Where:** spec parser (`@scope/name[@range]` npm-first; `git+`/`path:` reserved spec forms for later backends — parse, reject with "not yet supported"). Registry client in Rust: metadata GET, semver range resolution (semver crate + dist-tags), tarball fetch, sha512 integrity verify — no node anywhere on the consume path. Manifest `.ctx/traits.toml [dependencies]` + lock `.ctx/traits.lock` recording exact version, npm tarball integrity, AND per-trait canonical digests (dual-hash: registry compromise after install is caught by the second hash). Vendor into `.ctx/traits/vendor/<name>/` via the existing vendor root; the reconciler verb (today's `sync`) learns the project manifest so post-clone/CI is one command. `info <spec>` fetches remote metadata + the publisher's digests claim WITHOUT installing (inspect before trusting); `outdated` diffs manifest ranges vs registry. RIDER (2026-07-21 competitive sweep): `info` — and install's completion output — also prints the trait's declared capability surface straight from the canonical: every command-step argv (`runs: git, cargo, node`), resource roots (package/repo), and expected agent roles. The reviewed bytes ARE the permission manifest; zero new model. When a tarball carries a `ctx.digests` claim (P440 emits), verify claim-vs-content before vendoring; absent claim = warn, not refuse (pre-P440 packages exist). Record the package's required canonical schema-version; an installed trait newer than the binary's schema refuses at load with "upgrade ctx" — never a parse wall (today's TOML-decode lesson). Trust unchanged and printed: install ends with each trait's digest + `trust approve` hint — installed ≠ trusted. Install/remove mutations append to a machine-wide audit journal (`~/.config/ctx/audit/YYYY-MM.jsonl`).
      **Done when:** on a machine WITHOUT node, install → vendored + locked + listed with origin; a tampered vendored file is caught by lock verify; `info` shows digests before any bytes land outside the cache; `remove` cleans manifest+lock+vendor; re-`sync` from a fresh clone reproduces the store byte-identically; first run of an installed trait refuses until trust-approved.
      **Watch (cont.):** P440's claim format is consumed opportunistically once it lands (not blocking).
- [ ] **P439** — Global tier + run-anywhere.
      **Why:** owner order: run a trait anywhere — not only inside repos. A global trait runs in every project, which is exactly where the per-machine trust gate matters most.
      **Where:** mirror the manifest/lock/store at the user root: `~/.config/ctx/traits.toml`, `traits.lock`, `traits/<name>/`; `install -g` / `remove -g` / `update -g`. Resolution order: repo-authored → repo-vendored → user-global → built-ins, nearest wins; run start prints origin (`trait: useful-agents (global v1.2.0)`); `list` gains the origin column; a family/name shadowing across tiers is surfaced by list/doctor, never silently ambiguous. Run-anywhere: outside any repo, resolution uses global+built-ins; P426 RIDER — non-repo cwds get an `adhoc-<cwd-slug>` run key in repos.toml so the global run store and the TUI ALL-view cover them; `worktreeRequired` traits refuse outside a git repo with a clear error. Package-granular trust: `trust approve <package>` reviews once and approves ALL current variant digests of that one package (per-digest records underneath, all revert on change).
      **Done when:** `cd /tmp && ctx traits run <installed-global-trait>` works end to end on a machine with a PATH harness (composes with P427); the same trait installed at both tiers resolves repo-first with the shadow surfaced; package-granular approve admits a P396 family in one review; global mutations appear in the audit journal.
      **Watch (cont.):** the run-store rider rides P426 (resolution half is independent of it).
- [ ] **P440** — `publish`: preflight, dual-use pack, digests claim.
      **Why:** closing the loop makes P345/P370 (owner-gated npm publishes) a product flow instead of a hand ritual, and the embedded digests claim is what P438 verifies — publisher-signed content identity, our supply-chain story made mechanical.
      **Where:** `ctx traits publish [path|--trait <id>]`: preflight = check green, `[package].status = ready` (draft REFUSES), CDK drift clean, lock fresh, version not already on the registry (no republish); pack the Group 93 dual-use tarball — trait package content + a GENERATED package.json (name/version/exports for authoring-time TS consumers) carrying `ctx.digests` (per-variant canonical digests) so installers verify tarball-content-vs-claim; `--dry-run` prints the file list + digests; then shell to `npm publish` under the caller's npm auth (2FA/OTP stays npm's problem). One rule: ONE npm package = ONE trait package (P396 families ride along as variants; aggregation stays in `[dependencies]`). Rider: pass through npm provenance attestation (`--provenance`) when publishing from CI.
      **Done when:** a draft package refuses with the reason; `--dry-run` output matches what lands on the registry; a published package round-trips through P438 install with the claim verified; P345/P370 execute through this flow.
      **Watch (cont.):** the publish side itself has no dependency; the round-trip proof in Done-when needs P438.

## Standing non-goals — competitive posture (recorded 2026-07-21 from the sx 2.0 / Slate sweep)

Answer future "should we do what X does?" from this list instead of re-litigating.

- **Sync-folder vaults** (Dropbox/Drive/iCloud as skill server): content-addressed trust wants integrity-verified transport; sx's own 2.0 post names Dropbox latency its least-certain bet.
- **Cloud relay** of local stores to web AI tools; **desktop app** (the TUI is our surface); **multi-vault profiles** (repo + user-global tiers suffice).
- **Org-governance SaaS** (fleet-wide pushing, admin allowlists): Sleuth's business, not ours.
- **Hooks / MCP-servers as distributable asset kinds**: revisit post-launch; the trait stays the unit.
- **Auto-propagating collections** (assets reaching machines without explicit `update` + re-approval): ANTI-feature — it is precisely the supply-chain hole the digest-trust model closes; P443's `extends` is the sanctioned shape.
- **Swarm orchestration / emergent multi-agent quality claims**: we sell provable procedures with receipts; Slate embodies what we deliberately do not claim to out-orchestrate.

Research + writing only, no code; safe for the loop.
