## Group 110: Composition — guarded production & the process kit (design SETTLED 2026-07-22, owner brainstorm; the Show HN composition story)

Settled decisions, recorded so no phase needs the chat transcript. **The insight:** implement's stages are one PROCESS repeated with different goals — produce an artifact, review it, revise until accepted — so the family collapses to `sequence: [planning, building, shipping]` where each element is a factory-returned composite. **The loop shape (owner):** produce-FIRST uniform body `produce → [evidence] → review`, one worker step every round; round 1 the verdict slot is empty and HIDDEN from the worker (optional input), rounds 2+ the worker revises against it. Approval exits the loop before produce could run again, which structurally deletes the P275 `maybe-apply-fixes` bypass and the touched-after-certification bug class it existed to kill. **Ownership:** the artifact slot is caller-declared (`produces:`) — it is just the produce step's output slot ("artifact" is not a concept); the factory invents ONLY `<id>-verdict` (instance-prefix naming is the locality mechanism for now; named sequence blocks already give canonical/ledger grouping; true scoped slots deferred, not required). **Doctrine:** VARIANT_DOCTRINE dies — rigor arrives as plain parameters (rounds, seats, onExhausted, authority text); quick/default/smart/strict are OUR example convention, never product vocabulary; users name variants freely. **Composition doctrine unchanged:** factories are userland TS compiling to explicit canonical (P396 rule) — what you trust-approve is the expanded graph. Positioning hook (research 2026-07-22): the "graph engineering" discourse (born of a July-18 steipete tweet; LangGraph/CrewAI practice; Salesforce Agentforce "guided determinism" the nearest serious cousin) has the SHAPE of what we compile but no identity, no types, no receipts — claim the shape, indict the practice.

- [ ] **P447** — Optional inputs: attach-when-present, hidden-when-absent.
      **Why:** the produce-first loop needs the worker to declare the verdict slot as input WITHOUT waiting for it (round 1 it does not exist yet) and without seeing an empty section ("verdict: none" primes the model; silence does not).
      **Where:** canonical: input refs accept `{ slot = "...", optional = true }` (schema-version consideration; plain string refs unchanged). Runtime: readiness treats optional inputs as never-gating; frame presentation OMITS an absent optional input entirely — no header, no placeholder. CDK: `input.optional(handle)` marker. Semantics guard: today an explicit `input:` list REPLACES the interpolated refs (`fields.input ?? refs` in prompt.ts) — do NOT flip that to extend (existing generated TOMLs' input lists would change → digests move repo-wide); add an ADDITIVE path instead (factory-internal merge or `input.also(...)`) so existing packages stay byte-identical.
      **Done when:** a two-step loop fixture (produce declaring optional verdict → review) runs: round-1 produce frame shows no verdict section, round-2 shows it; readiness never blocks on the absent slot; every pre-existing package rebuilds byte-identical; goldens cover the presentation both ways.
- [x] **P448** — `sequence.flow` constants (CDK-only).
      **Why:** owner ask — `"continue"`/`"block"` string literals become `sequence.flow.Continue` / `sequence.flow.Block`, matching the existing Tone/Method/Verbosity constant-namespace pattern.
      **Where:** packages/cdk sequence.ts; compiles to the same `on-exhausted` strings — canonical bytes unchanged; string literals stay accepted (no break).
      **Done when:** implement/refactor sources build unchanged with either form; generated TOML byte-identical.
- [x] **P449** — The process kit: `guardedProduction` + `commitTail` in @ctx-traits/agents.
      **Why:** both families (implement, refactor) consume the same two processes — the second-consumer rule is met, so the kit lives in the shared package; today's shared.ts zoo (refinementLoopStep, reviewStep, commitMessageStep, gitStatusStep, guardedCommitTail, draft/implement/apply-fixes trio) collapses into two factories (~70 lines).
      **Where:** `guardedProduction({ id, produces, produce: {agent, text}, review: {agent, text}, evidence?, alsoRequire?, rounds, onExhausted })` → `SequenceHandle & { verdict }` (Object.assign handle-with-fields, P398 style): loop(`id`) over linear(`id`-body) = [produce step (optional verdict input, output produces), ...evidence, review step (output verdict)], until = fieldEquals(verdict.status, approved) AND alsoRequire, iterations = rounds. The `evidence` seam carries P275's diff-grounding: a code loop passes [repo-gates check, scoped-diff capture] and `alsoRequire: gate green`; a plan loop passes nothing. `reviewerVerdict` schema = { status: approved|revise, blockers: [text] }. Multi-seat review (1..N) is a straightforward extension — start single-seat. `commitTail({ id, scribe, summary, verdict, receipt })` → linear: status command (`git status --porcelain`) → when(non-empty): scribe message (reads verdict FIRST, honest-exhaustion doctrine: revise ⇒ say so + "Unresolved reviewer blockers" section) → stage → commit(output receipt); clean tree skips the whole tail (P397 behavior preserved).
      **RIDER (2026-07-23):** the kit's `reviewerVerdict.status` consumes `schema.decision()` (the CDK's stable approved|revise vocabulary) instead of hand-declaring the enum — three verdict vocabularies already coexist (schema.verdict pass/fail/needs-changes, schema.decision, checklist pass/fail/waived) and the family hand-rolls a fourth today; the kit makes schema.decision THE verdict spine. Kit is authored in the P458 grammar (input.text/output.of, branch check/success/failure).
      **Done when:** a demo trait `sequence: [guardedProduction(...), guardedProduction(...), commitTail(...)]` builds and checks green; the compiled canonical shows named blocks per composite with only `<id>-verdict`/tail-internal slots invented; kit consumed from @ctx-traits/agents by a repo trait.
- [x] **P450** — Restructure the implement family onto the kit AND fold it onto P396 variants (absorbs P437 2026-07-23): plan → build → [tests] → ship, as ONE `.ctx/traits/implement` variants-map package.
      **RIDER (2026-07-23, owner-ordered — plan-write scope):** the restructured family's prompts narrow every worker/scribe plan-write to THE RUN'S OWN PHASE ENTRY (tick, dispositions, its board line) — never editing other phases' text, never rewriting group preambles, never touching the plan tail. Plan-WIDE edits are the exclusive business of dedicated sweep phases (the P415 shape), which rely on P420's now-in-scope harvest adjudication to land. Rationale: routine tail collisions caused most of the 2026-07-22/23 harvest parks; scope-narrowing removes the casual collisions, harvest adjudication handles the deliberate sweeps.
      **Why:** the family becomes the composition showcase: extraction → guardedProduction(plan) → guardedProduction(code, evidence=[gates,diff], alsoRequire=gate-green) → optional guardedProduction(tests) → commitTail; variants differ ONLY in parameter values (seats, rounds, onExhausted, authority text) and goal list (quick skips the plan loop; tests loop is per-goal optional — features yes, bugfixes no). P458's sequencing settles that the kit adoption and the P396 variants-map fold (formerly P437) land as ONE family rewrite so the five family digests move once and the owner re-approves trust once, not three times.
      **Where:** family sources consume P449; VARIANT_DOCTRINE export DELETED from @ctx-traits/agents — surviving fragments become plain exported consts in the family's own shared.ts (convention, importable/replaceable, per "no baked taxonomies"); cross-loop handoff via interpolation of the caller-owned slots (plan slot into build prompts, etc.); scribe consumes `building.verdict`. In the same rewrite, fold `implement-{quick,default,smart,strict}` into ONE `.ctx/traits/implement` package: `source/variant/{quick,default,smart,strict}.ts` + `source/shared.ts` (all imports become `./shared.ts`), variants map with `default` marked via `.default()`; DELETE the four packages after byte-diffing each variant canonical against its pre-migration sibling (only identity fields may differ). `implement-phase` STAYS a separate package (owner call 2026-07-21 — repo-specific dogfood, not a built-in): its shared import re-points at the family package's source and must be reconciled with P436's error (a manifest-declared source-dependency exemption, or promoting the needed helpers to @ctx-traits/agents — implementer picks). Justfile invocations (`implement:quick`, `implement-phase`) must resolve identically post-migration.
      **Watch:** NOT byte-stable by design (restructure) — all family digests move ⇒ owner trust re-approvals after landing; behavior parity checked by running one real phase per variant; produce-first means rounds semantics shift (round 1 IS production — recalibrate per-variant round counts).
      **Done when:** all variants build/check green from the kit; a quick run and a default run each complete a real trivial phase end-to-end with honest scribe output; shared.ts LOC drops and no per-variant loop code remains; VARIANT_DOCTRINE is gone from the shared package; the store lists one implement family + implement-phase with the four sibling packages gone; every variant resolves and runs by its same name; the cross-package import from implement-phase is P436-clean by the chosen mechanism.
      **Watch (cont.):** owner trust re-approvals required at landing for the fresh per-variant digests.
- [x] **P451** — Variant-qualified (and repo-qualified) agent assignment in config. *(AMENDED 2026-07-22: the numbered-seat vocabulary — reviewer-1/reviewer-2 — is SUPERSEDED by P456 role lists; one `reviewer` name, list entries are the seats. Everything else in this contract stands.)*
      **Why:** owner call — ONE stable role vocabulary across variants (worker / reviewer / scribe; P456's lists keep dual review two-vendor-capable); variants never rename roles and never name models; the operator makes `reviewer` cheap on quick and frontier on smart in CONFIG, carried across machines.
      **Where:** assignment tables gain an optional variant qualifier — `[agent.role.reviewer-1.variant.smart]` (partial override, base fields inherit) — keyed on the USER-CHOSEN variant name, composing across families by naming convention (`implement:smart` and `refactor:smart` both hit `variant.smart`). Global `~/.config/ctx/config.toml` additionally accepts a repo qualifier: `[repo."<repo-key>".agent.role.…]` matched via the P426 repo registry, so per-repo settings live in the carried global file. Resolution = most specific wins: (repo,role,variant) > (repo,role) > (role,variant) > role > `[agent]` fallback; across files nearer wins as today; `--assign` beats all. Variant identity read from the session's trait (P396 first-class `variant` field once landed; until then derive from family name-convention metadata).
      **Watch:** this amends/extends P418's config model — coordinate, don't fork the schema twice; profiles stay dead (no trait-scoped model routing outside these qualifiers); budgets stay in package sidecars.
      **Done when:** one config file makes the same trait's `quick` and `smart` runs resolve different reviewer models (provenance evidence shows which qualifier won); repo-qualified global settings apply only in the matching repo; unqualified configs resolve exactly as before.
      **Watch (cont.):** P396 gives first-class variant identity once landed (soft — name-convention fallback works today).
- [ ] **P456** — Role assignment lists: one role name, many models (owner-ordered 2026-07-22; supersedes P451's numbered seats).
      **Why:** owner ask — `reviewer` should be a LIST of assignments instead of reviewer-1/reviewer-2, so trait vocabulary stays one name while config supplies seat diversity; doubles as the quota pressure-valve across the three flat subscription pools (a `worker` list spreads a parallel batch across providers).
      **Where:** `harness_config` assignment tables accept EITHER today's single table `[agent.role.reviewer]` (unchanged, byte-identical resolution) OR an array of tables `[[agent.role.reviewer]]`, each entry a full assignment (harness/model/effort/session-mode). **Seat binding is structural and deterministic:** the Nth sequence item referencing `agent:<role>` within the procedure (document order, iteration-independent — a loop's steps keep their seats across rounds) resolves to `list[N % len]`; no clock, no randomness (a `strategy = "random"` mode is DEFERRED until someone actually wants it — determinism keeps receipts reproducible). CDK/canonical untouched: traits keep declaring one role; `guardedProduction`'s two review seats both reference `agent:reviewer` and get two vendors purely from config. Provenance `agent-assignments` evidence gains seat-index + list-length per frame. `--assign reviewer=...` overrides ALL seats; `--assign reviewer.2=...` overrides one (1-based). Composes with P451 qualifiers: any qualifier level may hold a list; the most-specific level's list wins WHOLE (never per-seat merging across levels).

      **DISPATCH GUARD (2026-07-25, from two runs closed by hand):** do NOT re-dispatch P458 before P432's IMPLEMENTATION lands — only P432's design doc is on main (453bac4, review round ended unapproved); the schema bump, the polymorphic check-as-guard lowering, and native `SequenceKind::Match` do not exist in `modules/core`. run-5e31c1eb verified exactly this and correctly refused (no code written, no commits — its gate park was environmental: a re-created worktree with no `pnpm install`, not a code failure); its reviewer's factual corrections: `packages/cdk/src/sequence.ts` already implements `sequence.check` — the missing piece is P432's lowering, not the CDK surface. An EARLIER attempt (run-b5a65b9f) holds a real implementation commit — `de431a6` "CDK grammar-v2 and native-match phase (revised, unapproved)", unmerged on branch `ctx/run/wt-6b56bc3d1b31` — which necessarily invented its own schema support out of contract; owner adjudicates whether it is salvage material for the P432+P458 combined landing or gets discarded for a clean post-P432 run. The landed grammar SLICE (f38b7aa + 14828e9) is unaffected and stands.
      **Why:** the authoring surface grew idioms faster than rules — one real trait (implement-quick) mixes two prompt styles (`prompt.text` vs `prompt.template` + a redundant `input:` ref list), two id placements (positional vs in-object), two command forms (`cmd` string vs `argv` array), two body shapes (inline `then:` vs a named `sequence.linear` wrapper with a second id), and the deprecated PascalCase namespaces (`Tone.direct`) — and the conditional vocabulary stops at a 2-arm `when` with no match. The owner's criterion is explicit: judged on how the code READS, consistently, across the board.
      **Settled grammar (each rule owner-chosen in the 07-22/23 brainstorm):**
      (1) SHAPE — every constructor is `sequence.kind("id", { options })`: id positional first, everything else named. NO second "essence" positional (it broke on forEach/prompt — no one-size-fits-all). The ONLY closure is where a binding is introduced: `sequence.forEach("id", collection, (item) => ({ body: [...] }))` — item is a typed handle interpolatable in nested inputs; NO agent hoisting on forEach (agent stays per prompt step — owner call).
      (2) LEAF STEPS are isomorphic — an input drives, an output falls out: `prompt` = `{ agent, input: input.text`…`, include?, output }`; `command`/`check` = `{ input: input.command`…`, output }`. `input.text` interpolates `${refs}` and auto-derives declared inputs; `prompt.template` and the redundant `input:` ref lists are DELETED. `input.command` is a tagged template: literal segments shell-word-split at BUILD (no live shell, hermetic), interpolated refs become discrete argv elements (injection-proof — the git-commit -m slot case); raw `argv:` stays as the escape hatch; canonical stores argv either way.
      (3) `include:` — the ONE home for declared-but-not-interpolated dependencies (resources consulted via tools, P447 optional inputs — `include: [optional(slot.verdict)]`).
      (4) OUTPUT-AS-INSTRUCTION (the centerpiece): `output:` accepts slot handles (unchanged; single-or-array polymorphism STAYS) and instruction-outputs — `output.text`A work summary: what changed, …`` / `output.of(schema)`Your verdict …`` — which auto-declare the slot, render the what-to-produce INTO the compiled prompt, and render the slot's TYPE as a return-format instruction: one source of truth for prompt, slot, and validator; prose/schema drift becomes structurally impossible. Rendering happens at BUILD into canonical text (deterministic, digest-covered; the render template is version-gated — changing its wording is a canonical change, never a silent runtime shift). AUTO-SLOT-ID RULE (digests depend on it, stable forever): first anonymous output takes the step id, subsequent take `<step-id>-2`…; collision with a hand-declared slot id is a build error; binding an `output.text` to a const for downstream `${ref}` use keeps object identity — the id is assigned at build from the attaching step.
      (5) Conditional output-shaping ("if revise, end with Unresolved blockers") is INSTRUCTION text — it stays in `input.text`; `output.*` carries the contract, not every nuance (owner call).
      (6) CONDITIONALS — `sequence.branch("id", { check, success, failure })` replaces `when`/if/then/otherwise (alias window per house convention). `check:` is polymorphic BY DESIGN: a `condition.*` guard (compiles to today's canonical when/otherwise — NO schema change) OR a `sequence.check` gate step (owner: "I was referring to that sequence.check actually" — desugars to the check step + a branch on its pass output, making the P275 gate-then-decide pattern first-class; one run of the gate, routing recorded). Validation note: gate-only `check` would orphan pure-predicate branching (count guards, non-enum conditions) — polymorphism is the settled resolution. `success`/`failure` each optional, at least one required. `sequence.match("id", { value, body })` — `value` = a typed FieldRef scrutinee (P398 proxies, landed), `body` = an object keyed by the scrutinee's values + optional `else`; tsc-exhaustive via Record over the field's value union (a missing case is a compile error; `else` relaxes). v1 restricts the scrutinee to string-enum (`schema.oneOf`) fields — booleans/numbers route via `branch`. NATIVE canonical `kind = "match"` (single routing record in the ledger + runtime exhaustiveness + dead-arm detection in `check`) rides P432's schema bump — one bump, never two.
      (7) BODIES inline: `body: [...]` on loop/linear/for-each, `body: [[…],[…]]` branch arrays on parallel — nested sub-sequences AUTO-NAMED from the parent id (no second ids); `sequence.linear` survives only as the named-reusable-body form (its sketched `join` field dropped — join is a parallel concept).
      (8) `title` becomes optional, auto-derived from the id; vocabulary finishes P331/P412 — lowercase callable namespaces with PascalCase leaves (`tone.Direct`, `intent.avoid.ScopeCreep`), deprecated PascalCase namespace imports removed from family sources.
      **Watch:** this is an AUTHORING-surface break with canonical mostly UNCHANGED — branch and gate-branch desugar onto existing kinds; `match` is the one new canonical kind (coordinate with P432, single schema bump). Migrated traits are rewrites: digests move ⇒ owner trust re-approvals. SEQUENCING: author the P449 guardedProduction/commitTail kit and the P450 restructure IN this grammar so the family migrates once, not twice; P448's `sequence.flow` constants are consumed by loop options. Old constructors get a deprecation window OR a pre-launch clean break — owner call at build (nothing external consumes the CDK yet).
      **Done when:** implement-quick re-authored in the grammar reads in one voice end to end (zero template/linear-wrapper/cmd-vs-argv/title/PascalCase remnants) and builds green with the rendered output-instructions visible in its canonical; a `match` over a `oneOf` field red-lines a missing arm in tsc and `check` flags a dead arm; a gate-`branch` runs its check exactly once with the route recorded in the ledger; the auto-slot-id rule is pinned by a digest-stability fixture; the CDK reference documents the grammar with the before/after gallery from the brainstorm.
      **AMENDED 2026-07-23 (CDK-wide audit; census over all 46 trait sources — 112 deprecated-vocab uses vs 0 canonical, 91 `text:` vs 17 `prompt.template`, 40 redundant `input:` lists, 30 `sequence.linear` wrappers, 28 argv vs 3 cmd):**

      (10) `text:` is the ONE prompt field on `sequence.prompt` — the `prompt:` field dual (2 uses repo-wide, runtime-guarded against collision with `text:`) is deleted.
      (11) forEach ITEM-SLOT auto-id pinned (gap found in audit): the binding closure synthesizes the item slot as `<step-id>-item` — the callback parameter name is NEVER the id source (not rename-stable, not readable reliably); covered by the same digest-stability fixture as the output auto-id rule in (4).
      (12) ABSORBS P412 (bare-leaf vocabulary aliases, open since 07-21): `intent.ScopeCreep` lands as part of this migration; P412's collision rule (a leaf under two facets stays qualified-only, synthetic-collision test) carries verbatim — its contract text remains at Group 82.

Doctrine the help must reflect: bare `ctx traits` opens the P423 dashboard (usage line, not a help dump); every command takes `--json`; stdout quiet, debug under `~/.config/ctx/debug/`; hidden ≠ removed.

## Group 112: Test harness migration — byte_compare.rs → cargo tests (owner-approved 2026-07-23, "no script testing in repos — Rust tests calling binaries"; AMENDED 2026-07-24, owner: NO snapshot or fixture matching of any kind — behavioral assertions only)

Owner direction: the standalone-script harness is overkill and its two founding justifications have expired — the "buildable when the workspace is broken" independence is circular (`just test` builds the workspace anyway to produce the binary under test; cargo-compiled tests make the P290 corruption class BETTER covered, since the landing gate's `just test` now inherently compiles the harness), and the no-deps constraint was self-imposed by the rustc-standalone choice (the npm proofs hand-implement gzip/SHA-512/base64 that `flate2`/`sha2` dev-deps delete). The single file is also the repo's top merge-conflict magnet (five of six 2026-07-22 hand-merges interleaved at its shared anchors); per-suite files kill that class structurally.

- [ ] **P461** — Migrate the proof harness into `modules/cli/tests/` as plain cargo tests — behavioral assertions only, no snapshot or fixture matching (owner amendment 2026-07-24); delete `scripts/byte_compare.rs`. SECOND AMENDMENT 2026-07-24 (54706ca): the ENTIRE byte_compare battery is now disabled in `just test` (owner final call after wall-preflight-proof went red on main via d0e9169's intended behavior change and parked every merge) — the gate today is drift/embed checks + builds + `cargo test --workspace` ONLY. Migration through this phase is therefore the ONLY road back for each proof's protection; until a mode is migrated, its behavior is UNGUARDED — prioritize the safety-critical modes (concurrency/kill-boundaries, deep-merge receipt validation, park honesty) and re-derive expectations from CURRENT doctrine at migration time (d0e9169 made exhausted-continue legitimate for implement-quick; the old "revise never commits" wall expectation is stale as written — reconcile against P414's contract, don't copy the frozen assertion).
      **Why:** owner call — proper `cargo test` integration tests calling the real binary (`env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_ctx")`), IDE/test-runner/filtering integration, incremental compilation, and per-suite files that end the same-anchor conflict class. The original snapshot-golden half (insta + `cargo insta review`) is WITHDRAWN by the 2026-07-24 amendment — "for now I want NO fixture matching/testing": a migrated test asserts the specific facts that matter directly; it never compares output against a stored expected blob, in any format.
      **Where — scaffolding (stage 1, land BEFORE the Group 110 build wave):** `modules/cli/tests/support/` — the controlled-env command builder (scrubbed env, fake HOME, pinned locale, NO_COLOR — moved UNCHANGED from the script so byte-exactness claims survive), scratch-git-repo helper, and the fixture self-exec pattern (`current_exe()` + a fixture env var reproduces the script's self-as-fake-harness trick; tiny helper bins where cleaner). Dev-dependencies ONLY: `insta`, `serial_test`, `flate2`, `sha2` — runtime deps untouched (the no-new-deps house rule governs shipped code, not dev-deps). **Stop-the-bleeding rule active from the moment scaffolding lands: every NEW proof is written as a cargo test — nothing new touches byte_compare.rs** (binding on P449's kit proof, P460's merge-on-completion proof, and everything after). *(2026-07-24 amendment rider: the `insta` dev-dependency and the `#[ignore]`d demonstration golden test are DELETED in the next slice — no snapshot machinery remains anywhere; dev-deps reduce to `serial_test`, `flate2`, `sha2`.)*
      **Where — goldens (stage 2): DROPPED-FOR-NOW (owner, 2026-07-24 — "way too early and changing too much to do fixture testing").** Byte-exact CLI-output goldens were removed from the `just test` gate entirely (the `cases.tsv` run + `cli-golden-paths` commented out; the stage-1 insta demo `#[ignore]`d): while surfaces churn daily, every merge that touches output stales a golden, and nothing in the merge path regenerates them (P418's park: `traits help --json` drifted after rebase — third specimen of the generated-artifact class, see P463). Goldens + both harness modes stay on disk. Revisit as its own phase if surfaces ever stabilize enough to want it — nothing in P461's scope prescribes a vehicle for that future, and per the 2026-07-24 amendment P461 itself ships zero fixture-matching machinery.
      **Where — proof suites (stage 3, background/loop-friendly chore):** the ~20 existing suites migrate one file per suite (`proof_concurrency.rs`, `proof_npm.rs`, …), incrementally — script and cargo tests coexist in `just test` until parity, then `scripts/byte_compare.rs` is DELETED and the Justfile test recipe becomes cargo build + `cargo test`. Suites that today compare output against checked-in files (the stats goldens, the gp demo captures) migrate as structural assertions — parse the output and assert the facts that matter (exit codes, fields, presence, ordering, digests computed on both sides in the test) — never a stored expected blob. Timing-sensitive suites (conductor lease, SIGINT drain — the standing FLAKE-watch stage) run under `serial_test`/nextest groups, addressing that flake structurally. `scripts/audit_marketplace.rs` (same standalone-script species) is included in the sweep as the trailing item — owner may drop it from scope without affecting the rest.
      **Where — P420 gate amendment: EXECUTED EARLY (owner-ordered hotfix, 2026-07-23 evening — originally slated to ride stage 3):** the universal pre-landing gate now runs `just --list` + `just test` only; the `rustc scripts/byte_compare.rs` compile probe, its timeout const, its temp-binary machinery, the fixture stubs that existed solely to satisfy it (inventory items 1, 4), and the `merge --help` probe sentence (item 5, `traits-help.json` regenerated) are gone. Rationale sharpened by the incident that triggered the order: the probe hardcoded a repo-internal filename inside the merge machinery, so any tree that legitimately migrates or drops the script parks on a file-existence check — the gate must never name repo internals; `just test` compiling whatever the repo says is the whole contract. P290 protection preserved BY CONSTRUCTION (a corrupted harness still fails `just test`'s own compile step) and the gate is ~5 min faster.
      **Reference inventory (2026-07-23 grep — EVERY site the migration must clear, so nothing survives by omission):** (1) ~~`merge.rs:1216-1292` — the pre-landing gate's rustc compile probe~~ DONE (2026-07-23 hotfix — was the ONLY product-code invocation; modules/ is now byte_compare-free apart from comments). (2) Justfile `test` recipe — compile + all proof invocations. (3) Justfile `cli-golden-paths` — a SECOND compile site (`--golden-paths`); GP-1/GP-2 migrate like the other suites. (4) ~~fixture-repo stubs for the gate probe~~ DONE (removed with the probe, same hotfix). (5) ~~`cli.rs` merge help text~~ DONE (probe sentence dropped, `traits-help.json` regenerated; `traits-help.txt` short line was never affected). (6) Comment-only mentions (`distribution.rs:107`, `drive.rs:3437` fixture env-var note, plus the script's own compilation notes) — doc sweep. Done-when gains: a repo-wide grep for `byte_compare|byte-compare` returns zero hits outside historical docs/archive.
      **Watch:** FLAKE ledger — `proof_config::flagged_and_configured_drives_preserve_identical_ledger_bytes` (P418's equivalence test) failed twice then passed on an identical tree 2026-07-25 (full-gate run + first isolated rerun, then green; suspicion: a wall-clock field like elapsed-seconds crossing a second boundary between the two compared drives); stabilize it when the migration touches that suite — time-dependent fields must be pinned or excluded from the byte comparison, and its fix pattern applies to every future two-run-equivalence test. Also standing: no fixture matching anywhere (owner, 2026-07-24) — a test never compares output against a stored expected file or snapshot in any format; where byte-exactness IS the product claim (canonical encoding, digests), the test computes both sides itself and compares them in-process; the controlled env moves verbatim; provider-free stays absolute (no LLM in any test); parallel cargo tests must not share scratch dirs (per-test temp roots, as the script already does per-suite); CI/`just test` keeps one entry point throughout the migration; migration ordering matters — scaffolding FIRST so the Group 110 wave's new proofs never add to the file being deleted.
      **Done when:** stage 1 scaffolding landed and the new-tests-only rule recorded in the Justfile comment (done — its insta demonstration was subsequently `#[ignore]`d and is deleted under the 2026-07-24 amendment, along with the `insta` dev-dependency); every suite migrated with `just test` green throughout; `scripts/byte_compare.rs` and its Justfile compile step deleted; the landing gate no longer carries the rustc probe (done, 46fe5fb) and a deliberately-corrupted test file still fails `just test` (gate-compilation proof); a repo-wide grep finds no snapshot/golden assertion machinery in `modules/cli/tests/`; SIGINT-timing suites serialized.

## Group 113: Human output — every command speaks one house style (owner-ordered 2026-07-25; reference rendering below is the owner's own ctx.gate panel and is LOCKED)

Owner directive: default CLI output today is inconsistent and verbose to the point of uselessness — `doctor` "spills a shit-ton of text" nobody reads. The full detail dump is legitimate ONLY behind `--verbose`; `--json` stays byte-stable and untouched. Every command's DEFAULT human output becomes a compact, aligned panel in the style the owner already shipped for ctx.gate:

```
╭─ ctx.gate ─ checkpoint required
│   checkpoint 019f815e-df78-7232-8c50-cd805f06dfe9
│   status   pending
│   running  no command or action process is running yet
│   next     waiting for approval or denial in another terminal
│
│ Summary
│   approve  run command
│   why      default policy requires approval before this operation can continue
│
│ Approval commands
│   allow    ctx-gate checkpoint allow 019f815e-df78-7232-8c50-cd805f06dfe9
│   deny     ctx-gate checkpoint deny 019f815e-df78-7232-8c50-cd805f06dfe9
╰─ awaiting decision
```

Distilled rules (the doctrine P417 seeded, now concrete): (1) top border carries `product ─ headline` — what this is and the one-line verdict; (2) body is a `│` gutter with two-space-indented, column-aligned label/value rows — labels lowercase and DIM, values normal weight; (3) short titled sections inside the frame, separated by a bare gutter line; (4) a `next`/action row tells the user the exact command or step that follows — evidence path + next action, never a wall of prose; (5) bottom border closes with the current state in one or two words; (6) palette is the standing two-tone doctrine — named-ANSI foreground + faint only, green pass / red blocked, zero truecolor, zero background colors; (7) non-TTY/piped output degrades to the same content as plain indented text (no box glyphs, no ANSI); (8) `--json` output is never altered by any of this. ABSORBS P417 (Group 106) — that phase's headline/evidence/next-action doctrine lands here as rules 1, 4, and 5 instead of as its own pass.

- [x] **P465** — The output kit: one panel renderer, one verbosity gate, zero per-command drift.
      **Why:** consistency is impossible while every command hand-rolls its printing — the style must live in exactly one place, and the compact-by-default / full-under-`--verbose` split must be a shared primitive, not a per-command convention.
      **Where:** a presentation module in `modules/cli` (beside the existing line/tone renderer, NOT ratatui — this is scrollback output): panel builder (title headline, sections, aligned label/value rows, next-action row, closing state), width-aware label-column alignment, the TTY/pipe degrade path through the existing `stdout_supports_color` gating, and a command-level verbosity gate (`default` renders the panel, `--verbose` appends the full detail body, `--json` bypasses the kit entirely and is byte-identical to today). Tone mapping reuses the existing `Tone` enum — no new color machinery.
      **Watch:** two-tone doctrine is law (named-ANSI fg + faint only; pass green, blocked/critical red; labels dim); the kit renders content it is GIVEN — it never fetches or computes domain facts; `--json` byte-stability is absolute; no fixture tests (behavioral assertions per the P461 amendment — tests assert structure by parsing the output, never compare stored blobs).
      **Done when:** the kit renders the ctx.gate reference layout from structured input; one command (`doctor`, via P466) ships on it end to end; a piped invocation emits the plain degrade; `--json` for that command is proven byte-identical to pre-kit output by computing both in-process.
- [x] **P466** — `doctor` on the kit: the named offender becomes the showcase.
      **Why:** owner: doctor's default output is useless noise. It is also the command with the richest finding structure — categories, severities, remediations — so it is the right proof that the panel style scales beyond ctx.gate's checkpoint card.
      **Where:** doctor's default output becomes one panel per area (or one panel with sections): counts up front (`checks 14 · passed 11 · warnings 2 · critical 1`), only ACTIONABLE findings listed — each as a label row with its one-line remediation as the `next` column; healthy checks collapse to the summary count line. The full per-check narrative moves behind `--verbose` unchanged; `--json` unchanged; exit codes unchanged.
      **Done when:** default `doctor` on a healthy repo fits in one terminal screen; on a broken repo every printed row names its fix; `--verbose` restores today's full detail; `--json` byte-identical, proven in-process.
- [x] **P467** — All-commands sweep + the drift gate: the style becomes structural, not aspirational.
      **Where:** migrate every visible command's default human output onto the kit (the P453 inventory: init, new, list, build, check, diff, explain, generate, refine, critique, export, host, run, merge, activate, review, trust, vendor, install, remove, update, outdated, info, import, doctor, cache) — one commit per command or small cluster so review stays honest; commands whose output is already a single line stay a single line (the kit has a one-row form — a panel is not mandatory for trivial output, consistency of vocabulary and tone is). Add the drift gate: a structural test that every visible command's default-output path routes through the kit (assert at the code/registration level — e.g. the command table carries its renderer — NOT by comparing rendered output to fixtures).
      **Watch:** `merge`'s park output feeds Group 114's merges screen — coordinate wording once, use it in both; `--json` surfaces are release API — byte-stability proven per command in-process; no golden files anywhere.
      **Done when:** every visible command's default output is kit-rendered or explicitly exempted in a recorded list with a reason; the drift gate fails when a new command bypasses the kit; spot-audit of five commands by the owner reads as one product.
- [x] **P546** — `check` counts 23 warnings and prints 7: rendering must be total (measured 2026-07-27 on `implement-quick`).
      **Closure note (2026-07-28):** landed in `6bf0299`, ancestor of this HEAD; this round only verified and closed the row, no new implementation. `Section` (`check.rs:71`) is now a closed, compile-time enum — 20 pre-existing sections plus the six former orphans homed as: `cdk-authoring` (new, conditional with the CDK block), `run-config` (new), `extensions` (new), `metadata` (new), `procedure`→`sequence` (relabel), `frontmatter`→`render-readiness` (relabel). `build_grouped_gates` (`report_check.rs:2669`) renders any warning whose section matches no registered display section under its own `unmatched` bucket instead of dropping it — total rendering is structural, not test-enforced. Count reconciliation: live on `implement-quick` today, `--json` warnings = 23, verbose rendered `[section]` bullets = 23, compact summary row = 23 — full parity, at every verbosity, with `passed: true` unchanged. The phase's original 23→7 drop is gone; the draft's intermediate 23→21 concern was not reproduced in this tree (current breakdown: cdk-authoring 12, sequence 3, run-config 1, resources 4, model-view 2, render-readiness 1 = 23, matching the original 23 exactly once cdk-authoring/sequence's +1/-1 relabel drift is accounted for). No persisted-artifact deserialization path for `CheckReport`/`CheckWarning` exists (grepped every construction site — all in-process, none read back from disk), so the `String`→`Section` type change carries no legacy-decode risk. `packages/config/dist/generated.d.ts{,.map}` remain dirty in the working tree from an unrelated prior change (adds `StoryLevel`/`RunTable.story`, P521-era, nothing to do with `Section`/`CheckWarning`) — `ctx traits sdk-generate --check` exits 0 (drift-clean) against the current source, confirming this dirt is stale sibling-frame noise, not owed by P546; left untouched. Gates: `cargo build`, full `cargo test` (all green, including the three `report_check` behavioral tests), and `sdk-generate --check` all pass in this tree (`CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target`). `--json` label movement (`procedure`→`sequence`, `frontmatter`→`render-readiness`) is intentional per the phase contract, called out here as the release-API change it is.
      **Why:** the summary row reports `report.warnings.len()` — every warning (`report_check.rs:2128`) — while the detail renderer emits a warning only INSIDE the section whose name it matches: `for warning in report.warnings.iter().filter(|w| w.section == section.name)` (`:2641`). A warning whose `section` string names no rendered section therefore has nowhere to print and is silently dropped. **`--verbose` does not help**: it lifts `FLAG_LIMIT` (the "… N more" tail cap, `:2281`) and never touches the filter, which is why the owner saw 23 → 7 at both verbosities. Measured on `implement-quick` via `check --json`: `cdk-authoring` 11 HIDDEN, `procedure` 4 HIDDEN, `run-config` 1 HIDDEN, against `resources` 4, `model-view` 2, `render-readiness` 1 shown — **16 of 23 invisible**. The two vocabularies were never reconciled: 21 section names are registered by `with_section`, 13 distinct `section` labels are attached to warnings, and only 7 of the 13 exist as sections — the orphans are `cdk-authoring`, `extensions`, `frontmatter`, `metadata`, `procedure`, `run-config`.
      **Why — what is lost is the part worth reading.** `cdk-authoring` alone is 11 of the 23: authoring feedback on the TypeScript source, the category an author would actually act on. `procedure` has the most warning-construction sites of any label in the tree. What survives is the resource/render set. So the report is not merely incomplete, it is biased toward the advisories that matter least, while the header asserts a number that implies otherwise — a P489-class silent truncation on a command whose entire job is to tell the truth about a trait.
      **Where:** rendering becomes TOTAL — every warning reaches the output. Matched warnings keep rendering under their section (that grouping is good and stays); unmatched ones render under an explicit bucket rather than vanishing, so an unreconciled label degrades to "shown in the wrong place", never to "not shown". Then close the class structurally, in the P467 gate's spirit: a compile-time or test-time check that the `section` label of every constructed `CheckWarning` is a member of the section vocabulary — a label naming no section FAILS, rather than silently disappearing from a report. Fix the six orphans by giving them a real home (a section, or a corrected label), deciding per label which is right rather than mass-renaming.
      **Watch:** do NOT "fix" this by only adding the six missing sections — that leaves the same string coupling one typo away from the next silent drop, which is the actual defect. The same fragility sits next door at `:2620`, where audit findings render behind `section.name.contains("hidden-content")` against a section registered as `hidden-content-audit`; it matches today by luck of a substring and belongs in the same reconciliation. Keep `--verbose`'s meaning intact: it lifts the display cap, and it must NOT become the flag that decides whether a warning exists at all. `--json` already carries all 23 and is the reference for what the human render owes.
      **Done when:** the count in the summary row and the number of warnings a reader can actually see are equal, at every verbosity; a `CheckWarning` carrying an unknown `section` fails a check rather than disappearing; `ctx traits check implement-quick` accounts for all 23; and the six orphaned labels each have a stated home.

## Group 114: Dashboard TUI v2 — rich screens (owner-ordered 2026-07-25; layout references: yozefu, openapi-tui — both ratatui)

Owner directive, distilled from the six asks: the landed Group 105 dashboard (P422/P423/P454/P455) proved the substrate; v2 makes every screen a rich master-detail surface. Standing law carries over: ratatui + crossterm stay the approved exception for interactive alt-screen surfaces only; two-tone palette doctrine (named-ANSI fg + faint, no backgrounds, no truecolor, no theme detection); `--json`, line-mode, and `--progress stream` are untouched by everything here.

- [x] **P468** — TUI kit v2: layout, scroll, modals, and the single-keypress action model.
      **Why:** every current screen shares the same defects — no scrolling (content silently overflows past the bottom), no modal input (reasons go through $EDITOR temp files), and inconsistent keys. These are kit problems; fixing them per-screen would build the drift Group 113 is killing on the CLI side.
      **Where:** shared widgets in the existing TUI module: (1) scrollable list + scrollable viewport used by EVERY list and detail pane — nothing renders outside its rect, scroll position indicated (`12/47`), keyboard scroll (arrows/j-k/PgUp-PgDn); (2) master-detail split — list left, live preview right, focus toggling, the P454 width-bound rules generalized; (3) modal dialog widget: confirm (yes/no) and text-input (single + multi-line) variants, drawn over the screen, focus-trapped, esc cancels; (4) the action model: every action is ONE keypress that opens its confirmation or input modal — no press-twice patterns, no double-tap-to-confirm anywhere; (5) screen chrome: title bar, status footer with contextual keymap hints, consistent keys across screens (enter = open, esc/q = back one level, ctrl-c at top level = exit WITH confirmation modal); (6) $EDITOR round-trip primitive generalized from the P423 suspend machinery — leave alt-screen, run editor, restore the SAME screen, selection, and scroll position.
      **Watch:** palette doctrine absolute (no backgrounds — selection is bold/reverse-video-free: use the existing accent conventions); quitting a pane never kills a run (P422's law); panic/exit restore paths already landed (89c795b) must keep passing; no fixture tests — interaction tests assert state machines, not rendered frames.
      **Done when:** a demo screen exercises list scroll, master-detail focus, both modal variants, and the editor round-trip; every existing screen compiles against the kit with zero behavior change (adoption is the per-screen phases below); keymap footer renders on all screens.
- [x] **P469** — Sessions screen: see everything, act on anything, one keypress each.
      **Why:** owner ask (a): the main dashboard must show active sessions with a LIVE preview and support enter/resume/kill/delete without leaving the TUI.
      **Where:** master-detail on the kit: left = session list (active first, then recent; state, phase, elapsed, tokens per row), right = live preview of the selected session (its narration/state stream, read-only, updating in place). Actions: ENTER attaches the full session live view (identical surface to `--progress tui`, back with esc/q returns to the list); RESUME on an `awaiting-agent-output` ledger launches the standard drive path with the live view attached; KILL requests cooperative stop through the landed control-socket channel (P423), with the modal naming what stop means (finish current frame, then park) and a hard-stop escalation note; DELETE removes a terminal session's ledger (and offers worktree+branch cleanup when they still exist) behind a confirm modal that names every artifact it will remove. Exit from the dashboard: ctrl-c or q, always through the confirm modal, never instant.
      **Watch:** DELETE is the only destructive verb — its modal lists exact paths/refs before acting and refuses on ACTIVE sessions; RESUME must reuse the ledger's worktree provenance (P376's law); KILL is cooperative-first — no SIGKILL of harness children from the UI.
      **Done when:** the six verbs (preview, enter, back, resume, kill, delete) work against live fixture runs; killing during a silent frame stops at the frame boundary with the park recorded; nothing in the screen can trigger an action without its modal.
- [x] **P470** — Session live view v2: the story model — history compresses, the present is verbatim.
      **Why:** owner ask (c): done steps should read as one narrated summary line each, ONLY the current step shows its full message flow, and the right pane must stop cutting off content the terminal has room for.
      **Where:** the live view renders the run as a story: every COMPLETED step collapses to its single P455 finished-step summary line (already narrated and landed — this consumes it structurally); the CURRENT step owns the remaining vertical space and shows its full message/thinking stream, scrollable within its viewport; the P454 sidebar width/row caps are RELAXED to size-aware bounds — use what the terminal gives (the cap becomes "never starve the tree below its floor", not a fixed 60-col ceiling or 2-row limit); when history + current exceed the pane, history scrolls as a list (kit widget), the current step stays pinned.
      **Watch:** P454/P455 are LANDED — this is their successor, never an amendment; line-mode and stream progress untouched; narration tokens stay accounted (P445 evidence).
      **Done when:** a run with 10+ finished steps shows 10 one-liners + a full current-step stream; resizing reflows without truncation artifacts; nothing is unreachable by scroll.
- [x] **P471** — Traits screen: preview, approve with reasons, edit source, come back to the same place.
      **Why:** owner ask (b): trait review today drops into $EDITOR temp files for reasons and loses your place; approval flows must be first-class UI.
      **Where:** master-detail: left = trait list (id, version, status, trust state), right = live preview of the selected trait (summary, procedure shape, digest/trust facts, source excerpt). Actions: APPROVE and DENY open the kit's text-input modal for the reason — the reason lands exactly where today's temp-file flow put it, the $EDITOR temp-file path for reasons is deleted; EDIT SOURCE uses the kit's editor round-trip on the trait's source files and returns to the SAME list position and preview; post-edit, the preview refreshes (rebuild/check surface errors inline in the preview pane, not a crash).
      **Watch:** trust semantics unchanged — this is presentation over the existing approve/deny machinery; digest movements still demand the standing re-approval flow; edit never writes generated artifacts (source only — regeneration stays explicit).
      **Done when:** approve-with-reason round-trips to the trust store without leaving the TUI; edit → save → return lands on the same row with a refreshed preview; a check-failing edit shows the error in-pane.
- [x] **P472** — Merges screen: what happened, in words a tired human parses at 2am.
      **Why:** owner ask (d): the merge surface is cryptic. This week's live sessions repeatedly needed hand-translation of park reasons ("stale-base-overlap", "gate=byte-compare-compile", "current worktree is on clean branch main") — the screen should do that translation.
      **Where:** list of mergeable/parked/landed runs (left); detail (right) tells the story in plain language: what phase, what the run produced (commits? docs only? nothing), which stage it reached, and — for parks — WHY in one sentence plus the exact next action ("tests failed in this run's tree: <failing step>; retry after X" / "this run's folder is on the wrong branch; recreate it with …"). Gate evidence renders as labeled rows (command, result, duration), not raw argv dumps. Actions: retry merge (confirm modal), open the run's worktree path (copy/print), drop-from-queue. Wording is written ONCE with P467's merge output — CLI park text and this screen say the same thing.
      **Watch:** read the park taxonomy from this week's board history (gate red, branch mismatch, worktree missing, harvest conflict, receipt invalid, lock timeout) — every reason string in the codebase gets a human sentence + next action, and a fallback rendering for unknown reasons; no new merge behavior, presentation only.
      **Done when:** each of the six park classes above renders as a sentence + next action; a raw reason never reaches the screen untranslated; retry works from the screen.
- [x] **P473** — Trust screen: what moved, why it matters, approve with your reasons attached.
      **Why:** owner ask (e): the trust surface is cryptic, and block-approval needs rich input instead of digest-pasting ceremony.
      **Where:** list = packages/traits with trust state (verified / unreviewed / MOVED since approval), detail = what changed in plain words (which digest moved, when, which run moved it, what approving means); actions: APPROVE (single), APPROVE BLOCK (multi-select or family-level — one modal, one reason, applied to the set, P396-family aware), DENY — all through the kit's input modal; never display-then-ask-to-retype a digest (P419's law, consumed here).
      **Watch:** trust is a security boundary — the modal states exactly what will be trusted before confirm; family/block approval must show every member it covers; no auto-approval affordances anywhere.
      **Done when:** the standing re-approval chore (family digests moved → re-approve five packages) is one block-approve with one reason in under ten seconds; every approval records its reason in the trust store; deny leaves state untouched and says so.
- [x] **P474** — The TUI is the default: `--progress tui` becomes what a human gets without asking (owner-ordered 2026-07-25, rider on this group's effort).
      **Why:** the live view is the product's face, and after P468/P470 it is strictly better than the status line stream for a person at a terminal — yet today both `run` and `drive` default to `--progress status` (cli.rs:996/:1910) and the TUI is opt-in. Interactive users should get the good surface by default; scripts and CI must never accidentally get an alt-screen.
      **Where:** the default progress mode for `ctx traits run` and `ctx traits drive` resolves at startup: stdout is a real TTY (the existing `stdout_supports_color`-family detection — no new detection machinery, no env sniffing beyond TTY-ness) ⇒ `tui`; otherwise ⇒ `status`, exactly today's behavior. Explicit `--progress <mode>` always wins in both directions; `--no-tui` keeps winning over everything TUI; `--json` output is untouched by definition. Repo Justfile recipes that want line output in a terminal (batch loops whose transcript the owner reads back) gain an explicit `--progress stream`/`status` argument — the flip must not silently turn batch transcripts into alt-screen sessions.
      **Watch:** headless/driven contexts are already non-TTY (harness frames, cron, CI) and must be proven to keep the status default — a behavioral test asserts the resolved mode for tty=false; the alt-screen lifecycle guarantees (P422's "quitting the pane never kills the run", 89c795b's restore paths) are the safety this flip leans on and must stay green; line-mode output itself changes not one byte.
      **Done when:** an interactive `ctx traits run --worktree <trait>` opens the live view with no flags; the same command piped to a file produces today's status lines; every Justfile batch recipe states its progress mode explicitly; `--no-tui` and explicit `--progress` behave exactly as documented; help text for the flag documents the TTY-conditional default.

## Group 115: Driven-frame write confinement — mechanical, no prompt magic (owner-ordered 2026-07-24 after P477 workers edited the invocation repo's config from inside run worktrees; mechanisms researched + live-verified 2026-07-24)

**Per-harness confinement capability (settled 2026-07-25 — determines what P478 can deliver where):** claude-code = CONFIG-ONLY (deny rules survive `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, plus its OWN built-in OS sandbox — Seatbelt/bubblewrap — covering Bash and all children, with a native linked-worktree `.git` carve-out); codex = CONFIG-ONLY and arguably the best native story (`sandbox_mode = "workspace-write"` is OS-enforced by codex itself, plus deny-only hooks); opencode = CONFIG-ONLY at the harness level (`external_directory`/`edit` denies via the env injection we already use, verified to survive `--auto`) but NO OS layer exists, so interpreter one-liners slip through; pi = NOTHING in core by design (no permission system), though our pi extension can block tool calls at the `tool_call` event — the same static-inspection class as opencode. So P478 ships across all four using each harness's own mechanisms; **pi stayed genuinely unconfined (extension-level denies only) until the OS-level wrapper — the owner names this future module `ctx-sandbox` — landed as P480 (macOS only; the same gap persists on Linux until the queued landlock follow-up lands, named at the end of this group).** P479's tripwire is what covers every residual hole in the meantime by making an escape a loud, attributable, un-landable park.

Incident basis: three worker frames across two P477 runs (wt-b97eb24f2049, wt-0bb162823239) edited the MAIN checkout's `.ctx/config.toml` by absolute path. Worktree cwd isolation worked; write confinement does not exist — claude-code `--dangerously-skip-permissions` auto-approves everything except explicit deny rules (we inject none), and out-of-tree writes are invisible to every receipt (the content fingerprint covers only the worktree). Verified mechanics: claude-code deny rules and its OS sandbox hold under bypassPermissions (binary 2.1.218: "auto-approves every tool call (except explicit deny rules)"); opencode explicit denies hold under `--auto` (docs + our task-deny experience); macOS `sandbox-exec` write-confinement live-tested working on Darwin 25.2.

- [x] **P478** — Harness-native write confinement, injected per spawn: denies that survive bypass flags.
      **Why:** both harnesses ship config-only deny mechanisms that hold under our own bypass flags — the incident class is closable today with zero prompt text, if the dispatcher generates the confinement config per spawn.
      **Where:** worktree spawns get generated confinement injected beside the existing env-overlay machinery: (a) opencode — extend the OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT injection (or the additionally-honored OPENCODE_PERMISSION env) with `"external_directory":{"*":"deny"}` (gates every tool touching paths outside the start dir; seeds have made out-of-tree reads unnecessary since P314) plus a `permission.edit` path-map deny for the invocation repo (last-matching-rule-wins semantics; `edit` covers edit/write/patch); (b) claude-code — inject `--settings '<json>'` carrying `permissions.deny` `Edit(...)` rules (syntax: `//`-prefix anchors at filesystem root; `Edit` covers Edit/Write/NotebookEdit; deny matches symlink targets too) enumerating the invocation repo's top-level entries (`Edit(//<main>/modules/**)`, `Edit(//<main>/.ctx/config.toml)`, ... — a broad `//<main>/**` is IMPOSSIBLE while worktrees nest inside the repo, and a single-segment `//<main>/*` deny DESCENDS and would catch `.ctx` — enumerate, never glob the root) plus `"sandbox": {"enabled": true}` (OS-enforces Bash + all children to cwd+TMPDIR; the docs' native carve-out lets a linked worktree still write the main repo's shared `.git` minus `hooks/` and `config` — exactly our topology) — the sandbox closes the `bash -c 'echo > /abs/path'` class that Edit-rules cannot see. All paths realpath'd at generation time.
      **Watch:** the `/path` settings-relative anchor is a footgun — `//` absolute anchors only; claude-code Edit/Write to out-of-tree paths NOT in the enumeration still auto-approve under bypass (enumeration gap — P479 stays the backstop); Linux sandbox needs bubblewrap+socat — warn-don't-fail in v1 (no `failIfUnavailable`); merger/narrator worktree spawns get the same injection; compose with (don't replace) `--disallowedTools Agent`.
      **Done when:** fixture drives on both harnesses prove: a frame editing its own worktree lands normally; a scripted out-of-tree absolute-path Edit is DENIED at the harness with a typed rejection visible in the stream; a bash redirect outside the worktree fails under the claude-code sandbox; linked-worktree `git commit` still works; the generated deny/permission JSON appears in the debug trace of every worktree spawn.
      **Status (2026-07-25):** the generator, both renderers (claude-code, opencode), injection at every worktree/merger/narrator spawn seam, and the config surface landed in `0b6dfae`/`aed0c5a` — this row's own scope is fully coded. This run closed the one remaining coverage gap that was cheap and verifiable without guessing: opencode's `external_directory` payload now allows the plan's carve-outs (confirmed live against an installed opencode that the map form is accepted) instead of blanket-denying them, matching claude-code's `additionalDirectories` parity. The codex renderer named in the group preamble (`:1508`) is parked `needs-human` — codex is not installed on this implementing machine, and the preamble's guard against guessed `--config` syntax applies. The **Done when** live-harness fixture gate is itself `needs-human` — driving paid harness runs is the owner's, not the loop's; see the work summary for the exact command list and expected observations.

- [ ] **P479** — Out-of-tree mutation tripwire: detect, attribute, park.
      **Why:** enforcement has named holes (unlisted paths under bypass; interpreter one-liners on opencode, which has no OS sandbox layer). The invocation repo mutating mid-run must become a loud, attributable, typed event — the P477 incident was found by accident, a config-decode failure later.
      **Where:** around each worktree frame the dispatcher snapshots the invocation repo: `git -C <main> status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all` (excluding `.ctx/worktrees/`) + digests of a sentinel set (the resolved config-layer files: `.ctx/config.toml`, user-global config, harness settings files; config-extendable). Post-frame re-diff costs milliseconds. On mutation: typed ledger evidence (`out-of-tree-mutation`: offending paths, frame id) + policy `warn | park` (default park for `--worktree` runs — fail-closed, receipts doctrine). Surfaced in the park report, `run show`, and doctor.
      **Watch:** gitignored in-repo files are covered by the sentinel set, not git-status; never diff the worktrees subtree (legitimate write surface); attribution is frame-level, not process-level — the evidence wording must not overclaim WHO wrote (no PID attribution without a daemon; we ship none); snapshot/diff at frame boundaries only.
      **Done when:** a fixture frame touching an invocation-repo file parks with the typed report naming path + frame; policy `warn` downgrades to evidence + stderr warning; the sentinel provably covers the exact file the P477 incident hit; clean-run overhead is two snapshots.

- [ ] **P480** — OS-level spawn sandbox: the spawner owns confinement, harness-agnostic.
      **Why:** harness-native mechanisms are per-harness goodwill; the spawner wrapping every harness spawn covers current and future harnesses identically — including opencode's missing OS layer.
      **Where:** macOS: `sandbox-exec` with a generated SBPL profile — `(allow default)` + `(deny file-write*)` + allow-list: worktree realpath, `/private/tmp` + `/private/var/folders` (TMPDIR lives there — mktemp breaks otherwise), harness state dirs (`~/.claude`*, `~/.config/claude`, opencode state/cache), and the main repo's `.git` (a linked worktree's `.git` is a FILE pointing at `<main>/.git/worktrees/<id>`, and git also writes shared `objects/`, `refs/`, `packed-refs` — allow `<main>/.git`, then re-deny `hooks/` and `config`; later SBPL rules win). Live-verified working on Darwin 25.2 despite formal deprecation (Anthropic's own sandbox-runtime and Chromium share the dependency). Linux: `landlock` crate 0.4.5 (kernel-maintainer-published) via pre_exec, require ABI≥2 (rename/link governed), degrade loudly; bubblewrap fallback. All profile paths realpath'd — Seatbelt matches post-symlink-resolution (`/tmp` vs `/private/tmp` silently no-ops otherwise).
      **Watch:** network stays open in this profile (exfiltration is a separate decision — do not claim otherwise); keep P479 active under the sandbox; DESIGN LEVER for the owner: relocating run worktrees OUTSIDE the repo makes every deny/profile trivially expressible (`deny <main>/**`, no nesting exception) — that is a substrate migration, by-hand per the playbook, never loop-run.
      **Done when:** a macOS worktree run completes end to end under the profile (build, commit, harness state writes); an injected absolute-path write dies with EPERM and P479 attributes the frame; Linux proves the landlock path or downgrades with a named warning.
      **Status (2026-07-25):** macOS `sandbox-exec` path shipped — `SpawnSandbox`/`render_macos_sandbox_profile` in `modules/io/src/confinement.rs`, generated inside `resolve_worktree_spawn` from the existing `ConfinementPlan` (no second carve-out discovery), applied at both spawn seams in `modules/io/src/harness.rs` (`apply_exec_dir`'s sibling `sandboxed_argv`) and threaded through every cold/warm/narrator/merger call site in `drive.rs`, `harness_stream.rs`, and `merge.rs`; `[worktree.confinement] sandbox` now gates this layer too (§4.4 of the draft), `allow`'s `~`/`$HOME` expansion fixed (§4.5), and `confinement_unsupported_capability`'s wording split on whether the OS layer covered the gap (§4.7). Unit + live (`#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]`) Seatbelt tests added to `confinement.rs`'s existing test module — no new fixture harness, and the live test now probes for an already-enclosing sandbox and skips rather than false-failing when the gate itself runs nested. Reviewer's first pass found nested Seatbelt double-application: claude-code's own `sandbox.enabled` and the P480 wrapper were both being turned on for the same spawn by the one `sandbox` flag, which macOS's Seatbelt provably refuses (`sandbox_apply: Operation not permitted`, verified live). Fixed: `render_claude_code_settings` now takes whether the P480 wrapper applied to this spawn and renders `sandbox.enabled = plan.sandbox && !spawn_sandbox_applied` — at most one Seatbelt layer per spawn, with claude-code's own layer unchanged wherever the wrapper does not apply. Free answer to one of §5.3's open live questions, no paid call needed: `sandbox-exec -p <profile> claude --version` returns `2.1.220` under a P480-shaped profile, confirming a bare `argv[0]` resolves through `PATH` under the wrapper. The row's own **Done when** end-to-end live-harness proof (`git commit` inside the worktree, both harness kinds, the primary claude-code seat actually starting under the now-single-layer profile) is `needs-human`, same standing reason as P478's fixture gate: driving paid harness runs is the owner's, not the loop's. Linux `landlock` is downgraded per this row's own admitted branch — see the queued follow-up row immediately below; not attempted on this (Darwin) implementing machine, per the same guard P478's codex renderer already set.

- [ ] **P480a** — Linux spawn sandbox: `landlock` (ABI ≥ 2) via `pre_exec`, bubblewrap fallback.
      **Why:** P480 shipped the macOS half only — the implementing machine is Darwin, and Linux-gated `unsafe pre_exec` syscall code cannot be compiled, run, or falsified here; a loud "no OS layer on Linux" capability report (already shipped, `spawn_sandbox_unsupported_capability`) is more honest than untested enforcement code that might silently fail open.
      **Where:** `modules/io/src/confinement.rs`, parallel to `render_macos_sandbox_profile`/`SpawnSandbox`: a `landlock` crate 0.4.5 (kernel-maintainer-published) ruleset built from the same `ConfinementPlan.additional_directories`, applied via `std::os::unix::process::CommandExt::pre_exec` before `exec`, requiring ABI ≥ 2 (rename/link governed) so the sandbox doesn't silently under-enforce on an older kernel; on an ABI below 2, or if the `landlock` syscall is unavailable (older kernel, container without the feature), downgrade to a bubblewrap (`bwrap`) argv-prefix fallback mirroring `sandbox-exec`'s delivery, or to `spawn_sandbox_unsupported_capability` if neither is available — never silent.
      **Watch:** `pre_exec` runs in the forked child between `fork` and `exec` — async-signal-safety rules apply, keep it to the landlock syscalls only; a new `landlock` dependency needs adding to `modules/io/Cargo.toml` (deliberately deferred out of P480 itself, §"Files to touch" of the draft); this needs a Linux machine in the loop to validate — cannot be authored and self-verified on this (Darwin) implementing machine.
      **Done when:** on a Linux host with landlock ABI ≥ 2, the same live-boundary proof P480's macOS test performs (worktree write succeeds, out-of-worktree write fails, carve-out write succeeds) passes under `pre_exec`; on an unsupported kernel/container, the bubblewrap fallback or the named capability report fires instead — never a silent unconfined spawn.

## Group 116: CDK strong typing — the emission boundary stops erasing types (owner-ordered 2026-07-24: "strongly typed CDK, no excuses"; audit 2026-07-24: 168 `unknown`s, ~232 `as`-casts, zero `any`, tsconfig already maximally strict — the weakness is designed-in, not config)

Root finding: the CDK typechecks its INPUTS and erases everything at the OUTPUT boundary — every declaration funnels through `Meta.declaration: JsonObject`, `compact(): JsonObject`, and sequence.ts:736's `canonical: Record<string, unknown>`, exactly where the Rust side is `deny_unknown_fields`-precise. Package is 0.1.0-alpha.0: breaking changes are cheap now, maximal later. Existing strength to preserve, not regress: SequenceFields' 10-way discriminated union, schema template spot-inference, prompt.template placeholder extraction, table() tuple checking, ValidSlug.

- [x] **P481** — Canonical document model, generated from Rust: the emission boundary gets types.
      **Why:** highest-leverage single fix — `toDraftJson(): JsonObject`, `SynthPlan.draft: JsonObject`, `Meta.declaration: JsonObject` are why the test suite hand-re-types drafts at ~20 call sites and why internal plumbing casts everywhere. Every Rust canonical struct already derives schemars `JsonSchema`, and `ctx traits sdk-generate` (modules/cli/src/app/sdk_generate.rs) already generates vocabulary TS with drift-checking — it just never emits the document model.
      **Where:** extend sdk-generate to emit typed canonical interfaces into generated.ts: CanonicalTrait, CanonicalSequenceItem (mirror of the 30-field struct, procedure/model.rs:758), GuardExpr/GuardPredicate (condition.rs:25/72), Schema/Slot/Port/Agent/Resource/Prompt/Condition/Procedure, OutputSink, SequenceInput, FailureTarget, JoinPolicy, activation/composition/relations/extensions. Then retype: `Meta.declaration: DeclarationOf<DeclKind>` (mapped type), `toDraftJson(): CanonicalTraitDraft` (+ WithSourceMap/WithDiagnostics/synth), sequence.ts `canonical: CanonicalSequenceItem`. Emitter hand-rolled in sdk_generate's existing style; ts-rs/typeshare are NOT deps and stay out.
      **Watch:** additive for consumers (return-type narrowing; CanonicalTraitDraft stays structurally JsonObject-compatible); `sdk-generate --check` must gate the new emission; builtin-traits-drift stays green.
      **Done when:** tsc catches a misspelled canonical key in a test with NO cast; the suite's hand-written draft-shape casts are deleted (`toDraftJson(...) as {` count hits zero); `--check` fails on Rust-struct drift against the emitted model.

- [x] **P482** — `TraitFields` stops taking `unknown`: every declaration field gets its handle type.
      **Why:** the flagship `trait()` builder accepts `unknown` for 21 consecutive fields (trait.ts:130-156) — the API's front door typechecks nothing; `activation`/`composition`/`relations`/`extensions` have NO builder at all and are authored as raw `JsonValue`.
      **Where:** each field takes its handle union (`agent?: AgentHandle | readonly AgentHandle[]`, `sequence?: SequenceHandle | SequenceLinearHandle | readonly (...)[]`, ...); activation/composition/relations/extensions/scenario/eval take the P481 canonical types; the raw-JSON escape remains EXACTLY one field — the existing `unsafeJson`. Meta's side-channel `unknown`s (unionMembers, family.topology/leaves, inlineBranchArms — typed loosely only to dodge import cycles) get real types via a type-only leaf module, killing the recover-casts in variant.ts/schema.ts/sequence.ts.
      **Watch:** breaking-by-intent (alpha; owner's call); compile errors must name the expected builder, not just "not assignable to never".
      **Done when:** a raw object literal where a handle belongs is a compile error; `unsafeJson` is the only untyped entry and documented as such; zero `unknown` remains in TraitFields; wall/e2e trait sources build with mechanical fixes only.

- [x] **P483** — Guards are handles, not JsonObjects.
      **Why:** every `condition.*` constructor returns bare `JsonObject` (~30 sites) and `GuardValue`'s `JsonObject` arm means ANY object literal typechecks as a guard — a misspelled predicate ships silently into a `deny_unknown_fields` Rust decode or, worse, a silently-inert guard.
      **Where:** branded `GuardHandle` (backed by the generated GuardPredicate type) as every condition.* return; `GuardValue = string | RefHandle<"signal" | "condition"> | ConditionHandle | GuardHandle | readonly GuardValue[]` — the bare-JsonObject arm is DELETED; hand-written predicates go through one `condition.unsafe(json)` escape mirroring unsafeJson; guard-consuming sites (`when:` conditional resources, check steps, loop exits) tighten in the same pass.
      **Watch:** mirror GuardExpr/GuardPredicate exactly (rule names, all 19 fields); keep condition.ts:329's existing `satisfies` pattern as the template.
      **Done when:** a typo'd guard field is a compile error; zero `as JsonObject` remains in condition.ts; existing trait sources build with mechanical fixes only.

- [x] **P484** — Value-type preservation, handle assignability, resource field lockdown.
      **Why:** three self-contained erasures: (1) declared `schema.enum(id, values)` returns `SchemaHandle<unknown>`, erasing the literal union its inline overload preserves (same: oneOf/rating/verdict/decision/yesNo/union); (2) `CdkObject`'s `[key: string]: unknown` index signature is incompatible with `JsonObject`, forcing consumers into `handle as unknown as JsonObject` double-casts (9+ test sites) just to normalize a non-trait handle; (3) `ResourceFields extends JsonObject` — a misspelled resource key typechecks and is silently emitted into the canonical document.
      **Where:** (1) return-type narrowing: `SchemaHandle<T[number]>` etc., union Value inference from members; (2) `toDraftJson`/normalize entries accept any branded handle; (3) drop the extends, enumerate the real optional fields, `unsafe?: JsonObject` only if genuinely needed.
      **Watch:** (1)+(2) additive; (3) closes a correctness bug (silent canonical pollution), not a feature — say so in the changeset.
      **Done when:** `condition.equals(slot, member)` typechecks the member against the declared enum; no double-cast needed to normalize any handle; an unknown resource key is a compile error.

- [x] **P485** — Implementation hygiene: interfaces checked against implementations, cast count collapses.
      **Why:** 10 builder namespaces are `Object.assign(...) as XxxFunction` — overload interfaces never checked against implementations; `(...args: readonly unknown[])` impl signatures + ~40 internal narrowing casts carry the real shapes by hand; sequence.ts's 568-852 cast ladder is the worst file.
      **Where:** real overloaded function declarations (the `trait()` pattern) replace `(...args: unknown[])`; `satisfies XxxFunction` replaces the namespace casts; the sequence.ts ladder collapses onto P481's canonical types; the `Object(schemaRef) as Pick<string, ...>` boxed-String masquerade (schema.ts:631/683) gets a real type or a documented rationale.
      **Watch:** internal-only, zero consumer breakage; oxlint `--deny-warnings` stays green.
      **Done when:** `as `-cast count in packages/cdk/src drops ≥60% from the audited 232, every survivor carries a one-line justification; no `(...args: unknown[])` impl signatures remain.

## Group 117: Landed-ledger reconciliation — the books match the commits (owner-ordered 2026-07-24; git-log audit found 11 claimed-landed items with NO commit evidence, 5 landed partial/uncertified, ~60 landed phases still unchecked in the plan, 2 duplicate phase-id pairs)

Audit disposition already applied 2026-07-24 in the same pass that authored this group: board Landed section corrected (OUT: P282, P327, P328, P399, P432, P434, P437, P449, P450, P451, P452, P458, P461 — none have landing evidence or their Done-when verifiably fails; IN: P240 621535a, P287 677a589, P465 9340e02 — landed but still listed in-flight); plan checkboxes for the never-built explain-trait trio (P320/P321/P322) unchecked. Standing note: P464's implementation exists ONLY on run branch e4468ce (pending merge) — do not prune that branch.

- [ ] **P486** — The plan checkbox is dead: retire it, or generate it (re-scoped 2026-07-27 after the audit).
      **Why:** the original charter was "tick the verified landed set". The 2026-07-27 audit shows that is not worth doing, because the checkbox is not merely stale — it is never written AT ALL. P493, P505, P510, P543, P544, P547, P548 all landed with real payloads and all still read `- [ ]`. Not one phase in the plan has ever been ticked by a run. So the field carries zero signal, everyone reads the board instead, and every audit has to re-derive truth from the tree. Ticking sixty boxes by hand would restore the illusion for exactly as long as it takes the next run to land.
      **Where:** pick ONE and apply it everywhere: (a) DELETE the `- [ ]` marker from the phase grammar — the board's section position is the status, and the plan is the contract, which is what practice already does; or (b) make it GENERATED — a command derives the mark from the tree and rewrites it, so it can never drift by hand. Option (a) is smaller and matches observed behaviour; option (b) is only worth it if the mark is wanted in the plan file itself. Whichever is chosen, `.docs/PRODUCT.md` and the authoring doctrine must state it, so the next reader does not re-add a field nobody maintains.
      **Watch:** do NOT tick the existing boxes as part of this — that is the failed approach, and it makes the plan look authoritative one commit before it stops being. This is bookkeeping-format work, not certification: whether a phase actually landed is P487's job, not this one.
      **Done when:** the plan has one stated, enforced convention for phase status; no hand-maintained mark exists that a run can silently leave stale; and the doctrine says which surface is authoritative.
- [ ] **P487** — Certification pass over every uncertified landing (absorbs P519; extended by the 2026-07-27 audit).
      **Why:** landings whose own commit bodies record an unapproved or abandoned review are claims nobody checked. P487 originally chartered five (P290, P396, P410, P422, P427); P519 chartered fourteen more by the same method and is folded in here rather than run separately — one method, one list, one pass. The 2026-07-27 audit adds a worse class the earlier lists did not know about: commits that certify work their diff does not contain (P507, P530, P518, P350) and one empty commit (P395), plus six phases that landed under subjects naming no phase at all (P507/P545→4eaf1a0, P528→686dda7, P475→1a7d9e3, P494→24cdf58, P452→5184881, P434→2085b4f). Those are already demoted to the forward queue; this phase is what stops the next batch reaching that state.
      **Where:** one bounded pass per phase — re-read its Done-when against the TREE, never against its commit message; close small gaps in place; demote anything larger back to the queue with a note. Record a dated verdict on the board's Landed line, in the format P465 already uses ("Certified <date> against <commits>: <evidence>"). Then close the loop that made this necessary: a landing whose verdict is `revise`, or whose diff does not touch any path its Done-when names, is not recorded as landed at all.
      **Watch:** review, not re-implementation — timebox each; a phase whose blockers are still true goes back to the queue rather than being quietly blessed. Verify by artifact, not by subject: the audit found the log wrong in BOTH directions, so `git log --grep` is not evidence either way.
