      **Done when:** every uncertified landing carries a dated verdict naming what was checked in the tree; every user-facing claim is proven or withdrawn; and a run can no longer record a landed line for a diff that does not match its own Done-when.

## Group 118: Release polish — genericization, silent truncation, identity scrub, honest errors (owner-ordered 2026-07-24 deep review: fix what is repo/Rust-specific, hardcoded, or will bite users; full inventories in the 2026-07-24 audit)

- [x] **P488** — The default branch is discovered, never assumed: `main` leaves the merge machinery.
      **Why:** `MAIN_BRANCH: &str = "main"` (merge.rs:32) welds every landing path — rev-parse, rebase target, and the hard refusal `expected "main"` (merge.rs:1797) — to a branch NAME; a `master`/`develop`/`trunk` repo can complete a PAID worktree run and then find merge refuses forever, no knob. Worst single user-bite found.
      **Where:** resolve once per merge: `[merge] branch` config (P418 project-fact layering) > `origin/HEAD` symref > `init.defaultBranch` > literal `main` fallback with a named warning; thread the resolved name through rev-parse/rebase/refusal text AND the `git merge-file -L main` conflict labels (worktree.rs:1408); `doctor --config` names the resolved branch + source layer.
      **Watch:** refusal messages name BOTH expected and actual; at least one merge proof runs its fixture repo on a non-main default branch; zero literal `"main"` branch refs remain in modules/ product paths (rg-gated).
      **Done when:** a fixture repo with default branch `trunk` lands a worktree run end to end with untouched config; `[merge] branch = "release"` overrides discovery; the rg gate holds.

- [x] **P489** — Silent-truncation and silent-omission sweep: nothing feeds corrupt data forward quietly.
      **Why:** the run-12b7ce77 lesson (64KiB git-capture truncation corrupting merges) has LIVE siblings: (1) `conflicted_paths` parses `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U` under the same 65_536 cap with NO truncation check (worktree.rs:433) — a big conflict set silently under-reports to the merger and the pre/post-continue comparisons; (2) port default commands store stdout into typed input slots with NO truncation check at 16_384 (run.rs:1297-1322) — downstream digests/receipts build on corrupt input; (3) worktree setup commands run under a silent 120s/16KiB budget (worktree.rs:1735) — a real `pnpm install` blows the cap and the failure's own stderr is truncated; (4) publish/pack walks silently skip ANY dir named `.git`/`node_modules`/`target`/`.turbo` at ANY depth (distribution.rs:747, publish.rs:205) and pack verification uses the SAME skip — a package with a legitimate `target/` resource dir ships incomplete and verifies clean; (5) command-step capture fixed at 262_144 (run.rs:1490) feeds truncated text to `schema:text` slots with evidence-only flags; (6) `run_git`'s blanket 30s timeout (worktree.rs:1847) bounds rebase and `worktree add` — a multi-GB repo's slow rebase parks disguised as a merge failure.
      **Where:** (1) loud-fail via the run_git_full_listing pattern; (2)+(5) truncation on a slot-feeding capture = hard frame error (fail-closed, receipts doctrine), caps per-command-configurable; (3) `[worktree] setup` per-command timeout/capture config + untruncated tail persisted to a file on failure (the parked-gate output-persistence pattern); (4) skip-list becomes explicit config, defaults DOCUMENTED in pack output ("skipped: node_modules (default excludes)"), pack verification runs against the pre-skip manifest; (6) git plumbing vs porcelain timeouts split, long-side configurable; shared DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS/DEFAULT_CAPTURE_LIMIT (command.rs:15-16) become named, documented defaults. Also fold in: frame_prompt's 128KiB hard inline-prompt cap (frame_prompt.rs:1078) gets a config override + an error that names the cap; dependency clone/fetch 120s (dependency.rs:1225) configurable.
      **Watch:** the correctly-loud sites (worktree.rs:697 `git show` 64MiB, run_git_full_listing 16MiB) are the pattern — copy, don't invent; P475 owns agent-role TIME budgets, this phase owns process-capture budgets — no overlap; proofs use behavioral assertions, no fixture matching (P461 doctrine).
      **Done when:** every capture that feeds state (slots, conflict lists, fingerprints, packs) verifiably completes or fails the operation loudly naming the cap; one >cap fixture per site proves the loud path; a fixture package with a real `target/` resource dir ships complete or refuses loudly.

- [x] **P490** — Identity scrub: the binary stops shipping the owner's machine.
      **Why:** six embedded builtin trait.lock files carry `vendored-path = "/Users/rpunkfu/ctx-company/ctx-trait/.ctx/worktrees/wt-f8d96f9a2280/..."` (modules/core/builtins/traits/*/trait.lock) — compiled into every release binary and materialized into every user's builtin store; leaks username/company/dogfood-worktree and contradicts project_lock.rs:87's own relative-path convention. Also: 23 hardcoded model ids in CORE (agent_model.rs:12-39 OPENCODE_TOP/FAST_PREFERENCES — outside the P427-sanctioned registry; `tier = "top"` silently resolves to this build's frozen preference order); ~170 comments cite gitignored .plans/.docs artifacts and dogfood run-ids in public source.
      **Where:** regenerate the six builtin locks with relative vendored-path + a build-time guard (embedded lock containing `/Users/` fails the build); move the model-preference tables beside the P427 builtin registry in io as data, with the staleness trade-off documented and a config override story; comment sweep: gitignored-artifact references get self-contained one-line context or lose the pointer — owner decides keep-engineering-history vs scrub ONCE, applied uniformly; merge.rs:12's stale byte_compare gate doc line goes regardless.
      **Watch:** lock regeneration must keep trait digests stable or re-approve trust deliberately; the `/Users/` guard covers future regressions; relocating the model lists without the override story just moves the problem — don't.
      **Done when:** `rg "rpunkfu|/Users/" modules/` returns zero product hits and the guard enforces it; a fresh install's materialized builtin store contains no foreign absolute paths; model preferences live in one sanctioned site with staleness documented; the owner's keep-or-scrub comment decision is recorded and applied.

- [x] **P491** — Honest errors v1: the reason survives to the user, exits are typed everywhere.
      **Why:** `concise_error` (command_handlers.rs:265-272) keeps ONLY THE FIRST LINE of every command error — the P477-incident TOML error printed the location and DISCARDED `unknown field 'gate'` on the last line (a live debugging round paid for a message we had and threw away); `ctx traits build` on a TS error shows one line of a node stack (cdk_build.rs:126-141); the codebase even engineers messages "to survive concise_error" (harness_config.rs:3239 — the workaround documents the bug). Git failures render as debug telemetry `git error: timed-out=false stderr=...` dropping command/exit fields (git_process.rs:67-94) — also the literal first-run experience outside a repo. Debug leaks in human output (`exit=Some(101)`, `status: AwaitingAgentOutput`, `selection: NoMatch`, `argv=["cargo","build"]`) while `--json` speaks kebab-case — two vocabularies for one fact. Standalone `merge` collapses parked/lock-timeout/failed to generic exit 1 (merge.rs:177-190) while `run --merge` has typed 3/4/5; doctor exits 0 on critical findings; `check`'s "found findings" is indistinguishable from "couldn't run". Usage errors wear `filesystem error at <not-a-path>` costumes (run_queue.rs:106, run_session.rs:113). Failed-import restore errors are silently discarded (import_handlers.rs:530-535, distribution.rs:905-913) — data-loss adjacent. `run`'s no-match prints a `run-info` header (run_format.rs:84).
      **Where:** (1) delete the first-line flattening — full Display indented on stderr, per-type opt-in one-liners where a type earns it; (2) git error Display: command + exit code, timeout named only when true, not-a-repo gains "run inside a git repository"; (3) Debug-leak sweep: serde kebab-case names + unwrapped Options in human lines; (4) standalone merge returns the SAME typed exits 4/5; doctor gains a documented findings exit; check separates findings from failure-to-run; (5) a plain usage-error variant replaces fake-path Filesystem wraps; (6) restore failures surface with the backup path; (7) drop the `command error:` prefix; (8) fix the run-info header.
      **Watch:** Group 113 owns panel STYLE — this phase owns error CONTENT and exit semantics, landing independently of the kit; `--json` unchanged; delete the harness_config.rs:3239 workaround comment with its reason.
      **Done when:** the P477 TOML error prints the unknown-field line verbatim; `ctx traits build` shows the compiler message; scripts distinguish merge-parked from merge-broken by exit code on BOTH verbs; rg finds zero `{:?}` in non-verbose human-output format strings under modules/cli/src/app (explicit allowlist for intentional debug surfaces).

- [x] **P492** — Env-var hygiene: test hooks cannot arm in production by accident.
      **Why:** release binaries ship fault-injection hooks armed by plain env vars — CTX_FIXTURE_CHECKPOINT_*, CTX_FIXTURE_FAIL_*_WRITE_ORDINAL (drive.rs:3766-3802): an environment exporting one gets a conductor that stalls at durable-state boundaries or fakes ledger-write failures, undocumented. CTX_TRAITS_REGISTRY_BASE (distribution.rs:238) is a self-described test hook that is ALSO the only private-registry pointer. CTX_CONFIG (harness_config.rs:1754) is load-bearing and undocumented.
      **Where:** fixture hooks → `CTX_INTERNAL_TESTHOOK_` prefix + a second arming var that must match the running binary's build hash (or compile-out behind a feature the release profile disables — the P402 kill-boundary proofs need the hooks in a REAL binary; whichever gate lands must keep those proofs green, and the choice gets documented); CTX_TRAITS_REGISTRY_BASE promoted to a documented `[registry] base` config knob (P418 layering) with the env var as override; CTX_CONFIG + internal parent→child vars (CTX_TRAITS_ELAPSED_SECONDS_BASELINE) documented in one env-var reference.
      **Watch:** npm-registry/npm-forged-lock proofs must stay honest through the registry-knob change; the hook gate must not weaken the P402 proofs' claim of testing the production binary.
      **Done when:** exporting today's CTX_FIXTURE_* names against a release build provably does nothing; private-registry users have a documented config path; every CTX_* var the product reads appears in shipped docs with its contract.
- [x] **P496** — Narrator fallback diet: narration works on redacted-thinking (Claude 5) streams.
      **Why:** Claude 5 family models stream thinking REDACTED through claude-code — verified from a raw worker attempt trace (debug/wt-eb5e84e3b592/…/007-apply-worker@claude-code.jsonl, ~1MB): 287 `thinking_delta` events ALL `{"thinking":"","estimated_tokens":N}`, zero non-empty thinking anywhere; live probe confirmed sonnet-5 redacts while haiku-4-5 still streams text; claude-code's own binary docstring names the "redacted-thinking phase (where the API otherwise streams only pings)". The narrator's diet is thinking-only by P330 design (`drain_thinking_deltas` + the `has_new_thinking` gate, harness_stream.rs:426), so on claude-code seats the narrator is NEVER invoked and the live panel shows `(initialization)` for entire runs (narrated panels receive no raw fallback — drive.rs:334-336 routes chunks only to the narrator). The stream still carries plenty to narrate: sparse assistant `text_delta` prose, dense tool activity (126 `tool_use` + 317 `input_json_delta` with commands/paths in the sampled trace), and the live `estimated_tokens` counter.
      **Where:** per-stream fallback diet in harness_stream: when a stream yields no thinking TEXT, ingest assistant `text_delta` content plus tool-call activity (tool name + salient argument — command, file_path, pattern — from `tool_use`/`input_json_delta`) into the narration window; `estimated_tokens` drives an honest between-narrations pill ("thinking… ~1.2k tokens") on the live line instead of a frozen placeholder. Streams that DO expose thinking/reasoning text (opencode) keep the existing thinking-only diet unchanged. Also fix the latent key bug for the day redaction lifts: `collect_kind_texts` reads payload keys `text`/`content`/`delta` only (harness_stream.rs:1076) while Claude's thinking payload key is `"thinking"` — add it.
      **Watch:** narration stays presentation-only garnish (never gates, never joins the drive); pacing floor and window caps unchanged; do NOT ingest `tool_result` bodies (volume + secrets risk — tool INPUTS only); the pill is display state, not a narrator call (zero extra model spend).
      **Done when:** a claude-code sonnet-5 driven frame produces real narrations within the first pacing interval (fixture from recorded redacted-stream shapes; live run confirms); the thinking pill shows during redacted thinking; opencode narration behavior byte-identical to today; the `"thinking"` key extraction is covered by a shape test.
- [x] **P497** — Trust gates the render family: `prompt`/`export`/`host install` refuse what the trust store refuses.
      **Why:** the run family hard-refuses blocked/unreviewed/draft traits (`preflight_lifecycle_trust`, io/run.rs:1186) and the resolver gates selection — but the render family does NOT: `build_render_context` → `load_trait` never consults lifecycle/trust (verified: zero trust references in cli/report_render.rs or either host_install.rs), so a BLOCKED trait's model view renders, prints, exports, and host-installs today without a warning. Render v2 (P493) makes `prompt` the behavioral-injection source for every harness adapter — the product's trust thesis must hold on exactly that path.
      **Where:** render-family verbs consult the same lifecycle/trust classification the run family uses: blocked → hard typed refusal naming the remedy (`ctx traits trust approve <id>` is NOT suggested for blocked — blocked is a decision, name `trust list` instead); unreviewed → refuse by default with `--allow-unreviewed` escape; draft → allowed for `prompt`/`export` WITH a visible advisory line (authors preview their own drafts constantly — the authoring loop must stay fluid) but `host install` refuses draft without `--allow-draft`. Adapter flows are already resolver-gated and unaffected; direct CLI calls gain the same posture. The future MCP serving path (utilization step 2) inherits this gate by construction — note it in the module doc.
      **Watch:** per-verb default posture documented in help text; `--json` refusals use the typed envelope; do not break the P349 opencode plugin's prompt calls for resolver-selected traits (selection already implies trust-pass — the gate must agree with the resolver's verdict, one classification source, never two).
      **Done when:** `prompt`/`export`/`host install` on a blocked trait refuse with typed errors; unreviewed refuses without the flag and renders with it; a draft prompt/export carries the advisory and host-install refuses it flagless; run-family and resolver behavior byte-identical; one shared classification call site (rg finds no second lifecycle/trust decision path in the render family).

## Group 119: Trait render v2 — XML behavioral render + real summary level (owner-locked spec 2026-07-25, reviewed snippet-exact; grounded in the 2026-07-25 harness-utilization research)

The locked format rules (owner-approved verbatim; do not re-litigate):
1. Imperative bullet directives — one imperative line per intent/behavior entry; NO second person anywhere; connected prose only inside `<summary>`.
2. Tags mirror the trait model, nothing invented: `<trait id version model-view>`, `<summary>` (declared identity summary verbatim), `<intent group="require|focus|avoid|block" id>`, `<behavior axis="tone|method|format|verbosity|directness|scope-control|initiative|uncertainty" id>`, `<resource id digest render>`. No `<mission>`, no `<item>`, no wrapper containers.
3. Behavior mode carries ZERO procedural content — no prompts, agents, ports, sequences, signals, activation. Renders exactly: summary, intent, behavior, resources.
4. Directives are vocabulary data: each vocabulary value gains one `directive` field (imperative mood); absent → mechanical fallback from `summary`. Summary/Details duplication dies at authoring time.
5. Directives judgment-shaped, never guardrail-shaped ("Keep output compact", never "NEVER exceed three sentences") — the intent/render split is the model-generation calibration seam.
6. No CDATA: model-readable tagged text, not parser XML. Delimiter integrity = renderer check that a body contains no renderer-owned closing tag; on collision escape those occurrences (or fence). Hidden-content audit allowlists renderer tags, still scans content inside them; code exemplars containing `<` are legal.
7. Real summary load level: same tags, one element per group/axis holding comma-separated ids, bare `<resource>` id list, no semicolon mashing (~45 tokens).
8. One shared section renderer, two envelopes (behavior + Mode A frame `<persona>`) — the frame half is OUT OF SCOPE here: moved to `.plans/FUTURE.md` (owner 2026-07-25, belongs with a larger frame-rendering effort — ASCII diagrams and structured evidence layouts — not with this render). Build the emitters so a second envelope stays possible; do not build one.
9. Digest staleness waived pre-release: REPLACE the current model-view renderer outright, no parallel profile; exports/locks re-stamp on next build.

Canonical fixture (behavior mode, full — Done-whens compare against this shape):
```xml
<trait id="socratic-guidance" version="0.1.0" model-view="sha256:9f3e…">
  <summary>Guide by questioning, not answering: the learner reaches the solution; the questions make that possible.</summary>
  <intent group="require" id="understanding-first">Establish what the learner currently believes before advancing anything.</intent>
  <intent group="require" id="one-question">Ask exactly one question per turn; wait for the answer before the next.</intent>
  <intent group="avoid" id="answer-leakage">Do not give the solution, partial solutions, or leading questions that contain the answer.</intent>
  <intent group="avoid" id="premature-advance">Do not move on until the learner commits to a position, even a wrong one.</intent>
  <behavior axis="tone" id="warm-curious">Be patient and genuinely curious; treat wrong answers as material, not failures.</behavior>
  <behavior axis="verbosity" id="minimal">Keep each turn to the question plus at most one sentence of framing.</behavior>
  <resource id="question-ladder" digest="sha256:c41b…" render="inline">
    Escalate through: clarify the claim → surface the assumption → ask for evidence →
    probe implications → ask for one alternative view. Drop back a rung when the learner stalls.
  </resource>
  <resource id="exemplar-dialogue" digest="sha256:88d2…" render="reference">
    Reference transcript of a full session; open with your tools when calibrating question depth.
  </resource>
</trait>
```
Summary level of the same trait:
```xml
<trait id="socratic-guidance" model-view="sha256:9f3e…" level="summary">
  <summary>Guide by questioning, not answering.</summary>
  <intent group="require">understanding-first, one-question</intent>
  <intent group="avoid">answer-leakage, premature-advance</intent>
  <behavior axis="tone">warm-curious</behavior>
  <behavior axis="verbosity">minimal</behavior>
  <resource>question-ladder, exemplar-dialogue</resource>
</trait>
```

- [x] **P493** — Render v2, full level: the XML behavioral render replaces the markdown model view.
      **Why:** the current model view is markdown label-lists with duplicated Summary/Details lines (e.g. `robustness` prints the identical sentence twice), meta-noise ("Static render advisory…" scattered mid-doctrine), label indirection the model must expand, procedure plumbing mixed into behavioral payload (~70% of implement-quick's render), and heading-based structure any resource body can forge (`## Intent` in content is indistinguishable from a real section). The locked format above fixes all five; the utilization path (harness adapters injecting via `ctx traits prompt`) consumes this directly.
      **Where:** modules/core model_view compile/compile_sections gain the tag emitters implementing rules 1-6 and 9 (behavior mode = rule 3's four sections ONLY; the full/authoring render keeps its additional sections but restyles onto the same emitters); builtin vocabulary TOMLs gain `directive` per value for every value used by repo + builtin traits (imperative mood, judgment-shaped; mechanical second-personless fallback from `summary` covers the rest); `ctx traits prompt` emits the new form (plain and --json `text` field both); the hidden-content audit gains a renderer-tag allowlist while continuing to scan tag CONTENT (a smuggled `<intent>` inside a resource body must still flag; a code exemplar's `<div>` must not); delimiter-integrity check per rule 6 with the escape-on-collision path proven; SKILL.md export body and the opencode plugin injection consume the same emitter output unchanged.
      **Watch:** ids become load-bearing at summary level — establish the self-describing-slug convention (`one-question`, never `rule-7`) in authoring doctrine now; no fixture matching in tests (behavioral assertions parse the output structure — P461 doctrine); rule 9 means export/lock/host-placement digests all move on next build — expected, not a regression; keep the sanitize pipeline exactly as is underneath the new emitters.
      **Done when:** `ctx traits prompt` on a behavior-only fixture matches the canonical fixture's SHAPE (tags, attributes, grouping — asserted structurally, not byte-compared) and on implement-quick contains zero procedure/prompt/agent/port/signal content; no `Details:` duplication remains; a resource body containing a literal `</resource>` renders with integrity intact; audit passes renderer tags and still flags content-smuggled markup; vocabulary directives cover all values in-repo traits use, fallback proven on an uncovered value.
- [x] **P494** — Summary level made real: the resolver's middle rung renders, prices, and round-trips.
      **Why:** load levels exist only as pricing vocabulary today — resolve charges 1/4/16 for discovery/summary/full, but Summary RENDERS THE FULL TEXT (core/context/pack.rs:142-158) and `ctx traits prompt` has no level flag at all, so the budget story is nominal and the accounting dishonest (charging 4 for 16's bytes). The data model already carries everything summary needs (identity summary, intent/behavior ids + groups/axes, resource ids) — this is emitter + plumbing, zero trait-model work.
      **Where:** summary emitter per rule 7 and the canonical summary fixture (one element per group/axis, comma-separated ids, bare `<resource>` id list, no escalation pointer in the artifact — that belongs to the adapter/stub that injected it); `--level full|summary` on `ctx traits prompt` (default full); the context-pack path renders Summary selections with the summary emitter and Discovery stays empty-content; resolve/pack token estimates priced from the actual rendered forms (estimate-source updated accordingly) so budget downgrades reflect reality.
      **Watch:** discovery unchanged (awareness only); reason codes (`budget-downgraded-to-*`) keep their meaning; summary must stay genuinely small — a measured-token assertion, not a vibe; the id-slug quality doctrine from P493 is what makes this level readable — cross-reference it.
      **Done when:** `ctx traits prompt <trait> --level summary` on the fixture matches the canonical summary shape; measured summary tokens are a small fraction of full for implement-quick and the fixture; a budget-constrained resolve whose report says summary actually yields summary bytes on fetch; pricing in the resolve report reflects rendered sizes.

## Group 120: Trait utilization inside coding harnesses — the delivery layer (research-complete 2026-07-25, owner-ordered scenario; PROPOSAL awaiting dispatch order)

**Session context (2026-07-24/25).** Owner asked: support using traits (WITHOUT procedures) from inside claude-code / opencode / codex / pi with the best UX, then MCP, then agent-driven CLI. Research landed in `.docs/research/HARNESS_UTILIZATION_2026-07.md` (four harnesses, binary-verified) and `.docs/research/CONTEXT_ENGINEERING_REFLECTIONS_2026-07.md` (five external sources); the render half is chartered as Group 119, the trust gate as P497. This group is the delivery half.

**Owner decisions locked this session.**
- Reliability ladder: hook/plugin calling `ctx` = the only DETERMINISTIC activation (our code runs every message, model has no discretion) > `/trait` command = reliable manual > SKILL.md = model-elective discovery + ctx-less fallback. Skills are our object code, never the activation mechanism.
- V1 = slim SKILL.md stub telling the agent to call `ctx traits`; V2 = hooks/plugins, gated on solving context pollution (no re-inlining the same trait) — this group is V2.
- Owner read (recorded, not yet acted on): "our own runtime will be a must in near future, to control this without tons of plugin/hook hacking" — every phase below is us reimplementing session state a real runtime would own. Treat as the strategic exit, not a v1 blocker.

**The load-bearing finding: the two harness families need OPPOSITE designs.**
- **Family A (claude-code, codex) — hooks, append-only conversation.** Injected text persists, so genuine per-session dedup is required. VERIFIED (probe + 7,564 local transcripts): `session_id` stable across every turn; `--continue`/`--resume` = NEW PROCESS reusing the SAME id (so in-memory dedup is wrong, state must be on disk); `/clear` and `--fork-session` mint a new id (correct — re-inject); compaction does NOT change the id (so the id alone can never detect context loss). `SessionStart` carries `source: startup|resume|clear|compact|fork` and its output is spliced into the REBUILT post-compaction context — that is both the compaction detector and the re-inject channel. Injected context does NOT survive compaction (verified on a real 825k→17k-token compaction: `preservedMessages.uuids` held 4 entries, zero attachments).
- **Family B (opencode, pi) — plugin/extension, system array rebuilt per request.** `experimental.chat.system.transform` (opencode) and the `context` event (pi) hand back a freshly-built array on EVERY LLM call, so "inject once and track" is the wrong model: re-assert the current set every call. Nothing accumulates; context pollution is structurally impossible; fork/resume/restart need no handling. "Not previously used" survives as a design choice — assert the MONOTONIC UNION of traits matched so far this session (sticky, matches the owner's intent) rather than only this message's matches.

**Our side, audited:** the entire ledger data model already exists and is 100% DEAD CODE — `modules/core/src/context/ledger.rs` has `Entry`/`Ledger`/`State{Missing,Unsupported,Loaded}`/`StaleReason{DigestChanged,Compacted,MissingHostKey,LoadLevelChanged}`, `Entry` is never constructed anywhere, `Compacted` has zero producers, `context status` returns a hardcoded constant (context_cache.rs:183-208). The rule is already specified verbatim at `.docs/PRODUCT.md:3014`. This group is plumbing, not modeling.

- [x] **P498** — Session context ledger + one-call `context plan`: the engine both hook families call.
      **Why:** every adapter needs the same three answers in one fast call — which traits match this prompt, which of them this host session has already seen, and the text to inject. Today an adapter must call `resolve` then `prompt` PER TRAIT (N+1 process spawns) because `resolve --json` emits no digests even though it loads them (command_handlers.rs:1448), and nothing anywhere persists what a host session was shown.
      **Where:** (a) DEFINE "host key" — undefined in code AND docs today; it is `(harness id, host-reported session id)`. (b) Wire the dead model: new IO module (`modules/io/src/context_ledger.rs`) reading/writing `<harness>-<host-session>.json` under a NEW state family `~/.config/ctx/context/<repo-key>/` (sibling of runs/, never nested under cache/ — pruning must never touch it, per builtin_store.rs:16-21 doctrine); flock-serialized (write_bytes_atomically uses a FIXED `.tmp` name, so the lock is mandatory, not optional) + symlink-guarded + `validate_bare_session_id` (run_session.rs:456) on the host-supplied id, which is an untrusted path component. (c) FIX `Ledger::reconcile` (ledger.rs:192): it checks host_key for ABSENCE only, never equality — a wrong host key passes reconciliation clean. (d) New verb `ctx traits context plan --host <harness> --host-session <key> --task <text> [--files …] [--mode] [--language …] [--budget] [--level] [--commit] --json` → per selected trait `{trait-id, model-view-digest, action: "inject"|"skip-fresh"|"reinject", stale-reason?, text}`; `--commit` records optimistically (there is no post-injection callback; a lost injection self-heals via the digest/compaction paths). (e) `ctx traits context clear --host --host-session --reason compact|clear|fork` for the SessionStart edge. (f) Emit digests in `resolve --json` (already loaded, just unserialized) so non-ledger adapters collapse their round trips too.
      **Watch:** do NOT name the flag `--session` — that already means the ctx RUN session at top level (cli.rs:97-100) and is inert on resolve/pack; PRODUCT.md:1546 states the two-ledger doctrine ("the run ledger is separate from the session context ledger") and three meanings of "session" already collide. Envelope decision required and must be stated: `resolve`/`prompt`/`pack` emit BARE objects, `context status`/`cache *` emit `Envelope` — existing adapters parse bare. Retention: NO time-based sweeper (zero precedent in repo — mtime is display-only by deliberate choice); if pruning is ever needed mirror `prune_named_build_cache` (declaration-gated, --dry-run, typed outcome) and default to REPORTING stale entries. The ledger is evidence of what we SUPPLIED, never proof of model memory — keep `HostInjectionWarning`'s standing non-claim (pack.rs:20-40) honest; a real host report may set its `status` for that host only.
      **Done when:** two consecutive `context plan --commit` calls with the same host-session and task return `inject` then `skip-fresh`; a `context clear --reason compact` makes the next call return `inject` again; an edited trait returns `reinject` with `stale-reason: digest-changed`; the ledger survives process restart (resume case) and a forced-id collision cannot corrupt another session's file; `resolve --json` carries digests.
- [x] **P499** — claude-code hook adapter: `ctx traits hook`, the deterministic activation path on our primary harness.
      **Why:** claude-code has NO behavioral-injection path today (our plugin teaches the run loop only) — the main harness is the biggest gap. The hook is the only mechanism with zero model discretion.
      **Where:** a `ctx traits hook` subcommand reading the harness hook JSON on stdin and emitting `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"…","additionalContext":"…"}}`. Two events: `SessionStart` — source ∈ {startup, clear, fork, compact} → `context clear` then inject the always-on set (its output is spliced into the rebuilt context, so this IS the post-compaction repair); source = resume → keep the ledger, inject nothing. `UserPromptSubmit` — payload verified live: `{session_id, transcript_path, cwd, prompt_id, permission_mode, hook_event_name, prompt}` (the prompt field IS `prompt`; `session_id` always present) → `context plan --commit` → emit. Ship the settings block as a `ctx`-emitted snippet (or `host install` artifact), never hand-written JSON.
      **Watch:** HARD CAP 10,000 chars on additionalContext — over it the content is spilled to a file and the model gets a 2KB preview plus a path, defeating injection entirely (makes P494's summary level load-bearing); 30-SECOND timeout on UserPromptSubmit with output SILENTLY DISCARDED on overrun (resolve+render must be fast; no partial credit); `hookEventName` must match the event exactly or the harness throws; multiple hooks run CONCURRENTLY and their outputs accumulate into one attachment with UNSPECIFIED order — never encode meaning in ordering; `--session-id <uuid>` lets a caller force an id, so two concurrent runs can collide on the dedup key (consider folding transcript_path or start time into the marker). FUTURE UPGRADE (do not build now): the hook receives `transcript_path`, prior injections appear as `{"type":"hook_additional_context"}` records, and the current prompt is not yet written — so a transcript scan is a zero-state self-verifying dedup; it MUST gate on the last `compactMetadata` record + its `preservedMessages.uuids` or it gives permanent false negatives after any compaction.
      **Done when:** a real claude-code session injects a keyword-matched trait on the first prompt, does NOT re-inject it on later prompts, injects a second trait when its keyword appears, and re-injects everything after a compaction; rendered payloads stay under the 10k cap; the whole handler completes well inside the 30s budget on a cold start.
- [x] **P500** — opencode plugin v2: re-assert per call, and fix the channel bug we already shipped.
      **Where:** move injection from `output.message.system` (chat.message) to **`experimental.chat.system.transform`**, asserting the monotonic union of this session's matched traits on every request; keep a `sessionID → {digest, text}` map in the plugin closure purely as a CACHE so `ctx` is not re-shelled per LLM call (plugins are instantiated once per directory via a ScopedCache with infinite capacity — closure state is safe within a server lifetime, and losing it costs only a re-shell). Guard `if (!input.sessionID) return`. Bump `@opencode-ai/plugin`/`sdk` 1.3.9 → 1.17.18 (pinned types are stale: missing `dispose`, `provider`, `experimental.compaction.autocontinue`).
      **Watch:** THREE verified traps — (1) `system.transform` ALSO fires for the compaction summarizer's own request with that session's id, so trait text leaks into the summary unless a flag set in `experimental.session.compacting` and cleared in `experimental.compaction.autocontinue` suppresses it; (2) APPEND, never `unshift` — mutating `system[0]` skips opencode's two-block collapse and breaks the prompt-cache prefix (directly relevant to the caching-dividend work); (3) the `event` hook is FIRE-AND-FORGET (`void hook.event?.(…)`), so `session.compacted` is racy against the next request — use the awaited `experimental.*` hooks for anything ordering-sensitive. Also verified: the current `message.system` channel is not merely per-turn — routine auto-compaction rebuilds the continue message WITHOUT `system` and bypasses `createUserMessage`, so `chat.message` never fires and the injection vanishes undetectably. Resolver stays the sole activation authority; failures throw, never degrade to local activation. Fix the envelope-parsing skew (plugin parses bare objects) in the same pass.
      **Done when:** an opencode session shows the matched trait set in the SYSTEM prompt on every request; a trait matched at message 3 is still asserted at message 10 (sticky union); a compaction changes nothing and the summarizer's own prompt contains no trait text; `ctx` is shelled at most once per new match, not per call.
- [x] **P501** — The second harness of each family: codex hook + pi extension.
      **Where:** CODEX rides P499's handler unchanged (field-for-field the same wire contract) with a different config location (`~/.codex/hooks.json`, project `.codex/`, or inline `[hooks]` TOML). PI rides P500's model: an extension shipped as a pinned pi package — `before_agent_start` returning `{systemPrompt}`, the `context` event re-asserting per call, `pi.registerCommand("trait", …)`, `resources_discover` pointing at the ctx store.
      **Watch:** CODEX SHIP-BLOCKER — hooks are content-hash trust-gated (`trusted_hash`); editing the handler command changes the hash and silently re-arms a TUI trust prompt, so every deploy disables hooks until someone clicks through; codex compact hooks CANNOT inject (their output schema has no `hookSpecificOutput`), making `SessionStart(source=compact)` mandatory there; `transcript_path` is NULLABLE (ephemeral threads) so the ledger is the only option; no plain-stdout fallback (JSON only); three-way casing across config/telemetry/schema ids. PI — findings are SINGLE-SOURCED from the packed tarball with no live verification (pi is not installed); verify `context`-event behavior for internal compaction/branch-summary calls before building; project-local `.pi/` resources only load after `/trust`, so user-global install is the frictionless route; pin package refs (weekly releases).
      **Done when:** codex injects and dedups exactly as claude-code does, with the trust-hash re-arm documented in the install output; pi asserts the union per call and `/trait` applies manually.
- [x] **P502** — Static delivery: skill stubs, real skill directories, and the four host rows (the V1 fallback).
      **Why:** the ctx-less path and the discovery surface. Skills are our object code: the stub body says "run `ctx traits prompt <id>` and follow it" so even the model-elective trigger converges on fresh, trust-gated bytes; a fully-rendered skill is only for machines without ctx. Today `export` is SINGLE-FILE (declared resources are never placed — `export-partial` warning), and host-install knows cursor/copilot/gemini/cline/kiro but none of the four harnesses.
      **Where:** stub render mode; multi-file skill DIRECTORY export (resources as real supporting files with digests); four `builtin_hosts()` rows (`.claude/skills/{trait}/SKILL.md`, `.opencode/skills/…`, `~/.codex/skills/…`, `.pi/skills/…` — render profiles and default dirs already exist); `host status` drift verb (today update silently overwrites a same-identity local edit and only removal digest-checks); project activation rules into host-native scoping (file-glob → Claude `paths:` / Copilot `applyTo`; phrases → descriptions, respecting Claude's 1,536-char description cap and Codex's 2%-of-context skill-description budget).
      **Watch:** description budgets cap how much activation logic can live in host metadata — the resolver stays the brain; generated files keep ownership markers + source digest; never place an unapproved trait (P497).
      **Done when:** one command installs a trait as a stub skill on each of the four harnesses; a trait with resources exports a complete directory; `host status` names drift without mutating.

## Group 121: Trait adherence evidence (proposal — product capability + launch evidence, NOT a gate)

- [ ] **P503** — Adherence evals: prove a trait changes behavior, per directive.
      **Why:** every skills vendor ships prose with zero evidence it changes anything. We can ship behavior packages with receipts — and it is the only mechanism that tells us WHICH directives still earn their tokens as models improve (the calibration feed for the intent/render seam and the unhobbling doctrine). Also settles internal format questions (e.g. bullets vs composed prose for `<communication>`) with data instead of taste.
      **Where:** per trait, a probe set (N openers that should trigger its behavior) + per-directive checkable predicates keyed by the directive id (`one-question` → "reply ends with exactly one question"; `answer-leakage` → "contains no solution code"); run the same harness twice — baseline vs injected — score both; emit an adherence report keyed by directive id with the model-view digest, model id, and date. Deterministic checks where possible, rubric-judge where not. Composes with the landed eval/scenario fields and the auto-research loop pattern.
      **Watch:** this is a PRODUCT capability and launch evidence, never a gate on our own code — the 2026-07-24 no-validation-testing-during-exploration ruling stands and this must not become a merge gate or a frozen expectation; report numbers with model + date attached (they age with model generations, which is the point).
      **Done when:** one behavior-only trait has a probe set and an adherence report showing per-directive pass rates injected vs baseline, stamped with digest/model/date; re-running on a different model produces a comparable report.

## Group 122: The normalized activity model — one typed vocabulary for what is happening (owner-ordered 2026-07-25; consumed by Groups 123/124/125 and by the narrator)

Owner ask: *"normalize and standardize at each harness level and really detail fan-out of all different events happening — awaiting-agent-output, awaiting-user-input, agent-thinking etc, really add some depth to what's happening."*

Today's state, audited 2026-07-25: 8 session statuses (`session.rs:98-107`), but the "what happened" field `DriveOutcome.outcome` is a **free-form String** with ~20 distinct tokens observed in the wild (`harness-failed`, `max-iterations-exhausted`, `frame-timeout`, `attach-wait-expired`, `driver-lock-busy`, …); every harness stream is a different shape (claude-code redacted thinking pings + tool blocks, opencode reasoning parts, pi JSONL events) and NOTHING normalizes them — which is exactly why the narrator went silently dark when claude-code's thinking shape changed (P496). This group is the herdr "verified lifecycle contract" idea pointed at ourselves: we are the runtime for these harnesses, so we own the honest vocabulary.

- [x] **P504** — Two typed layers: session state and current activity, with per-harness adapters mapping into them (ABSORBS P496).
      **Why:** every downstream surface — TUI panes, narrator, `--json`, receipts, the dashboard's grouping — currently re-derives meaning from raw harness bytes or from a free-form string. One normalization layer, many consumers; without it every new harness multiplies the special cases and every harness change breaks a consumer silently.
      **Where:** (a) SESSION STATE — a typed enum of what the ledger says (running / waiting-on-human / waiting-on-agent / blocked / completed / failed / cancelled), terminal-vs-resumable explicit on the type rather than re-derived at each call site (today `dashboard.rs:389-402` is the only place that decides). (b) CURRENT ACTIVITY — a typed per-frame event stream: dispatching, thinking (with the token estimate where the harness gives one), running-tool { name, salient-arg }, streaming-output, validating-output, retrying { attempt, of }, stalled { since }, compacting. (c) Each harness adapter translates its native stream into (b) — claude-code: `thinking_delta.estimated_tokens` → thinking pill, `tool_use` + `input_json_delta` → running-tool, `text_delta` → streaming-output (this is P496's fix, delivered structurally instead of as a diet patch); opencode: reasoning parts → thinking, tool events → running-tool; pi/codex adapters follow the same table. (d) `DriveOutcome.outcome` becomes a typed enum over the observed token set, with the free-form escape kept only for a harness's own verbatim stop-reason. (e) The narrator consumes the normalized stream, not raw bytes; the TUI consumes the same.
      **Watch:** additive to the ledger — old ledgers without the new fields must round-trip unchanged (schema-version discipline); the activity stream is PRESENTATION + evidence, never a gate; a harness that reports nothing must degrade to a truthful "no activity reported" rather than a fabricated state; do NOT ingest tool RESULTS into the activity stream (volume + secrets), tool inputs only; keep the per-harness mapping tables in the harness registry's neighborhood so adding a harness is one table, not a sweep.
      **Done when:** a claude-code frame and an opencode frame produce the same typed activity vocabulary from different raw streams; the narrator narrates on a redacted-thinking claude-code run (P496's Done-when, satisfied here); `--json` exposes both layers; `DriveOutcome` is typed; a fixture harness emitting an unknown event degrades honestly.

- [ ] **P521** — `ctx traits story <run-id>`: the run as a readable document, at three depths (owner-ordered 2026-07-26; supersedes P383).
      **Why:** a run you did not watch live leaves NO story behind. The ledger holds the step sequence, titles, role→harness assignments, slot values, timings and outcomes — but the narration history and the P455 step summaries live only in the TUI's memory (capped at 200 entries) and die when the pane closes. The raw debug traces are the opposite problem: 1.7 GB of unnormalized per-harness JSONL nobody can read. Owner ask: open a document and see what each step actually did.
      **Where:** (a) PERSIST — P504's normalized activity stream is appended to a small per-run sidecar beside the ledger (~150 bytes/event; a 10-step run is ~45 KB against the same atomic/symlink-guarded discipline as the rest of the store). This is the missing half; everything below renders from it plus the ledger. (b) THREE LEVELS on one source: **default** — one section per step: `title — what it produced — (role @ harness/model) · duration`, then derived bullets (files edited with line counts, commands with exit code and duration, retries with their reason, slot writes, thinking-token totals); **detailed** — every activity event in order with timestamps, nothing collapsed; **assisted** — an LLM pass that summarizes the detailed level per frame into prose, using the same narrator/agent config the run itself used. (c) The per-step summary line resolves in this order: the run's own P455 step summary if one was recorded, else the step's typed output if it is short, else derived from activity ("edited 3 files, ran 2 commands") — so the default level ALWAYS works with no narrator configured and no model spend. (d) Renders to markdown (a file you can open), to the terminal through the P465 kit, and to `--json`.
      **Watch:** `assisted` is the ONLY level that costs model calls and must be explicitly requested — never the default, never implicit; it summarizes the recorded stream and may not invent anything not in it. The sidecar is derived evidence, never authority — a missing one degrades to a ledger-only story that says so. Bullets come from ACTIVITY (tool inputs, commands, edits), never from tool RESULTS (volume + secrets). Story is read-only: it never advances, mutates, or re-renders a session.
      **Done when:** `ctx traits story <run-id>` on a completed run prints the default document with per-step role@harness attribution and derived bullets; `--level detailed` shows timestamped events; `--level assisted` adds per-frame prose and is the only level that spends tokens; a run whose TUI was never opened produces the same document as one that was watched.

## Group 123: TUI v3 — the pane tree (owner-ordered 2026-07-25; layout reference: Zellij named panes with border titles; supersedes the hand-rolled Rect math)

Audited 2026-07-25: there is NO pane abstraction — layout is hand-rolled `Rect` arithmetic (`tui_kit.rs:225-276` `SplitBounds`) copy-pasted in three places, `Borders::ALL` appears exactly once (the modal), `Tabs`/`Scrollbar` are never imported (tabs are a hand-built `Paragraph`, `dashboard.rs:1688-1705`), there is one shared `ScrollList` reset on every tab switch, no multi-select, and no filtering of any kind. ratatui 0.29 already ships everything needed (`Tabs`, `Scrollbar`, `Block::title_top`, `BorderType`, nested `Layout`) with zero manifest change. Palette doctrine is unchanged and binding: named ANSI only — default fg, DIM, BOLD, green/red/yellow, **no backgrounds** (`.docs/TERMINAL_TUI_STYLING_SPEC.md:17-81`; PART I's gold/truecolor design is superseded, do not mine it).

- [x] **P505** — Pane tree + chrome kit: named panes, border titles, focus ring, real tabs, scrollbars, per-pane scroll.
      **Why:** every screen hand-rolls its own geometry today, so "make each screen consistent and nice" is unbuildable without a shared layout layer; and the aesthetic the owner wants (Zellij: full-width/height tree of named panes, title in the border, focused pane visually distinct) is precisely a pane-tree feature.
      **Where:** `PaneTree { Leaf { id, title }, Split { dir, children: [(Constraint, PaneTree)] } }` resolved to `[(PaneId, Rect)]` through NESTED ratatui `Layout` (not Rect math); pane chrome = `Block::bordered()` + `title_top` with the name in the border's top-left; FOCUS = default-fg border + BOLD title, unfocused = DIM border + DIM title — deliberately NOT green, which means "pass" in the house grammar and must not be overloaded; focus ring over leaf ids with Tab/Shift-Tab plus directional movement; real `Tabs` widget replacing the hand-built row (with Shift-Tab, which today does not exist); `Scrollbar` on scrollable panes replacing the `"12/47"` footer string; per-PANE scroll state replacing the single shared `ScrollList`; multi-select support in the list widget (P473 needs it and no representation exists). Prove it in `tui-demo` first, exactly as P468 did.
      **Watch:** two-tone doctrine is law — no backgrounds, no reverse video, no truecolor; BOLD on box-drawing glyphs is unreliable across terminals, so never encode meaning in a bold border; the kit renders what it is GIVEN and never fetches domain facts; keep the landed `Modal`/`ModalHost`/`edit_*`/`ScrollList` primitives — this extends the kit, it does not replace it.
      **Done when:** `tui-demo` shows a three-pane tree with named borders, focus moving between panes and visibly indicated, independent scroll per pane with scrollbars, and a real tab bar with forward AND backward cycling; resizing reflows without artifacts.
- [x] **P506** — Every dashboard screen on the tree: grouping, filtering, short ids, in-pane input.
      **Why:** consistency across screens is the owner's explicit ask, and three concrete defects ride along: the attached view is a fullscreen MODE SWAP rather than a focused pane (`dashboard.rs:1519-1521`), session ids render as full 72-character strings that push every other column off-screen, and the dashboard still routes free text through `$EDITOR` (`dashboard.rs:1400`, `:1422`, `:1436`) although the kit's text-input modal has been landed and unused since P468.
      **Where:** all four screens (plus the attached view, which becomes a pane, not a mode) compose from P505's tree — sessions list left, preview right split into progress-top / narration-bottom per the owner's reference layout. GROUPING in the owner's order: **Needs User Input → Pending → Failed → Completed**, Completed collapsed to a count row that expands, live-driver indicator inside Pending, sourced from P504's typed session state. SHORT IDS: display `session-` + 12 hex (matching the existing `wt-<12hex>` convention), uniqueness computed against the WHOLE store, not the visible list — prefix resolution scans the store and hard-errors on ambiguity (`run_session.rs:89-126`), and the `session-` prefix must be preserved literally or the shortened form will not round-trip; full id stays in the detail pane. INPUT: every reason/approval/spawn prompt moves to the kit's modal; the `$EDITOR` temp-file path for reasons is deleted (P471's clause, delivered here); `$EDITOR` survives only for editing a trait SOURCE file. Multi-select lands on the trust screen (P473's block-approve).
      **Watch:** P470-P473 keep their CONTENT contracts (story model, plain-language merge parks, trust wording) and gain `Deps: P505` — this phase delivers the layout and the cross-cutting behaviors, not their per-screen copy; `format!("{r:?}")` for park reasons (`dashboard.rs:529`) must not survive into the new merges pane; per-screen scroll memory replaces the shared reset.
      **Done when:** all screens use the pane tree with named borders and a focus ring; sessions group in the owner's four buckets with Completed collapsible; ids are short and still resolve; no dashboard flow opens `$EDITOR` for a reason; trust supports block-approve with one modal reason.
- [x] **P507** — The live run view moves onto the tree, and the Rect math dies.
      **Why:** the live view does NOT go through the generic frame hook — its layout is welded into terminal-ownership code (`tui_ratatui.rs:322-375` `render_split`) and, worse, its content is budgeted against **terminal rows rather than its own rect** (`run_view.rs:821-828` via `tui::repaint_line_limit()`), so it renders incorrectly inside any pane smaller than the whole terminal.
      **Why — RE-MEASURED 2026-07-27 after an owner run; the original "no key handling at all" premise is STALE and the real defect is worse.** P470 landed real key handling: `apply_keys` (run_view.rs:635-666) binds Tab to `LiveFocus::cycle` and ↑/↓/j/k/PgUp/PgDn to a per-region `tui_kit::ViewportScroll`. So the keys exist — they are simply **bespoke and invisible**. `rg 'FocusRing|PaneScrolls|PaneTree|render_scrollbar|tab_bar|MarkSet' run_view.rs` returns NOTHING: the live view adopted none of P505's kit, so **no scrollbar is ever drawn and no focus ring is ever shown**. A user pressing Tab sees no indication that focus moved, and a user pressing PgDn on a region that fits sees nothing happen and cannot tell the difference from a dead key — which is precisely how "scroll and tab are absolutely broken" gets reported against code that technically runs. Second measured defect: the scroll budget is `state.region_rows`, documented as "the LAST frame's own visible-row budget", initialized to `RegionRows::default()` and only populated after the first `render_story` (run_view.rs:701-714) — so scrolling a region that has not yet painted is a silent no-op against a zero budget. THE ROOT CONDITION for all of it: **this phase has never landed**, so the live view is the one surface still on hand-rolled Rect math while every dashboard screen moved onto the tree in P505/P506 — the two surfaces diverged, and the live view is the half that was left behind.
      **Watch — ALSO VERIFY UNDER A LIVE RUN, not statically:** `tick()` is the SOLE key-polling path (`poll_and_apply_keys`, run_view.rs:516-524) and its own comment claims scroll/focus apply "even during a silent multi-minute frame". That holds only if the harness invokes the `TickObserver` at a usable cadence during a long frame; `RunOutput::tick_observer` (drive.rs:385-392) returns `Some` on the TUI path and `None` for `Self::Stream`, so the wiring is right, but the actual in-frame cadence was NOT established by static reading and must be measured on a real run before this phase claims the keys work.
      **Where:** `RunPanel` renders through `RatatuiPane::draw` and the P505 tree (progress pane + narration pane); every row budget is derived from the pane's rect; the pane switches from `poll_detach` to `poll_key` so panes focus and scroll while ctrl-c keeps its stop semantics; the narration pane consumes P504's activity stream (the thinking pill, running-tool lines) instead of raw stream text. CLEANUP, in this phase: `SplitBounds`/`SplitLayout` and the terminal-row budgeting are DELETED once nothing uses them; the manual clip/wrap helpers survive only where the line-mode renderer needs them (it has no ratatui underneath).
      **Watch:** absorbs P470's STRUCTURAL half — P470 keeps its story model (completed steps collapse to one narrated line, current step verbatim and scrollable) and depends on this; the alt-screen lifecycle guarantees (quitting the pane never kills the run; the panic/suspend/restore ladder) are the safety this leans on and must stay green; narrator threads hold panel clones, so the explicit-close path must survive the refactor.
      **Done when:** the live view renders inside the tree with focusable, independently scrollable progress and narration panes; a pane smaller than the terminal renders correctly; `rg SplitBounds modules/` returns nothing; ctrl-c still stops the run.
- [x] **P543** — The live view's viewport: inline and resizable (owner decision: A, 2026-07-27).
      **Why:** two owner observations — "the live view no longer auto-adjusts to available space, and for long procedures there's never a chance to fully extend" and "it's not full-screen anymore, more like inline" — are ONE root cause, and it is a deliberate design decision, not a regression. `RatatuiPane::new_inline()` (tui_ratatui.rs:299-312) puts the live run pane on `Viewport::Inline` while the dashboard, demo, and trait editor keep the alternate screen (`PaneScreen`, :55-76). P244 chose that on purpose: the caller's scrollback above the pane survives the whole run, and the final frame commits to scrollback on clean teardown instead of vanishing with an alternate-screen restore. **The cost was not stated at the time and is now being paid.** `inline_viewport_height` (:91) resolves the pane's rows ONCE at construction from `crossterm::terminal::size()`, and the code documents the upstream constraint verbatim: *"ratatui 0.29's `Viewport::Inline` never resizes after creation"* (:82). So a terminal resized (or a window maximized) after the run starts never grows the pane — the run is stuck with whatever the terminal was when it launched. Compounding it for long procedures, the run-tree column has its own fixed floor (:99), so a deep sequence has neither a taller pane to grow into nor a wider column to claim.
      **Disposition (owner, 2026-07-27): A — keep the inline viewport and resize it.** This preserves the caller's scrollback and P244's final-frame commit while allowing long live runs to use newly available terminal rows. `Event::Resize` is coalesced by the existing input pump; the run tick transactionally replaces only the inline ratatui terminal at `inline_viewport_height(rows)`, retaining the pane, panel state, scroll/follow state, decode capture, Ctrl-C policy, and terminal lifecycle.
      **Watch:** whichever way this goes, it gates P507 — that phase renders the live view into a rect, and this decides what the rect is and whether it can change size, so deciding after would mean building the tree twice. Do NOT quietly drop P244's scrollback guarantee as a side effect of a layout change: it is a real property the owner uses, and losing it must be a stated decision, not an emergent one. The alt-screen lifecycle safety ladder (panic hook, suspend/restore, quitting the pane never kills the run) is what both mechanisms lean on and must stay green — a reconstruct-on-resize path is a new way to leave a terminal in raw mode, and needs the same rollback discipline `new_with_options` already applies on every failure branch.
      **Done when:** the decision is recorded in the phase with its reason; a terminal resized mid-run reflows the live view (or the phase records that inline-without-resize was deliberately kept and why); a long procedure can use the full height available to it; and if the scrollback property changed, that change is stated in `.docs/PRODUCT.md` rather than discovered.
- [x] **P544** — The live view's input is drained once per second: scrolling is literally 1 FPS (measured 2026-07-27 on a release build, after P507/P543 landed).
      **Why:** owner report — "performance on scrolling in live view is ridiculous, like 1fps." It is not a rendering cost and not a release/debug difference. It is an **input drain rate of 1 Hz**, and the chain is four links: (1) a dedicated pump thread polls crossterm every **100 ms** and pushes keys into an mpsc channel (`tui_ratatui.rs:404`) — keys are READ promptly; (2) that channel is drained ONLY by `RunPanel::tick()` → `poll_and_apply_keys` → `poll_detach()` (`run_view.rs:516-525`, and nothing else drains it); (3) `tick()` is invoked only through the harness `TickObserver`; (4) both harness process-wait loops gate that call behind `if last_tick.elapsed() >= Duration::from_secs(1)` (`modules/io/src/harness.rs:370-374` and `:641-646`). So a keypress lands in a queue within 100 ms and then waits up to a full second for the only thing that empties it. The wait loop itself spins at 50 Hz (`sleep(20ms)`, harness.rs:640) — the one-second gate is a deliberate throttle on the REPAINT heartbeat, and key polling was bolted onto the same callback. **Verified still present at HEAD after P507 and P543 landed**: both gates and the single-drain path are unchanged, so the pane-tree move did not touch input cadence.
      **Why — it is also lurchy, not merely slow.** `apply_keys` drains EVERY queued key in one pass, so holding ↓ for a second queues ~30 auto-repeat events and applies all thirty deltas in a single burst: nothing, then a jump, once per second. And the same 1 Hz latency is why Tab reads as dead — up to a second of delay with (pre-P507) no focus ring to confirm it landed.
      **Where — two mechanisms; the second is the real fix and the first unblocks today.** (a) MINIMAL: move the one-second throttle off input and onto repaint, where it already belongs — `RunPanel::tick` ALREADY separates them (a key-driven change sets `changed = true` and bypasses the `last_timer_paint` throttle; only timer repaints are throttled), so the harness loops can invoke the observer at their own 20 ms cadence and the existing internal throttle still prevents repaint storms. (b) OWNERSHIP: stop pulling. The pump thread already owns input and already has a channel; let it WAKE the panel when a key arrives instead of making the panel wait to be ticked. That removes input responsiveness as a property of "whatever the drive loop happens to be doing" — and fixes the windows where NO harness is running (between frames, during command steps, during a merge), where today nothing ticks at all and input is fully dead rather than merely slow.
      **Watch:** `tick()` takes the state mutex, so raising its call rate to 50 Hz without care puts it in contention with `push_bytes` on a chatty stream — mechanism (a) needs a cheap early-out BEFORE the lock (nothing queued and the repaint throttle not elapsed ⇒ return) or it trades a slow UI for a slow run. Do not "fix" this by lowering the repaint throttle: repainting the story 50×/s is the actual expensive operation and the throttle is correct — input and repaint are two cadences and the bug is that they were ever one. Ctrl-c/detach semantics ride the same drain (`poll_detach` handles ctrl-c inside), so any change here must keep the stop path at least as responsive as it is now, not just the scroll path.
      **Done when:** a held scroll key moves the live view smoothly rather than in one-per-second jumps; Tab reflects focus within ~100 ms; input stays responsive during a step where no harness process is running; and a chatty streaming frame shows no measurable slowdown from the raised tick rate.
- [x] **P545** — The live view onto the pane tree: focus and scroll become VISIBLE (the half of P507 that never landed; verified 2026-07-27 by content search, not by commit message).
      **Why:** P507 was two halves and only one landed. Its INPUT half arrived early under a differently-named commit — `290ded8 "P470: Story model for live --progress TUI run pane"` added `LiveFocus` and `ViewportScroll`, which is why P507's original premise ("no key handling at all beyond ctrl-c") is stale and why searching the log for "P507" answers the wrong question. Its RENDERING half has never existed in any commit on any ref: `git log --all -S"tui_panes" -- run_view.rs tui_ratatui.rs` and the same pickaxe for `FocusRing` both return NOTHING, and `tui_panes` is imported by exactly three modules — `dashboard.rs`, `tui_demo.rs`, `tui_kit.rs`. The live view is not among them. Confirming from the other side, `run_view.rs` at HEAD still carries 12 occurrences of the pre-tree machinery (`region_rows`, `render_story`, `poll_detach`) and zero references to the P505 kit.
      **Why — the user-visible consequence, which is the whole point.** The keys WORK and produce no feedback. Tab cycles `LiveFocus` but nothing renders a focus ring, so the user cannot tell which pane will scroll. Scroll deltas apply to a `ViewportScroll` but nothing draws a scrollbar, so "already at the end" is indistinguishable from "dead key". That is why this surface gets reported as "scroll and tab absolutely broken" against code that runs correctly — the state changes and the screen never says so. Second, concrete defect that dies with the migration: the scroll budget is `state.region_rows`, the PREVIOUS frame's row count, initialized to `RegionRows::default()` — so scrolling a region that has not yet painted is a silent no-op against a zero budget. Deriving budgets from each pane's own rendered rect removes the class.
      **Where:** the live view renders through `PaneTree` + `render_pane` (which returns the inner rect) instead of `RatatuiPane::render_story`'s hand-rolled columns (`tui_ratatui.rs:572` and `render_story_column` at `:1144`); focus moves through `FocusRing`/`tab_cycle_key` (`tui_panes.rs:200,305`) replacing the bespoke `LiveFocus::cycle`, so Shift-Tab works and focus is drawn; every pane gets `render_scrollbar` (`:347`) with `scrollbar_visible` (`:338`) so a fitting region honestly shows no bar; per-pane offsets move to `PaneScrolls` (`:432`), and every row budget derives from the pane's rendered rect rather than `region_rows`. `RegionRows` and `render_story` are deleted once nothing calls them.
      **Watch:** P470's story semantics are BEHAVIOR, not layout — completed steps collapse to one narrated line, the current step shows its full stream, follow-mode is derived from the resulting scroll position (never the key's direction, the `down-key-forces-follow-jump` fix) — all of it must survive verbatim. P543's `apply_resize` transactionally swaps the inline `Terminal` and must keep working across the layout change: scroll offsets, follow flags, cached final-frame lines and decode capture all survive a resize today and must still. Ctrl-C cooperative stop rides `poll_detach`; if the drain changes shape, the stop path must come out at least as responsive, not merely as correct. Coordinate with P544 — that phase changes WHEN input is drained and this one changes what the drain feeds; landing them in either order is fine, landing them blind to each other is not.
      **Watch — LANDING FIDELITY, this phase specifically.** `b21331d` carries the message "SplitBounds, SplitLayout, RegionRows, render_story, and the custom live PaneLayout have been deleted" and "the review verdict is approve — certified the implementation correct with no blocking defects", against a diff of `Justfile | 7 +++----`. All five symbols are alive at HEAD. Whatever else is true, a re-run that finds nothing to do must not emit an approving certification naming deletions that did not happen — this phase's own completion is checked against the TREE (the greps in Done-when), never against a commit message.
      **Done when:** `rg 'tui_panes' modules/cli/src/app/run_view.rs` matches; `rg 'region_rows|render_story' modules/cli/src/app/` returns nothing outside deleted code; the live view draws a visible focus indicator and per-pane scrollbars that hide when a region fits; Tab and Shift-Tab both move focus; scrolling a region works on its first painted frame; and a resize mid-run still preserves scroll offsets and follow flags.
- [x] **P547** — The live view scrolls honestly: a thumb that means something, a slim one, and panes that start at the top (owner-reported 2026-07-27, after `4eaf1a0` put the live view on the pane kit).
      **Why (1) — the scrollbar jitters, jumps to a third or half, then snaps back to the top.** Follow-mode does not clamp the existing offset; it **throws the scroll away and rebuilds it from zero every frame**. `follow_progress` and `follow_tail` (`modules/cli/src/app/run_view.rs:1052-1078`) both run `*scroll = tui_kit::ViewportScroll::new()` — offset 0, i.e. the top — then `set_len(len)` and scroll back down to the target. The scrollbar reads its position from that same state, so on every repaint the thumb is reconstructed against a `len` that grows with the live stream and a `rows` budget captured from the previous frame. Reset-to-top, jump-to-target, resize-because-len-moved, once per repaint: exactly the reported behaviour, and it is a rendering artifact of the follow implementation, not the user's scrolling.
      **Why (2) — the thumb is a full block.** `render_scrollbar` (`tui_panes.rs:347-372`) takes ratatui's default vertical symbols, so the thumb is `█` against a DIM track: correct two-tone, far too heavy for a chrome element that sits on a pane border.
      **Why (3) — there is dead space above the panes, and resize leaves debris in it.** The live pane runs on `Viewport::Inline`, which by construction draws BELOW whatever the terminal already had, so the first rows of the window are never the pane's: the cursor line the run started on stays there for the whole run. `apply_resize` (P543) does clear both the old and the new terminal (`tui_ratatui.rs:391,399`), but an inline viewport that shrinks or grows pushes its previous rows into real scrollback, which no clear can reach — that is the "1-2 lines of artifacts at the top" after a resize. **This one reopens P543's decision.** The owner chose option A (keep inline, resize in place) to preserve scrollback; the ask "make border panes start from the top of the window" is option B (alternate screen). It cannot be satisfied while the view is inline — the gap and the residue are the price of the scrollback guarantee, not a bug on top of it.
      **Where:** (1) follow becomes a CLAMP, never a rebuild — keep the offset, update `len`, and re-pin to the tail only when the offset was already at the tail; the P470 rule that follow is derived from the RESULTING position (never the key's direction) is the invariant to preserve, and rebuilding from zero is what currently violates it. Feed the scrollbar the same window computation the rows are drawn from, the way `render_lines_pane` already pairs them. (2) slim symbols via `Scrollbar::thumb_symbol`/`track_symbol` (a half-block or bar glyph), keeping the DIM-track/default-thumb two-tone. (3) take the owner's call between: stay inline and accept the top gap as documented, or move the live view to the alternate screen and state in `.docs/PRODUCT.md` that the scrollback-preservation property from P244 is gone.
      **Watch:** (1) is the one with a correctness edge — a clamp must still let a user who has scrolled up STAY there while the stream grows underneath them, which the rebuild silently defeats today; test with an actively-streaming frame, not a finished run. Do not "fix" the jitter by throttling repaints: the thumb is wrong, not too frequent. (3) is a decision before it is an implementation — do not switch screens as a side effect of a scrollbar fix.
      **Done when:** the thumb keeps a stable size while a stream grows, tracks the rows actually drawn, and never snaps to the top unless the view is at the top; a user scrolled up stays scrolled up; the scrollbar reads as chrome rather than a solid bar; and the top-of-window question is decided and recorded either way.
- [ ] **P551** — The live view answers immediately, quits deliberately, and kills instantly (owner-reported 2026-07-27).
      **Why (1) — quitting has no deliberate exit, and stopping is not instant.** `q` is unbound in the live pane: P454 narrowed input to ctrl-c only, and `apply_keys` still binds nothing but Tab and the scroll keys, so the one key every terminal user reaches for does nothing. Meanwhile ctrl-c is the opposite problem — it is a COOPERATIVE stop, not a kill: `new_inline()` takes `CtrlCPolicy::RequestStop` (`tui_ratatui.rs:229`) and the pump calls `crate::app::interrupt::request_stop()` (`:335-338`), which asks the run to wind down at its next checkpoint. During a multi-minute harness frame that is a long wait for a key the user pressed to make something stop NOW. The two are swapped: the graceful one is unbound and the abrupt one is graceful.
      **Why (2) — a run opens with a blank pane for as long as setup takes.** The trait is local and cheap to load, but `resume_or_prepare_worktree` and the `[worktree] setup` commands run BEFORE `RunPanel::new` (`drive.rs:~978` vs `:3225`), and this repo's setup is `pnpm install --prefer-offline`. So the terminal shows nothing at all through git worktree creation plus a package install, on a run whose procedure — every step, its title, its role — is fully known from the canonical the moment the trait decodes.
      **Why (3) — the scrollbar reads as noise.** Owner call: the box is enough. `render_scrollbar` (`tui_panes.rs:354`) draws a DIM track with a full-block thumb on the pane's outer rect, and is called from `render_list_pane`/`render_lines_pane` (`:405,:433,:460`).
      **Where:** (1) bind `q` to a confirm dialog (the kit's modal, the same one P468 landed) — Y exits the pane, N returns; the pane closing NEVER kills the run, which is the existing and correct separation. Change `CtrlCPolicy::RequestStop` to an immediate kill: terminate the child process group, not just the ctx process, then restore the terminal and exit non-zero. Update the footer keymap (`run_view.rs:905`, currently `"ctrl-c stop  up/down scroll  tab focus"`) so the two keys read as what they now do. (2) Reorder startup: decode the trait, paint the full procedure with every step pending, and run worktree preparation and setup UNDER that view with their own progress rows — the pane is the first thing on screen, not the last. (3) Delete the `render_scrollbar` calls from the three pane helpers, keeping the bordered box; if any surface genuinely needs one later it opts in explicitly rather than every pane getting it by default.
      **Watch:** an instant kill must still restore the terminal — raw mode off, inline viewport released, cursor shown — or it hands back an unusable shell; the existing panic/restore ladder is what to route through, NOT a bare `exit`. It must also not orphan the harness child or leave the worktree locked: kill the group and release the driver lock, and state plainly in the ledger that the run was killed rather than leaving it looking parked. For (1), confirm which surface the owner is actually seeing a scrollbar on — `run_view` calls only `render_pane` (border, no bar), so the bars come from the dashboard/demo helpers; removing them there is right either way, but do not "fix" the live view by deleting something it never drew. For (2), the procedure is known but the SESSION is not — paint steps as pending from the canonical and never as started until the ledger says so, or the view will claim work that has not begun.
      **Done when:** `q` opens a Y/N confirm and only the pane exits; ctrl-c kills immediately with the terminal restored, no orphaned child, and a ledger that says killed; a run paints its full procedure before worktree setup begins and shows setup progress in-pane; no pane draws a scrollbar.
- [ ] **P552** — The live view reads like a log: one line per event, a four-pane grid, and a narrated session title (owner-specified 2026-07-27, layout sketched by the owner).
      **Why:** three asks on one surface, cheaper together than apart — two of them change the same rect arithmetic.
      **Where (1) — one event, one line, ellipsized.** Target form: `00:19:13  ·  Building Produce: Checked .docs git tracking and finalize...`. The line NEVER wraps: the tail truncates with an ellipsis sized to the pane's inner width. Today both history and current run through `tui_panes::wrapped_lines` (`run_view.rs:917,932`), which reflows a long activity across rows and destroys the one-line-per-event rhythm. Truncate on DISPLAY width, not byte length, and never elide the duration prefix — it is fixed-width and always survives; only the tail goes. **Keeps P548's `{hh}:{mm}:{ss}` exactly as landed** (owner confirmed 2026-07-27); this phase changes wrapping, not glyphs.
      **Where (2) — the 2x2 grid, per the owner's sketch.** `live_pane_tree` (`run_view.rs:813-859`) is a 60/40 horizontal split with `history` over `current activity` on the right and a single `progress` pane on the left. The left column becomes a vertical split mirroring the right, and the content divides as:
      `progress` (top-left, SHORT — a fixed handful of rows): the run's standing facts. `input phase P521`; `harness scribe→opencode · smart-1→opencode · worker→claude-code`; `progress 1/5 · in-progress · Building Produce · 17m 27s · 7.6k tok · narrator 382 tok`.
      `journey` (bottom-left, the tall one): the step list with per-step status and slot flow — `✓ Run the repository gate chain   default   check   done` followed by indented `in none` / `out repo-gates-passed` rows.
      `history` (top-right) and `current activity` (bottom-right) keep today's content and stacking.
      Text sits flush against the border — **no pane padding** (owner dropped it 2026-07-27); the sketch is the reference.
      **Where (3) — the session title, narrated once and persisted.** A header line above the panes: the narrated title in BOLD, then `· <trait name> · Started at <HH:MM:SS>`. It renders EMPTY until the narrator produces it — no placeholder, no fallback text. The narrator is asked exactly once per session, and the result is written onto the run/session record, so it survives the pane closing and the DASHBOARD renders the same title for that run instead of a bare id.
      **Watch:** the title must be generated once and read from the session thereafter — never per repaint, or a 1 Hz repaint becomes a 1 Hz model call. It must never gate the first paint (P551 makes painting-first the rule): the header is simply title-less until the narrator answers, and a run with no narrator configured stays title-less forever, which is correct rather than degraded. Persisting it is what makes it worth doing at all — a title that dies with the pane is not worth a model call. On space: the header costs a row and the fourth pane costs two more rows of border, on an inline viewport already one row short of the terminal — recheck the `area.width < 109` breakpoint and `CURRENT_MIN_OUTER_ROWS`, and keep `progress` bounded to its few standing-fact rows so `journey` gets the remaining height.
      **Where (4) — one renderer, three surfaces (owner-ordered 2026-07-28; absorbs the dashboard-attach ask).** The same pane layout serves the live run, the dashboard preview, and an attached session — only the pane SET and the data source differ. `render_live_panes` (`run_view.rs:1000`) already takes plain line slices plus scroll/focus state and is not coupled to the drive loop, so this is decomposition, not a second renderer.
      LIVE (`ctx traits run`): all four panes, from the running drive.
      DASHBOARD PREVIEW (unattached side pane): `progress` + `journey` ONLY — no history, no current activity. This is what makes the whole thing tractable: a preview needs no live stream, so it renders from the ledger alone. `render_ledger_run_view` returns one flat line list today and must yield those two pane sets instead.
      ATTACHED: FULL SCREEN, identical to what `ctx traits run` shows — all four panes, the session list hidden while attached — ending on the same story (P550) the run itself would show. Today attach reconstructs one flat ledger list into a shared preview pane, which is the "raw lines" this replaces.
      **Watch — for ATTACHED the limit is the data source, not the layout.** A live run is driven by another process, so the dashboard sees its ledger plus `latest_trace_tail`; there is no first-class activity feed until P504's stream is persisted (the P521/P550 sidecar).
