Attach renders history/current from the trace tail until then and moves to the persisted stream when it lands — say which is in use rather than presenting a degraded feed as the real one. The pane SET must be data-driven, never hard-coded per caller: a pane with no source is omitted, which is exactly what makes the preview two panes, so one function serves all three surfaces with no mode flag.
      **Done when:** every history and current-activity entry is one ellipsized line prefixed with its `HH:MM:SS  ·  `; the live view matches the owner's four-pane sketch with progress carrying standing facts and journey carrying the step list; and a bold narrated title appears above the panes once, is stored on the session, and shows for that run in the dashboard. The dashboard preview shows progress+journey only; attaching goes full screen with the same view a live run renders, and ends on the same story.

- [x] **P548** — Activity durations read as clock time: `2m 8s` becomes `00:02:08` (owner-ordered 2026-07-27).
      **Why:** a running timer is something you watch tick and compare against another row, and unit-suffixed text is bad at both — the string changes WIDTH as it crosses each unit boundary (`8s` → `2m 8s` → `1h 5m 3s`), so a column of live durations reflows under the reader and two rows cannot be compared at a glance. Fixed-width clock notation is stable, sorts lexically, and is what every other tool showing elapsed work uses.
      **Where:** `elapsed_text` (`modules/cli/src/app/tui.rs:36-49`) is the single formatter — it already decomposes to hours/minutes/seconds and only the `format!` arms change, to zero-padded `{hh}:{mm}:{ss}`. Its callers are the live activity/frame line (`harness_stream.rs:1027`) and the dashboard's session rows (`dashboard.rs:1091,1371`).
      **Where — the scope call this phase must make, not assume.** `elapsed_text` is ALSO the source of the dashboard's relative-age text at `dashboard.rs:1520`, which renders `format!("{} ago", ...)`. `00:03:20 ago` is worse than `3m 20s ago` — clock notation is right for a DURATION you watch accumulate and wrong for an AGE you read as prose. So this splits the helper rather than editing it in place: `elapsed_text` becomes the clock formatter for activity/session durations, and the relative-age call site gets its own human-units formatter (keeping today's output). Do not change one and let the other inherit it.
      **Watch:** decide and state what happens past 24 hours — unbounded hours (`26:03:04`) keeps it sortable and honest, `1d 02:03:04` reads better; pick one and apply it everywhere rather than letting each surface choose. Column widths in the dashboard's session list are computed from the rendered string (`elapsed_width`, `dashboard.rs:4799`), so a fixed 8-char duration is a chance to stop measuring that column per frame. Test fixtures pin literal duration strings (`dashboard.rs:5549` uses `"1234567890s"`) and will need updating with the format.
      **Done when:** every live activity and session duration renders as fixed-width clock time; relative-age text still reads in human units; the over-24h rule is stated once and applied everywhere; and no column reflows as a timer crosses a unit boundary.

## Group 124: Human input — declared, signal-gated, answerable from the dashboard (owner-ordered 2026-07-25)

Owner decision, after rejecting an agent-raised undeclared pause: *"it still needs to be declared somehow — reviewer emits signal `needs-human-input`, there's a declared ask step that only runs when that signal is present… we shouldn't allow agents to raise all over the place (at least for now)."* Verified 2026-07-25 that this composes from primitives that already exist: steps carry a `when` guard; emission is conditional via `SignalEmissionRule::Derived { signal, when }`; undeclared emission is already REJECTED by the runtime (a signal must be in the current step's `emits`); and — answering the owner's loop question — **signals are scoped to their repeated activation** (`guards.rs:1283-1298` requires same control + same iteration + same item index), so a signal raised in iteration 1 is invisible to a guard in iteration 2. **Nothing needs clearing.**

- [x] **P508** — The `ask` step: a declared, signal-gated human frame with a dynamic reason.
      **Why:** the only mid-run pause today is structural (`AwaitingInput` = a missing `port:` value), so an author cannot say "stop here and ask a human," and a host cannot tell "a value is missing" from "a person must decide." Attach mode already proves the runtime can wait for a human (`drive.rs:2520-2575`) but it is a per-ROLE machine-config knob with no reason payload and a status indistinguishable from "a model is thinking."
      **Where:** new step kind `ask` (CDK `sequence.ask`) + a 5th runtime frame kind (`SequenceFrameKind` has only 4 today) that dispatches NO agent and NO command — it exists to park the run; new session state `waiting-on-human`, strictly distinct from `awaiting-input` (the `starts_with("port:")` discriminator must not absorb it); the REASON is dynamic — the raising step writes the question into a slot and the ask step's prompt interpolates it (`{slot:human-question}`), reusing existing interpolation rather than inventing a payload; the answer binds to the step's declared output slot through the ordinary sink path, so it is schema-validated and readable by later steps like any other value. Canonical shape, all inside a loop so scoping self-resets: `review` emits `needs-human-input` WHEN its verdict says so and writes `slot:human-question`; `ask` declares `when = signal:needs-human-input`; the next step consumes `{slot:human-answer}` as an optional input (P447).
      **THE INVARIANT (non-negotiable):** the pause MUST present as a FRAME (`next_frame == Some(_)`). If the session instead parks with `next_frame == None`, `ctx traits set` routes to `SetResolution::InitialPortValue`, which calls `start_run_session` again and **RESTARTS THE RUN AT STEP 0** — every slot, revision, signal and elapsed second wiped (`io/run.rs:781-841` → `transitions.rs:20-58`). A proof must assert this directly.
      **Watch:** known limits to document rather than paper over — a signal raised INSIDE a loop still matches a guard evaluated AFTER the loop (the scope comparison only constrains levels both sides have), which is the correct one-shot post-loop gate but is a one-way door; and there is NO way to clear a signal at the same scope level, so asking twice at the top level for two separate occurrences is not expressible today (if a real case demands it, add a "consumed" marker on the ask step, never a general clear primitive). Cross-iteration answers hit the landed loop rule that a step cannot read a slot it writes within the same loop — thread those through a pre-loop-seeded slot. Agents stay unable to raise anything not declared in the current step's `emits`.
      **Done when:** a fixture trait pauses at a signal-gated ask with the reviewer's question rendered in the frame; the session reports `waiting-on-human` distinctly from `awaiting-input`; the pause survives a process restart; a `set` against the paused frame lands in the declared slot and the run continues WITHOUT restarting (asserted against the invariant above); iteration 2 does not re-ask unless the signal is re-raised.
- [x] **P509** — Answering from the dashboard, and what the driver does while it waits.
      **Why:** a gate nobody can release is a hang. The dashboard is where a human sees "needs input", so it is where they must be able to answer — in a modal, not `$EDITOR`.
      **Where:** the sessions screen surfaces `waiting-on-human` as its own top group (P506) showing the reason and the wait duration; answering opens the kit's text-input modal and submits through the existing non-destructive current-frame path (`SetResolution::CurrentFrameCall`); the run then resumes. DRIVER SHAPE — **the driver exits while parked** (the landed `paused-provider-credits` shape) rather than blocking attach-style, because a blocking wait charges the run's 30-minute wall-clock `total_seconds` budget (`drive.rs:952-963`), holds the driver lock for the whole human delay, and cannot be killed (`wait_for_attach_advance` never checks the interrupt flag, `drive.rs:2537-2574`); the dashboard already spawns and resumes detached runs, so exit-and-respawn costs nothing. Fix the two attach defects in passing (interrupt check; the double-charged clock) since attach mode remains supported.
      **Watch:** `call`/`set`/`next` do NOT take the driver lock (in-tree ticket `P423 driver-lock-does-not-cover-ledger-writes`) — writing into a live-driven session is an unserialized race resolved only by the optimistic digest guard; with the driver-exits shape this is avoided by construction, and that must stay true. Human-authored answers need honest provenance: `ValueSource::ManualOutput` is currently stamped on the drive loop's OWN agent output too (`session.rs:1445-1449`) and `ModelOutput` is never assigned anywhere — fix that here or a "human wrote this" claim is false.
      **Done when:** a run parked on an ask appears in the dashboard's Needs-User-Input group with its reason; typing an answer in the modal resumes the run to completion; the ledger shows the answer with provenance distinguishing it from model output; killing a parked run works immediately.
- [x] **P536** — The approval gate: see it before it lands — approve, edit, or send back, composed on the ask primitive.
      **Why:** the highest-value human moment is reviewing an agent's PROPOSED output before it becomes accepted state — approve unchanged, edit in place, or send back for redo. The ask primitive gathers input; this phase composes it into the gate pattern every guarded procedure wants at its commit boundary, and `ctx-annotate` finally gets its intended role as the edit channel.
      **Where:** no new runtime machinery — pure composition: an `ask` whose output is a decision schema (approve | edit | reject, with an optional edit body), a `branch` on the decision, the edit arm landing the human's replacement text through a write-op `Replace` on the proposal slot, the reject arm routing through the existing redo loop (the same shape the review loop already uses — a human gate is a human reviewer). CDK sugar (`sequence.gate(step, …)`) so authors declare it in one call; the frame presents the proposed output as the evidence under decision. `ctx-annotate` serves the edit arm where installed (the typed annotation contract); the dashboard modal (P509) and plain CLI both work without it.
      **Watch:** a human decision is audit-critical — it must be authenticated and provenance-stamped, never spoofable by the agent whose output is under review (rides P509's ValueSource fix; the edited text carries human provenance end-to-end into the receipt); reject must NEVER silently fall through as approve — an unanswered or malformed decision stays parked; the gate never mutates the proposal except through the explicit edit arm; reuse the existing redo path exactly, never a second correction flow.
      **Done when:** a fixture procedure parks at a gate showing the proposed output; approve advances unchanged; edit lands the human's text with human provenance in the receipt; reject re-runs the producing step; an agent cannot fabricate a decision; the pattern is one CDK call.

## Group 125: Live tracking and reclamation — a local liveness index, and getting the disk back (owner-ordered 2026-07-25; absorbs P462)

Measured 2026-07-25: **64 GB** of ctx state (22 GB `~/.config/ctx` + 42 GB repo `.ctx/`). The largest single item — **34 GB of build cache** — is unreachable by the prune command the repo's own config comment points at (verified live: `cache prune --build-target` reports zero caches, for two independent reasons — nothing declares `[run.build-cache.*]`, and the real path `cache/<key>/build-target` does not match the required `cache/<key>/build/<name>` shape). Also: **130 of 260 session ledgers have no deletion path at all** (delete is TUI-only and gated to Completed/Failed, so every cancelled run is undeletable), 21 zero-byte corrupt ledgers, 220 zero-byte driver-lock files never unlinked, 206 of 216 trust records orphaned with no removal verb, 106 run branches already merged into main, 40 worktree registrations whose directories are gone, 165 empty debug dirs from per-worktree repo-key explosion, and an inert 530 MB `salvage/` directory written by nothing in the codebase. SQLite was considered and rejected for this (owner agreed): the live set is 6 runs while the cost is re-parsing the 254 dead ones; a database row cannot tell you a process died, so the lock probing survives either way; and today's opencode cleanup was the counter-example — deleting 3,616 rows freed zero bytes without a VACUUM while its DB sits at 6.2 GB.

- [x] **P510** — A local liveness index + a scan that stops re-reading everything.
      **Why:** the dashboard re-parses **49 MB across 260 ledgers every 2 seconds** (159-434 ms per tick measured; 0 of 260 ledgers actually changed in a live 2-second window) and it still cannot answer "what is running machine-wide" without a full multi-repo scan; separately, nothing cheaply links a dead run to what it left behind (192 ledgers reference worktrees that no longer exist).
      **Where:** (a) LIVE INDEX — one small file the driver upserts on start and removes on clean exit, carrying session, repo, worktree path, branch, log path, start time, pid. It lives in a **per-user runtime directory on LOCAL disk** (owner requirement), the same class of path the control sockets already use for the same reason — so `flock` is always reliable, a reboot correctly clears all liveness state, a copied/moved ledger cannot inherit stale liveness, and an NFS home directory stops being a silent correctness bug. Ledgers, traces and receipts stay in the durable config store. If no local runtime path is obtainable, report liveness as UNKNOWN rather than claiming dead. (b) LIVENESS IS THE LOCK, NOT THE ROW — the row is the breadcrumb (it is designed to survive death); the file lock is the oracle (it cannot: the kernel drops it when the process dies by any means, including `kill -9`). Orphan = row present + lock free, and the row names exactly what to clean. Self-heal both ways: drop rows whose ledger is gone, adopt held locks with no row (externally started runs must appear normally). (c) SCAN — mtime+size-gated parse cache (measured 352 ms → 0.7-1.0 ms) plus a ~200-byte `<ledger>.summary.json` sidecar written inside the existing atomic-rename boundary, carrying the three facts that exist NOWHERE today: **start time, numeric exit code, worktree path**. The sidecar is derived evidence, never authority — a missing or unparseable one falls back to parsing the ledger. (d) BUG: `run_control::probe` clears metadata on every uncontended probe, so the dashboard currently flocks and TRUNCATES ~226 files every 2 seconds just to draw a list.
      **Watch:** no daemon (standing doctrine: "the store is the interface"); the index is a pointer index like `repos.toml`, never a payload cache; host-supplied and ledger-derived ids stay path-validated; sidecar drift is contained by making it non-authoritative, exactly as lock metadata already is.
      **Done when:** a steady-state dashboard tick is ~1 ms with 260 ledgers; "what is running" machine-wide is one small read; a `kill -9`'d run is correctly reported dead with its worktree and branch named; probing no longer writes to lock files; start time and exit code are recorded.
- [x] **P511** — Retention: caps on regenerable things, tiered pruning, and never a timer on evidence.
      **Why:** nothing in the product deletes anything by age today (deliberate — a receipt should not vanish because it got old), but nothing deletes them at all either, so everything accumulates forever. The owner's constraint: reclaim aggressively where it is safe, and **never destroy the only copy of work**.
      **Where:** RULE — session ledgers and the audit journal are receipts and are never age-deleted (optionally compressed after merge). Everything regenerable gets a configurable limit with a default: shared build cache — size cap (~10 GB/repo, evict oldest); debug traces — last ~50 runs or 7 days; spawn/resume logs — 7 days; corrupt zero-byte ledgers — immediate; driver-lock files — unlink on clean release (a bug fix, not policy). TIERED WORKTREE PRUNE, the load-bearing part: a worktree holds three different things and they get three different rules — build artifacts (typically >90% of the bytes) pruned IMMEDIATELY and automatically on any terminal outcome including cancel; committed work lives on the branch, so a CLEAN worktree is redundant and its directory can go (recreatable on demand); UNCOMMITTED work is never auto-deleted. Within artifacts, tier by rebuild cost: cheap-and-huge (incremental caches) goes at once, expensive (compiled deps) survives a grace window — default "until the branch merges, or N days without a resume" — so a run resumed the same day rebuilds warm. DECLARED, NOT HARDCODED: ctx must not know what `target/` is (P428 doctrine) — the worktree config declares regenerable paths per tier, e.g. cheap `target/**/incremental`, `node_modules/.cache`; expensive `target`. Always state the cost before it is paid: once the expensive tier is gone the row reads "resume will rebuild (~8 min)".
      **Watch:** rehydration is REBUILDING, not restoring — build artifacts are derived and unrecoverable, and archiving them is not worth it (compressing 8 GB of object files costs minutes to save perhaps half); the shared build cache exists but the TEST path deliberately opts out per checkout, because two worktrees sharing one target dir let cargo accept a sibling's stale artifacts as fresh — that correctness-over-disk tradeoff stands, and the tiered prune is what makes it affordable, not a reason to undo it; expiration NEVER runs in a background daemon — it applies at explicit sweep time or at the end of a run, and always reports what it removed.
      **Done when:** a cancelled 8 GB run drops to a few hundred MB within seconds of ending with no prompt; its branch and any uncommitted work are untouched; resuming inside the grace window is warm and outside it is warned; every limit is configurable and every default is documented.
- [x] **P512** — The reclaim surface: one honest list, one command, and the prune bug that hides 34 GB.
      **Why:** what cannot be auto-resolved must be visible and one keypress away, or it silently becomes 64 GB again. Today there is no such view, and the one command that claims to cover the largest item does nothing at all.
      **Where:** (a) FIX the build-cache prune so it reaches the real directory (both the declaration gate and the path-shape gate), because the remedy printed in this repo's own config comment is currently a verified no-op. (b) ONE INVENTORY, two surfaces — a command (`--dry-run` by default) and a dashboard screen reading the same data, sorted by bytes, each row naming what it is, how old, and WHY it is still there: `wt-38495d9d  8.2G  cancelled 3d ago  clean tree, branch merged  → reclaim` / `wt-b041fb16  1.1G  failed 5d ago  UNCOMMITTED CHANGES  → keep`. Classes covered, all measured today: orphaned worktree registrations (40), run branches already merged into main (106), unmerged branches (49 — report only, never offer bulk delete), corrupt ledgers (21), undeletable-but-dead sessions (130), orphaned trust records (206 — report-first, never automatic; trust is a security boundary), empty per-worktree state dirs (165), the inert `salvage/` directory (530 MB). (c) A typed CANCELLED outcome so a deliberate stop is distinguishable from a crash worth resuming. (d) Give ledgers a deletion path that reaches dead-but-resumable sessions and works outside the TUI.
      **Watch:** never offer a bulk action over unmerged branches or dirty worktrees; report-don't-delete is the default posture everywhere (matching `find_orphans` and doctor's remedy-strings-only stance); every destructive action names exactly what it will remove and its size first; absorbs P462, whose charter predicted this debris class before it recurred.
      **Done when:** the list accounts for essentially all reclaimable bytes on a machine like this one; the build-cache prune actually reclaims; one approved sweep takes this repo from ~64 GB to single-digit GB without touching an unmerged branch or an uncommitted file; a cancelled run is distinguishable from a crashed one.

## Group 126: Commit-declared debt — what the last 120 commits admit is unfinished (audited 2026-07-26)

Audit method: read the FULL bodies of the last ~120 commits on main (`d133f4d` 2026-07-22 → `c13a980` 2026-07-26) for self-declared incompleteness — typed `Leftovers:` blocks, forgiveness notes, "design-first slice only", "review round ended unapproved" — then verified every claim against the working tree. Result: 21 items no phase covers, 13 chartered-but-narrower, 19 uncertified landings: P487 covers 5 and P519 covers the other 14 named below. The in-code marker sweep came back CLEAN — no stray TODO/FIXME/unimplemented survives; the real "not yet" surface is typed refusals (`NotYetSupported` for `git+`/`path:` specs, `unsupported: bool` on the drift/check layers, ~30 deliberate `CapabilityReport::unsupported` sites).

- [x] **P513** — The gate is green again: four red checks, none of them in `just test`.
      **Why:** the gate is currently red AND empty at the same time. `just test` runs `cargo test --workspace`, which includes `proof_identity_scrub` — and that suite **fails against its own source file**, because its needle literals (`"rpunkfu"`, `"/Users/"`, `"/home/"`) live at `modules/cli/tests/proof_identity_scrub.rs:59` with no self-allowlist entry. Four consecutive phases have shipped past it, each re-diagnosing it in its own commit body. Separately red and NOT in the gate: `cargo fmt --check` (a one-line module-ordering diff at `modules/cli/src/app.rs:59-60` traced to P244's merge, rustfmt's default `reorder_modules`), `just sdk-check` (below), and `just ts-format-check` (pre-existing dprint drift in `packages/cdk/src/condition.ts`, named in P500 and P501 and never applied).
      **Where:** allowlist the identity-scrub suite's own source; apply `cargo fmt` to the module ordering; apply `dprint fmt` to condition.ts; then decide which of fmt/sdk-check/ts-format-check belong IN the gate rather than beside it — a red check nobody runs is not a check.
      **Watch:** SDK-CHECK IS THE INTERESTING ONE — `just sdk-check` has been dark for days because `packages/cdk/src/trait.ts:665` exports `Tone` via `deprecatedNamespace(tone, "Tone")` while `sdk_generate.rs:808-822` requires a literal `export const Tone = tone;`, and nine constants share that shape (`Tone, Method, Verbosity, Directness, ScopeControl, Initiative, Uncertainty, Format, Intent`). It has been named as a leftover in TEN separate commits, each spending review budget re-proving it. Deleting the PascalCase shims closes it — but that is P459's clause, blocked behind P458, blocked behind P432's implementation (see P520). So EITHER fix the validator to accept the shim shape as an interim, OR record that sdk-check stays dark until that chain clears — do not leave it ambiguous a eleventh time.
      **Done when:** `just test`, `cargo fmt --check` and `just ts-format-check` are green on a clean checkout; `sdk-check` is either green or has a recorded, dated decision naming what unblocks it.
- [x] **P514** — Owner migration: this repo's config is one `install-bin` away from bricking (OWNER-RUN).
      **Why:** P476 made legacy `[agent]` / `[agent.master]` keys a **hard decode error** naming their replacement. This repo's `.ctx/config.toml` still declares `[agent]`, `[agent.master]`, `[agent.merger]`, `[agent.merger-deep]` and `[agent.narrator]` alongside the new `[agent.role.*]` tables. The moment the new binary is installed without a simultaneous hand-edit, EVERY `ctx traits` invocation in this checkout dies at config decode — the same failure class as the 2026-07-24 `[merge] gate` incident. Standing policy forbids the loop from migrating its own substrate, so this is an owner operation by construction.
      **Where:** migrate the five legacy tables to `[agent.role.*]` at the same moment the new binary is installed; while in the file, re-declare `[merge] gate = [["just","--list"],["just","test"]]` — it is currently COMMENTED OUT, so every landing since 2026-07-24 has run zero repository checks (combined with the byte_compare battery removal in 54706ca and the compile probe removal in 46fe5fb, this repo's pre-landing gate is presently nothing). Ship a `doctor --migrate-config [--apply]` that prints the exact rewrite so this is mechanical rather than hand-edited, and so USERS hitting the same wall have a path.
      **Watch:** install and config edit must be one operation, in that order, with the loop stopped — a half-migrated config is a dead checkout; verify with `ctx traits doctor --config` before starting any run.
      **Done when:** the config parses under the current binary with no legacy tables; the merge gate is declared again and a landing demonstrably runs it; the migrate path exists for users.
- [x] **P515** — Repo-trait drift: a live dogfood trait whose source and canonical disagree, with no gate that could notice.
      **Verdict (2026-07-26):** the row understated the damage — two more canonicals had drifted beyond implement-quick (`benchmark-refactor`, and `refactor-direct` whose drift silently dropped the `annotation-fidelity` intent requirement; restored in `source/index.ts` before rebuilding so the rebuild is a behavior no-op). All three rebuilt, ten stale `trait.lock` files refreshed (incl. `process-kit-demo`'s first lock and two projection-lock exports), and `just repo-traits-drift` lands in `just test` reusing `ctx traits check`'s in-memory `cdk-drift` section rather than cloning `builtin-traits-drift`'s temp-root mechanism (repo traits import `@ctx-traits/agents`, which breaks that mechanism outside the real worktree). Trust re-approval for the three moved digests is a human step, not done here.
      **Why:** `bfcd016` rebuilt `generated/index.toml` for implement-default/phase/smart/strict but NOT implement-quick — which got only `source/*.ts` and `trait.toml` — and refreshed zero `trait.lock` files across the five implement plus refactor-*/auto-research packages. A later commit confirms it: *"implement-quick's committed generated/index.toml is still pre-kit."* `just builtin-traits-drift` covers only `modules/core/builtins/`; there has NEVER been a drift gate over `.ctx/traits/*`, which is where every dogfood trait lives.
      **Where:** rebuild implement-quick's canonical from its kit-shaped source; refresh the locks; re-approve machine trust for the moved digests; then extend the drift check to repo traits so source/canonical divergence cannot recur silently. NOTE (2026-07-26): if Group 128 is dispatched first, its P532 subsumes the rebuild/lock/trust half for the whole store — this row then reduces to the drift GATE alone. Do not do the rebuild twice.
      **Watch:** trust re-approval is a human step (digests move); the drift gate must handle the legitimately-absent case (a trait with no TS source) rather than failing it.
      **Done when:** every `.ctx/traits/*` package rebuilds byte-identically from its source; locks are current; a deliberate source edit without a rebuild fails the gate.
- [x] **P516** — Deferred riders that lost their home: six orphans with no phase.
      **Verdict (2026-07-26):** three implemented, three retired with dated reasons recorded at the sites the leftovers named. (a) `StaleReason::Compacted` deleted (`ledger.rs`) — zero producers, and `last_cleared_reason` already covers noticing a compaction. (b) `argv-from` refuses a `{resource:x}`-shaped token with a typed error instead of silently passing it through (`frame_builders.rs`/`command_contract.rs`) — the doctrine hole was not real, the silence was. (c) `gitRefCapture`'s inline `--eval` retired-in-place (`steps.ts:7`), no rebuild, no digest movement — inline argv is already canonical-digest-covered; extraction is a style question for Group 128, not a security fix. (d) `doctor --config` renders `worktree.confinement.{enabled,sandbox,allow}`, and the tripwire pair's provenance bug (winner lookup on the row key instead of the whole-table key, always reporting `built-in`) is fixed alongside via `add_as`. (e) harness conversation ids now persist to a per-run sidecar (`run_branch::{write,read}_harness_sessions`) seeded on `drive` start and threaded through both the cold and warm dispatch channels; a resumed id that cannot actually be sent (harness declares neither `session-flag` nor `resume-flag`) is now a reported capability, not a silent no-op. The live-harness acceptance test (does `--resume` compose with warm `stream-json`) is owner-run, not done here. (f) same-cache concurrency retired: ctx does not add cross-ecosystem locking (`PRODUCT.md:3369-3375` rewritten in place, dangling `P343` pointer removed), plus one visibility row (`run.build-cache.<name>.dir`) so the shared directory is inspectable at `doctor --config`.
      **Why:** each was deferred out of a landed phase with an explicit leftover and no successor row. (a) `StaleReason::Compacted` was deferred out of P498 INTO P499, then P499 landed and deferred it again — the variant at `context/ledger.rs:68` now has ZERO producers, so the ledger advertises a capability (noticing an unreported compaction) that no code path provides. (b) `argv-from` command steps cannot resolve `{resource:<id>}` refs (`frame_builders.rs:631-661` never calls `collect_resource_argv`; `command_contract.rs:183` skips them), so a caller-supplied argv can never be digest-pinned — a hole in P430's "code launched from a resource is pinned" doctrine. (c) `gitRefCapture`'s inline `--eval` script (`.ctx/traits/auto-research/source/steps.ts:7`, used twice) is still unpinned, with `needs: []`. (d) `doctor --config` has no `worktree.confinement` rows although P479 added them for `worktree.tripwire`, `needs: []`. (e) a shared harness session does NOT survive a `drive` resume — a `--session <id>` resume opens a fresh conversation instead of rejoining, `needs: []`. (f) two runs writing one declared build cache can race; the "separately scoped follow-on" P428 named was never created.
      **Watch:** (a) is the one with a correctness smell — either give `Compacted` a producer or delete the variant, but do not ship an enum that lies; four of the six carry `needs: []`, meaning nothing blocks them and they were pure budget casualties.
      **Done when:** each of the six is either implemented or explicitly retired with a dated reason recorded where the leftover was raised.
- [x] **P517** — Confinement completion: the two harnesses P478 did not reach, and one unresolved permission question.
      **Why:** P478 landed `render_claude_code_settings` and `render_opencode_permission`; there is no `render_codex_*`, and `confinement_unsupported_capability("codex", …)` still fires — while Group 115's own preamble names FOUR harnesses. The commit says so outright and asks for an owner reading of whether P478 closes at two or four. Separately, the opencode payload unconditionally seeds `permission.edit "*": "allow"` (pre-existing from `0b6dfae`), and the commit flags that nobody has confirmed how opencode resolves unlisted paths under `--auto` — a live unknown inside shipped confinement code.
      **Where:** the codex renderer (codex's own `sandbox_mode` is OS-enforced, so this is config generation, not a new mechanism); resolve the opencode `edit` seed by either narrowing it or recording the permissive default as a deliberate, documented limit; pi stays extension-level until `ctx-sandbox` (P480), as already recorded.
      **Watch:** do not claim four-harness confinement in docs until it is four; each harness's honest capability report is the contract users read.
      **Done when:** a codex worktree spawn is confined rather than reported-unsupported; the opencode edit-permission question has a dated answer in code or docs.
- [ ] **P518** — `.docs/PRODUCT.md` reconciliation: 30+ named-stale sections, and one line actively blocking work.
      **Why:** PRODUCT.md is named as a leftover in SEVENTEEN commits in this window, every one deferring on "the phase contract outranks the product doc." It has now crossed from stale into harmful: `PRODUCT.md:81` still reads *"do not add software tests or test harnesses beyond the existing byte-comparison harness"* while the Justfile has that battery commented out and `modules/cli/tests/` holds twenty `proof_*.rs` suites — and a 2026-07-26 commit cites that exact line as the POLICY GATE preventing a P502 integration test. A stale doc is now vetoing work. It also contradicts the Justfile on park exit codes (doc says 2; wrapper uses 3/4/5).
      **Where:** one reconciliation pass over the named-stale sections, led by `:81`/`:3098` (the testing non-negotiable) and `:2835` (exit codes); state the current gate honestly (drift/embed/brand + builds + `cargo test --workspace`, byte_compare disabled, per 54706ca) and the current exit contract; sweep the remaining named sections against the code.
      **Watch:** the doc must not re-enter the same state — either something regenerates the volatile sections or the doc stops asserting facts that live in code; do NOT reintroduce a testing prohibition that the exploration-chapter ruling already superseded.
      **Done when:** no commit-named-stale section remains wrong; the testing line reflects reality; the exit codes agree; the blocked integration test is unblocked or the block is a deliberate, current decision.
- [x] **P520** — Unblock the longest chain, and finish what P485 measured short of.
      **Why:** one root blocks five items. `P432`'s design doc landed with four unresolved blockers and its S2 (count-to-count comparison) and S3 (the shared `sequence.check`-as-loop-guard CDK lowering helper) were never implemented; P458 carries a hard DISPATCH GUARD forbidding re-dispatch until P432's implementation lands; P459's shim deletion waits on P458's call-site migration; and that shim deletion is what would close `just sdk-check` (P513). P435's `plan-gate` is meanwhile shipping a deliberately weak two-independent-range form pending P432's count-to-count support. Separately, P485 LANDED WITH ITS OWN ACCEPTANCE TEST FAILING: its Done-when requires cast count down ≥60% from 232; the commit records 217→179, a 17.5% reduction, with the shortfall filed as leftovers naming normalize.ts (32 casts) and sequence.ts (36).
      **Where:** implement P432 S2 + S3 and clear the design doc's four blockers; then P458's guard lifts. Separately decide P485: either finish to the 60% target (the two named files carry 68 of the 179) or amend the Done-when to what the codebase can honestly reach — but do not leave a phase marked landed whose acceptance test it failed.
      **Watch:** this is the highest-leverage sequencing item in the plan — five stalled rows share one root; P458's guard exists for a real reason (an earlier attempt invented schema support out of contract) and must not be lifted informally.
      **Done when:** P432's implementation lands and its blockers are closed; P458 is dispatchable; P485 either meets its target or carries an amended one.

## Group 127: The shipped dashboard is unusable — landed defects, diagnosed 2026-07-26

Owner report while using the installed binary: live preview frozen even when attached; stop/remove do nothing; no way to give human input; traits scroll only moves the list; traits show generated TOML instead of authored source; trust shows "(orphaned) (orphaned)"; every view still shows full ids; views convoluted. Diagnosis against HEAD `c13a980` (the installed binary is byte-identical to `target/release/ctx`): **P465-P474 ALL LANDED and are in the running binary** — the plan checkboxes are stale, not the code. Every complaint is either a bug in landed code or a chartered-unbuilt item (P505-P510), and ONE defect explains most of the symptoms. Group 123's pane tree remains the structural answer; this group is the triage that makes the dashboard usable before it.

- [x] **P522** — The reload storm: ~4 seconds of blocking work every 2 seconds, on the render thread.
      **Why:** THIS IS THE ROOT CAUSE of "frozen preview" and "keys do nothing". `State::reload()` runs synchronously on the UI thread every 2s (`dashboard.rs:1145-1148`) and unconditionally, on EVERY screen, does three expensive things: fully deserializes every ledger (291 ledgers / 60.9 MB measured, plus a legacy dual-read of 79 more, ~0.38s); probes 257 driver locks — each of which OPENS, FLOCKS, READS AND TRUNCATES the lock file (`run_control.rs:400-401`), so the dashboard rewrites 257 files every 2s to draw a list; and calls `load_traits_and_trust()` (`dashboard.rs:690`) which runs a FULL `build_check_report` drift check per trait — 8 traits × ~420ms = **3.37s**. Total ≈4s of blocking work per 2s tick: the loop cannot draw or drain the key channel while inside it.
      **Where — TWO HALVES, in this order.** (1) STOP DOING THE WORK: screen-gate `load_traits_and_trust()` to the Traits/Trust screens; move `dashboard_trait_drift` out of the inventory and compute it lazily for the selected row only (the list no longer shows drift — P471 moved it to the preview); mtime+size-gate the ledger parse; stop `probe()` truncating metadata. This alone takes the steady-state tick from ~4s to ~1ms. (2) THE RENDER THREAD NEVER TOUCHES THE FILESYSTEM (owner-ordered 2026-07-26): even after (1), unavoidable slow operations remain — the cold first load, a drift check when selection lands on a trait row (~420ms), the merge retry which today freezes the TUI for MINUTES and whose confirm modal literally admits it, `explain` (P526), spawn, and the editor round-trip. Split responsibilities: the render thread only draws and handles keys; a single worker owns all IO and publishes immutable state snapshots the renderer swaps in (`Arc` handoff — the renderer never locks against IO); long actions become tracked tasks whose pane shows a live "working…" state with elapsed time and stays interactive. Drawing itself is microseconds — the ONLY reason the UI has ever frozen is IO on the render thread, and after this it is structurally impossible.
      **Watch:** one writer for state (the worker), so no lock contention and no torn reads; a task that fails reports into the pane, never panics the loop (see P524's error containment); P510 is the full storage-side version (liveness index + summary sidecar) — do not build a second caching mechanism it would replace, and keep the snapshot shape compatible with it.
      **Done when:** a steady-state tick is ~1ms with 291 ledgers; NO filesystem call happens on the render thread; a merge retry runs with the UI still responsive and its progress visible; keys respond immediately during a cold load.
- [x] **P523** — Display hygiene: short ids everywhere, reasons before ids, columns that clip instead of shift, no Debug leaks.
      **Why:** ids are `session-` + 64 hex = **72 characters**, formatted with `{:<24}` — a MINIMUM width, so the row never clips, it SHIFTS: the SESSIONS row needs 137 columns (158 in ALL mode) into a pane capped at 100, so state/phase/elapsed/tokens are unreachable at ANY terminal width; TRUST needs 83 into 70; MERGES needs 92 into 70. Below 91 columns SESSIONS does not split at all — no preview pane appears and nothing says one exists. The same truncation hides every refusal message, because each one LEADS with the 72-char id (`dashboard.rs:2321, 2352, 2375, 2405, 2409, 2675...`) — which is why a refused stop/delete looks like "nothing happened". And P467's own Done-when ("zero `{:?}` in human output") is unmet: `SequenceKind::ForEach` renders as `foreach` (`dashboard.rs:1615`), the MERGES list stage column is raw Debug while the same screen's preview uses proper sentences (`:3585` vs `:2254`), and `run_view.rs:2264` emits `loop {name:?}` with literal Rust quotes into the preview.
      **Where:** a `short_session_id()` helper (`session-` + 12 hex, matching the existing `wt-<12hex>` convention; a `short_digest()` already exists and is used only in two places) applied to all four list widgets, all modal bodies, and every `state.message`; refusal messages put the REASON first and a short id second; `{:<n}` becomes explicit truncation so columns cannot shift; the remaining `{:?}` sites use the serde names.
      **Watch:** display shortening must not break prefix resolution — uniqueness is computed against the whole store, and the `session-` prefix must be preserved literally (P506's clause); full ids stay in detail panes and `--json`.
      **Done when:** every list fits its pane at 80 columns; a refused action's reason is readable; `rg '\{:\?\}' modules/cli/src/app` finds no human-output format strings.
- [x] **P524** — Stop and delete actually work, and a failed action does not kill the TUI.
      **Why:** the key routing and modal plumbing are CORRECT — the gates are the problem. `can_kill` requires `SessionClass::Live` (driver lock held) and `can_delete` requires `Terminal` (Completed|Failed only), while everything else — `awaiting-agent-output`, `blocked`, `rejected`, `awaiting-input` — classifies as `Resumable` and can do NEITHER. Measured on the owner's store: 158 completed (deletable), **112 rows that can do neither**, plus 21 unreadable rows that can do nothing at all; rows sort Live-first then most-recent, so the top of the list is exactly the undeletable ones. Worse, action errors propagate out of `handle_key` and tear down the whole dashboard instead of setting a message (`:2683`, `:1247`, `:1266`), and the confirm modal renders its body as a single unwrapped line in a hardcoded 60×6 rect (`tui_kit.rs:636-668`), so the artifact list P469 promises before a delete is never visible.
      **Where — replace the four-value class with a PER-VERB decision from (lock state, status, artifacts)** (owner-ordered 2026-07-26: "we need to be able to remove/stop orphaned stuff"). DELETE is allowed whenever no live driver holds the lock — Resumable included (the 112), Unreadable included (the 21 corrupt zero-byte ledgers, which hold no evidence and are currently undeletable forever), and orphaned rows whose worktree or branch is gone; a non-terminal row gets a stronger confirm that says what is being discarded ("this run is resumable — deleting it discards that"). STOP branches on the lock rather than refusing: lock HELD → cooperative stop as today; lock FREE but the ledger claims running → the row is ORPHANED, so offer "clear the stale lock" (and record the run as cancelled per P512's typed outcome) instead of a refusal. Contain action errors into the pane; wrap the confirm body and size the rect from it; fix the wording — "is not currently attached" describes the dashboard's own state, not the absence of a driver process.
      **Watch:** never delete a row whose lock is HELD (that check stays absolute, re-verified after the modal confirms, as the existing code already does); deleting a session still removes ledger + lock + sidecars + worktree + branch only under the existing safety checks, and never touches an unmerged branch or a dirty worktree without saying so (P511/P512's rules apply here — this screen is where a human meets them).
      **Done when:** a cancelled/blocked/corrupt/orphaned session can be removed from the dashboard; a stale-locked row can be cleared; a failing action shows a message instead of exiting the TUI; the delete confirmation lists exactly what it will remove and what it will leave.
- [x] **P525** — The preview is genuinely live.
      **Why:** three independent causes. (a) The reconstruction is fed EMPTY presentation state and `narration: None` (`run_view.rs:1039-1077`: `step_summaries: &BTreeMap::new()`, `finished_durations: &BTreeMap::new()`), because P470's story model lives only in the in-process `RunPanel` that owns the live narration channel — so the dashboard can only ever draw a static step tree. (b) `state_digest` moves only at frame boundaries, so during a 5-15 minute agent turn the ledger is byte-identical and the refresh correctly short-circuits — there is no heartbeat. (c) A LATCH BUG: the `Err` arm of `refresh_attached_view` (`dashboard.rs:1404-1429`) writes an `(unreadable: …)` line but does NOT invalidate `view.state_digest`, so the next successful read short-circuits and the pane shows the error FOREVER until the digest happens to move — a transient read during the driver's atomic rename sticks the pane permanently.
      **Where:** clear `view.state_digest` in the `Err` arm (one line, fixes the permanent stick); append the debug-trace tail to the SUCCESSFUL reconstruction as well as the fallback (`dashboard.rs:1480-1489`) — it is the only sub-step-granular signal on disk today; the real fix is P504's persisted activity stream, which gives the reconstruction the same material the live panel has.
      **Watch:** do not add a ledger heartbeat write just to move the digest — that would put write amplification on every run to serve a viewer; the activity sidecar is the right carrier.
      **Done when:** an attached preview of a live run visibly advances during a single agent turn; a transient read error self-heals on the next tick.
- [x] **P526** — Traits show the source you wrote, and can explain themselves.
      **Why:** an off-by-one directory. `dashboard_trait_editable_source` (`lifecycle_reporting.rs:1068-1086`) does `path.parent()` on a trait path that is already `<root>/generated/index.toml`, so it looks for `source/index.ts` inside `generated/`, finds nothing, and falls through to returning the generated TOML itself. Consequences: the preview shows 40 lines of generated TOML, the header labels it `source:`, and `e` opens the GENERATED ARTIFACT in `$EDITOR` — edits there are silently discarded by the next build. `ctx_traits_io::layout::package_root_for_manifest()` already implements the correct resolution and is not called.
      **Where:** use `package_root_for_manifest(path)` instead of `path.parent()`; render the resolved editable path in the header. Then the owner's second ask: an LLM-assisted plain-language explanation of a trait, requested from the dashboard and **cached per canonical digest** so it is generated once per version and re-read instantly afterwards (the built-in `explain-trait` meta-trait is the natural engine; note P320-P322 charter its LLM half and were never built — decide whether this consumes them or supersedes them).
      **Watch:** the explanation is a CACHE keyed by digest, never authority — a digest change invalidates it; never present a generated explanation as the trait's own declared text; explaining costs model calls and must be explicitly requested, never on preview.
      **Done when:** the traits preview shows authored TypeScript; `e` opens the authored file; requesting an explanation produces plain language once per digest and is instant thereafter.
- [x] **P527** — Trust: stop saying "orphaned" twice, and stop rendering 208 dead rows.
      **Why:** the doubled label comes from two independent sources in one format string (`dashboard.rs:3644-3650`): the id column falls back to `"(orphaned)"` when `trait_id` is None, and the very next column renders `TrustClass::Orphaned` → `"orphaned"`. The preview doubles it too (`(orphaned record)` + `origin: orphaned`). Because the pane caps at 70 columns and the row needs 83, those two words are often ALL that fits — hence the owner seeing literally "(orphaned) (orphaned)". Underneath is the real problem: 216 records of which **208 are orphaned** (96%), deliberately all rendered "so nothing in trust.toml becomes invisible", with no filter, no collapse, and no removal verb anywhere; every one refuses approve/block.
      **Where:** for an orphan row render the digest as the identity column and drop the redundant class column; drop `origin:` from the orphan preview; collapse the orphan block to a single expandable count row (or a filter key). Pair with P512's `trust prune --orphaned` so the rows can actually leave.
      **Watch:** trust is a security boundary — collapsing the display must never make a record harder to find deliberately, and pruning stays report-first and human-approved.
      **Done when:** no row says "orphaned" twice; the screen leads with the 8 live records; orphans are reachable in one keypress and removable through P512.
- [x] **P528** — Focus you can see, and scroll that stays where you left it.
      **Why:** TRAITS detail scrolling IS wired correctly (`Enter` → `Focus::Detail` → `route_detail_focus_key` → `detail_scroll`) — but `Enter` produces ZERO visual change on TRAITS/MERGES/TRUST: no border, no title, no footer change, list keeps its highlight. So the user presses `j`, watches the master list move (because Master focus routes `j` to selection), and concludes the preview cannot scroll. The footer never mentions it. Separately, `State` holds ONE shared `ScrollList` and `switch_screen` resets it, so every Tab press sends every screen back to row 0; and `route_detail_focus_key` clamps with `usize::MAX` so the stored offset can run past the real bottom.
      **Where:** SPLIT THE KEYS SO THE COMMON CASE NEEDS NO FOCUS DANCE (owner-ordered 2026-07-26): `j`/`k`/arrows always move the LIST, `PageUp`/`PageDown` always scroll the PREVIEW — on every screen, in Master focus, with no `Enter` required. Focus (and `Enter`) survives only for the modes that genuinely need it, e.g. the sessions attached view. Then make focus visible when it IS engaged (the interim is a title row / footer change; P505's bordered panes are the real answer), add the key hint to the TRAITS/MERGES/TRUST footers, give each screen its own `ScrollList`, and clamp the detail offset against real content length (`route_detail_focus_key` currently clamps with `usize::MAX`, so the stored offset can run past the bottom).
      **Watch:** this is deliberately the cheap interim — P505's pane chrome supersedes the focus-indicator half; do not build a second chrome system.
      **Done when:** pressing Enter visibly changes the pane on every screen; the footer says how to scroll and how to get back; switching tabs and returning preserves position.

## Group 128: Trait store consolidation for launch — one package per family, authored like `refactor-direct` (owner-ordered 2026-07-26)

Measured 2026-07-26: **21 packages under `.ctx/traits/`, and three families account for fourteen of them** — implement {quick, default, smart, strict, phase} = 1,632 loc; refactor {direct, default, quick, smart, strict} = 982 loc; plan {plan, direct, quick, complex} = 452 loc. They should be three packages. **`variant.import()` is used NOWHERE** — not in `.ctx/traits`, not in `modules/core/builtins`, not in the CDK's own tests: P396 landed the variants API and nothing has ever exercised it, so the first fold is also its first contact with reality.

**THE REFERENCE STYLE — `refactor-direct` (108 loc, five files).** `index.ts` declares ONLY the trait: identity, metadata (family/variant), intent, and the procedure wired from named steps — a table of contents a reader can hold in their head. `data.ts` holds the domain's nouns (slots and ports as named consts, exported as `slot.*` / `port.*` namespaces). `schema.ts` the shapes, `agent.ts` the roles. `sequence/` holds one file per step named for the **domain concept** — `annotation.ts`, `checklist.ts`, `implementation.ts` — each a default export. Ubiquitous language, domain separated from wiring, one bounded concept per module.

**PRAGMATISM DOCTRINE (owner, 2026-07-26) — binding on every phase in this group.** Split by **entity / domain / intent**; split genuinely **shared** things by **primitive** (one `data.ts` for slots+ports, one `schema.ts`) exactly as the reference does. Do **NOT** oversplit: no file-per-slot, no helper layers, no factory patterns, no indirection introduced for symmetry. **The test: a module must earn its filename with a domain word, and a reader must be able to find a step by its concept name. A module that exists only to be imported once and adds no domain name gets inlined.** Fewer, well-named files beat many tidy ones. If a fold produces more files than the five-file reference for a comparable procedure, that is a smell, not progress.

- [x] **P529** — Prune first: delete what we are not shipping, so nothing dead gets refactored.
      **Why:** refactoring or folding a package we are about to delete is pure waste, and launch should not ship a store full of experiments. Candidates found: `benchmark-refactor` (3 loc — literally `autoResearch("benchmark-refactor")`, a parameterized instance of auto-research, already a "variant" expressed as a function call), `gp2-demo` (21 loc, golden-path demo), `process-kit-demo` (75 loc, demo for P449's kit which has never landed), `engineering-standards` (26 loc), plus any package nothing references.
      **Where:** for each of the 21 packages establish: is it referenced (Justfile recipes, built-ins, other traits' dependencies/variants, docs, the launch narrative), is it superseded by a sibling or a built-in, is it `status = "draft"`, and when was it last touched. Then delete, or keep with a one-line reason recorded. Demos are a deliberate decision, not a default: either they are shipped examples users will read (keep, and make them exemplary) or they are scaffolding (delete). `benchmark-refactor` specifically: decide whether the function-call instantiation pattern stays or becomes a `variant.import()` leaf.
      **Watch:** deleting a trait moves digests — trust re-approval follows (P532); check the launch content (Groups 58-60) before deleting anything a post references; keep `guarded-change` (the launch-blog hero trait) regardless of size.
      **Done when:** every surviving package has a stated reason to exist; nothing in the store is unreferenced scaffolding; the deletions are one commit with the reasons in its body.
- [ ] **P530** — Fold the implement family: five packages into one, restyled in the same pass.
      **Why:** the largest family (1,632 loc across five packages) and the one carrying today's drift (implement-quick's canonical is still pre-kit). Folding and restyling are ONE operation — restyling five packages you are about to collapse is wasted work — and this is the first real exercise of `variant.import()`.
      **Where:** one `implement` package in the reference layout: `index.ts` declaring the family and its variants map; `data.ts` / `schema.ts` / `agent.ts` for the shared nouns; `sequence/<concept>.ts` per step named for what it does (draft, implementation, review, fixes, commit — not `step1.ts`); each variant a `variant.import()` leaf carrying ONLY what genuinely differs (iteration counts, reviewer seats, gate chains, exhausted behavior). Quantify before writing: diff the five members' sources and confirm they are variants of one procedure rather than different procedures — if a member is genuinely a different procedure (implement-phase may be), it stays its own package and that is recorded, not forced.
      **Watch:** THE PRAGMATISM DOCTRINE ABOVE — the fold's success metric is a smaller, more readable store, not a deeper one; `variant.import()` has never run in anger, so expect to find gaps in it and fix the API rather than working around it in trait source; every variant must still resolve by its existing name (`implement:quick` etc.) so config, Justfile recipes and muscle memory keep working; behavior is preserved exactly — this is a restructure, not a redesign.
      **Done when:** one `implement` package resolves all existing variant names; the store loses four packages; the source reads like the reference; a live `implement:quick` run behaves as before.
- [ ] **P531** — Fold refactor and plan, using what P530 learned.
      **Why:** refactor {direct, default, quick, smart, strict} = 982 loc and plan {plan, direct, quick, complex} = 452 loc, same shape as implement. Doing them after implement means the variants API's rough edges are already fixed and the layout is proven, so these are mechanical.
      **Where:** same target layout and doctrine. `refactor-direct` is the reference — it becomes a leaf of the folded `refactor` package without losing what makes it exemplary. The plan family folds the same way, with `plan` as the family root.
      **Watch:** `refactor-direct` is annotation-driven and structurally unlike the review-loop variants (P362 kept it "annotation-driven" deliberately) — if it does not fold cleanly, it stays separate and the reason is recorded rather than forced into a variants map; same pragmatism metric.
      **Done when:** `refactor` and `plan` are each one package resolving all existing variant names; the store is down to roughly a third of today's package count.
- [ ] **P532** — Rebuild, relock, re-approve, and gate the store so this cannot rot again.
      **Why:** the whole store's canonicals, locks and trust digests move during this group, and today NOTHING gates `.ctx/traits/*` for source-vs-canonical drift — `just builtin-traits-drift` covers only `modules/core/builtins/`, which is exactly how implement-quick's canonical went stale unnoticed.
      **Where:** rebuild every surviving package's `generated/index.toml` from source; refresh every `trait.lock`; re-approve machine trust for the moved digests (a human step — digests are the trust boundary); then extend the drift gate to repo traits so a source edit without a rebuild fails, handling the legitimately-source-less package rather than failing it.
      **Watch:** absorbs P515's rebuild half — do not do it twice; trust re-approval is deliberately human; the gate lands only after the churn settles, since gating a shape mid-refactor just creates noise.
      **Done when:** every `.ctx/traits/*` package rebuilds byte-identically from its source; locks current; trust clean; a deliberate source edit without a rebuild fails the gate.

## Group 129: Authoring style, codified (follow-up to Group 128 — write down what the folds proved, owner-ordered 2026-07-26 as a follow-up, not a prerequisite)

- [ ] **P533** — The trait authoring pattern becomes doctrine plus a structural check.
      **Why:** "follow the `refactor-direct` style" is folklore until it is written down, and it will drift the moment someone authors the next trait — the store already contains three different layouts (domain-split, primitive-split, everything-inline) which is how it got here. Deliberately a FOLLOW-UP: the folds are what prove which rules are real, so this codifies observed practice rather than guessing in advance.
      **Where:** the pattern as authoring doctrine — index declares only; domain nouns in `data.ts`; one step per `sequence/<concept>.ts` named for the concept; variants carry only their differences; and the pragmatism rule as a first-class clause (**split by entity/domain/intent, share by primitive, never oversplit — a module earns its filename with a domain word; no helper layers, no factory patterns**). Then a cheap structural check in the build lints (P436's neighborhood) for the mechanical parts only: index.ts declares rather than defines, no orphan modules imported once with no domain name, no inline prompt body past a threshold. It ships with the CDK docs so external authors get the same pattern.
      **Watch:** lint the mechanical, document the judgment — do NOT try to enforce taste; a check that fights a legitimate exception is worse than no check, so every rule needs a stated escape and a reason.
      **Done when:** the pattern is documented with `refactor-direct` as the worked example; a new trait scaffold produces that layout; the structural check catches the three legacy layouts and passes the folded families.

## Group 130: Trust across checkouts — the 2026-07-26 approval-ping-pong incident, both levels fixed (owner-ordered 2026-07-26)

Incident, fully diagnosed: ctx-trait and ctx-gate carried hand-copied copies of `implement-quick` that diverged after P515's rebuild (ctx-gate: trait.lock recorded the NEW canonical while `generated/index.toml` still held the OLD bytes — internally contradictory, and no verb noticed). The owner's `trust approve` from the old-bytes checkout stamped the old digest, and P419's one-lineage-per-trait upsert SILENTLY REVOKED the approval the sibling repo's runs depended on — every approve from one side broke the other, while `trust list` said "verified" and the run refusal said "no verified record" (a verified-but-stale record existed; the message hid it). Design verdict: content-addressed MACHINE-GLOBAL trust is correct (same bytes = same trait everywhere; worktrees and P439 run-anywhere depend on it); the flaw is the NAME-keyed one-lineage constraint assuming a trait id has exactly one current content per machine — plus three missing guards; plus the underlying disease of hand-copied duplicate packages.

- [ ] **P534** — Trust mechanism: append-only approval history, start-time pinning, and the guards approve was missing (owner-settled semantics 2026-07-26; three live incidents in two days are the spec).
      **Why:** the one-lineage rule was downgrade protection (approving a new version auto-revoked the old), executed silently and absolutely — which produced approval ping-pong across checkouts, a clobbered record mid-batch, and a working-tree-vs-HEAD split where every check the owner ran said "verified" while every run said "no verified record". The owner's scenarios (2026-07-26) settle the model.
      **Where — THE SETTLED MODEL, four parts.** (1) APPEND-ONLY HISTORY: every approval is a permanent record `(trait-id, digest, approved-at, seq)` — `seq` is a monotonic sequence the store assigns under its existing flock (total order; wall-clock kept for humans, never for logic). The FACT of an approval is never deleted; what changes over time is only startability. (2) DERIVED CURRENCY: the newest approval per trait id is CURRENT; older approved digests are SUPERSEDED — historically approved, not startable. Starting a run on a superseded digest refuses with the honest sentence: "approved <t1>, superseded by <digest> at <t2> — re-approve to run this version" (re-approving IS the deliberate, journaled downgrade — owner scenario C). (3) PIN AT START — THE OWNER'S RULE, VERBATIM DOCTRINE: the trust gate evaluates EXACTLY ONCE, at session start, and records the verdict (digest + approval seq) into run provenance. **A started session never re-checks trust — not at resume, not at call/set/next, not after a days-long ask pause, not at completion. Started = approved, by definition; the receipt carries the evidence.** Implementation consequence: `preflight_lifecycle_trust` is currently invoked on EVERY mutating verb (io/run.rs call sites at start/call/set/advance) — those become: check at session CREATION only; every later verb verifies the session's pinned evidence exists, never re-resolves the store. This makes a run's behavior a pure function of its start state (scenarios A and B fall out: supersession and later approvals never touch an in-flight or paused run). (4) `trust block <trait> [--digest]` gates NEW STARTS only — it does NOT stop live or paused sessions; instead blocking REPORTS the live sessions running that digest ("2 sessions run this digest — kill explicitly if intended", composing with the dashboard kill and P512's typed cancelled outcome). Stopping work is always a separate, explicit, human act — never a side effect of a store write.
      **Where — the guards.** (a) approve REFUSES when the package's trait.lock canonical ≠ the computed canonical of the bytes being approved ("lock records a different canonical than the built output; rebuild before approving" — would have stopped ALL THREE incidents at first contact). (b) approve states what it supersedes, by digest and age ("supersedes dfcd90e3, approved 26h ago"). (c) approve warns when the bytes being approved differ from HEAD's committed state ("approving UNCOMMITTED trait content — worktree runs load the committed version, which stays superseded") — the working-tree/HEAD split made visible at the moment it matters. (d) the preflight refusal distinguishes "no record at all" from "superseded" from "blocked", each with both digests and the change commit. (e) `trust approve --all-current`: after a deliberate store-wide rebuild (the P515 case — fifteen moved digests, fifteen commands, fifteen chances to miss one), review once and approve the resulting digest set as one journaled act.
      **Watch:** store schema evolution must migrate the existing records cleanly (legacy digest-only records keep working); P419's never-paste-a-digest law holds for interactive flows (digest flags are the script surface; the TUI picks from lists); P473/P527's trust screen renders history (current / superseded / blocked per trait); the store stays flock-serialized + atomic; scenario A's bytes caveat is real and stays documented — "an X-run finishes" holds wherever X's bytes still exist (worktrees pin them; an in-place run whose trait was edited mid-run fails the SOURCE-pinning check on resume, which is correct and is not a trust decision).
      **Done when:** the owner's three scenarios pass as written (A: update-unapproved — in-flight X-run finishes, new Y-runs blocked; B: update-approved — in-flight X-run finishes on its pin; C: downgrade — new X-runs refuse as superseded until re-approved, and the re-approval is one journaled command); a session paused for days on an ask resumes with zero trust consultation; blocking a digest reports its live sessions and stops none of them; approve on lock-drifted or uncommitted bytes says so; the receipt of any completed run proves its start-time approval from the ledger alone.
- [ ] **P535** — Trust practice: one canonical home for shared dogfood traits — hand-copies become unrepresentable.
      **Why:** the incident's enabling condition was two repos carrying independently-drifting hand-copies of one trait id. The product's own machinery exists to eliminate exactly this (vendored install + lock + digest, P438-440; manifest `extends`, P443 landed) — and the dogfood repos do not use it. With one canonical package consumed by both repos, digests are identical by construction and even the OLD one-lineage trust would have worked.
      **Where:** the shared family (implement/refactor/plan + their @ctx-traits/agents kit dependency) gets ONE canonical home — this repo — and ctx-gate consumes it through the product, not `cp`. Mechanism decided in-phase against what actually exists: `path:` specs are currently a typed `NotYetSupported` refusal on the install surface (distribution.rs:21) — lifting that for local path-form install (vendor + lock + digest intact, no registry round-trip) is the natural fit for same-machine sibling repos and turns a designed refusal into the designed feature; npm publish is the alternative but carries P519's uncertified-publish debt. Propagation stays EXPLICIT (P443's no-silent-propagation law): a rebuild here reaches ctx-gate only via its own `update`, never automatically.
      **Watch:** SEQUENCING — fold first (Group 128), then share: one folded `implement` package crosses the repo boundary instead of five siblings; CROSS-REPO CONSTRAINT (standing rule: edits scope to the cwd repo, never fan out) — this phase delivers the ctx-trait side and the mechanism; the consumption step in ctx-gate is an owner-run command this phase documents, or a phase in ctx-gate's own plan; digest identity across repos becomes an assertable property (`ctx traits list --json` in both repos shows the same canonical per shared trait) — make it a check, not a hope.
      **Done when:** ctx-gate consumes the shared family with no hand-copied trait files; a rebuild in ctx-trait reaches ctx-gate only via an explicit update and arrives digest-identical; one `trust approve` covers both repos by construction; the lock-vs-generated divergence class from the incident is structurally impossible in the consuming repo (vendored content is never hand-edited — the ownership-marker discipline applies).

## Group 131: Leftovers stop being free — an earned exit for guarded loops (owner-ordered 2026-07-26; diagnosed from the commit log)

The refinement loop exits after one round while shipping a pile of leftovers, and the git log is the evidence: P510 ships two (one of them "add the sidecar to `.docs/PRODUCT.md`", reason `out-of-budget`), P505 three, P461 three, P492 one — repeatedly for work a competent engineer could have done inside the run. **The cause is structural, not motivational, and three facts compose it.** (1) `guardedProduction`'s exit predicate is `approved(v1) ∧ … ∧ approved(vN) ∧ alsoRequire` (packages/agents/src/process.ts:176-186); the `leftovers` slot is only an `extraOutputs` sink on the review seat (.ctx/traits/implement-default/source/shared.ts:413) — written every round, read by nothing that decides anything. (2) `LEFTOVER_DOCTRINE` states this as doctrine: *"a leftover list is never treated as failure and never affects status or loop exit"* (packages/agents/src/index.ts:335). Emitting one therefore costs NOTHING, and a round-1 approval carrying ten of them is a legal exit. (3) The only counter-pressure is prose — the two-question test in that same string — which is exactly the prompt hacking this group rejects, and the log shows it losing. Compounding it, the `out-of-budget` reason class is self-contradictory: its description says "explicitly outside this phase's stated scope" while the doctrine's own second question says work outside the phase's scope "is not this phase's leftover to report at all" — so the class describes something that should never be reported, and its only live use is the thing its description forbids ("the round ran out").

**THE RULE (owner, 2026-07-26):** a leftover costs rounds. *You may stop the moment you are carrying nothing; if you are carrying something, you owe the floor.* Default floor **3 rounds**, early exit when the round is genuinely clean.

**NO NEW RUNTIME.** The whole mechanism is expressible in the guard vocabulary that already ships and that **no trait uses today**: `condition.count(slot).equals(0)` and `.where(field, value)` (packages/cdk/src/condition.ts:56-66,134 — `where` compares with `serde_json::Value` equality at modules/core/src/procedure/runtime/guards.rs:645, so an array literal such as `[]` is a valid filter value), `condition.iterationAtLeast(n)` (packages/cdk/src/condition.ts:209,388, evaluated against the loop frame's **0-based** `iteration_index` at guards.rs:532-550), and `guardedProduction`'s existing `alsoRequire: GuardValue` conjunct. A first-class `min-n` loop primitive was considered and rejected for now: it expresses the floor but not the clean-exit escape, so the guard composition would still be needed alongside it.

- [x] **P537** — Earned exit: the kit makes carrying something cost rounds, and seals the round so it cannot be gamed.
      **Why:** the exit predicate is where the behavior actually lives; every prose fix so far has been argued with rather than obeyed. Making the carried list a term in `until` inverts the economics in one place, for every consumer, without touching a prompt.
      **Where:** two new options on `GuardedProductionOptions` (packages/agents/src/process.ts). `carry?: SlotHandle` — the round's carried follow-on list; `minRounds?: number` — the 1-based round floor, lowered INSIDE the kit to `condition.iterationAtLeast(minRounds - 1)` so no caller ever carries the off-by-one (round 1 is index 0). The composed predicate becomes `approved(v1..vN) ∧ alsoRequire ∧ sealed ∧ count(carry).where("needs", []).equals(0) ∧ ( count(carry).equals(0) ∨ iterationAtLeast(minRounds - 1) )`. The `where("needs", [])` conjunct is how a leftover is forced to NAME its external prerequisite: an entry with no `needs` names nothing outside the run, which makes it unfinished scope rather than follow-on work, and it can never earn an exit at any round. Build-time validation: `minRounds` requires `carry`, must be `> 1` and `<= rounds` (a floor at or above the budget makes early exit unreachable and every run exhaust). **Both options absent ⇒ the built loop stays byte-identical**, so the `planning` composite and every family that never adopted the leftover contract (refactor, plan, auto-research) are untouched and provable by the drift gate.
      **Where — THE ROUND SEAL, and it is correctness, not polish.** `until` is re-evaluated **after every step of the body**, not at the body's end (modules/core/src/procedure/runtime/guards.rs:4-15,52-62). That is harmless today only because every term is a verdict slot written late in the round. `iterationAtLeast` breaks it immediately: at round 3 the guard flips true right after the **produce** step while the verdict slots and gate result still hold round 2's values, so round 3's fresh code would ship **unreviewed**. Fix with a per-round seal built from the same deterministic `project` + `operation.literal` pattern `deriveParkReportStep` already uses (.ctx/traits/implement-default/source/shared.ts:476-498): an invented `<id>-sealed` boolean, an `<id>-unseal` step prepended to the body clearing it, an `<id>-seal` step appended after `afterReview` setting it, and `condition.equals(sealed, true)` conjoined into `until`. Declared only alongside `carry`, to keep the byte-identity promise above.
      **Watch:** the seal also closes TWO pre-existing latent defects worth naming in the commit rather than fixing silently — (a) because `until` can fire immediately after the last review seat, `afterReview` is SKIPPED on an approving exit, so `port:park-report` currently carries the PREVIOUS round's revise entries on a successful run; (b) in a multi-seat loop where round 1 was approved by seat 2 but revise by seat 1, round 2's `until` sees seat 1's fresh approval beside seat 2's STALE round-1 approval and can exit before seat 2 ever reviews round 2's work. Both disappear once the seal exists. Do not widen the seal to loops that pass no `carry` in this phase — that is a bytes change to every other family and belongs with its own evidence.
      **Done when:** a `guardedProduction` declaring `carry` + `minRounds` lowers to that exact predicate and refuses `minRounds >= rounds` at build time; a composite declaring neither rebuilds byte-identically under `just builtin-traits-drift`; the seal steps bracket the body in the built canonical.
- [x] **P538** — Delete the escape hatch from the type, and let the doctrine describe the guard instead of arguing with the model.
      **Why:** `out-of-budget` is the class actually being used to ship undone work, and it cannot be repaired by better wording — its own description contradicts the doctrine it lives in. Deleting it makes "I ran out of rounds" **unrepresentable** rather than discouraged, which is the difference between a rule and a sentence about a rule. What remains — `needs-unlanded`, `needs-human` — both name something outside the run by construction, and P537's guard forces that something to be named concretely.
      **Where:** `leftoverSchema.reason` drops to `["needs-unlanded", "needs-human"]` (packages/agents/src/index.ts:348), and its description loses the whole "never merely that a round ran out" clause it no longer has to argue. `LEFTOVER_DOCTRINE` (:335) loses the sentence that made leftovers exit-neutral and gains one line stating the mechanical rule — a DESCRIPTION of the guard, never a substitute for it; the reviewer's only channel for in-task-doable work stays a blocker, which already keeps the loop running. Reconcile `.docs/PRODUCT.md` §2793: the three-class list, and the now-false claim that a non-empty list "never affects a verdict's approval status" — approval is unchanged, but loop EXIT is not, and the section must say which.
      **Watch:** `out-of-budget` appears in exactly three places outside generated output — packages/agents/src/index.ts (both sites above), .docs/PRODUCT.md:2793, and scripts/byte_compare.rs (:2116, :2121-2126, :20780, :20802, :20830). **The byte_compare battery is off `just test` by owner decision (54706ca) and stays knowingly stale — no repair pass here**, per the standing no-behavior-freezing-tests ruling. Also REJECTED and worth recording so it is not re-proposed: enforcing non-empty `needs` via the repo's `{first, rest}` pattern (.ctx/traits/implement-phase/source/index.ts:64-73) — it works, but nests an object inside a rendered table row (PRODUCT.md:2823 warns that path degrades to unrendered) and invalidates both documented `jq` adapters at PRODUCT.md:2801 and :2813, for no force the guard does not already supply.
      **Done when:** no non-generated source outside the disabled battery mentions `out-of-budget`; the doctrine string states the mechanical rule and no longer claims exit-neutrality; PRODUCT.md §2793 describes two reason classes and the exit rule accurately.
- [x] **P539** — The implement family adopts the floor, and the digests move once.
      **Why:** the kit change is inert until a trait passes `carry`; this is where the behavior the log complains about actually stops.
      **Where:** `familyProcedure` (.ctx/traits/implement-default/source/shared.ts:528-611) threads `carry: leftovers` and a `minRounds` onto the **`building` composite only** — never `planning`, which carries no leftover list and must stay byte-identical. Floor **`minRounds: 3`** for every variant: default/smart/strict/phase run `buildRounds ?? 5`, leaving two rounds of headroom above the floor. Then rebuild all five packages' `generated/index.toml`, refresh `trait.lock`, and re-approve the moved digests (human step — digests are the trust boundary).
      **Watch:** **implement-quick's `rounds: 10, onExhausted: "continue"` (70e40de) stays UNTOUCHED by owner decision** — this phase adds only `carry`/`minRounds` to it. Stated consequence, so it is not mistaken for an oversight: a quick run that exhausts unapproved still reaches the commit tail and commits with unresolved blockers, which remains a live divergence from `.docs/PRODUCT.md`:2827 ("`onExhausted: "block"` is the shared, unconditional default"). The floor makes exhaustion rarer; it does not make that path honest, and closing it is a separate owner call. Sequencing: this lands BEFORE Group 128's fold, so P530 folds a family that already carries the rule and P532's rebuild is the second and final digest move, not a third.
      **Done when:** all five implement packages rebuild from source with the floor in the canonical; `just implement-phase-gates` green; `ctx traits trust list --stale` names exactly the five expected moved digests and nothing else; and the two live proofs the owner runs — one small phase carrying a genuine external leftover takes the floor instead of exiting at round 1, and one genuinely clean phase still exits early at round 1.

## Group 132: The correction retry actually corrects — what we send a model after we reject its output (diagnosed 2026-07-26 from run-7619031a)

**The incident, fully traced.** `implement-quick` on P504, reviewer seat `smart-1` (opencode, gpt-5.6-sol, `session-mode=persistent`).
